## Detailed development record: root_literature_audit.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

## Root literature and source audit

The audit date is 15 November 2026. The attached original manuscript and attached arXiv versions are treated as mathematical inputs, not as verified authorities. Source-specific comparisons occur in the paper. No result is claimed to have priority on the basis of a search failing to find it.

### Smooth monodromy and characteristic numbers

Searches used the titles of Eells–Kuiper, Kervaire–Milnor and Cerf, and combinations of “mapping torus”, “homotopy sphere”, “spin-c Eells-Kuiper”, “equivariant diffeomorphism”, “semifree circle action” and “Blakers-Massey”. The primary scans of Eells–Kuiper (1962), Kervaire–Milnor (1963), Cerf (1970) and Poénaru (1971) were opened. The paper uses the exact statements and formulas recorded in its Part C and in C_push_report.md. The current arXiv source of Crowley–Nordström's Exotic G2-manifolds was checked for the spin-c normalization. Durán–Rigas, arXiv:0708.1577, §3.2, was read to test and reject the proposed inference from circle equivariance to a small mapping-class order.

The original Cerf1970 scan initially exceeded the web extractor's capacity; an independent local download succeeded, and primary page8 was inspected. The Γ4=0 book was bibliographically verified only and is explicitly marked not opened. It is not cited as the proof of the dimension-six identification. The scripts c_fetch_primary.py, c_review_primary.py and c_render_primary.py preserve the access record while leaving the scans and private OCR outside the deliverable.

Further searches and primary readings on circle actions, orbit knots and lift independence are documented in the C-related section notes and their bibliography. These establish comparison tools. They do not identify the annular twist of this family with an integer Haefliger invariant, and no numerical value of [Phi] is inferred from them.

### New trace obstruction and its limits

Queries included “Shioda On Mordell Weil lattices 1990 theorem 8.6 discriminant formula PDF”, “Oguiso Shioda Mordell Weil lattice rational elliptic surface 1991 pdf”, and the exact phrases “elliptic surface genus 3 I12 Mordell Weil”, “b^3 a^4 Mordell elliptic”, and “y^2=x^3 3b 2a elliptic surface”. The equation-specific searches did not locate the exact trace statement in B.3.4. This absence is not a claim of novelty.

The Rikkyo repository verified the original metadata of Shioda (1990), On the Mordell-Weil lattices, Commentarii Mathematici Universitatis Sancti Pauli39(2),211–240, DOI10.14992/00009994. Its direct PDF endpoint returned429. The primary author-uploaded PDF was then opened successfully at https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Tetsuji-Shioda/publication/247043151_On_the_Mordell-Weil_Lattices/links/5704b7fe08ae13eb88b69184/On-the-Mordell-Weil-Lattices.pdf . Theorems1.3,8.4,8.6 and8.7, Corollary5.3, and equations(8.11),(8.12),(8.20) were checked. The determinant relation follows from the narrow-lattice statement and its finite index.

Schütt and Shioda, Elliptic Surfaces, arXiv:0907.0298v3, was also opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/0907.0298 . Section11.10 equation(22) explicitly records the determinant relation; Section6.10 gives the Hodge numbers and Corollary6.13 the rank formula. This is corroboration by the originating author, not a substitute for checking the original height theorem. The concrete sections, intersection numbers, local infinity expansions, saturation congruences and trace obstruction in B.3.4 are calculated afresh in the accompanying exact programs.

The obstruction applies to a split elliptic normalization. A genus-one torsor without a section is a separate possibility. The report and statement preserve this distinction. Likewise, the polarized hypotheses in Mumford/Alexeev–Nakamura and the polarizable-variation hypothesis in analytic Néron-model results are not discarded when considering an arbitrary nonpolarized analytic filling.

### Interval arithmetic and boundary levels

The primary Arb paper, Johansson, arXiv:1611.02831, §§2–3, and the official python-flint Arb constructor documentation were opened. The E.4 proof gives its own series, geometric tail bounds and strict contraction argument; only rational rectangle endpoints were imported from the earlier certificate. The exact cover was checked separately with rational arithmetic. The D.4 certificate explicitly depends on these newly certified roots; it does not claim arithmetic independence from them. Its i-neighbourhood proof is analytic, and its rho/cusp code implements its own Fourier series and derivative bounds at256 bits. The compact part uses192-bit Arb enclosures and an exact refinement tree.

The D.4 source comparisons with Lin–Wang, Chen–Kuo–Lin and Chen–Fu–Lin are recorded in D_global_literature_audit.md and integrated into D.4.8. The distinction is between a Green function on a fixed torus and the composite modulus function evaluated at a zero of the Weierstrass function. The precise latter level-set theorem is proved by the included certificates and C1 level transport.

### Hodge obstruction and analytic cusp alternatives

Queries on 15 November 2026 included “six sphere semistable degeneration”, “normal crossing limit complex S6”, “Moishezon central fibre Hodge de Rham”, and “Conjecture 8.2 Hodge”. They did not locate the particular application proved in B.4.3. We make no priority claim. Chen's *On the Hodge Structures of Global Smoothings of Normal Crossing Varieties*, arXiv:2404.19229v2 (16 October 2026), was opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.19229 and https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19229v2 . Definition 2.3 and Theorem 2.4(4), including the K1 condition preceding parts (3)–(4), were read. Graf and Kovács, *Potentially Du Bois spaces* (2014), was opened at https://sites.math.washington.edu/~kovacs/current/papers/Graf_Kovacs__Potentially_Du_Bois_spaces.pdf ; §1.D, p.120, gives the proper Du Bois cohomology assertion. The exact hypotheses, derivation and remaining analytic scope are recorded in B_hodge_report.md and the independent E_B_hodge_referee.md.

To test whether the unproved class-C hypothesis might follow automatically, we searched “non Kahler semistable degeneration K3 Hopf Nishiguchi” and “Toric Kato manifolds degeneration”. We opened Nishiguchi's original MPI/88-48 preprint, *Degeneration of K3 surfaces, II*, at https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/3390/1/preprint_1988_48.pdf . Theorem 1.4 and Example 2.1 explicitly describe the non-Kähler alternative. We also opened Istrati, Otiman, Pontecorvo, and Ruggiero's *Toric Kato manifolds*, Journal de l'École polytechnique—Mathématiques 9 (2022), 1347–1395, DOI10.5802/jep.208, at https://www.numdam.org/item/JEP_2022__9__1347_0.pdf . Theorem 7.1 and its construction were read. These sources are cautionary analogues, not an S6 construction. A further search for “semistable degeneration compact complex manifolds first Betti number irregularity central components” did not provide the needed converse forcing arbitrary vertical components into class C.

### Annular twist, graphing and framing

Roseman and Takase, *High-codimensional knots spun about manifolds*, arXiv:math/0609055v2, was opened and §2.2, Theorem 2.1, §4 and Remark 5.1 were checked. Its graphing construction and characteristic formula concern a specified framed embedding. Koytcheff's *Graphing, homotopy groups of spheres, and spaces of long links and knots* (2025) was opened in the original Cambridge publication; Remark 5.9 supplies an associated-graded finite-type analogy with the Casson invariant, not an identification of the present annular orbit map with a classical torus knot. The primary-page comparisons are also recorded in the agents' C reports. The numbers obtained from a classical T(3,4) knot or an unrelated Brieskorn sphere were not assigned to the present monodromy. No comparison proving that assignment was found.

Engel's original Remark 8.3, printed p.23, was reopened and compared directly with the actual cusp periods. The literal extension of the quotient by translational ker γ leaves is false in the scope stated there: C.3.12 proves that every Hausdorff-valued continuous extension constant on those regular leaves collapses the entire cusp fibre. The supplied notes already recorded the simpler raw-character nonextension; that provenance is acknowledged. Earlier attempt notes which inferred an actual marked S³ quotient from the remark are retained but expressly superseded. C.3.13 then constructs a different, corrected map by an integral one-form extension which changes those leaves near the cusp. Its three-dimensional target framing is proved; the six-dimensional comparison remains open.

For B.4.5 we additionally searched “normal crossing H1 Kähler Friedman 1.5”, “proper normal crossing Dolbeault normalization surjective”, and “normal crossings ddbar cohomology analytic”. Friedman (1983), *Global smoothings of varieties with normal crossings*, was located at the original Annals metadata page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/1983/118-1/p06 ; the text of Proposition 1.5 was not opened and is not used as a proved premise. The direct analytic degree-one proof in B.4.5 instead constructs the branch resolutions, justifies local analytic exactness and lifts a Dolbeault cocycle. This removes the former simple-crossing restriction without importing an unchecked theorem.

### Citation format and evidence retention

The bibliography uses author–date citations and records opened/not-opened status in every entry. The official CTAN apacite page was checked as an available APA6 format implementation; it is a typesetting source, not a mathematical reference. Web result files in evidence contain metadata and retrieval identifiers only. Full copyrighted page bodies, primary scans and private OCR extracts are not republished.

## Detailed development record: root_B_analytic_attempts.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

## Further attack on the unrestricted analytic filling

This record concerns the actual family after arbitrary finite parameter base change. It does not weaken the open problem to a polarized or toroidal subclass. The status remains Open.

The marked map to the t-line extends meromorphically by B.4.4. One proposed next step was to compare a graph modification with the explicitly constructed local toroidal models and conclude that every central normalization is class C. That comparison is not proved. A punctured isomorphism between proper families need not extend meromorphically: multiplication by exp(1/s) on the projective-line product gives an explicit elementary obstruction. The example invalidates the general extension principle, not the existence of the S6 filling. A special argument controlling the actual comparison is still required.

Kajiwara, Kato, and Nakayama (2008), original journal PDF, Theorem 0.1 and Section5.1, especially Theorem5.1.3, were opened at https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms150104.pdf . The proper-model construction does allow unpolarized log complex tori. It therefore improves on the hypothesis of a polarized Mumford theorem. It nevertheless presupposes a log torus with an admissible pairing. The missing global log extension and marked affine-torsor comparison are not supplied by that theorem. No claim that it classifies arbitrary proper analytic fillings is made.

Kamenova's original four-page author manuscript was opened at https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~kamenova/homepage_files/degen.pdf . Its Theorem3.1 assumes a Kähler total space and trivial canonical bundle. These conditions have not been established for a surface slice of an arbitrary filling here. The undated manuscript is cited as n.d.; an unverified publication date is not invented.

Schröer and Siebert (2002), original manuscript at https://www.math.uni-duesseldorf.de/~schroeer/publications_pdf/kodaira.pdf , introduction and Section3 Proposition3.1, were checked. The author bibliography supplies the published collection and pages193–222. Hopf normalizations in their construction belong to degenerations of primary Kodaira surfaces, so that construction is not a degeneration of the present regular torus fibres. It is retained as an analytic alternative to examine, without changing the nearby family.

The already-read Nishiguchi example was reopened at Example2.1. It shows why proper semistability alone does not imply class-C normalization, even for another family with Kähler nearby fibres. It does not demonstrate that a Hopf component occurs in this S6 problem. The finite-orbit fan components in A.2.4 are all class C: mixed components are complete toric threefolds, and pure-top components are bimeromorphic to the stated ruled bundle over S. The analytic unramified torsor alternative is still Kähler by B.3.8.

A further candidate is a non-Kähler cusp component with rational attaching surfaces. A naive fundamental-group argument only excludes attachments that leave a component loop alive. It must not be used when a Kato divisor in the overlap can kill that loop. The stronger degree-one comparison criterion now being checked in B.4.6 explicitly follows the maps on the overlaps and addresses that restricted construction. It does not force an unknown analytic component into a toric-Kato class.

The attempted horizontal classification also remains unproved. Non-isotrivial elliptic torsors over the genus-three cover are Kähler, but it has not been shown that every horizontal normalization of an arbitrary filling is such a ruled torsor model. Meromorphic extension of the base map alone does not give this identification. An analytic MMP or a generalized Albanese construction cannot be invoked with unstated Kähler or Hodge-degeneration hypotheses.

Searches on 15 November2026 included: “proper analytic degeneration semistable central fibre non Kahler bimeromorphic extensions uniqueness Moishezon graph”; “degeneration complex tori Hopf surfaces semistable Ueno Nakamura”; “analytic semistable degenerations complex tori non Kahler central components classification”; “analytic Néron complex tori degeneration”; and the exact KKN, Kamenova and Schröer–Siebert titles. These searches did not supply the required unrestricted comparison. This is a search record, not a priority or nonexistence claim.

## Root check of the all-fan topology proof

The source of A.4.8 was read in full. The original AKMW Theorems1.6.1 and2.7.1 and their finite polyhedral proof on p552, analytic correspondence in Section5.5 p565, and barycentric construction in Section5.6 were opened at https://personal.math.ubc.ca/~karu/papers/semist.pdf . Matsuki's original erratum, Section2 p2, was opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0006038 . The invalid strong-factorization algorithm is not used. The proof factors finite strict cone complexes, then realizes their monomial blow-ups analytically. Temporary pure-q blow-ups are explicitly allowed and their noncompact centres have no top cohomology contribution. This is the scope required for the claimed rank invariants. The result is accepted in the stated toroidal category, independently of the separately preserved D referee.

## Root C framing diagnostics

The proposed shortcut that the quotient torus gamma-u-w bounds a four-chain inside the cusp body was rejected after E's check: it represents the central oriented normalization three-cycle and need not bound. The relative-Chern-class approach therefore still requires a global chain and an actual comparison of stable frames. No Pontryagin number or cap integer is inferred from the local affine frame.

The exact program root_C_exterior_h3.py computes A0 and the two third-exterior-power norm matrices. Their stacked rank is3, and their common kernel is generated rationally by (0,1,1,6) in the basis (gamma-u-w, gamma-u-delta, gamma-w-delta, u-w-delta). The gamma-u-w class has nonzero norm at both finite places. These are exact lattice diagnostics; integral homology of the finite quotients is not silently identified with invariant lattices.

For a possible global-chain construction, the cusp-complement orbit space is examined as a pushout of its two oriented three-dimensional finite orbit fibres along the regular three-torus. The degree-three cover and degree-four cover give the top-dimensional relation (3,-4), so the regular three-torus has coefficient12 in its resulting rank-one cokernel. There is an additional degree-three term from the degree-two kernel, so this observation is not a complete homology or framing calculation. The coefficient12 agrees with C.3.16, but has not been assigned to the missing characteristic integer J.

## Fifth-hour continuation and superseded C stopping point

The preceding C diagnostics are historical. C.3.18–C.3.22 subsequently constructed the actual chain, identified its circle line, and calibrated both signs. C.3.24 now proves the actual one-loop class zero, and C.3.25–C.3.27 give a second invariant conversion and direct integer-additivity proof. The old unproved shortcuts remain rejected; they were not promoted into the completed proof.

For B, the automatic-comparison approach was checked against Biliotti–Ghigi, Meersseman, and the definability discussion of Katzarkov–Lee–Lupercio–Meersseman. The exact hypotheses fail or have not been proved here, as recorded in B_comparison_literature.tex and evidence/root_B_comparison_sources.md. The abstract C* automorphism group does not make its action meromorphic: its nonconstant orbit inside a regular compact torus cannot extend to P1. This is a direct obstruction to that shortcut.

B.4.7 is a complete proper semistable Hopf family, with a simply connected centre and h1(O)=1. Its total neighbourhood and the constant product have different fundamental groups, so there is no proper meromorphic comparison over both. It isolates an actual mechanism left outside the ddbar obstruction. Its nearby fibre is not S6. The candidate exceptional elliptic interface can acquire a triple divisor, so the normal-degree −1/0 mismatch by itself does not exclude all attachments.

Fujimoto's logarithmic transformations give the established cocycle machinery behind that example. The original 1988 Example3.4(1) also supplies an elliptic abelian product comparison. Nakayama2002 Theorem7.1.3 answers the associated elliptic codimension-two extension question under its precise hypotheses. Neither applies directly to a simple two-torus family. A broader proposed Cartier surgery with values in the full varying two-torus must use a monodromy-compatible integral cycle. Its compatibility across the two degenerating axes is being checked; it is not ruled out merely by generic simplicity. Possible non-toric blow-ups along elliptic curves and non-Kähler caps with higher intersections remain construction candidates, not constructed fillings or assigned topologies.

## Detailed development record: E_C_attempts_report.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent attack on the actual smooth monodromy

**Current state (17:02 UTC, 15 November 2026): the actual one-loop smooth monodromy is zero by two determinations.** C.3.18–C.3.22 construct the actual bounding four-manifold, identify the actual delta line, evaluate its square as -2, and compute the two cap pairings with one common sign. Their combination is J=0. The integer additivity argument in C.3.23 and the exact equivariant sixfold-to-onefold bridge in C.3.24 give the first proof. C.3.25–C.3.27 construct an actual spin-c eight-dimensional coboundary and give a second proof by direct characteristic-index additivity, avoiding the three-knot integer, Wall and Davis. The two proofs share the actual p,k geometry; their boundary-invariant conversions and integer-level one-loop arguments are independent. Earlier stopping points and rejected comparisons are retained below as a research audit; their former open statements are superseded by the new final sections. No invariant of the auxiliary untwisted filling is transferred to the actual six-sphere boundary. The specifically requested analytic eight-dimensional filling and its Chern data remain unfinished.

## Proven calculations and their scope

1. `C_untwisted_coboundary.tex`, C.2.2–C.2.3, computes the intersection form and a relative Pontryagin square for the **actual** semistable untwisted filling A.2.2. The form has rank two, signature +2, and nonzero Smith factors 1,11. Its nondegenerate quotient is `[[6,1],[1,2]]`; its first Pontryagin covector is `−4(a·F)−28h`; its rational relative square is 4512/11. The formal combination 79/176 is not an Eells–Kuiper value for MΦ. The determinant class restricts nontrivially to this other boundary. The branch self-intersection formula was independently checked by the A/B agent; `e_push_C_attempts.py` reconstructs the lattice independently of the A/B program.
2. The cyclic cover `y^12=t^4/(t−1)^3` is connected, with local deck shifts (4,−3,−1), ramification orders (3,4,12), and genus three. This is the component-label cover already identified in B.3.2. It is not a threefold filling. The same affine curve is the (3,4) torus-knot Milnor fibre after choosing coordinates; this observation does not identify an associated seven-sphere.
3. On the cusp-link logarithm cover, use coordinates `(a,g,u,w,d)` for q-angle and the lattice coordinates γ,u,w,δ. The deck transformation is `(a,g,u,w,d)↦(a+1,g,u,w+u,d−g)`. Naive γ-translation by r has deck defect `−rδ`. Its commuting lift is `(a,g+r,u,w,d−ra)`. At r=1 its endpoint, after removing the full γ period, is the δ-gauge `−a`, of q-degree −1. Thus a loop in quotient γ-translations does not automatically lift to an identity-ended loop on the original cusp link. The δ-circle bundle over `S¹γ×Nil³` has a nonzero mixed Chern class on q-angle × γ. Exact algebra is in `e_push_C_attempts.py` and independently in the D agent's cusp-twist program.

## Proved orbit-space reduction

Along a real c-path, projection modulo the δ-circle discards Re ζ₂. The projected ordinary period lattice is independent of Re c. In a finite root chart, the c-dependent overlap is a real C* flow with time `(c−c₀) ε_j log|s_j|`; this can be extended by a smooth invariant cutoff which is bounded near the root centre and equals the logarithm on the overlap. The cusp toric charts themselves change by a δ-phase. These chartwise statements admit local equivariant horizontal vector fields; their differences are vertical circle fields, so an invariant partition of unity can produce a global horizontal field. This requires working with actual smooth equivariant maps in the slices; arbitrary maps of a chosen topological quotient need not be smooth at the fixed set.

For h=12, the ordinary closing scalar is `K=t^4/(t−1)^3`. The valid cusp unit is the bounded two-torus unit U in `C_closing_cocycle.tex`, with `U·(γ-deck)=(1,K)`. After projection, its additive logarithm u satisfies `u+vγ=R_log|K|`. Removing a cutoff copy of the unit isotopy and a cutoff real C* flow leaves, on the quotient annulus, translation by `χ(|q|)vγ`, identity at its two ends. **C.3.10 now proves that this specified orbit map has a smooth equivariant lift representing exactly 6[Φ], and that every such lift has the same mapping class.** Root and the D agent independently checked the finite-chart signs, the actual equivariant horizontal-field construction, and the cusp-unit cancellation. The preceding mixed Euler calculation shows why its full lift cannot simply be declared supported in the annulus. The outside gauge and its framing remain part of the global data. All intermediate isotopies are smooth upstairs; no general claim of smoothness in raw Hopf quotient coordinates at the fixed set is needed.

The direct gauge comparison lemma C.3.6 establishes that two **existing smooth equivariant lifts of the same orbit map** have the same mapping class. It does not prove that the orbit twist is isotopic to the identity, nor that every quotient isotopy lifts smoothly through the fixed stratum.

## Cusp orbit topology and why a local spanning ball is unavailable

The D agent independently verified the conductor description used here in C.3.7. The quotient of the normalization dP₆ by the δ-circle is a simply connected compact oriented three-manifold Y with two boundary two-spheres and H₂(Y)=Z. Its homology proof needs no three-manifold classification theorem. Besides the two fixed divisors it has two isolated toric fixed vertices; their quotients are interior R³ points. Gluing the fixed boundary spheres first gives the closed real normalization N; the remaining four edge intervals form two disjoint circles, each crossing the glued fixed sphere once, which are then identified by the other conductor pairings. C.3.9 proves that the oriented normalization circle has the exact primitive marking −γ, with no additional u component or integer factor.

For the full quotient H=W/δ, the normalized conductor has homology H₀=Z, H₁=Z, H₂=Z². The conductor quotient is the fixed S² with two arcs joining its two triple points; it has H₁=Z², H₂=Z. The degree-two map to Y and the conductor quotient has both columns `(1,−1)`. Complex orientations of the two fixed curves give the same generator in H₂(Y), because the two normal circle weights and the two boundary orientations both reverse. The hexagonal loop maps to a commutator of the two arc loops, so its H₁ map is zero. The pushout gives torsion-free homology `(Z,Z²,Z²,Z)` and π₁(H)=Z². The fixed S² is primitive and nonzero in H₂(H). In particular it cannot bound a three-chain, let alone a framed three-ball, in H. C.3.8 now supplies the required equivariant strong deformation retraction of the whole cusp body to W: the gradient estimate for g=|q|² is `|∇g|>=c g^(5/6)`, and the uniform finite tail-length estimate gives a continuous endpoint map. Thus the nonbounding conclusion holds in the entire cusp orbit body.

**Superseded assertion, withdrawn 15 November 2026:** an earlier version of this report said that the source's rank-three quotient gives an actual map S⁶→S³ and thereby identifies the ordinary γ-circle as a marked (3,4) attaching knot. C.3.12 disproves that literal extension: any continuous Hausdorff-valued map constant on every regular ker(γ) subtorus collapses all of W. Its fractional-u proof retains the actual nonunitary cusp multiplier and has been independently refereed in `E_C312_referee.md`. The supplied background already recorded the narrower failure of the fixed raw character coordinate; those notes were not used as an authority for the new proof. The abstract finite Seifert multiplicities and the algebraic (3,4) Milnor cover remain valid separate facts. They do not provide a marked global cusp knot or its framing.

C.3.13 subsequently constructs a corrected smooth map to the weighted S³ model by changing the translational leaves inside the cusp. Its integral character is extended by an invariant closed one-form; the finite radial corrections and the exact cusp meridian are retained. The target regular (3,4) knot has positive push-off linking number 12. The detailed referee is `E_C313_referee.md`. This repairs the existence of a smooth comparison map in the explicitly modified sense; it does not restore the disproved literal quotient or identify a six-dimensional normal framing.

C.3.11 additionally proves a nonzero normal Euler number for the immersion N into the cusp orbit five-manifold. At a fixed point the Hopf coordinate is a=xz, with x,z the two O(−1) weight lines; the normal complex line is therefore O(−2). At both triple points q=ay verifies that the line remains the a-line, not the q-line. Its Euler pairing on the fixed sphere is −2 in the a-complex orientation. A naive compression along that sphere cannot retain this normal bundle.

## Corrected knot-invariant literature

Takase (2007), §2.1, Corollary 2.6, explicitly corrects a sign error in his 2004 paper. With his orientation convention,

`Ω(F)=−(σ(V)−⟨e(νV)²,[V,∂V]⟩)/8`

for an embedded Seifert four-manifold of `F:S³↪S⁶`. Corollary 2.14 shows that any Haefliger knot admits Seifert surfaces of arbitrary signature, so signature alone cannot determine Ω. Moriyama (2011), Theorem 8.1, independently gives the same formula, and expressly restricts that statement to **embedded** Seifert surfaces. Neither formula can be applied to a conductor quotient or an immersed surface without the appropriate correction.

Moriyama's Theorem 1.2(3) gives a useful alternative. For an actual seven-dimensional quasi-e-cobordism `(Z,V,e)`, with boundary the relevant six-dimensional orbit pair,

`σ(∂(Z,V,e))=Sign(V)−4∫_V γ²`,

where `2ρ*γ=e|S(νV)−e(T_vertical S(νV))`. For a knot in S⁶ the left side is `−8Ω`. The meridian of an e-class is two, whereas the principal Hopf circle bundle has meridian one. This factor of two must be kept in converting circle-bundle data. A doubled principal-circle Euler class need only be a **quasi** e-class; its discrepancy along the fixed set is exactly the self-linking class γ.

Primary PDFs and SHA-256 metadata are retained by `e_push_C_sources.py/.json`. Search/open responses are `E_C_search1.json` through `E_C_search15.json`. ASIII's analytic Riemann–Roch theorem was also inspected visually on original pages 564–565, with page images and local OCR retained. Its compact-complex hypothesis applies to the non-Kähler R⁺.

## Local fixed-set calculation for the untwisted filling

The fixed surfaces in W_A must be disjoint. This is verified directly in its three total-space resolution charts, rather than inferred from the unresolved central fibre. In the conifold chart `ay=zv`, use `a=zA,v=Ay` or `z=aD,y=Dv`, with u unchanged. The circle weights in coordinates `(u,z,A,y)` are `(0,0,+1,−1)` and in `(u,a,D,v)` are `(0,+1,−1,0)`. Their fixed surfaces normalize the nodal curve times the u-disc. They join globally to the horizontal `P¹×D²`. The other chart `u=xb,y=bzv`, in `(x,b,z,v)`, has weights `(+1,−1,0,0)` and fixed surface `x=b=0`, the compact vertical S. On the overlap `a=1/b`, so the two fixed components do not meet.

The compact fixed S has normal weight lines with Chern classes `n=m−F` and `−n`. Conjugating the negative weight converts the scalar normal rank-two bundle to `L_n⊕L_n`; the quotient normal R³ bundle is trivial. Its quasi-e discrepancy has square `γ²=n²=−2`. Thus its local expression `Sign(S)−4γ²=−8−4(−2)` vanishes. The horizontal component has signature zero and scalar-normal determinant zero (the original two weight lines restrict to O(−1), with opposite signs). These are untwisted local contributions. They do not prove that the finite logarithmic transforms preserve the boundary orbit invariant.

For the nonsplit torsor constructed in B.3.6, the A/B agent has now established the proposed relative-degree-zero line M representing P, its square M²=−2, and the normalized affine line relation with divisor at infinity. The Brauer obstruction vanishes on the complex base curve; height base change and the affine norm A¹²=t₆P give the degree, and the isogeny 1−σ* on Pic⁰ of the genus-three base removes the remaining degree-zero discrepancy. This completes normal-line data for that auxiliary compact-core model. It still does not supply the actual six-sphere boundary: its twelve I₁₂ nodes give twelve distinct ordinary double points and resolution Euler number 24. Component rotation does not identify those centres, because each centre belongs to its own plumbing chart and is outside every core overlap.

## Other approaches tried and the precise missing step

- Taking `(MΦ×D²)/S¹` directly gives a cone on CP² along the fixed three-manifold. CP² has nonzero signature, so this is not a smooth eight-dimensional filling obtained by replacing each cone independently. A global fixed-surface cap and its normal data are needed.
- The Gromoll-filtration information in Crowley–Schick (2013), Appendix A, gives a proper subgroup in this dimension, but no membership of this specific Φ has been proved. Fibre preservation or circle equivariance does not supply that membership.
- Free circle actions and their characteristic bordism may provide surgery corrections; however even an empty-fixed-set six-dimensional pair with a nonzero Euler class has characteristic obstructions. Thus it is not valid to assert that modifications in the free region contribute zero merely because they avoid fixed points.
- An abstract (3,4) Seifert model suggests ±1 surgeries with Brieskorn boundaries Σ(3,4,11) or Σ(3,4,13). Their suspended singularity signatures can be computed, but C.3.12 removes the previously assumed literal global quotient comparison, and no other comparison map between their handlebodies and the actual cusp-annulus twist has been constructed. Their natural circle fixed sets also differ. No numerical monodromy value is inferred from these examples.
- The determinant eleven in the untwisted intersection form does not justify adjoining a guessed E₈ or other plumbing. The surgery framings and the resulting boundary have to be established before such an intersection completion has meaning for this problem.
- A relative mapping-class approach retains the framed-knot group FC³₃=Z²; a simplification to only π₃SO(3) omits data. C.3.14 now establishes the actual standard orbit pair and free complement S²×D³ from Fintushel–Pao's dimension-six-applicable orbit theorem, Θ₅=0, and Haefliger's unknotting range. A primitive Hopf Euler class and the trivial scalar normal rank-two bundle lift the pair identification to an actual equivariant linearization.
- C.3.14 also supplies a concrete cap, `Mψ=(S²×D⁴) union_doubleψ −(S²×D⁴)`, and verifies that it is exactly the framed-surgery manifold of the actual clutching orbit knot. The knot exterior is built from two half-disc complements; the third surgery cap combines with one half along its inner S²×D³ face, leaving the doubled ψ gluing. Wall's original Theorem 4, visually verified on pages 360–361, gives `x³=6φ+β'`, `p1*x=4β'`, hence `Jψ=(p1*x−4*x³)/24=−φ`. The 1967 corrigendum only changes Theorem 14. Davis's orbit-knot generator gives M₃⁷=S₂,−₁(H), with Eells–Kuiper value 1/28. Therefore the actual sixfold monodromy has value ±Jψ/28, with the fixed convention sign still to be compared. A pure framing rotation has an explicit quaternionic lift extending over D⁷, so its Θ₇ image is zero. **Jψ remains uncomputed.** A local affine Jacobian or the mixed cusp Euler class does not determine this global characteristic number.

## Historical stopping point, superseded below

The global capped quotient pair is now specified in C.3.14. Evaluate its characteristic number Jψ, retaining the finite-root/cusp attachment and stable framing, and compare the fixed Wall/Davis orientation sign as recorded in C.3.15. An actual equivariant smooth cobordism or an embedded Seifert normal Euler calculation remains an alternative route. Neither the target linking number 12 nor the auxiliary elliptic-surface signature supplies the missing number. No value, vanishing result, or nontrivial order bound for the actual [Φ] has been established by this attempt.

## Actual characteristic computation: C.3.16–C.3.22

C.3.16 upgrades the target linking calculation to the actual cusp inclusion: the globally defined function G2^3-G1^4 represents the integral meridian class, and the actual link row is (12,0,1) on (gamma,u,q). C.3.17 constructs a marked product cusp quotient E_I1 times S1 by deforming the bounded period units, prescribing exact outer-collar transport and unitary normal transport at the fixed sphere, and using the explicit small-resolution quotient at zero units. It extends the boundary gamma action. This does not itself trivialize the relative map because its isotopy moves the fixed sphere. It also proves that the gamma-u-w torus is a primitive nonbounding cusp H3 class.

C.3.18 rebuilds a genuine genus-one Lefschetz map from the actual orbit S5 to S3, matching the raw quotient on its exterior. A Milnor page of a nearby weighted (3,4) knot has twelve positive intersections with the critical knot. Its actual inverse image M is an embedded four-manifold with twelve I1 fibres and boundary the marked torus, with orientation (gamma,w,u). The determinant class with the specified complex boundary frame is pulled back from the base, and its square is zero. The relative top Chern number is Euler(M)=12. The page normal differs from the actual q-normal frame by a winding -12 rotation, stably null. Therefore the relative Pontryagin defect in the actual affine frame is -24. C.3.19 transports this through the actual derivative clutch and the mixed delta Euler class to prove the absolute closed-cap pairing |p1*x|=24. Referee: E_C318_C319_referee.md.

C.3.20 compresses the product-circle image of F to a proper arc while preserving the actual collar, then chooses the twelve page intersections outside that arc. This makes M disjoint from F. Its actual delta line L has a well-defined relative square s_delta, independent of boundary gauge. In the correct coefficient-coordinate deck convention, the positive q deck is (g,u,w,d,a)->(g,u,w-u,d+g,a+1), the invariant connection is dd-a dg and its Euler curvature is E=dg wedge da. This corrects the earlier forward-monodromy coordinate expression in item 3, without changing its nonzero-flux conclusion. Writing E^2=dB on P=S2 times D3 and using the actual outer class da+12dg gives the global primitive eta=-(1-chi)da-12dg. The curvature-cube expansion and Stokes yield k_cap=epsilon_cap*3*s_delta. The same square is the actual lifted-torus linking coefficient in X minus F. Code e_push_C_cubic.py checks the factor three, the wedge signs and the cover genus; it does not substitute for the geometric proof.

C.3.21 proves s_delta=-2 by an actual line comparison. The page is explicitly the affine curve b^3=a^4+1 with actual map t_epsilon=(a^4+1)/(1+epsilon a^4). The twelve original cusp preimages are unramified. Outside the twelve modified nodal discs, the actual line has the exact fibrewise Poincare multipliers (exp(2*pi*i*mu),1), so its full mixed class is that of the original normal-function section P, up to dualization. The affine torus torsor over the graph base has a smooth section. Nodal excision introduces only regular-fibre classes, which vanish on the base with boundary; after compactification such base classes have zero pairing with a fibre-degree-zero line. Integral multiplicative Leray identifies the square with the Jacobian Poincare square using the same R1 local system and fibre pairing, without a projectivity, multisection or global holomorphic-torsor assumption. The explicit comparator is Y^2=X^3-3(1-epsilon)bX+2(1-epsilon)^2 a^2, with P=(0,-sqrt(2)(1-epsilon)a) and discriminant 1728(1-epsilon)^3(1+epsilon a^4). It has twelve I1 fibres, a good capped fibre, chi(O)=1 and disjoint P,O. Thus (P-O)^2=-2. Root, A/B and D independently checked this argument; the A/B exact model check is ab_push_actual_line_comparison.py.

C.3.22 supplies the common signed comparison, independently calibrated by connections. An intermediate audit produced a spurious J=2 by treating a boundary frame and a coordinate clutch as the same transition. For a boundary frame f=t B the flat-in-f connection is -dB B^(-1); a mapping-torus coordinate clutch K uses K^(-1)dK. Their characteristic transgressions have opposite signs. The ordinary disc line with boundary frame exp(i*n*theta) confirms relative c1=+n and the opposite coordinate-clutch sign. With this correction, the same gluing trace gives p1_cap*x=epsilon_cap*p_rel and k_cap=epsilon_cap*3*s_delta. Consequently J=epsilon_cap*(p_rel-12*s_delta)/24=0, since p_rel=-24 and s_delta=-2. The unknown overall cap sign and the Wall/Davis generator sign do not affect this zero. The mismatch and correction are preserved in E_C_signed_cap_referee.md.

## Completed integer-level bridge and exact remaining scope

The equality J=0 is an equality in Z, not just modulo 28. The original one-loop monodromy has a delta-equivariant representative; the six-loop reduction used equivariant transports and isotopies. C.3.23 defines the unframed orbit-knot integer on equivariant mapping classes and proves additivity by compressing supports into disjoint invariant balls and taking equivariant connected sums. Root and D independently accepted the full source. C.3.24 checks the coherent closing identity b_h,c+b_k,c+h=b_h+k,c and the equivariant connection interpolation, so the actual six-loop integer is six times the original integer. Its zero forces the original integer to vanish and removes the otherwise genuine order-two ambiguity in Z/28. The weaker equation 6[Phi]=0 by itself would not suffice. Root and D independently accepted this final bridge as well.

At the end of the first method, the specified analytic filling and a second determination were still open. The latter stopping point has now been superseded by C.3.25–C.3.27, as follows.

## Independent spin-c and direct-index determination

C.3.25 constructs the actual clutched-sphere coboundary from the disc bundle of the meridian line over the actual cap, followed by one product four-handle. The handle replaces the free Hopf S3 times D4 piece by the actual fixed D4 times S3 tube. Its added four-cycle is CP2 with trivial normal D4. On PD(x),CP2 the form is [[k,1],[1,0]], with p1 covector (p+k,3), determinant-square covector (k,1), and signature zero. Its characteristic pairings are p1²=6p-3k, p1*c²=p+k and c⁴=k. The spin-c formula gives mu=(p-4k)/672. The actual six-loop values give (-126,-30,-6) times the common sign and a zero invariant. This uses no knot-generator conversion.

C.3.26 gives a separate integer-before-mod28 argument. The twisted spin Dirac index makes lambda=(p-4k)/24 integral. A Y-bundle over a pair of pants carries the actual line lifts and proves additivity on mapping tori. The explicit seven-dimensional trace is a Y-bundle over an annulus, with two N times I pieces attached along opposite outer arcs. Its boundary is the twisted cap, the oppositely oriented identity cap, and the inner mapping torus. Its actual tangent and line bundles extend, so p1*x and x³ individually transfer with the same orientation sign and are additive for relative maps. Normal-framing changes contribute S(eta) over S4; the vertical Euler class 2x proves p1=4x² and hence zero change of lambda. The three-knot integer and classification are not used in this argument.

C.3.27 combines the exact equivariant six-loop relation with 6*lambda(Phi)=0 in Z, then the actual eight-dimensional formula mu(Phi)=lambda(Phi)/28. It obtains [Phi]=0 independently of the first boundary-invariant and one-loop arguments. Root and D accepted C26 and C27; root and E accepted C25. Referee details are saved in E_C325_C326_referee.md, and exact arithmetic in d_push_eight_coboundary.py and e_push_C_second_method.py. These referee checks are validation rather than further mathematical methods.

The specified analytic eight-dimensional descended filling of C.2 and its c1,c2, intersection form, relative lifts and characteristic numbers remain Open. C.3.25 is an explicitly different smooth spin-c coboundary, with no asserted almost-complex structure. C.5.1 and its registry preserve this remaining scope and disclose the shared p,k geometric inputs of the two completed determinations.

The subsequent primary audit located the direct Hopf-handle precursor in Jiang (2014), proof of Lemma3.5, Case2, and the characteristic integer in Lemma3.6. Its known machinery is now cited in C_index_literature_context.tex; C25 is an application with its actual boundary and general cubic term verified. The publisher-authorized Montgomery–Yang1968 preview was opened only for historical context. No novelty in the surgery construction or invariant formula is claimed. The source metadata and search sequence are preserved without republishing the full papers.

## Detailed development record: D_cusp_twist_audit.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Cusp-twist investigation: exact progress and unresolved comparison

This is a supplementary research and source audit for Part C and Lead 7. It does not assign a value in `Theta_7`. The complete Part D results are in `D_push.tex`; they are unaffected by the unresolved Part C computation.

## Established comparisons

1. `C_closing_cocycle.tex`, C.3.5, verifies the known even-shift formula against both finite root charts and the full cusp toric filling. For the twelve-unit closing shift the ordinary scalar is `K=t^4/(t-1)^3`, while its cusp representative is a holomorphic two-coordinate torus unit. Their ratio is exactly the gamma deck transformation. The scalar pole alone is therefore not an obstruction to extending the actual map.
2. `C_orbit_lift_comparison.tex`, C.3.6, proves directly that smooth equivariant lifts of the same orbit map differ by an isotopically trivial gauge. It also lifts orbit isotopies supported in the free stratum. This does not supply the missing orbit isotopy.
3. `code/d_push_cusp_twist_linear.py` and its output multiply the prescribed integral monodromy matrices exactly. In the ordered basis `(gamma,u,w,delta)`, the cusp monodromy fixes `w,delta`, sends `u` to `u+w`, and sends `gamma` to `gamma-delta`.
4. `C_cusp_orbit_topology.tex`, C.3.7, proves the conductor pushout, including its attaching word, all integral homology groups, and the primitive nonzero fixed-sphere class. `C_cusp_retraction.tex`, C.3.8, supplies the previously missing equivariant strong deformation retraction of a sufficiently small compact cusp body onto the central fibre. The fixed sphere therefore does not bound a three-chain anywhere in that cusp body modulo the circle.
5. `C_cusp_marking.tex`, C.3.9, uses the actual matrix `B0=[[0,1],[-1,0]]` and the triangle labels of the Mumford fan. Its two conductor generators are exactly `a=u`, `b=gamma+u`; the real normalization circle `ab^-1` is therefore `-gamma`, primitive. Neither an extra factor two nor an unmarked change of basis is permitted.
6. `C_annular_reduction.tex`, C.3.10 (classification agent), constructs actual smooth equivariant transports through the finite root and cusp charts. After two explicit equivariant isotopies, the orbit map for six positive parameter loops is `z -> z+chi(q) v_gamma(q)`, supported in a free cusp annulus. The full lift can retain a gauge outside that annulus. The finite logarithmic overlap signs and the exact identity `a-u=v_gamma` were independently checked here.
7. `C_cusp_normal_euler.tex`, C.3.11, proves that the real normalization is immersed with one transverse double circle and that its oriented normal two-plane has Euler number `-2` on the fixed sphere for the complex orientation of the Hopf coordinate `a=xz`. Its absolute value is two regardless of orientation. Thus a compression to an immersed three-sphere must change the normal data; it cannot simply carry this two-plane bundle across a three-disc.
8. `C_translational_quotient_obstruction.tex`, C.3.12, proves that every Hausdorff-valued continuous map constant on the literal regular `ker(gamma)` translational subtori collapses all of `W`. The key is the nonunit modulus of the actual cusp `u` multiplier, `|exp(2 pi i mu0)|=5+2 sqrt(6)`. This supersedes the earlier tentative use of Engel's claimed global Seifert quotient below. The supplied notes already recorded failure of the raw invariant-circle map; C.3.12 proves the stronger universal collapse statement without any priority claim.
9. `C_corrected_seifert_comparison.tex`, C.3.13, now constructs a corrected actual smooth map `X_c -> S^3`, invariant under delta, retaining the actual finite quotient outside a chosen cusp body and changing its leaves inside that body. It uses an invariant integral closed one-form upstairs to extend the exact boundary character. The transverse `q` meridian is the toric section `z1=z2=1`, has raw gamma value zero and bounds an actual disc. The explicit weighted-model formula includes the finite radial logarithm corrections and gives cusp gauge `vartheta=theta-Re(kappa0)`. Thus a positive marked gamma circle on the outer collar really maps to a positive `(3,4)` regular fibre, with target push-off linking twelve. E independently refereed all signs, smoothness and the target inverse. The six-dimensional normal-framing comparison is still open.

Consequently the orbit collar modulo the delta circle has boundary `S^1_gamma x Nil^3`, where `Nil^3` is the mapping torus of the two-torus shear `u -> u+w`. The principal delta-circle bundle on that collar is nontrivial: its Euler class has evaluation of absolute value one on the cusp-angle/gamma two-torus. For an explicit sign convention use the mapping-torus relation `(theta+1,a,b)~(theta,a,b-a)`, with `a,b` the gamma and delta coordinates. Its covering deck map is `(theta,a,b)->(theta+1,a,b+a)`; the invariant connection `db-theta da` has curvature `-dtheta wedge da`, giving Euler number `-1`. This `theta` orientation is the stated monodromy convention and must not be silently replaced by a positive `q`-loop convention.

This mixed Euler class is the concrete reason that a fractional gamma translation on the orbit collar cannot simply be lifted with identity phase at both ends. A full smooth equivariant lift nevertheless exists for the original monodromy, with its outside gauge. C.3.6 removes the ambiguity only after an orbit map or orbit isotopy has actually been constructed.

## Cusp conductor geometry independently checked

The classification agent computes the conductor pushout. Independent checks made here are:

- On the toric normalization `dP_6`, the delta circle fixes two opposite divisors and also the two toric vertices not incident to them. The latter have normal weights `(1,-1)` and quotient neighbourhood `R^3`; they are not extra boundary components.
- The orbit quotient of the normalization is an oriented three-manifold with two spherical boundary components, identified by the toric argument as `S^2 x I`.
- At a fixed divisor of normal circle weight `+1`, the boundary orientation of the orbit quotient is the complex curve orientation; at weight `-1`, it is the opposite. Thus the two fixed divisors with their complex orientations represent the same class in the quotient's second homology. Both conductor maps have degree `+1` on the resulting fixed sphere.
- Gluing just those two fixed boundary spheres gives an oriented `S^2` bundle over the circle, hence `S^2 x S^1`: the gluing is a holomorphic automorphism of `P^1`, isotopic to the identity. The four remaining toric-edge intervals then form two disjoint embedded circles. Each crosses the glued fixed sphere once, and the other two opposite-side identifications identify these circles with an interchange of their two distinguished vertices.

These observations are now proved, with the precise scope of each step, in C.3.7--C.3.11. The invariant gradient flow of `|q|^2` gives the equivariant retraction used for the full cusp body. In a normal-crossing chart `q=z1...zk`, `k<=3`, the estimate `|grad |q|^2| >= c |q|^(5/3)` gives a uniformly finite tail length. No retraction theorem is assumed without checking its hypotheses. The nonzero fixed-sphere class excludes a bounding three-chain in the entire small cusp body modulo the circle.

## Global structure that may supply the missing framing

**Superseded inference, retained for audit.** An earlier version of this note treated Engel's attached Remark 8.3 as providing the actual continuous rank-three quotient onto `S^3`, and thereby viewed the regular gamma circle as a marked `(3,4)` torus knot. The literal extension in that remark is false. The supplied `background/notes/A1_notes.md`, Section 2, A1--1, and `background/S6_SURVEY_20261112.md`, F9, already record nonextension of the raw invariant-circle coordinate. C.3.12 now proves that every continuous map preserving those regular translational leaves collapses the entire cusp fibre; no base-dependent circle reparametrization can retain a central circle. Thus the earlier inference of an actual marked global `S^3` quotient is withdrawn. The finite Seifert pieces and the abstract `(3,4)` filling remain valid comparison data, but a modified cusp map and its framing would have to be constructed and checked.

There is a second numerical/geometric match: AB's component cover `b^3=a^4+1` is the affine Milnor fibre of the `(3,4)` plane-curve singularity. Its compactification has genus three and one point at infinity. The split elliptic pullback studied in B.3.4 has `chi(O)=1`, a single `I_12` fibre, and signature `-8`. No map has been constructed that makes this elliptic surface a Seifert four-manifold for the orbit knot. Its signature cannot be substituted into a Haefliger or Eells--Kuiper formula without that construction and the normal Euler-class comparison.

The elementary weighted model was initially only abstract; C.3.13 supplies the corrected comparison map. On the standard sphere in `C^2`, use the action `(z1,z2)->(lambda^3 z1,lambda^4 z2)` and the quotient coordinate `t=z2^3/(z2^3-z1^4)`. If `F^3=t`, `G^4=t-1`, use `(z1,z2)=(r^3 G exp(6 pi i Theta), r^4 F exp(8 pi i Theta))`, with the unique positive `r` making the norm one and `Theta=theta_gamma-Re(ell)+d(t)`. The function `d` explicitly cancels the actual finite radial logarithm term from `sigma_j=w_j Pi v_j`; it is zero near the cusp. The root deck changes are `(s0,theta0)->(zeta3^-1 s0,theta0+1/3)` and `(s1,theta1)->(zeta4^-1 s1,theta1-1/4)`. Near `q=1/t=0`, the smooth target fibre coordinate is `Theta-Re(s)/12=theta_gamma-Re(kappa0(q))`, where `q=exp(2 pi i s)`. The relative integral-one-form extension replaces `theta_gamma` inside the cusp, so no false literal-quotient extension is used. Its positive target push-off linking number is twelve; this number has still not been transferred to the six-dimensional framing needed for the Haefliger invariant.

Roseman--Takase's explicit generator resolves an immersion with two ordered crossings, with a two-angle parameter torus. Their Remark 5.1 changes the Haefliger invariant by the product of the two angular speeds. Our real normalization has one double circle. No matching of these resolution parameters, nor a global normal framing, has been constructed. The equality `v2(T(3,4))=5` therefore supplies no value for the present twist. Koytcheff's 2025 Remark 5.9 relates graphing phenomena at the graded finite-type level; it does not supply a canonical operation identifying this specific annular map with a knot formed from `T(3,4)`.

A separate arithmetic consistency check is mandatory: the annular map represents `6[Phi]` in `Theta_7=Z/28`, so its residue is even. A direct claim that its Davis residue is the odd number five would contradict this already proved relation. This check does not determine the even residue.

## Relative mapping-class route: source correction and exact scope

Krylov's 2004 author preprint, Lemma 1, describes the relative pseudo-isotopy group of `S^2 x D^3` as the framed-knot group `FC^3_3`, since `Theta_6=0`. The preceding sentence in that preprint claims `FC^(p+1)_q = pi_q SO(p+1)` whenever `q<2p`. This simplification cannot be used at `(p,q)=(2,3)`: Haefliger's original 1966 Theorem 5.17 explicitly gives `FC^3_3=Z+Z`, and Section 5.16 exhibits the independent knot and framing generators. Olbermann (2010), Section 4, independently states this same two-integer result. The actual classification therefore retains the framing and knotting information sought here.

In Haefliger's Section 5.16 the knotted generator has stable differential class zero and its normal push-off gives six times the Hopf generator of `pi_3(S^2)`; the framing generator is the standard unknotted sphere with one generator of `pi_3(SO(3))`, whose stabilization is twice a generator of `pi_3(SO)`. These are potential computable invariants, but neither has been evaluated for the present annular map. Applicability to a selected identification of the actual orbit complement, and the resulting comparison map to the framed-knot group, must be proved before using the classification.

The orbit-complement prerequisite can be justified by the primary results just checked: Fintushel--Pao's Section 1 assumes `n>5`, its Proposition 1.3 smooths the orbit space with the Hopf slice model, and Proposition 1.4(a) makes it a homotopy `(n-1)`-sphere. For `n=6`, `Theta_5=0` gives the standard `S^5`; Haefliger's Corollary 6.6 gives `C_2^3=0`, so the fixed `S^2` is unknotted and its free exterior is `S^2 x D^3`. The classification agent is writing the resulting capped-six-manifold reduction. No characteristic numbers of that cap have been evaluated here.

There is also an explicit harmless framing generator in the linear Hopf model. If `r:(D^3,partial)->(SO(3),id)` lifts to `R:D^3->SU(2)` with `R=1` near the boundary, the quotient map `(x,y)->(r(y)x,y)` on `S^2 x D^3` lifts on `S^6=partial(D^4_H x D^3)` to `(a,y)->(R(y)a,y)`. It commutes with the right-complex circle and extends over the seven-disc. Its homotopy-sphere class is therefore zero. Applying this to the actual action requires the equivariant comparison with that linear model; no integer for the actual annular map is supplied by this observation.

## Page-preimage route under investigation

The corrected map of C.3.13 gives a concrete place to seek a filling. A page of `z2^3-z1^4` is transverse to the weighted Seifert fibres with degree twelve. Over the finite base its marked cover is the affine curve `F^3-G^4=1`, the same genus-three one-ended curve appearing in the elliptic pullback. In the free orbit five-manifold the residual fibres are real two-tori with lattice `(u,w)` and cusp shear `u->u+w`; pulling around the end twelve times gives the `I_12` shear (with the sign determined by whether the boundary is oriented as the complement of a cusp disc). Thus a truncated page preimage has a torus-bundle boundary, not a three-sphere.

An affine torus torsor on that punctured surface can be smoothly trivialized because its degree-two obstruction group vanishes. Such a trivialization does not fix the boundary marking, which can retain the entire cusp correction. To turn this route into a Seifert four-manifold for the actual orbit knot, one must build the specified cusp surgery and calculate its normal Euler class. Simply closing the torus-bundle end by an `I_12` neighbourhood and removing a ball does not prove the required ambient embedding or boundary-knot identification.

As a consistency check only, the genus-three elliptic surface of holomorphic Euler characteristic one has characteristic class `w2=F mod2`. An even proposed normal Euler class would not satisfy the necessary characteristic condition for a Seifert four-manifold embedded in `S^6`. Adding `F` can repair that parity while leaving its square unchanged when the other class pairs trivially with `F`; consequently parity alone does not determine or exclude a cancellation in the Haefliger formula. The proved normal Euler value `-2` on the fixed sphere must also be matched to an actual handle class; identifying it with a narrow `I_12` component would give the wrong pairing.

## Exact remaining step

Construct, with the actual finite-root and cusp overlap maps, either:

- an isotopy of the annular gamma orbit twist through maps that lift smoothly, or
- the associated embedded `S^3` in the six-dimensional orbit sphere together with an embedded Seifert four-manifold and its normal Euler class, or
- a projected immersion of that specific knot with its double-linking, Ekholm and Smale data.

The central conductor incidence and the collar monodromy do not supply these framings. Counting its two triple points or its one double circle, or using generic circle equivariance, does not determine a residue in `Z/28`. No order bound is asserted.

## Actual cap progress after checkpoint03

The following advances are proved and independently refereed; they replace several earlier speculative routes without changing the retained source-correction record.

* **C.3.14:** the actual six-loop orbit clutching has the specified cap `(S2 × D4) union_double(psi) −(S2 × D4)`. Wall's original Theorem4 on pp360–361 gives `k=6φ+β'` and `p1x=4β'`, hence `J=−φ`. His corrigendum changes Theorem14 only. Independent note: `D_C314_referee.md`.

This promotes the number12 from a target-knot datum to an actual linking datum in the orbit five-sphere. Source and exact algebra: `C_actual_linking.tex`, `d_push_actual_linking.py/.out/.json`.
* **C.3.17:** deforming the bounded period units to zero at fixed fan gives a marked cusp orbit product `E_I1 × S1`. Prescribed boundary period transport and unitary fixed-tube transport ensure smooth orbit comparison. The γ-u-w three-torus is a primitive, locally nonbounding H3 generator. Source and checks: `C_cusp_product_extension.tex`, `d_push_cusp_product.py/.out/.json`.
* **C.3.18:** the exterior torus bundle and marked interior product glue to an actual positive genus-one Lefschetz map `Q→S3` with critical knot `(3,4)`. A Milnor page for a nearby weighted circle meets that critical knot in twelve positive points. Its inverse image is a concrete embedded four-manifold bounding the marked three-torus. Relative determinant evaluation through the nodes makes c1 base-pulled, so c1 squared is zero; the outward relative Euler class is12. The relative p1 defect is−24 in the boundary orientation `(γ,w,u)`, or+24 in the ordered `(γ,u,w)` convention. The page/q-normal-frame difference rotates twelve times and is stably null; the defect therefore belongs to the actual affine stable frame. Source/checks: `C_affine_frame_defect.tex`, `d_push_affine_frame_defect.py/.out/.json`.

Thus the cap's Pontryagin pairing has absolute value24 and Wall's framing coordinate has absolute value6. No primitive H3(SO) or Spin generator normalization is silently inserted. ## Closure of the actual numerical comparison after checkpoint04

The outstanding steps in the preceding checkpoint paragraph are now closed by actual comparisons. The historical paragraph is retained to distinguish the earlier open state from the later proof.

* **C.3.21 (E-owned, independently refereed):** the actual page is the affine curve `b^3=a^4+1` with marked map `(a^4+1)/(1+epsilon*a^4)`. On its untouched exterior the actual delta line has precisely the varying Picard section P. Excision makes restriction injective across the twelve actual I1 fillings. Integral Leray multiplicativity then compares its mixed-class square with the Jacobian Poincare square without asserting a holomorphic torsor or a global Jacobian diffeomorphism. The explicit Jacobian has twelve I1 fibres, holomorphic Euler characteristic one, and disjoint P and O. Thus the **actual** relative square is minus two. Referee: `D_C321_referee.md`.

With one common cap sign, `p1x=epsilon_cap*p_rel` and `x^3=epsilon_cap*3*s_delta`. Therefore the actual cap integer vanishes. Referee: `D_C324_referee.md`.

This conclusion rests on the actual line, its mixed class, the actual annular framing, and the integer group operation. It is not inferred from the classical `(3,4)` knot number, an auxiliary elliptic surface, or a local unipotent derivative. It does not imply an analytic extension of the complex family.

# Fourth-hour mathematical audit and remaining comparisons

The earlier attempt records describe the state when each route was tried. The following supersedes their statements that the unrestricted fan geometry, second central topology calculation, restricted Kato-cap criterion, or affine framing defect are still pending.

A.2.5 and A.4.9 were read in full by the root agent. The second topology calculation is a derived sheaf splitting on the central support, constructed by integral exceptional Gysin classes and checked against the alternating normalization complex. The separate root referee record states the exact scope. All classical Kähler Clemens–Schmid qualifications remain in force.

B.4.6 was checked by the root and D agents. It excludes the specified horizontal affine-torsor block with hyperbolic toric Kato caps and the listed overlaps. The proof follows the topological-to-coherent degree-one comparison on both blocks and overlaps, so it does not assume that a component's fundamental-group generator survives. It remains a restricted obstruction and supplies no classification of arbitrary analytic cusp components.

C.3.17 was checked independently before integration. The marked smooth deformation to the two-Tate product and the chosen Hopf smoothing give an actual cusp product and a circle extension. Its three-torus is primitive in cusp H3, so the earlier local bounding-chain shortcut is invalid. C.3.18 supplies a global bounding four-manifold instead. Its positive genus-one Lefschetz fibration has twelve nodes. The base-pulled relative determinant has square zero; its outward relative Euler number is12. This gives the affine stable-frame Pontryagin defect−24 in the Lefschetz orientation, with the displayed opposite sign on the ordered gamma-u-w torus. The stable normal rotation has even winding12. Root and E independently checked that calculation.

C.3.19 then uses the suspension of the actual universal Pontryagin class, the relative annular trace, and the actual circle Euler class. It gives an absolute cap Pontryagin pairing24. Using an unspecified primitive generator of H3(SO) was explicitly avoided because it could conceal a normalization factor. Root and E independently checked the full-period endpoint and relative cup pairing.

C.3.20 identifies the cubic with three times an actual relative Euler square, up to the one stated cap orientation sign. Its four-manifold is moved off the fixed sphere by an interior circle-coordinate change which leaves the marked outer collar unchanged. The connection interpolation, the exterior linking class12gamma+q, and the Hopf-normalized three-form are explicit. The relative square is independent of the boundary gauge. Root and D independently checked this nonnumerical formula.

The next unresolved comparison is the actual circle line on this four-manifold. A proposed route identifies its relative Picard class with the degree-zero Poincaré line of the original elliptic normal function on a degree12 genus-three page cover. Its mixed base–fibre class, finite affine descent and the vanishing-cycle extension must be checked; the number on an auxiliary torsor is not sufficient. This comparison and the correlated cap signs are under active investigation. No numerical cubic, J, or monodromy residue is assigned in this checkpoint.

If an exact integer knot coordinate is eventually computed for six loops, a further possible step is its additivity under equivariant clutching. Such an integral argument could be stronger than knowing six times a residue modulo28. It requires the actual relative isotopies and compatible fixed tubular models. The elementary order-two ambiguity after multiplication by6 in Z/28 is not discarded without that additional proof.

# Root mathematical referee: C.3.25–C.3.27 and B.4.7

15 November 2026, fifth working hour. These checks are AI checks of the proofs, not human verification.

## The independent index route

The root read the full constructions C.3.25 and C.3.26 and accepted them after the following points were made explicit. The actual free-circle exterior has its line fixed by the meridian Euler class. Alexander duality gives its second cohomology generator, while H1(S3×S2;Z)=0 removes the possible boundary gauge discrepancy. Attaching the specified product-framed four-handle to D(Lx) therefore replaces the free Hopf piece by the actual fixed tube of the clutched sphere. It identifies the boundary geometrically before using an invariant.

The added four-cycle is CP2, not S4. Its normal bundle is the displayed product D4 bundle, so its self-intersection is zero, p1 evaluates as 3, and the determinant square evaluates as 1. The other cycle is the zero-section image of PD(x). The two integral cycles give Q=[[k,1],[1,0]], signature zero, and characteristic covectors (p+k,3), (k,1). Inverting this unimodular form gives p1²=6p−3k, p1c²=p+k, c⁴=k. Substitution into the independently established spin-c index formula gives (p−4k)/672. Boundary connected sum with S1×D7 supplies the actual mapping torus as boundary, but does not supply the analytic filling required by B.4.

For C.3.26, integrality comes from the six-dimensional twisted spin Dirac index. Additivity comes from an actual seven-dimensional bundle over a pair of pants, followed by the explicit annular cut-and-reglue bordism carrying the tangent and meridian line bundles. The framing correction is the S2-bundle over S4 associated with a map S3→SO3. Its vertical Euler class is 2x, and stable tangent splitting gives p1=4x², so its index correction is zero. This argument does not use the Haefliger integer, the Wall formula, the Davis generator, or the knot connected-sum law.

The six-loop geometric inputs are shared with the first calculation: p=−24 epsilon and k=−6 epsilon. This shared geometry is disclosed. The eight-dimensional boundary identification and index conversion, together with direct integer additivity for a single loop, form the second route. The two agent referee passes alone would not have constituted a second method. The zero conclusion is obtained before reduction modulo28, so there is no division by6 inside Z/28.

## The Hopf family

The root checked the line-bundle degrees on the blown-up P1×disc and the E-valued cocycle. Its central Chern vectors are (1,0) and (0,1), while the generic vector is (1,1). The generic product statement follows from the actual trivialization of the exceptional line away from the centre. The Hopf multipliers, the two coordinate-quotient fundamental-group maps, the unimodular integral Mayer–Vietoris map, and the coherent map (s,t)↦s−t all agree. In particular the simply connected centre has h1(O)=1 without contradicting semistability.

The total neighbourhood and the constant product have different fundamental groups; hence no punctured comparison can have a proper meromorphic graph over both. This is stronger than merely exhibiting a badly chosen exp(1/s) comparison. The example is not an S6 filling: its nearby fundamental group is Z and its Euler number is zero.

The generic-simplicity calculation for the actual two-tori was checked separately. The existence of a base value with mu outside Q tau+Q needs the countability/Baire argument, which was added. The proposed −1 versus0 normal-degree obstruction only applies without a triple divisor. A section in the triple locus can contribute degree+1; the proof explicitly leaves that possibility open.

# Fifth-hour mathematical and artifact audit

The integrated paper remains INCOMPLETE. This record distinguishes proved additions from active construction candidates as of the fifth hourly checkpoint on 15 November 2026.

## Monodromy

The actual positive one-loop monodromy is now determined to be zero in the oriented group of homotopy seven-spheres. C.3.21 computes the actual delta-line square as −2. C.3.22 combines it with the signed relative Pontryagin defect −24. The first determination uses the integral equivariant three-knot class and its additivity before reducing modulo 28. The second determination constructs an actual smooth spin-c eight-manifold by attaching a product-framed four-handle to a disk line bundle and computes its Eells–Kuiper invariant; a separate characteristic-index bordism proves the needed integral additivity. The second route does not use the Wall–Davis or Haefliger conversion. Both routes share the explicitly disclosed geometric cap pairings and annular representative. Independent rereading is a referee check, not a third method.

The smooth eight-dimensional coboundary is not the analytic filling requested in B.4. Its actual boundary is identified geometrically, and its intersection matrix and characteristic covectors are computed exactly. The requested analytic W and its almost-complex Chern data remain open in C.5.1. Jiang's original 2014 circle-bundle surgery supplies known predecessor machinery, now cited beside the construction; no novelty is claimed for that handle technique.

## Analytic filling

B.4.7 gives an explicit proper semistable Hopf degeneration with simply connected centre and h1(O)=1, and a punctured product whose isomorphism has no proper meromorphic extension. It is a concrete limitation of broad comparison arguments, and is not an S6 filling. Its elliptic Cartier-logarithm machinery is compared with Fujimoto's original papers. Nakayama's codimension-two extension theorem is elliptic and does not apply directly to the generic simple two-tori of the prescribed family.

B.4.8 proves an integral normal-line obstruction for every semistable good-reduction elliptic interface, including rational tails and arbitrary punctured translations. The line's mixed first Chern covector is (0,mN) after a base change of degree N. An all-rational genus-one centre has nontrivial positive Picard–Lefschetz monodromy and is excluded; an elliptic core supplies two bounding solid tori and gives the contradiction. The direct diagonal Hopf bridge is therefore excluded. This theorem does not classify arbitrary analytic interfaces.

B.4.9 identifies all ordinary globally specified logarithmic cocharacters at the double cusp as the two toric invariant cycles. It also computes the full abstract torsor group over the punctured bidisc as Z². Crucially, an explicit quadratic section in the logarithmic parameter proves that the actual marked cusp family has zero class in that group. Choosing a nonzero abstract torsor would change the prescribed punctured germ. The section is holomorphic for nonzero parameter and supplies no extension across the missing parameter axis.

An ordinary blowup repair of the Hopf axis remains obstructed if its exceptional-intersection Cartier divisor extends: tensoring the new normal with that divisor restores the original degree-zero winding line. A genuinely different parabolic Kato construction is now under investigation. Its contracted germ changes from a blown-up Hopf model to a parabolic monomial model, and a candidate extending negative-degree elliptic normal is being checked. No gluing of such a cap into the actual S6 family, total-space construction, descent action, least exponent or topology has yet been proved. Unintegrated working notes are retained without promoting them to the statement registry.

## Smooth deformation limits

E.2.5 proves that the fractional canonical pencil has two independent central sections in every smooth disc specialization of a fixed known multiplicity pair. The retained non-holomorphic-map theorem of Campana–Demailly–Peternell, also stated and proved in the opened Lehn–Rollenske–Schinko source, makes the central reduction holomorphic. The different holomorphic-fibration theorem from the same CDP paper is not used.

E.2.6 proves relative persistence of this fibration when the canonical slope is positive. The key steps are a surface-fibre canonical argument eliminating horizontal fixed components, and connectedness of an actual flat Cartier divisor eliminating vertical fixed components and degree loss. It separates different positive-slope pairs at a common smooth junction and excludes a common junction of opposite signs. It does not prove separation along arbitrary deformation chains. A further zero-period-end obstruction is drafted separately and awaiting integration; a Hopf-general-fibre argument for the negative-slope case is being examined without importing the invalid general CDP assertion.

## Source and build audit

Primary sources newly checked include Jiang's original circle-action manuscript, Fujimoto's 1988 and 1990 constructions, Nakayama's original RIMS manuscript and published metadata, the restricted CDP theorem, and the Lehn–Rollenske–Schinko proof. The exact scope of every borrowed theorem is recorded in the corresponding sections. Ueno's 1983 title was located through original references but its original was not opened and no theorem from it is invoked.

Drafts16–18 compiled without overfull boxes. Intermediate integration checks caught source additions made after bibliography assembly; these were build-tree timing mismatches, not successful full consistency audits. Draft19 is the current clean 140-page PDF, with112 numbered statements and88 bibliography entries. All four build stages succeeded, with zero overfull boxes, and the final log has no unresolved references, citations or rerun warnings. The integrated audit passes. An audit parser initially mistook the unnumbered heading “Scope after E.2.6” for a duplicate statement; anchoring declaration IDs to the start of theorem headings corrected that parser error. No mathematical statement was removed to make the audit pass.

Eleven pages spread across the entire paper and six additional pages containing the new B, C and E propositions were rendered and visually inspected. The title-page conventions, long displays, matrices, proof paragraphs, page breaks and bibliography margins are legible, with no clipping or overflow found in those samples. The inspection is a sample of the document, not a claim that every page was read visually.

The fifth-hour progress assessment continues work because the main analytic-filling question has gained a proved cusp-normal obstruction, an exact identification of its actual torsor class, and a concrete new parabolic-cap construction to test. The monodromy question has also reached a checked value by two specified routes, and smooth-limit persistence has advanced the remaining deformation problem. The global analytic filling and arbitrary deformation-chain questions remain exact open items.
