## Detailed development record: root_B48_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent root check of B.4.8

Checked on 15 November 2026 against the explicit line-factor construction, without using a conjectural analytic filling.

The chosen logarithm of the contracting unit gives a smooth marked torus family over the full parameter disc. The normal line has fibre degree zero and clutching factor `q^m b(q)` along the second lattice generator. Its mixed first Chern class evaluates to `m` on the second generator swept around the positive parameter circle and to zero on the first. Under `q=s^N epsilon(s)` this becomes `mN`, since a holomorphic unit on the disc has zero winding. This proves an integral obstruction, independently of any divergent representative of a logarithm.

For a proper connected semistable genus-one curve family, the normalization sequence gives `1=sum genera+b1(dual graph)`. An all-rational centre consequently has a unique graph cycle. Each cycle neck contributes a positive Dehn twist about the same primitive genus-one class. Their product is `T^k`, with `k>0`, and cannot have the identity homological monodromy of the given good-reduction elliptic family. Separating rational tails contribute no homological transvection. Thus the centre must have an elliptic core and rational trees.

Two loops on that core can avoid its finite set of attaching points and have intersection one. The submersion near those compact loops transports them over a small full disc. Each resulting boundary torus bounds a solid torus in the total interface. The first Chern class of an extending line bundle therefore pairs to zero with both. Under an arbitrary isomorphism of the punctured elliptic families, their mixed projections are an integral basis; translation winding can add fibre classes but these have zero pairing with the degree-zero line. The nonzero covector `(0,mN)` cannot vanish on both. This checks both the rational-tail extension and the unmarked-translation qualification.

An additional tensor factor extending on the same interface has zero pairings by the same bounding argument. This includes Cartier divisors already defined on that interface. It does not include a replacement interface that is singular or non-flat outside the stated semistable-curve category. Cancellation of opposite windings in a product of normal lines does not extend either individual factor.

The diagonal Hopf axis and its derivative give exactly this line factor. The actual cusp's nonzero contracting period unit is sufficient for good reduction of the axis. No claim that every possible filling contains this axis has been established or used.

The independent rerun of `code/ab_push_hopf_winding.py` is retained as `code/root_ab_push_hopf_winding_rerun.out`. It passes the exact transvection, common-invariant lattice, Smith-form and cusp-unit algebra. Those finite checks supplement the preceding geometric proof; they do not prove the existence or nonexistence of an unrestricted S6 filling.

## Detailed development record: root_E25_E26_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent root check of E.2.5 and E.2.6

Checked on 15 November 2026. The hypotheses are a proper holomorphic submersion over a disc with smooth six-sphere fibres and a fixed known multiplicity pair on the puncture. The conclusions are restricted to this one-sided specialization and to a common smooth junction; they do not settle arbitrary deformation chains.

The relative root exists on the total space, not merely fibre by fibre. Ehresmann triviality over the disc gives vanishing integral H1 and H2 of the total space. The exponential sequence therefore identifies its Picard group with the complex vector space H1(O). Multiplication by the nonzero canonical weight D is bijective. This gives the unique root of K^A, and fibrewise uniqueness identifies it with the actual coarse pencil line. The direct image is coherent and torsion-free of generic rank two. The exact parameter-multiplication sequence gives an injection of its central fibre into H0 of the central line bundle, even if H0 jumps. Thus both central sections persist independently.

The primary CDP source, https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.11179, Theorem 2.1, was opened independently by root; its statement concerns a non-holomorphic meromorphic map to P1 and forces c3=0. The independently opened Lehn–Rollenske–Schinko source, https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.09719, Theorem 3.1 and Corollary 3.2, states the same obstruction explicitly. Their different assertion about a holomorphic algebraic reduction is not used. Because the central six-sphere has c3=2, the ratio of the two sections is holomorphic. Two algebraically independent meromorphic functions would give a local rank-two map, whose coordinate ratio has a genuine indeterminacy; thus the algebraic dimension is one. Stein factorization, H1=0, and connected fibres give the intrinsic P1 reduction.

For positive D the restriction of the root identity to a very general smooth surface fibre makes a positive canonical power effective. A positive Kodaira dimension of that fibre would supply two independent fibrewise canonical sections. A sufficiently positive twist of their coherent pushforward to P1 supplies global sections whose ratio varies on the fibre, contradicting algebraic reduction. Therefore the fibre has Kodaira dimension zero. Integral c1=0 excludes exceptional curves; minimal surface classification makes the canonical line torsion. An effective torsion divisor is zero. This removes every horizontal component of the fixed divisor.

The remaining step uses an actual proper divisor, not a formal limiting cycle. A generic combination of the relative frame has a flat Cartier zero divisor with smooth connected general torus fibre. It has no vertical component and is generically reduced, hence is reduced and irreducible. Its normalization is proper; its Stein factor over the normal disc is finite birational and therefore the disc. Its central support is connected. The proposed central zero divisor is the disjoint union of e smooth fibres and the vertical fixed part, chosen over other target points. Connectedness forces e=1 and an empty fixed part. The evaluation map is consequently base-point-free and extends the relative fibration. Local flatness follows from the two regular base parameters, or from the local flatness criterion applied first to the disc parameter and then to the torsion-free surface map to the target curve.

The common positive-slope junction calculation uses the same intrinsic pencil line on the central manifold. It is non-torsion, since a positive power has a nonzero section with a nonempty zero set. Equality of its two fractional canonical expressions forces equality of the slopes and hence of the multiplicity pairs by E.2.3. A common positive/negative junction would have sections of opposite canonical powers; their product forces the canonical line to be torsion. The Picard group of the smooth six-sphere is torsion-free, while a trivial canonical line would contradict Stokes' theorem for its nonzero holomorphic three-form. This checks the opposite-sign exclusion.

No gap was found in these restricted claims. The proof does not establish persistence on an arbitrary outgoing branch from an unconstructed central member, and it does not remove the horizontal fixed-divisor possibility for negative canonical slope. E.2.4 must remain Open.

# Sixth-hour mathematical and source audit

Work after checkpoint05, starting 17:39 UTC on 15 November 2026.
This record is cumulative; the checkpoint05 archive remains unchanged.

## Auxiliary parabolic and elliptic-cone constructions

- B.4.10 is now integrated. The proper Kato family, marked elliptic normal and nonextending exceptional correction were independently checked by boundary_d; its exact code passed. The former working note is retained as a historical draft.
- Root independently read the complete B.4.11 proof. The exact linear tubular neighbourhood identifies the contracted germ with the degree-eight elliptic cone. The logarithmic tangent restriction is the nonsplit Atiyah extension of a negative degree-eight line. Its higher positive normal pieces have no H1; analytic formal functions gives R1 pi_* Tlog = C_p. Varying the marked elliptic period produces a nonzero local class, so the global-to-local H1 map is onto. Leray and H2(Tlog)=0 give H2(Theta_Y)=0. The general local-to-global criterion is supported by the opened Dloussky--Teleman primary paper and, subsequently, the actual scan of Manetti1991 Lemma1, printed p93, obtained by boundary_d. The rational-cycle-specific theorem is not invoked for the elliptic curve.
- Root wrote B.4.12: choose a square root of the degree-eight line and two distinct degree-two factors, embed E as (2,2) in Q=P1 times P1, use the degree-one section on the anticanonical affine cone of Q, and globalize this specific smoothing component via B.4.11. Blowing up the single totalspace vertex gives a proper smooth threefold over a disc with central surface S8 union_E Q, local equation t=xi*z, and normals O_S(-E), K_Q. boundary_d independently passed the full proof. The explicit computation root_quadric_smoothing.py passes four affine smoothness checks, the Hilbert difference, the three normal products and the Smith matrix (2,2) -> (2,0).
- The new surface smoothing completes a real analytic construction needed by the proposed third piece. Its totalspace over the open disc is noncompact, and it is not asserted to be a compact normalization component of a threefold centre. Pairwise normal identities do not by themselves provide a global compatible smoothing section or the required punctured S6 comparison.
- Root independently checked B.4.13. Any lift of the second marked Hopf period would preserve the finite support of the total exceptional locus; a generic translation on the elliptic axis has no such invariant finite set. The obstruction remains for infinitely near centres and finite cycles of marked copies. Infinite disjoint-copy shifting gives the wrong core, and gluing distinct full compact copies over a common dense core cannot be Hausdorff. Connected noncompact unfoldings are not excluded.

## E.2 smooth-specialization restrictions

- E.2.7 was integrated after checkpoint05; its exact monodromy cancellation and independent D/root reviews are retained.
- Root read E.2.8 in full and passed the proof. The rank-one Alexander-duality/Leray calculation proves irreducibility of every reduced fibre and trivial H1 monodromy. A horizontal Cartier divisor then restricts nontrivially on every normalization. Pullback to a Kähler resolution would be a nonzero effective divisor with zero real Chern class, impossible by a positive Kähler integral. For negative canonical slope the general fibre has effective nonzero anti-pluricanonical divisor and c1(K)=0; it is minimal, has Kodaira dimension minus infinity and is non-Kähler, hence is class VII with b1=1. Non-Kähler fibre normalizations remain a live case, not an excluded one.

## Newly opened primary sources

- Pinkham, H. C. (1974), Deformations of algebraic varieties with G_m action, Asterisque20. Complete Numdam scan, Remark7.6(iii), printed p46, and Section9, printed pp63--65, were opened by root. The degree-eight quadric smoothing component is explicit in the original; our algebraic chart proof retains the precise chosen component. Numdam metadata was opened separately. The scan is excluded from the deliverable.
- Dloussky and Teleman (2020), Math. Z.296,1521--1537, DOI10.1007/s00209-020-02481-0, original arXiv1803.07631v2. Root opened the general deformation-germ comparison in Section3.2. Its rational-cycle-specific results are not substituted for the new elliptic-axis calculation.
- Manetti (1991), Normal degenerations of the complex projective plane, J. reine angew. Math.419,89--118, DOI10.1515/crll.1991.419.89, Lemma1 printed p93, was obtained from the original GDZ scan by boundary_d. It gives the exact general compact isolated-singularity criterion used in B.4.11. The scan remains outside the package.

No conclusion about a compact S6 analytic filling, its least semistable exponent, its central topology or its component-derived Chern numbers follows solely from these auxiliary constructions.

## Further proved advances before checkpoint06

The nonzero-degree strengthening of B.4.13 was independently checked by root: an automorphism preserving an elliptic axis induces an isomorphism t_b^*N=N on its normal line. For degree d the Abel--Jacobi difference is minus d times b, so an infinite-order translation cannot occur when d is nonzero. Finite cycles of marked caps are excluded by taking a power. This remains a statement about a preserved marked axis, not a proof excluding caps that occur only in the central parameter.

B.4.14 is integrated after two independent reviews. The holomorphic Lefschetz calculation uses the actual compact parabolic surface, the fixed elliptic curve of normal degree minus eight, and the eight rational-cycle nodes of weights plus/minus one. Its equivariant Euler characteristic is minus eight. A direct Hartogs and contracting-germ calculation shows that the sole global vector field is the transverse Euler field. Consequently H1(TlogE) has nine trivial characters and H1(ThetaY) has eight. The associated compact Y-bundle over P1 has H2 of its relative tangent sheaf zero, also after fixing one entire fibre. The nonisolated local-to-global and analytic effectivity problems are explicitly not inferred from that vanishing. The exact independent program ab_push_kato_weights.py and root rerun both pass.

E.2.9 resolves the horizontal-divisor obstruction itself. On each irreducible reduced fibre, twisting a vector bundle by minus a sufficiently large multiple of a nonzero effective Cartier divisor kills sections. A finite filtration extends this to all nonreduced fibres. Singular Gorenstein duality then kills fibre H2, uniformly by nested closed loci on the compact base. Coherent direct images and a positive base twist kill global H2 and H3. A negative divisor twist separately kills H0. An explicit affine line of exponentiated additive Cech cocycles joins the two line bundles, and the three proper analytic jumping sets cannot cover that line. For B=TX this forces chi(TX) at most zero, contradicting the exact Riemann--Roch value one. Root and boundary_d both read and passed the complete proof, including the nonreduced and analytic-base-change steps. The exact HRR program and root rerun pass.

E.2.10 therefore proves relative-pencil persistence for both signs of the nonzero canonical slope. The zero-period endpoint is excluded for either sign, and distinct ordered multiplicity pairs cannot have a common smooth one-sided limit. These statements do not prove uniqueness of a smooth completion at an interior period or rule out new deformation branches on which the pencil disappears. Those are the remaining E.2.4 gaps.

E.2.8 was strengthened with a second use of the divisor restriction argument: every component of a reducible fibre on a complex six-sphere has normalization outside class C. Another meeting component cuts a nonzero effective Cartier divisor on it, pulled back from a topologically trivial line on the threefold; a Kähler resolution would give a positive integral equal to zero. This is a necessary constraint on possible critical-value collisions, not a classification of their non-Kähler components.

The sixth-hour source comparisons also explicitly retain two limitations. Cathelineau1978 concerns deformations with a fibrewise action over a trivial-action base, so it does not supply a moving-base action on the singular Y smoothing. Tziolas2009, Section9 and Remark9.7, warns that vanishing of H2 tangent alone does not preserve arbitrary specified local lifts for a nonisolated singular set. Neither result is stretched into the missing compactification theorem.

Fujimoto1988 Example3.4, read together with Example3.1 Step4 on printed pp98--99, provides an isotrivial punctured elliptic-surface family with non-Kähler central components. Choosing the elliptic product gives an abelian-surface instance. Thus even an isotrivial projective torus on the punctured disc does not justify a meromorphic comparison with every proper filling. The source does not construct the generically simple prescribed torus family.

## Build record

Draft20 stopped at its third stage because a new bibliography note contained an unescaped underscore in the official Springer preview URL. The URL was wrapped in the existing LaTeX url command; the original failed logs are retained. Draft21 then compiled all four stages with zero overfull boxes and passed the complete integration audit: 154 pages, 118 statements, 95 bibliography entries. Draft22 integrated the E.2.9--10 and B.4.14 proofs: 159 pages, 121 statements, 96 bibliography entries, all four stages successful and no overfull boxes. Draft23 includes the later strengthened B.4.13 and E.2.8 statements. Its four build stages and full integration audit pass. Root viewed the three contact sheets individually, spanning 22 distinct pages, and additionally inspected page 139 at full rendered resolution. No clipping, overflow or other layout issue was observed in this sample. The exact record is qa/draft23/visual_inspection.json.

## Actual regular-place insertion reviewed before checkpoint06

The separate working proof reserved for B.4.15 is in ab_push_regular_cap_working.md and is deliberately not counted among the 121 integrated draft23 statements. Root read it in full and reran its exact chart/incidence program successfully. boundary_d independently passed the actual semiabelian action, the affine-to-Jacobian comparison on the local punctured bidisc, the invariant blow-up ideals, both resolution charts, the Cartier transition cocycle, properness and the scalar Hopf identification; the full referee record is D_B4415_referee.md.

The construction blows up a regular point of the two-dimensional parameter base and twists by the exceptional line using an actual flat cocharacter u. It is canonically isomorphic to the prescribed local family off that base point. Twelve compact vertical normalization components appear, each a projective-line bundle over a scalar Hopf surface or its blow-up along two disjoint elliptic curves. Their first Betti number is one. The total fourfold is smooth and its divisor r=0 is reduced normal crossings; the map is flat over r and proper over the bidisc. It is not jointly flat over the bidisc, and no properness over the r-disc alone is claimed before a global insertion over a compact q-base.

This is a construction directly on the prescribed torus germ and preserves generic simplicity. It therefore rules out generic simplicity alone as an exclusion of non-ddbar vertical components in this two-parameter setting. It preserves component labels and does not repair the cusp rotation obstruction. It supplies neither a global S6 filling nor topology for such an unconstructed filling.

# Seventh-hour mathematical and source audit

Work after checkpoint06, starting 18:39 UTC on 15 November 2026.
The sixth checkpoint is immutable. This record distinguishes integrated proofs, source corrections, and remaining construction problems.

## Actual two-parameter constructions and their limits

B.4.15 and B.4.16 are integrated. Root and boundary_d independently checked the actual regular-place Hopf insertion, including the semiabelian action, the invariant small-resolution ideals, both local charts, the Cartier transition cocycle, and the identification with the prescribed family off the single base point. Root reran the exact regular-cap and dual-complex programs. The map is proper over the bidisc and flat over the degeneration parameter, but is not jointly flat: compact vertical threefold components occur over the base origin. This distinction is essential to the later torus-purity lemma. The attached dual complex retracts onto the old cycle; the exact elementary collapses and chain Smith forms verify that this operation does not remove the global component-rotation obstruction.

B.4.17 gives a compact counterexample to a published monodromy converse. Compactifying the base of the explicit Hopf degeneration yields a smooth compact total threefold and a map to P1 with one SNC singular fibre. Its nearby first Betti number is one, its central first Betti number is zero, and its local H1 monodromy is the identity. Root opened the original published two-page excerpt of Honda--Viaclovsky, in which Theorem1.1 on printed p553 explicitly states the contradicted converse. The complete journal PDF was not obtained; the full arXiv version had been opened. The valid inequality, the forward implication from equality to trivial monodromy, and the separate irreducible-fibre corollary are retained. The error is the converse inference from an inequality with two successive bounds. No claim is made that an invalid proof alone refutes a theorem: the explicit compact example and exact computation supply the refutation.

B.4.18 supplies a corrected cusp family on the bidisc with its origin removed. On the logarithmic cover the translation b=gamma/12-delta/24 has integral twelvefold norm gamma-6delta. The explicit quadratic logarithmic function has q-difference b and r-difference gamma, an actual period. The half-period scalar is necessary and is checked in the twelvefold multiplicative action. Thus the rotating central model and the actual reduced punctured Mumford cusp are holomorphically isomorphic on their overlap. Gluing these proper local models gives a proper smooth-total family over the punctured bidisc, with the required negative component rotation and the exact prescribed reduced cusp for every nonzero degeneration parameter. Root and boundary_d passed the full proof. This reduces the local missing base set to the origin; it does not construct a proper completion there. The comparison contains logarithms, and no meromorphic graph closure is assumed.

## Smooth fixed-pair limits in E.2

E.2.11 was written by root and independently reviewed by classification_e and boundary_d. It is a conditional purity statement: a proper flat torus torsor over a punctured complex bidisc, with smooth total space and a smooth torus group extending over the whole bidisc, extends as a smooth torsor. Invariant vertical vector fields extend across a codimension-two analytic set. Their period identities extend and produce the actual holomorphic torus-group action. Compact complex isotropy has trivial tangent representation on the fibre tangent space; the base quotient is also fixed. Compact averaging then makes any connected isotropy trivial. The resulting full-dimensional orbits reduce a possible singularity to a finite map between smooth surface germs, whose branch divisor cannot be supported at one point. Finite isotropy is excluded by the same linearization after smoothness. A good Jacobian is a hypothesis, not a consequence of flatness.

E.2.12 applies that lemma using the actual marked periods and the interior parameter limit. It proves that there are no additional central critical values beyond the three labelled limits. It distinguishes an ordinary constant source torus, which may be nonalgebraic, from a finite-root constant source torus, which splits up to isogeny as two elliptic curves. The cusp cannot be a constant source point because its nontrivial unipotent H1 monodromy cannot disappear. Both independent source-level referees passed.

E.2.13 controls an isolated finite multiple fibre. The normalized multiplicity-root base change is locally a disjoint union of copies of the smooth original fourfold and is therefore etale. Its exact good marked Jacobian extends over the root bidisc, so E.2.11 proves smoothness. The free deck action and exact multiplicity persist. Root and boundary_d passed the proof; normalization is not confused with the intersecting branches of the unnormalized fibre product.

E.2.14 extends the normalized delta vector field by a primitive generator of the rank-one direct image of the relative tangent bundle. Comparison on a regular central torus excludes a vanishing scalar. Its time-one identity and compact circle action extend; invariant Ehresmann transport preserves the fixed P1, its opposite normal weights, and its two degree-minus-one normal lines. A finite-root collapsed torus would give the same global field a second independent complex period, hence a nontrivial compact elliptic action. Such an action cannot have a fixed point, whereas the preserved P1 consists of fixed points. This excludes all finite-root collisions. Root and boundary_d passed the complete proof.

E.2.15 excludes the remaining ordinary-source collapse. The actual canonical identity K_X^A=f^*O(D) supplies a nowhere-zero local pluricanonical form over the compact inverse image of the last critical value. Its smooth positive density has finite mass. Constant marked torus periods make the fibre volume constant, so the scalar coefficient P on the punctured base lies in L^(2/A). Subharmonicity gives q^A P tending to zero, even when 2/A<1. The resulting pole bound forces D<-A. The actual slope satisfies D/A>=-1/2, a contradiction. Root and classification_e passed the full proof. This uses the holomorphic canonical identity, not just vanishing topological Chern classes, and makes no meromorphic-comparison assumption.

Consequently the target does not collapse: the three labelled critical values remain distinct and both finite multiple fibres are controlled by their actual smooth root charts. This is a substantial strengthening of the one-sided specialization theorem. The global analytic filling of B and arbitrary deformation chains in E are not declared settled by it.

## Original singularity source and the remaining cusp

Root searched for torus-fibration purity, isotrivial pluricanonical volume arguments, holomorphic torus actions and isolated critical-value degenerations. No exact source proving the new combined application was located; no priority is claimed. Bochner1945 metadata and a Hartogs survey were consulted, but no unread Bochner theorem is invoked: the averaging argument is included directly in E.2.11.

The original Lazzeri1973 paper, A theorem on the monodromy of isolated singularities, Asterisque7--8,269--275, was opened in full from Numdam. Theorem2 and its corollary on printed pp274--275 were read; the proof gives a strict numerical deficit for the sum of Milnor numbers when a singularity splits among several points of one deformed fibre. Root and classification_e each checked the original source. Greuel--Pfister2022, Journal of Singularities25,173--196, DOI10.5427/jsing.2022.25j, was also opened while investigating delta semicontinuity, but the shorter proof uses Lazzeri directly. A Milnor book scan was opened but had no extracted text; no unread theorem from it is substituted for Lazzeri.

The new E.2.17 draft applies non-splitting to the actual cusp, assuming the irreducibility and reducedness being formalized in E.2.16. At a free delta point a holomorphic flow box reduces the cusp map to a reduced plane function. Conservation of its critical multiplicities and the absence of nonzero critical values force one nearby singularity; because the known free locus has only nodes, the central germ is a node too. At the fixed P1, circle weights give q=xy*g(xy,z,s). The leading coefficient is a nonzero section of O(2); reducedness excludes its being identically zero. Collision of its two zeros would yield two distinct singularities of one nearby plane zero fibre, contrary to non-splitting and conservation. Thus the two triple points remain distinct and all cusp charts remain relative normal crossings. The complete source is E_cusp_normal_crossings.tex; its integration and full-source review are recorded separately as they complete.

The next identification problem is precise: classify the simultaneous normalization with its conductor hexagon, then recover the entire marked analytic cusp neighbourhood from that relative normal-crossing geometry and the known torus periods. No conclusion about that entire neighbourhood is imported from the local charts alone.

## Build and retention record

Draft24 integrated B.4.15--17 and E.2.11--12. All four compiler stages completed with zero overfull boxes, but the final log still requested a cross-reference rerun; its integration audit correctly failed on that warning. Incremental draft25 completed all four stages with zero overfull boxes and passed the full audit, including 126 statement rows and97 bibliography entries. No claim of visual inspection of draft25 is made; the next checkpoint build will receive its own rendered sample, including the seventh-hour additions. All intermediate logs remain in the package.

The metadata-only source record is evidence/root_seventh_hour_sources.json. Original external scans and full retrieved article bodies remain outside the deliverable.

Root subsequently opened the supplied AC26 original PDF at printed p30, Proposition4.7 and Corollary4.8, checking the actual toric covering, primitive vanishing lattice and extended zero section. The E.2.20 working proof now includes an independent star-shaped-chart and van Kampen derivation. Its cusp-neighbourhood reconstruction has independent classification_e and boundary_d passes. It remains a working proof until converted into the numbered source and applied to the global affine collars. The arbitrary-chain consequence remains separate: ordinary curve selection does not give a punctured holomorphic disc through the boundary of an arbitrary open known-member locus.


## Completed integrations before checkpoint07

E.2.16 and E.2.17 were fully reviewed by root, classification_e and boundary_d and integrated. E.2.18 identifies the normalized central pair by three analytic minus-one contractions and the compact-surface classification, and gives a global relative logarithmic frame by Grauert evaluation. E.2.19 descends that frame across the conductor, extends it over the full cusp bidisc, and constructs the marked holomorphic (C*)² action. ## Last orientation correction before checkpoint07

A final global-marking check found that B.4.18 had selected the negative affine deck for a positive q-loop while comparing it with the clockwise convention of B.3.1--2. The actual required choice is +b,+F for the positive q-root generator: its component shift is +1, and the inverse clockwise generator has shift minus one. Both-sign local constructions exist; this correction selects the one compatible with the finite shifts plus four and minus three. The source, CSV and report were corrected, and the map direction is now explicit: the Jacobian maps to the +b torsor by zeta plus F, and the retained actual family maps by zeta minus sigma plus F. # Independent full-source referee: E.2.11

15 November 2026. Read `sections/E_torus_purity.tex` in full. Verdict: **PASS** at the explicit good-Jacobian hypothesis.

Flatness puts the omitted fibre in complex codimension two in the smooth total space, so coefficientwise Hartogs extension applies to the infinitesimal action fields. Their verticality and commutativity continue by uniqueness. Completeness follows from compactness of each fibre and the ordinary differential equation extension criterion; local compactness gives uniform continuation on each finite time path. Period identities hold on the dense ordinary locus and therefore on the whole total space.

The fixed real Betti torus acts by holomorphic automorphisms: its real coefficients are constants, while the period vectors are holomorphic in the base. At an isotropy identity component, the representation on the Zariski tangent of the fibre is holomorphic in a compact complex torus, hence trivial. The base tangent quotient is fixed. Compact averaging supplies an invariant complement, so the full derivative is trivial. The explicit averaging-coordinate argument then eliminates positive-dimensional isotropy. This argument does not assume that the family of group tori is holomorphically constant.

The resulting independent commuting fields give a holomorphic local product. Equidimensionality makes the transverse map a finite germ; its Jacobian divisor cannot have image supported at one isolated base point. Thus the map is a submersion. The remaining finite isotropy acts trivially on the orbit tangent because the group is abelian, and on the base quotient because the action is vertical. The same compact linearization eliminates it. Local sections of the resulting submersion give genuine torsor charts.

No Kähler assumption, polarization, section of the original torsor, general discriminant purity theorem, or separatedness of arbitrary complex-space completions is used. The good Jacobian extension is indispensable and remains an explicit hypothesis. The proof is complete within these stated conditions.

## Detailed development record: D_E2211_E2212_referee.md

# Independent full-source referee: E.2.11 and E.2.12

15 November 2026. **PASS for both statements at their explicit scope.**
Read in full: `sections/E_torus_purity.tex` and
`sections/E_no_extra_critical_values.tex`.

## E.2.11: the abstract extension criterion

Flatness and the given fibre dimension make the preimage of the missing
base point complex codimension two. Thus the infinitesimal torus-action
fields extend coefficientwise by Hartogs on the smooth total space.
Verticality and commutation extend by the identity theorem. Every
complex-time flow is complete: it remains inside one compact fibre,
and local ODE existence on a finite covering of that compact set gives
continuation past every finite time.

The holomorphic period sections produce a fixed compact real torus
action by holomorphic automorphisms of the entire total space. Integral
period translations are the identity on the dense smooth locus, hence
everywhere. More explicitly, the relative Cn action descends to a
holomorphic action of G times_B X, since translation by every variable
period is already the identity on X. Its action on a scheme-theoretic
fibre, including its nilpotent structure, is therefore legitimate. This
explicit observation justifies using that fibre's Zariski tangent space
in the stabilizer argument without assuming it is reduced.

The connected stabilizer is a compact complex subgroup of the complex
torus G_b. Its representation on the fibre's Zariski tangent is
holomorphic and hence constant. The quotient tangent representation is
trivial because the action fixes the base. Averaging over the compact
stabilizer splits these two representations. The full tangent action is
trivial. The averaged-coordinate construction then gives an invariant
holomorphic coordinate system with invertible derivative; it makes the
stabilizer ineffective in a neighbourhood and hence on connected X.
Freeness on the dense given torsor rules it out.

Zero-dimensional stabilizers make the n commuting fields independent.
The transverse holomorphic two-dimensional slice gives a finite germ
h to the bidisc because its fibre is zero-dimensional. If its Jacobian
vanished, its nonzero determinant would have a curve of zeros, whose
finite image would be a curve of critical values. This contradicts
smoothness off the one base point. Hence g is a submersion.

Each compact full-dimensional orbit in a connected smooth fibre is
then the entire fibre. The residual finite stabilizer acts trivially
on the fibre tangent because the group is abelian, and trivially on
the base quotient. The same finite-group averaging argument eliminates
it. Local holomorphic sections of the submersion consequently identify
the extended family with a G-torsor. No polarization, Kähler hypothesis,
or extension of an affine origin was inserted.

## E.2.12: applicability of the good-Jacobian hypothesis

The three critical graph limits are actual central critical values:
the relative critical image is proper and closed, and smoothness of
the total deformation identifies the relative rank condition with
criticality of the central map. Any other central critical point can
therefore be isolated in a bidisc avoiding all three graph closures.

The meromorphic PGL2 matrix has either an invertible central matrix or
one rank-one limit with one exceptional target point. At least two
labelled preimages tend to that exceptional point, so a hypothetical
extra value is not that point. Uniform convergence to its constant
source image follows. In the finite-source-root cases the expression
is s^n times a holomorphic unit: a zero of the putative leading unit
would create a nonvertical divisor of zeros, precisely a labelled
critical graph, contrary to the choice of bidisc. This unit condition
is essential and is correctly included.

A nearby central regular target gives a smooth torus family over the
whole parameter disc, so its H1 return is the identity. Interiority of
the parameter supplies a single-valued logarithm and no parameter
shear. The finite root orders force 3|n or 4|n. The unit has a
holomorphic root on the simply connected bidisc; pulling back the
actual finite-root period model gives a smooth G over that bidisc.
Additional multiplicity translations do not affect this Jacobian.
The cusp alternative would give the nonzero u-to-w shear in A0^n and
is excluded. An ordinary source image directly gives the ordinary
smooth period model.

The punctured bidisc is simply connected. Its actual torus family and
the reference G therefore admit the same extended integral marking,
after one constant integral change. Their holomorphic period
subbundles coincide on s nonzero and hence on the entire punctured
bidisc. This identifies Jacobians as group families, not only
individual unmarked fibres, and does not assume an extended torsor
section. All hypotheses of E.2.11 now hold.

The final distinction is correct: a finite-root constant image gives
an abelian surface, by the two rational cyclotomic idempotents and an
isogeny to a product of elliptic curves. An ordinary constant image
gives an isotrivial complex torus which need not be abelian. No
projectivity or uniqueness statement for the total central threefold
follows. Excluding these remaining collapsed-target completions and
new deformation branches remains separate.

# Independent full-source referee: E.2.13 and E.2.14

15 November 2026. **PASS for both statements at their explicit scope.**
Read in full: `sections/E_finite_fibre_persistence.tex` and
`sections/E_delta_extension.tex`.

## E.2.13

The divisor equality g*Gamma=mD holds on the entire smooth fourfold:
an additional divisor supported over the specialization parameter would
have to be the whole central threefold, which the nonconstant central
map does not send to Gamma(0). Local factoriality makes D Cartier.
Thus g*xi=a^m u, including at a possibly initially singular central
restriction of D. The normalized root cover is locally the disjoint
union of the graphs r=zeta a u^(1/m), each a copy of the original smooth
space. It really is an etale cover, with free deck permutation even
over a=0. This is a statement about normalization, not the unreduced
fibre product.

Properness, equidimensionality and smooth source/base give flatness of
the map to the root bidisc. Connectedness on the dense punctured part
and the normal Stein factor give connected fibres. Known smooth root
charts for nonzero specialization parameter, together with E.2.12
away from the isolated section, put every possible remaining critical
point over the single bidisc origin.

The noncollapsed source coordinate is xi times a unit. In the collapsed
case it is s^n xi times a unit, and the isolated section must be the
preimage of the limiting finite marked source point. A nearby central
regular target forces e|n. Since e also divides m, both displayed
root coordinates are holomorphic on the full root bidisc. The actual
Jacobian identification is the same marking/period argument already
refereed in E.2.12. E.2.11 therefore applies, and makes the root family
smooth. The etale covering descends a smooth reduced finite fibre of
exact multiplicity m. The free affine quotient and its smooth parameter
family follow. No assertion concerning a cusp or a cusp cluster enters.

## E.2.14

The direct image of the relative tangent bundle is a coherent
torsion-free rank-one sheaf on the smooth parameter disc, hence a free
module after shrinking. The punctured normalized fields patch because
the fixed-parameter comparison maps preserve the marked delta period
and have identity fibre-linear action. On a central regular torus
bidisc, the good-Jacobian comparison extends that period as a nonzero
holomorphic Lie section. Comparing a nonzero coefficient makes the
scalar b(s) extend holomorphically. If b(0)=0, the generating field
would vanish on an open subset of the central connected threefold,
hence everywhere, and its coefficients would be divisible by s.
The quotient would be another global relative field, contradicting
primitivity of the generator. This proves the actual global delta
extension, not merely existence of some central vector field.

Completeness on compact fibres makes the period-one identity extend
to the entire family. Averaging an Ehresmann connection gives genuine
equivariant smooth transport for the resulting circle action. Its fixed
two-sphere, effectiveness, semifree weights and both normal degrees
persist. The fixed set is a complex submanifold by compact-group
averaged coordinates; genus zero makes the curve P1. Its two distinct
weight lines remain holomorphic lines of degree minus one, so the
normal bundle is precisely O(-1) plus O(-1).

At either finite-root torus, the rank-two fixed lattice spans the
complex delta line and supplies a second constant nonreal period.
Its flow is the identity on the dense regular part and therefore on
the entire central fibre. The resulting elliptic action is nontrivial.
A compact connected complex group's derivative representation at a
fixed point is holomorphic and constant; averaging holomorphic
coordinates then makes the action locally, hence globally, trivial.
This contradicts the fixed rational curve. Equivalently the field
would be everywhere nonzero and force Euler number zero. The argument
retains the explicit limitation that an ordinary torus may have only
one real delta period; E.2.15 treats that separate remaining case.

# Independent full-source referee: E.2.15

15 November 2026. Read `sections/E_one_critical_volume.tex` in full. Verdict: **PASS**.

On the simply connected ordinary base C, the integral marking is global. The period subbundle takes values in one countable integral-change-of-basis orbit. A connected subset of a metric space with more than one point is uncountable, by composing with distance to one of its points, so the marked period subbundle is constant. The corresponding invariant relative volume descends under translations of local origins. Its fibre integral is therefore the same positive constant on every ordinary torus, with no need for a torsor section or polarization.

The actual line-bundle identity K_X^A=f*O(D) produces σ with divisor Df*(p). Consequently Ω_A=q^(−D)σ is a holomorphic nowhere-zero A-canonical form on the entire inverse image of a small disc, including nonreduced fibres. This is stronger than a merely numerical canonical relation and is exactly the input available from E.2.10.

The coordinate-invariant density |Ω_A|^(2/A) is smooth and positive on the actual smooth compact total space. Fibre integration of its expression on the punctured neighbourhood gives the asserted L^(2/A) bound for P=q^(−D−2A)φ(1/q). The factor q^(−2A) is the A-th power of dt=−q^(−2)dq; the sign (−1)^A is harmless. No volume bound on an unconstructed comparison graph is assumed.

For every positive exponent, |P|^(2/A) is subharmonic. The mean-value estimate on a disc of radius |q|/2 gives |q|^A|P(q)|→0 uniformly. Therefore q^A P extends holomorphically with a zero, and P has pole order at most A−1. It follows that φ(1/q)=q^(D+A+1)H(q). If D≥−A this extends with value zero at infinity, contradicting the nonzero entire function φ. The conclusion D<−A and the application D/A≥−1/2 are correct.

The proof is independent of a Kähler metric, meromorphic extension of a comparison with a reference filling, and classification of non-Kähler singular fibres. It excludes the ordinary constant-image collapsed target, while not asserting uniqueness of a noncollapsed completion or persistence on unrelated deformation branches.

# Independent referee: E.2.16

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026. Full source reviewed:
`sections/E_cusp_reducedness.tex`, together with
`code/e_push_interior_cusp.py/.out/.json`.

**Conclusion: PASS at the stated scope.** The conclusion is reducedness
and irreducibility of the actual remaining cusp surface, with Euler
characteristic two and first rational Betti number two. It does not yet
classify that surface analytically.

1. E.2.14 and E.2.15 exclude the finite-root and ordinary constant-image
   degenerations respectively, while E.2.12 excludes a cusp constant
   image. A nondegenerate limiting projective transformation therefore
   preserves the three distinct labelled values. E.2.13 supplies exact
   finite multiplicities, not merely divisibility.

2. The degree-one Leray calculation is over the punctured projective
   line, which has cohomological dimension one for these local systems.
   Its invariant H1 space has rank one. Alexander duality on the actual
   S6 gives the number of irreducible components: top-degree rational
   cohomology of a compact pure complex surface has one generator per
   irreducible component, also when the surface is nonnormal. Thus the
   total of three components forces one in each fibre.

3. The meridian quotient is valid without a stack structure. The singular
   subsets of the reduced divisors have real codimension at least four
   in the smooth threefold; a generic null-homotopy disc avoids them and
   meets only smooth divisor points. The normal generation by meridians
   follows. The local defining function has the exact multiplicity of
   its divisor, so the induced quotient is the triangle presentation
   displayed in the proof.

4. For d at least two, the stated hyperbolic inequality fails only at
   (m,n,d)=(3,4,2). In the hyperbolic cases the displayed cosh is strictly
   larger than one: this is equivalent to alpha+beta+theta<pi. The
   product trace is -2 cos(theta); its eigenangle pi-theta has exact
   projective order d. The two factors have exact projective orders m
   and n. The exceptional permutations have product (24), with the
   stated composition convention. Hence the quotient cannot be trivial.

5. Euler additivity uses the proper smooth fibration off three points
   and zero Euler characteristic for ordinary tori and the two finite
   free quotients. It therefore gives chi(S)=2. For b1(S), I reopened
   the original Honda--Viaclovsky arXiv v1 PDF at
   https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.14420 (tool refs turn493view0/turn493view1).
   Printed page 11, Corollary 3.7 and its proof (3.22)--(3.23) give the
   H1 invariant-cycle equality under exactly the irreducibility now
   proved. The equality is independent of their incorrect unrestricted
   converse in Theorem 1.2/Theorem 3.2, audited in B.4.17. The cusp
   invariant rank is two, so the asserted value follows.

No use of a Kahler completion, unproved meromorphic comparison, or
classification of nonnormal cusp surfaces enters this proposition.

# Independent referee: E.2.17

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026. Full source reviewed:
`sections/E_cusp_normal_crossings.tex`.

**Conclusion: PASS at the stated scope.** The argument establishes the
relative local forms q=z1, q=z1*z2 and q=z1*z2*z3, with the original
specialization parameter unchanged, and therefore a proper smooth
simultaneous normalization. It does not yet identify the entire marked
Mumford neighbourhood.

I independently opened the original Lazzeri scan, not just its metadata:
https://www.numdam.org/item/AST_1973__7-8__269_0.pdf
(tool ref turn499view0). Printed pp.274--275 contain Theorem 2, its
corollary and the strict inequality in the corollary's proof. The final
remark explicitly gives the nonsplitting interpretation for a deformed
isolated hypersurface singularity. The proof uses the strict inequality,
not the weaker statement that a submodule is proper.

## Local arguments checked

- The critical scheme of a reduced isolated plane germ in a holomorphic
  one-parameter deformation is finite over a sufficiently small parameter
  disc. Its two gradient equations form a regular sequence in a regular
  three-dimensional local ring; the finite central fibre makes the base
  parameter a nonzerodivisor. Thus its total critical multiplicity is
  conserved. The actual no-extra-critical-value statement places all
  those critical points over q=0, precisely the extra hypothesis needed
  to apply nonsplitting.

- Off the fixed curve, the nonzero relative holomorphic vector field
  gives a flow box with q independent of its flow coordinate. Reducedness
  makes the transverse plane germ reduced. Every nearby singular point
  there is a node. Conservation and nonsplitting force a single node and
  central Milnor number one; the parameter Morse lemma then gives the
  relative double-crossing form.

- Along the fixed curve, compact-circle averaging preserves the
  specialization coordinate and gives weights (0,+1,-1,0). The invariant
  power series is q=T*g(T,z,s), T=xy. Its linear coefficient in T is a
  section of the dual product of the two normal weight lines, namely
  O(2). It cannot vanish identically on the central fixed curve: this
  would make q divisible by x^2*y^2, contradicting reducedness.

- If the two zeros of that section collided, F0(T,z)=T*g0(T,z) would be
  a reduced isolated plane germ. A repeated nonunit factor remains
  repeated after the substitution T=xy, and T does not divide g0; this
  verifies the reducedness used here. Both nearby triple points give
  distinct critical points of Fs with value zero. Every other critical
  point has value zero as well: at T=0 this is automatic; at T nonzero
  the substitution map (x,y,z)->(xy,z) is a submersion and its lifts lie
  in the actual coordinate neighbourhood after shrinking. Conservation
  plus nonsplitting is therefore a genuine contradiction.

- The two zeros are simple. At a zero, replacing z by g(xy,z,s) is an
  invertible holomorphic coordinate change; away from them g is a unit
  and is absorbed into one normal coordinate. These changes keep s
  fixed. They give precisely the triple- and double-crossing equations.

- Compactness of the actual central cusp allows one common shrinking.
  In each displayed chart normalization separates smooth relative
  branches and commutes with the parameter fibre. Finiteness of analytic
  normalization gives the asserted global properness.

No arbitrary quotient smoothness assertion, Kahler assumption,
separatedness theorem, or meromorphic comparison is used.

# Independent referee: corrected cusp over the punctured bidisc

## Superseding orientation correction, 19:41 UTC

The local existence/freeness/gauge calculation below remains valid for
either sign, but its original selection of the negative sign as the
retained global marking was wrong. B.3 uses **clockwise** finite root deck
generators with shifts +4 and -3. Its required clockwise cusp generator
has shift -1. The displayed H in B.4.18 rotates the q root **positively**;
it must therefore use **+b,+F**, with shift +1. Its inverse is the required
clockwise generator. AB has corrected the integrated statement and proof.
The correctly typed comparison from the actual affine family is
`zeta -> zeta-sigma+F`. In particular `F_1-sigma_1=mu s/2` is independent
of r; an obstruction computed using F alone does not apply to this map.

Reviewer: boundary_d. Date: 15 November 2026. Reviewed source:
`sections/ab_push_corrected_cusp_overlap_working.md`, with exact certificate
`code/ab_push_corrected_cusp_overlap.py`, its output and JSON. This is a
referee record of the stated partial construction, not a completion claim.

## Conclusion

**PASS at the stated punctured-bidisc scope.** The rotating model has an
explicit holomorphic comparison with the actual family on the two-puncture
overlap. Gluing this model to the prescribed reduced cusp gives a proper
Hausdorff family over the bidisc minus its origin, with smooth total space,
flatness over the degeneration parameter, the required component rotation
on the other axis, and the actual reduced cusp on the punctured cusp axis.
The missing origin is a substantive remaining analytic extension problem.

## Exact affine calculation

Write `gamma=(6 mu,C-s-12t)` and `delta=(0,1)`, where `mu,C` are
single-valued holomorphic functions of `q=exp(2 pi i s)`. For
`b=gamma/12-delta/24`, its twelve successive q-deck translates sum to
`gamma-6 delta`, an actual period. Its r-deck difference is `-delta`.
The proposed function

`f=(mu(s+12t)/2, C(s+12t)/12-(s+12t)^2/24+6t)`

has differences `Delta_s f=b` and `Delta_t f=gamma` exactly. Thus its
translation descends to the required torsor comparison. The r-difference
is a period, rather than zero; this is sufficient and must be retained in
the descent argument. Composing the previously established B.4.9 affine
gauge identifies the actual retained family, including its marking.

The `-delta/24` correction is necessary. In multiplicative coordinates the
twelfth iterate has a phase `exp(2 pi i (0+...+11)/12)=-1` from the q-root
rotation and a second phase `exp(pi i)=-1` from this correction. They cancel.
The first-coordinate iterate is `exp(12 pi i mu)`, the first multiplier of
the actual gamma period. The second-coordinate iterate is its actual unit
times `q r^12`. The certificate checks these rational identities without a
floating-point approximation.

For the desired negative rotation, use `-b` and `-f` with the same positive
rotation of the q-root coordinate. Its valuation shear is the inverse of
the displayed positive shear; its twelfth power is `G2^-1`. It preserves
the same fan. This distinction fixes the sign without changing any of the
properness or smoothness arguments.

## Fan, action, and local quotient

The specified unit-prism triangulation is preserved by the two integral
period shears and by the fractional shear. The height-one r-components
have labels `j mod 12`; the deck generator shifts them by one. At any
point of the r-central divisor at most two adjacent such branches meet,
so no nonidentity cyclic shift can preserve its set of local branches.
This proves freeness there, including over the common base origin.

On the q-central divisor with r nonzero, the bottom cone is a triangle in
one unit square. Its set of second labels lies in `{j,j+1}`. Translation
of these labels by a nonzero element of `Z/12` cannot preserve the set.
This is the explicit local-branch justification for the source's shorter
phrase "nonzero translation of the q-component grid." Off both axes the
root-base action is free. The smooth finite quotient therefore has the
asserted properties. Its q-fibre has multiplicity twelve; it is not the
actual reduced q-cusp, and the working proof correctly says so.

Properness on the root cover uses the already proved compact-core
plumbing and its unfolded toric charts, not the determinants alone.
The determinant checks establish smooth local charts; they do not by
themselves establish properness of an infinite period quotient.

## Two-open gluing and exact remaining gap

The q-nonzero and r-nonzero base open sets cover the punctured bidisc.
Their common inverse image is identified by the explicit holomorphic
translation above. There is no omitted triple-overlap condition. Being a
proper Hausdorff analytic map is local on the target in this situation,
and the two restrictions are proper Hausdorff maps; smoothness and
flatness over r are likewise local. On the r-nonzero open set the model is
the actual prescribed reduced Mumford cusp, not merely an abstract torus
family with matching periods.

The comparison contains quadratic logarithms. The proof supplies no
meromorphic extension of its graph across the origin, no proper analytic
origin fibre, and no global compatibility with the finite-place models.
One cannot invoke graph closure, a toroidal common refinement, or an
unrestricted analytic semistable-reduction theorem without proving their
hypotheses. The multiplicity-twelve q-model cannot simply replace the
reduced actual cusp. The next task is an actual completion of the glued
punctured-bidisc family, not a further verification of these affine
identities.

# The missing cusp origin: comparison and base-modification audit

## Superseding correction, 19:41 UTC

**The proposed application of the calculation below to the actual cusp
completion is withdrawn.** The retained finite-place convention in B.3
uses clockwise root deck generators. Consequently the required clockwise
cusp shift is minus one, while the positively rotating q-root generator
in B.4.18 must use **+b and +F**, with positive-q shift plus one. The
earlier choice of -b/-F had the wrong retained global orientation.

More importantly, the actual comparison is
`zeta -> zeta-sigma+F`, not translation by F alone. With
`c=-12t`, the known affine trivialization has
`sigma_1=6 mu t`, so

`F_1-sigma_1=mu s/2`.

Its first elliptic coordinate is independent of r. Thus the nonconvergent
elliptic orbit obtained below by studying F or -F alone in Jacobian
coordinates is **not an obstruction to the corrected actual comparison**.
That isolated calculation concerns a specified different comparison and
must not be used to rule out a base-blow-up construction or an origin cap
for the retained family. The cancellation is potentially useful for the
next constructive attempt. The actual origin completion remains Open.

Owner: boundary_d. 15 November 2026, 19:15 UTC.

## Current target and established input

The accepted partial construction in
`ab_push_corrected_cusp_overlap_working.md` is proper over
`(Delta_q x Delta_r) minus {(0,0)}`. It has the actual reduced Mumford
cusp on `r != 0` and the required negative component rotation on
`q != 0, r=0`. The exact comparison on the two-puncture overlap is the
translation `-f` (composed with the established affine gauge), where

`f_1=mu(s+12t)/2`,

`f_2=C(s+12t)/12-(s+12t)^2/24+6t`,

`s=log q/(2 pi i)`, `t=log r/(2 pi i)`.

The remaining problem is a proper analytic extension over the origin
with the required smooth-total-space and normal-crossings properties.
The present note does not solve it.

## A precise limitation of finite base blow-ups and graph comparison

The proposed translation has essential nonextension already on a fixed
generic slice `q=q_0 != 0` of the r-axis. This explains why blowing up
only the bidisc origin cannot turn the two full toric models into
meromorphically comparable models. The statement concerns the specified
comparison; it is not an obstruction to every analytic cap.

Fix a logarithm `s=s_0`, and abbreviate

`tau=tau(q_0)`, `mu=mu(q_0)`, `beta_0=C(q_0)-s_0`,

`a_mu=Im(mu)/Im(tau)`.

Here `mu != 0` for every sufficiently small nonzero q_0, since its
known limiting value is nonzero. The rank-three semiabelian group has
periods `(tau,mu)`, `(1,0)`, `(0,1)`. A globally defined logarithmic
Hermitian fibre norm is

`ell(zeta_1,zeta_2)=Im(zeta_2)-a_mu Im(zeta_1)`.

It is invariant under all three periods. This correction is essential:
the raw absolute value of `exp(-2 pi i zeta_2)` is not invariant under
the elliptic period. The closing period is

`gamma=(6mu,beta_0-12t)`.

Put `t=a+iT`, with a fixed real a and T tending to positive infinity.
Direct expansion gives

`f_1=constant+6i mu T`,

`ell(f)=constant+[Re(beta_0)+6-12a-6a_mu Re(mu)]T`.

Consequently the fibre-norm valuation relative to `|r|=exp(-2 pi T)`
is

`v=-Re(beta_0)-6+12a+6a_mu Re(mu)`.

As the ray argument a varies through an interval of length one, v
varies through an interval of length twelve. Choose a for which v is
not an integer. Subtracting a constant multiple of gamma places v
between two consecutive component labels of the standard twelve-cycle
model; the bounded unit in the closing period does not affect v.
The two node coordinates then have norms asymptotic to positive
constants times `|r|^(v-k)` and `|r|^(k+1-v)`, for the appropriate
integer k. They both tend to zero. Thus the image approaches one fixed
conductor stratum, whose elliptic coordinate is

`[constant+6i mu T] in C/(Z+tau Z)`.

This coordinate has no limit. Indeed, if a real one-parameter subgroup
`[vT]` of a compact torus had a limit as T tends to infinity, then for
each real h its translation by `[vh]` would have the same limit. Hence
`[vh]=0` for every h, and discreteness of the lattice would give v=0.
Here v in that argument is `6i mu != 0`. The same argument applies to
the negative comparison and after every finite base change in r.

On a sufficiently small simply connected q-neighbourhood, the zero
section of the rotating model's root-cover presentation extends to the
smooth part of its r-central normalization. Under the specified
comparison its image is the section represented by `-f`. If that
comparison extended meromorphically at all points of this zero section
over r=0, restricting its proper graph to a generic such section would
give a meromorphic map from a disc to the proper target. A meromorphic
map from a nonsingular curve to a proper target extends holomorphically
(its proper modification of the normal curve is an isomorphism).
The nonconvergence above contradicts this. Equivalently, the indicated
section itself admits no meromorphic extension.

A finite sequence of blow-ups of the bidisc origin is an isomorphism
near every `(q_0,0)` with `q_0 != 0`. It therefore cannot remove this
divisorial nonextension of the specified comparison. A common
modification/graph-closure construction cannot simply be assumed.

This argument does **not** prove that the glued partial family cannot
extend. A proper family can contain holomorphic sections on punctured
discs with essential singularities. Nor does it rule out a different
comparison differing by an automorphism which itself fails to extend.
It only prevents treating the displayed exact affine trivialization
as a meromorphic toroidal change of coordinates.

## Parallel cap route and unresolved compatibility

AB is examining compact Calabi--Eckmann/LVMB pieces. Such pieces can
supply non-Kahler normalization components; the already proved
B.4.15 insertions alone preserve the existing component cycle and
cannot close it. A useful new cap would have to match all marked
Hopf/elliptic boundary divisors and their normal lines, not only one
abstract Hopf surface. It must also carry actual analytic smoothing
charts whose punctured quotient is the prescribed torus family.

For the elementary quotient with weights `(a_1,1,b,b)`, one Hopf
divisor has multiplier `exp(2 pi i b)` and normal multiplier
`exp(2 pi i a_1)`, but the opposite divisor has Hopf multiplier
`exp(2 pi i b/a_1)`. Thus matching one boundary does not automatically
match the opposite boundary. No finite network or smoothing has yet
been constructed here. The exact origin completion remains Open.

## Detailed development record: B_elliptic_contraction_sources.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# B.4.11: elliptic contraction and globalization audit

Owner: boundary_d. Date: 2026-11-15. Root independently passed the full proof before integration. AB was also asked to audit the explicit Kato geometry.

## Exact result and scope

The result is about the auxiliary parabolic surface `S_8(a,kappa)` constructed in B.4.10. Its negative elliptic axis has an actual linear tubular neighbourhood, not merely a specified normal bundle. Contracting it gives the cone over the elliptic curve with the positive degree-eight line. The proof computes `H^2(Y,Theta_Y)=0` and proves regularity of the global-to-local deformation map. A specified local analytic smoothing component can therefore be globalized over a small disc.

This does not construct the requested punctured S6 comparison, a compact third threefold component, or its gluing to the original S6 family. A proper family over an open disc is not an absolute compact threefold. The parent owns the explicit local Q-cone smoothing and the next construction/scope statement.

## Primary sources actually opened

1. Grauert, H. (1960). *Ein Theorem der analytischen Garbentheorie und die Modulräume komplexer Strukturen*. Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS, 5, 5–64. https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02684746 . Original Numdam PDF: https://www.numdam.org/article/PMIHES_1960__5__5_0.pdf . **Opened** §6, Hauptsatz IIa, printed p.59 (PDF page index57): the analytic theorem on formal functions. Existing bibliography key `Grauert60push` is reused. No appeal is made to the incorrect closedness assertion corrected in the 1963 erratum to §7 Satz2.

2. Dloussky, G., & Teleman, A. (2020). *Smooth deformations of singular contractions of class VII surfaces*. Mathematische Zeitschrift, 296, 1521–1537. https://doi.org/10.1007/s00209-020-02481-0 . **Opened** primary author manuscript https://arxiv.org/pdf/1803.07631 , version2 dated28February2020; §3.2, Proposition3.3 and the discussion immediately preceding Proposition3.4, manuscript p.11. **Opened** publisher metadata https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00209-020-02481-0 . Proposition3.3 is formulated for a compact complex space with a single isolated singularity and a regular global-to-local base morphism. The discussion invokes the general H²Theta criterion via Manetti's Lemma1, p.93. The rational-cycle hypotheses of their Proposition3.4 and Theorem5.2 are not transferred to the elliptic contraction.

3. Istrati, N., Otiman, A., Pontecorvo, M., & Ruggiero, M. (2022). *Toric Kato manifolds*. Journal de l'École polytechnique—Mathématiques, 9, 1347–1395. Existing key `IstratiEtAl2022Root`; original PDF https://www.numdam.org/item/JEP_2022__9__1347_0.pdf . Opened in the B.4.10 audit: §2 construction, Proposition5.6 normal line, Example9.10 parabolic exponent matrix. The B.4.11 proof derives the exact canonical divisor directly from the invariant logarithmic two-form.

## General local-to-global argument

The proof does not treat a citation as the whole argument. On a finite Stein cover with only one member meeting the isolated singularity, local lifts over a small Artin extension can be glued except for a Čech two-cocycle in Theta tensored with the square-zero kernel. H²Theta=0 removes it. This gives the relative infinitesimal lifting property. Manetti's original Lemma1, p.93, was subsequently **Opened and visually inspected** and directly supplies its general analytic semiuniversal-base formulation. A regular analytic germ morphism is locally a projection and therefore lifts the specified local analytic disc. The opened Dloussky–Teleman discussion independently uses the same criterion.

## Original Manetti retrieval, accurate status

Manetti, M. (1991). *Normal degenerations of the complex projective plane*. Journal für die reine und angewandte Mathematik, 419, 89–118. DOI10.1515/crll.1991.419.89. The exact general lemma is cited in the opened Dloussky–Teleman primary source as Lemma1, p.93.

Institutional metadata https://iris.uniroma1.it/handle/11573/76993 and the author's bibliography were opened. The publisher PDF was inaccessible. EuDML153345 identifies the GDZ scan `GDZPPN00220875X`; its historical HTTP resolver works in the executor and redirects to https://gdz.sub.uni-goettingen.de/id/PPN243919689_0419?tify=%7B%22view%22:%22info%22,%22pages%22:%5B93%5D%7D . The GDZ HTML was saved under `/workspace/scratch/20a906f0fadd/external_sources/D_elliptic_contraction/Manetti1991_GDZ_locator.html`.

At initial integration, the individual lemma page was still being retrieved. This was completed before the next snapshot: **Opened and visually inspected** printed p.93, including Lemma1 and its full proof. The IIIF manifest is https://manifests.sub.uni-goettingen.de/iiif/presentation/PPN243919689_0419/manifest?version=8b7f2484 . Printed p.93 is scan97, exact image https://images.p.sub.uni-goettingen.de/iiif/image/gdz:PPN243919689_0419:00000097/full/1800,/0/default.jpg . It is saved as `/workspace/scratch/20a906f0fadd/external_sources/D_elliptic_contraction/Manetti1991_p93.jpg`; the manifest and neighbouring p.92 are retained too.

The lemma is stated for a reduced compact complex space with finitely many isolated singularities. Its proof uses the cotangent-complex local-to-global spectral sequence: H²Theta=0 makes the map on first tangent cohomology surjective and on second tangent cohomology injective, which is the formal smoothness criterion. It explicitly concludes that every local deformation can be globalized. Thus it directly applies to our normal compact surface; no rationality or projectivity assumption is present. Bibliography key `Manetti1991D` now cites this inspected original scan.

## Searches and discarded shortcuts

Queries included the exact Manetti title with PDF; the GDZ identifier; simple-elliptic smoothings and degree-eight elliptic cones; and the Dloussky–Teleman title. Search-result aggregators were used only as locators. The source argument uses opened primary papers and an explicit proof.

Rejected shortcut: applying Dloussky–Teleman Theorem5.2 directly. Its contraction is of a negative rational cycle, whereas ours contracts an elliptic curve.

Rejected shortcut: asserting every simple-elliptic neighbourhood is a prescribed cone. Here the cone identification comes from the actual transverse-linear Kato gluing and the normally generated degree-eight section ring.

Rejected shortcut: inferring surjectivity from one-dimensional target alone. The proof uses the actual period deformation and the natural isomorphism H¹At(N)→H¹Theta_E, so the map is proved nonzero even at CM elliptic curves.

No computational experiment is used as a substitute for the cohomological proof. No novelty claim is made.

## Detailed development record: B_equivariant_compactification_attempt.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Compact third-component attempt: proved reductions and exact gaps

Owner: boundary_d. Date:2026-11-15. This note is an attempt record, not a compactification theorem.

## Proven part

B.4.11 contracts the elliptic axis in the explicit S8 and proves H²(Y,ThetaY)=0. B.4.12 globalizes the specified degree-eight Q-cone smoothing and obtains a proper smooth threefold over a disc with central surface S8 union_E Q. The total threefold is not compact.

The independent character calculation in B.4.14 proves H¹(Y,ThetaY)=C^8 with trivial C* action and H⁰=C trivial. For a compact associated Y-bundle Z_L→P1, it follows that R¹Theta_relative=O^8 and H²Theta_relative=0. The same vanishing holds after tensoring by the ideal of one whole fibre, since H¹(P1,O(−1))=0. AB's independent proof and exact verification are in `sections/ab_push_kato_weights_referee.md` and `code/ab_push_kato_weights.py/.out/.json`.

## First candidate: equivariant global smoothing

The genuine C* action x↦lambda x on S8 descends to radial homothety on Y. The local cone(Q) sweep has parameter of weight one. A convergent equivariant global-to-local deformation map would admit a compatible section over the weight-one parameter disc: after pullback its base is smooth, and compact-circle linearization of that smooth base gives an equivariant transverse section. A genuine weight-one family would be holomorphically trivial over the punctured disc, allowing gluing to a product over the outer P1 chart.

This candidate is **not established**. Rim's theorem is formal/algebraic, and convergence cannot be silently added. Doan's convergent Kuranishi theorem covers compact smooth manifolds, whereas Y is singular. Cathelineau's theorem deforms pairs (compact complex space, group) over a base on which the group acts trivially; that does not supply the required moving-base weight-one action.

Opened primary sources: Doan, *Equivariant Kuranishi family of complex compact manifolds*, arXiv2004.07014v2, introduction pp1–2 and Corollary4.1; Cathelineau1978 was independently opened by root/AB. The primary Doan preprint *Equivariant deformations of isolated singularities and applications*, arXiv2606.09199v1, was downloaded with urllib after the web parser returned DisabledError and read locally. Its pp5–6 explicitly retain the general equivariant analytic-object formalism as a problem: Bingener1987 Remark4.8(3) announced a possible extension whose promised details seem not to have appeared. This is a source limitation, not a proof that the desired action is impossible.

Doan2026 files are retained under `/workspace/scratch/20a906f0fadd/external_sources/D_elliptic_contraction/Doan2026.pdf` and `.txt`.

## Second candidate: relative globalization on a compact associated bundle

Take L=O(1) and glue copies of Y by radial homotheties with the transition functions of L. The singular points form a section Sigma=P1. The explicit cone(Q) smoothing has the same radial action, so it glues as a local family near Sigma after setting its local parameter equal to epsilon times a section sigma of L. A section sigma with one simple zero would yield, for fixed nonzero epsilon, a compact threefold with one cone(Q) vertex. Blowing up that vertex would insert Q.

To keep the compact strict transform exactly S8, the deformation must retain the whole fibre above the zero b0 of sigma, not only its isolated singular germ. This explains the proved vanishing for Theta_relative(−F_b0).

The missing step is a precise analytic local-to-global theorem for this nonisolated singular section with the prescribed transverse cone family and fixed whole fibre. H²Theta_relative=0 alone does not establish it. Local automorphisms on overlaps may fail to lift, and prescribing an entire non-Stein neighbourhood may add matching obstructions. The cotangent-complex spectral sequence suggests also controlling H¹(T¹); Pinkham's degree-eight weight data may make the relevant associated sheaf nonnegative. Even a formal lift must be made analytic while retaining the selected Q smoothing component. No isolated-singularity theorem or lci theorem is substituted for that missing argument.

Root independently opened Tziolas, arXiv0808.0436v2, §9 Theorem9.4, which identifies successive obstruction locations H⁰T², H¹T¹, H²Theta for nonisolated singularities. It is algebraic/formal and does not by itself supply the remaining analytic realization here.

## Conditional Euler budget for this particular arrangement

If the proposed compact C→P1 existed with exactly one S8 union_E Q fibre and all other compact fibres of Euler number12, then chi(C)=24. For A=Bl_E(P3) and B=S8×P1, chi(A)=4 and chi(B)=16. The three double surfaces have Euler numbers0,4,8, and the triple curve E has Euler number0. A proper reduced normal-crossings smoothing of exactly these compact components would therefore have

chi(smooth threefold)=4+16+24−2(0+4+8)+3·0=20.

This is a necessary conditional calculation, not an invariant assigned to an unconstructed space. It also shows that completion of this particular auxiliary three-piece arrangement would not by itself produce an S6 fibre of Euler number2. Additional modifications or an actual comparison with the prescribed punctured family would still be needed.

No novelty or completed compactification claim is made.
