## Detailed development record: D_E229_E2210_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent referee: E.2.9 and E.2.10

Referee: boundary_d. Date:2026-11-15. Full source read: `sections/E_horizontal_vanishing.tex`. Outcome: PASS at its stated one-sided-specialization scope.

## E.2.9

The elementary vanishing is valid on a compact reduced irreducible analytic space: the decreasing finite-dimensional section spaces stabilize; their intersection is zero by Krull intersection at a point of the nonempty effective Cartier divisor and analytic continuation on the irreducible normalization. It does not require ampleness, a Kähler metric, or normality.

Every fibre is mS with S a reduced irreducible Cartier divisor in the smooth threefold. The finite ideal filtration has the exact vector-bundle quotients V|S tensor O_S(−jS), so the same negative-divisor vanishing applies even to nonreduced fibres. Local self-branches do not defeat the non-zero-divisor condition, as E.2.8's normalization argument verifies it branch by branch.

Flatness over the smooth curve follows from torsion-freeness over its local DVR. A Cartier fibre is Cohen–Macaulay and Gorenstein. The dualizing bundle is K_X restricted to the fibre, up to the harmless constant target tangent factor; therefore compact analytic Serre duality yields exactly the stated H2/H0 pairing for every fibre, including its nilpotents.

The bad sets for H2 are closed by upper semicontinuity and nested by injectivity of multiplication on dual sections. Their empty intersection and compactness of P1 give a common N. This is an ordinary compactness argument, not an appeal to Grauert's corrected closedness statement. Constant-zero cohomology and analytic base change give R2=0; higher direct images vanish by fibre dimension. A sufficiently positive base twist kills H1 of the coherent R1 on P1, hence global H2 and H3.

The negative horizontal twist independently kills H0. The exponential sequence identifies Pic(X) with the additive vector space H1(O_X), since H1(X,Z)=H2(X,Z)=0. More strongly, the explicit affine Čech cocycles give an actual holomorphic line-bundle family on X×C joining the two good classes, so no universal Picard-space claim is needed. The finite free cohomology complex makes each bad locus a proper closed analytic subset of C. Their finite union cannot be all of C, giving the simultaneous vanishing.

All line bundles are topologically trivial, so the Euler characteristic stays chi(B). For B=T_X, analytic HRR gives chi(T_X)=c3/2=1 because c1 and c2 vanish in cohomology. Simultaneous H0=H2=H3=0 would give chi=−h1≤0. This proves the contradiction. No unsupported holomorphic-fibration assertion of CDP is used.

## E.2.10

For negative slope, a horizontal fixed component forces Kodaira dimension minus infinity and non-Kähler general fibre, hence b1=1; E.2.8 then proves every reduced fibre irreducible. E.2.9 forbids the horizontal component. The remainder of E.2.6 uses only verticality, so its connected Cartier-divisor limit removes the vertical fixed part and forces degree one. Evaluation and local flatness then extend the relative algebraic reduction; the smooth general central fibre is diffeomorphic to T4 and hence a complex torus by surface classification. E.2.7 applies for both signs and excludes the zero and outer period ends.

For a common smooth limit, intrinsicness of the reduction identifies the pencil line L. Its non-torsion follows from a nonzero section with zeros in a positive power. Comparing the two canonical-root identities yields equal D/A and then the already proved arithmetic recovers the ordered pair. This does not identify the central manifold with the reference interior-period model and does not prove invariance along arbitrary new deformation branches; the source correctly retains those separate gaps.

No numerical experiment is necessary for this proof. The conclusion is based on the exact analytic cohomology, duality, and divisor arguments above.

## Detailed development record: root_eighth_hour_audit.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Eighth-hour root audit

Date: 15 November 2026. This record continues the seventh-hour audit. The work is AI-produced; the independent reviews below are additional AI reviews, not human verification or a priority claim.

## The actual partial filling

The complete source of B.4.19, `ab_push_global_partial.tex`, was read by root and independently by boundary_d. Both reviews pass at its stated scope. The original closing formula C.3.5 gives, for h=-12, the exact translation Pi_c gamma-(k+6)delta. Removing integral periods gives the scalar representative -k delta. The ordinary gauge zeta+k v delta therefore has transition W_j=r^(-n_ij)W_i. In the unfolded node charts this changes the chart index by -n_ij and leaves both coordinates unchanged. In particular the extension is checked at the nodes, and a shift by twelve is not incorrectly treated as the identity after the elliptic closing translation.

At the finite roots the parameter logarithms cancel exactly, leaving w_j Pi_part v_j, independent of r. At the cusp the corrected actual comparison zeta-sigma+F differs from the ordinary gauge by G(s)+6v delta. Its branch identity has the positive fractional q-deck +b, hence the clockwise component shift -1 required by the global relation. The exact checks include 1,887 node-chart shifts and passed. The local comparisons give an exact cocycle on the full central overlaps.

The resulting map is proper over Omega=(P1 x Delta_r) minus {(infinity,0)}, with smooth total space, a reduced normal-crossings r-central divisor, and flatness over r. Its r-nonzero family is the specified compact S6 family. Properness over the full parameter disc is not inferred from properness over Omega. The origin cap, global semistable filling, descent, least base-change exponent, central topology and analytic Chern numbers remain open.

## Analytic identification of every smooth one-sided limit

E.2.20 identifies the actual cusp neighbourhood, including its affine marking, with the prescribed Mumford neighbourhood. The proof obtains the action and cover from the actual relative normal-crossings space. Adjacent choices of origins differ by a monomial in q times a holomorphic unit; the crossing-chart path proves this extension rather than assuming a meromorphic comparison. A finite fundamental set of charts controls the size of the neighbourhood before its deck translates are used. Root checked the original supplied AC26 PDF, Proposition4.7 and Corollary4.8 on printed page30, for the topology of the known cover, the regular inclusion and the zero section. The external page image and OCR remain outside the deliverable archive.

E.2.21 then glues the ordinary period comparison, finite free root quotients and actual cusp comparison. Finite affine cocycles are treated by averaging after their integral classes are matched; the remaining additive cocycle is killed by H1(V)=0 for the exact vector-bundle extension used in E.1. This supplies a holomorphic family comparison for a full punctured-disc specialization. Root's full-source review and boundary_d's independent full-source review both pass.

## Propagation from an open set and arbitrary analytic bases

Root and boundary_d separately read all of E.2.22 and E.2.23. Their proofs pass. The following points received particular scrutiny.

The intrinsic fractional canonical line exists on the entire smooth family over a disc. Its direct image gives a two-dimensional pencil. Every excluded locus is obtained by a proper analytic image on the parameter domain then present. The relative critical image is finite over that curve, so its three sections can be labelled after a finite cover. Each dominating divisor component is a Cartier prime in a smooth total space, is flat over the parameter curve, and has pure two-dimensional fibres. This makes the irreducibility and multiplicity argument at a known fibre precise.

The ordinary compact surface fibres have b1=4 and integral c1=0. The compact-surface classification makes them Kähler. A nonzero effective pluricanonical divisor on a Kähler surface cannot have zero real Chern class; hence a pluricanonical section, if present, is nowhere zero and positive Kodaira dimension is impossible. The Kodaira-dimension-zero list then identifies these surfaces as two-tori. The ruled case is excluded by the canonical degree on a ruling fibre.

The cusp test normalizes the reduced total hypersurface. Smoothness of that normalization, its submersion to the parameter curve, immersion into the total space, and independence of the conormal lines of two or three distinct local sheets are tested by proper analytic loci. For a finite unramified map the partial diagonals are open and closed, so the loci of ordered distinct branches are themselves closed and proper. Excluding four distinct branches completes the finite test. The fact that the cusp is a reduced embedded total hypersurface makes its local ideal the product of the local sheet equations. Thus the resulting charts are actual relative normal crossings; no unproved commutation of normalization with arbitrary base change is used.

The full period comparison is an identity between holomorphic Grassmannian-valued kernel maps. It begins at a single fixed ordinary target point, where the exceptional determinant locus is analytic on the parameter curve. Equality of kernels then proves the necessary independence of the two chosen columns at every ordinary target. This avoids projecting a zero locus from a noncompact ordinary target. The sign of the real period determinant is fixed on the known open set and persists by connectedness. Smoothness of the finite root torus at the maximizing point makes the R3 inequality strict, so the period remains in the admissible interior.

The successive bad sets need not have a union discrete on the original disc. The proof fills them in reverse order, using E.2.21 on an actual full punctured neighbourhood at each step. This handles accumulation at an earlier removed point.

For an arbitrary reduced analytic base, the proof does not select a punctured analytic disc inside an arbitrary open locus. A finite local projection with one point over the origin produces an irreducible analytic curve through a nearby known point and the boundary point. Its normalization is a connected Riemann surface. Pullback preserves the proper smooth family, Ehresmann gives the S6 topology on the whole curve, and the disc propagation theorem applies. The known-pair locus is therefore open and closed. Chains preserve the pair; the connected period family gives the converse for equal pairs.

E.2.24 resolves the separate interpretation involving continuous families in the C-infinity space of integrable complex structures. Root and boundary_d both opened Meersseman's original published paper, Section3.2 Theorem3.4 and Remark3.6, pp898--899, including the actual retraction (3.11). Its connected local Kuranishi domain retracts continuously to the connected analytic deformation base, and every structure in the domain is biholomorphic to its representative by a small diffeomorphism. E.2.23 therefore makes each fixed-pair saturated locus open and closed, including near an initially unconstructed structure. No global bounded-automorphism hypothesis or global analytic-stack theorem is used. No claim that a fixed pair has only one marked Teichmüller component is made.

## Primary sources and current literature status

The eighth-hour searches used the phrases recorded in `evidence/root_eighth_hour_sources.json`, including the six-sphere deformation multiplicities, the exact arXiv identifier2610.39102, and recent arXiv deformation work. Root reopened both the abstract and original HTML of Viaclovsky's *Logarithmic transformations on the six-sphere*, arXiv2610.39102v1, submitted29October2026. Its Theorem1.1 gives biholomorphic separation, while Section1.1 Questions1 and2 ask about deformation classes and local Kuranishi dimension. These are distinct statements. No located result in these searches supplies the propagation proved here. This is a dated search record, not a claim of priority or an exhaustive impossibility claim about the literature.

Root also opened Demailly's original author-hosted *Complex Analytic and Differential Geometry*, version21June2012. ChapterII Theorem4.19 on printed pages95--96 gives the finite local projection used above. Theorem7.6 and Corollary7.7 on page113 give smoothness of a normal curve; Definition7.9 and Theorem7.12 on pages114--115 give finite proper normalization. Theorem8.8 on page118 is the proper analytic image theorem. The exact statements were read, not inferred from titles. The proof here supplies the curve argument and the geometric tests rather than attributing a general non-Kähler separatedness theorem to those sources.

Schroer and Siebert's original author-hosted paper *Irreducible degenerations of primary Kodaira surfaces* was opened as a main-origin comparison source. Its partial completion also omits a corner of P1 x Delta, and Section6 studies more complicated product-normal-crossing boundaries. Its positive-degree elliptic principal-bundle setting is not the present simple-torus family. It is being used to investigate a construction, not as a theorem that fills this origin.

## Checkpoint record correction

The exact checkpoint07 manifest and save-verification JSON record creation at19:41:42.507843UTC and verification at19:42:13.480574UTC. A live-report boilerplate said approximately19:39UTC. That prose has been corrected to approximately19:42UTC in the live report generator, checkpoint record and reproducibility script. The immutable saved checkpoint07 ZIP is unchanged. The delay was caused by the necessary positive-q/clockwise-q orientation correction documented in the seventh-hour audit.

## Main remaining construction

The construction now starts with the degree-one Kato family over q and makes the base change q=s^12. B.4.21 repairs the other axis by six point blow-ups and seventy-two curve blow-ups. Its actual regularized function H=h/q and a convergent product give a relative linear Tate-line neighbourhood, while the persistent elliptic axis retains normal degree minus one. The whole second-period comparison, elimination of the surviving rational curves, and origin attachment remain to be constructed.


## Full-source review of the embedded Tate interface

B.4.20 was read in full by root, including its final fixed-base plumbing restriction. The proof uses holomorphic units kappa(s),a(s), as already allowed by B.4.10; no unproved smallness condition on kappa is introduced. The equation h=pi_n^*w-s transforms by exactly a(s), and every blow-up coefficient in its central divisor is one. Its smooth total divisor is the marked semistable Tate surface. In the stated total-coordinate convention W=exp(-2pi i zeta2), the normal factor a=exp(-2pi i mu) represents M^(-1)=O(O-P); the independent scaling a^(-6)=exp(12pi i mu) represents translation by6P. The final Picard-sign clarification was adopted before integration. These are distinct data and are correctly distinguished.

At a node the Kato neighbourhood has base map s=uv-h, while the total normal line has s=uv. Their relative critical loci differ. The proposed local plumbing r=f(uv-s) is smooth with NC r-centre, but changes the local s-fibration. In addition, the exceptional rational curves of the generic blown-up Hopf surface are disjoint from the other axis and survive any fixed-base plumbing confined to that axis. Such a plumbing cannot have ordinary two-tori as its nearby surface fibres. The full-source statement passes precisely at its embedded-interface and restricted-obstruction scope. It does not fill the origin.

## Full-source review of the degree-one repair

Root and limits_ab independently read the complete B.4.21 proof. The six point charts resolve the Cartier A11 surface inside a smooth ambient threefold; the final exceptional conic gives the middle component, and all twelve central multiplicities are one. The exact normal coefficient vector is (0,2,4,6,8,10,12,10,8,6,4,2). The seventy-two curve blow-ups preserve the strict divisor itself and make every relevant vertical ambient divisor have s-order one and h-order twelve. Thus H=h/q is an actual holomorphic normal coordinate, with the punctured normal comparison in the direction q^-1.

The product comparison is checked by the characters u/s^i and s^(i+1)/u, whose pole divisors are absent and whose restrictions give the minimal resolution charts. The transverse coordinate H completes their differential to an isomorphism. The uniform nonzero product G(q,H)=product(j>=1)(1+a(q)^j H) linearizes the exact Kato closing map, so the assertion is an actual neighbourhood isomorphism and not only a normal-line calculation. The smooth proper modification is flat over its disc; it is not asserted to remain a submersion at the central parameter. Every centre is disjoint from the persistent elliptic axis, which retains degree minus one. The global second period, whole origin cap and generic S6 identification remain open.

## General completion obstruction: precise literature scope

Root visually read the original published Nakayama2002 Theorem7.1.3 on page604 and Step3 of its proof on page605. For a locally bimeromorphically projective elliptic fibration on the complement of a codimension-at-least-two analytic subset, an analytic elliptic extension exists precisely under the stated global bimeromorphic-projectivity condition. Step3 explicitly applies flattening to a putative extension; the theorem is not limited to extensions that were flat initially. On a punctured bidisc, the principal elliptic torsor with additive gluing class 1/(qr) has constant periods and a non-torsion Laurent cohomology class. It therefore supplies a counterexample to an unrestricted completion argument based solely on trivial monodromy. This does not prove nonextension of the actual family: its generic two-tori have no elliptic quotient on an open set, and its affine period class has not been identified with that counterexample. The exact attempt, calculations and source comparison are retained in E_B_general_completion_attempt.md.


## Inspected checkpoint build and hourly decision

The eighth-hour checkpoint build is draft30: 201 pages, 142 statement rows and 105 bibliography entries. All four compiler stages succeeded with zero overfull boxes; the final integration audit passes without unresolved references, citations or rerun warnings. 32 distinct rendered pages were visually inspected, including all listed new proof pages. The exact record is qa/draft30/visual_inspection.json. # Independent referee: B.4.19 global partial filling

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026. Full source reviewed:
`sections/ab_push_global_partial.tex`, together with
`code/ab_push_global_partial.py/.out/.json` and the actual closing
formula in `sections/C_closing_cocycle.tex`.

**PASS at the stated scope.** The proof constructs the actual global
family over `(P1 x Delta_r) minus {(infinity,0)}`, including all finite
and cusp overlaps through r=0. It does not construct the missing origin
cap or a proper family over the whole parameter disc.

## Closing and ordinary charts

For h=-12 the exact C.3.5 translation is
`Pi_c gamma-(k+6)delta`. Removing the integral gamma period and six
delta periods leaves `-k delta`. Hence the coordinate
`zeta_tilde=zeta+k v delta`, with `v=log r/(2 pi i)`, has trivial
parameter-deck translation modulo the actual period lattice.

On ordinary overlaps `k_j-k_i=n_ij` is an integer, giving exactly
`W_j=r^(-n_ij) W_i` for W=exp(-2 pi i zeta_tilde_2). In the unfolded
node chart this changes the component label j to j-n and preserves
both x_j and y_j. This proves extension at nodes, not only on ruled
open parts. It commutes with the actual closing translation, so descends
to the twelve-cycle. A shift of twelve can include an elliptic
translation after closing and is not asserted to be the identity.
The integer cocycle is exact, rather than merely modulo twelve.

Changing a coherent closing by a scalar gives a principal C* torsor
over the punctured parameter disc. Its triviality by Pic(Delta*)=0
legitimately gives a holomorphic isomorphism of the punctured families.
The proof correctly makes no extension claim for this scalar gauge.

## Finite charts

The finite unit `K s_j^(-12 epsilon_j)` is invariant because the relevant
root orders are three and four. Its holomorphic logarithm is exactly
invariant: an integral difference is constant and vanishes at the root
centre. Since the finite linear factors fix delta, the new local gauge
does not change the finite affine deck action.

The collar difference is

`w_j Pi_c v_j + 12 epsilon_j w_j v delta = w_j Pi_part v_j`.

The sign c=-12v is essential and is used correctly. This remaining
translation is independent of r and acts on the actual semiabelian
group; it therefore extends to both conductor sections and the node
plumbing. Under a change of the root logarithm, the difference between
Pi_part v_j and the actual period Pi_c v_j is an integral fan shift.
The earlier node calculation applies to this shift as well. Thus the
sector comparisons descend through the finite deck actions, and their
cocycle identities are the actual dense-locus identities continued
holomorphically across r=0.

## Cusp and orientation

Using k=-s+12 kappa_0, the retained actual-to-positive-rotating comparison
satisfies the exact identity

`F-sigma = k v delta + G(s) + 6 v delta`.

Its comparison to the ordinary gauge is therefore an r-independent
semiabelian translation G(s) followed by the integral shift r^-6.
The q-branch identity `Delta_s G=b+v delta` cancels the ordinary
coordinate's `-v delta`, leaving precisely the positive fractional
deck +b. The positive r-loop changes the residual translation by the
integral period six delta. These identities verify descent through both
loops.

The orientation now agrees with B.3: the positive q-root generator
has shift +1; its inverse, the clockwise cusp deck generator in B.3's
deck-identification convention, has shift -1. The local component
function `exp(2 pi i(k_i+i_i)/12)` is invariant on overlaps and gives
exactly the Kummer cover y^12=K. This is the corrected sign, not the
earlier negative-q-generator selection superseded in the B.4.18 audit.

## Gluing and limits of the conclusion

All comparisons are isomorphisms on entire preimages of base overlaps,
including their r-central parts. Their exact cocycle therefore gives
a Hausdorff analytic gluing. Properness is local on the target, and
each input model is proper on its stated base open set. Smoothness,
flatness over r and the reduced NC divisor are likewise local. For
r nonzero, the whole target P1 is present and the punctured family is
the prescribed compact family. For r zero the cusp base point is
missing, so properness over the r-disc does not follow. This limitation
is accurately retained in the statement.

## Detailed development record: E_E220_referee.md

# Independent referee: E.2.20 working reconstruction

Reviewer: classification_e. Date: 15 November 2026. Reviewed sources: `E_cusp_identification_working.md`, full working proof, followed by the complete final `E_cusp_identification.tex`. The final source includes the checked covering proof and retains the precise scope. Verdict: PASS for the stated actual cusp-neighbourhood identification, provided the elementary known-cover argument below is retained in the final source. This is not a conclusion about arbitrary deformation chains.

## Topology and the exact cover

The relative normal-crossing coordinates provide real lifts of the parameter directions which annihilate dq, including along every cusp stratum. A partition of unity preserves these linear requirements. Properness over a closed smaller q-disc gives complete transport along a sufficiently short parameter path, tangent to its boundary. This transports the entire cusp body, its strata and the regular-fibre inclusion map.

For the prescribed toric cover, each maximal chart restricted to the cusp disc is `{|z0 z1 z2|<epsilon}` and is star-shaped. Every chart intersection contains the same connected dense regular torus `(C*)^2 x Delta*`; its components are open and each meets that dense torus, so the intersection is connected. The intersection with a finite union of previous charts is connected for the same reason. Van Kampen proves simple connectedness of finite unions, and compactness of a loop reduces the infinite cover to this case. The known free proper period action therefore makes this the universal cover. Its regular torus inclusion kills exactly Zw+Zdelta, and the q-meridian bounds the explicit section `(z0,z1,z2)=(q,1,1)`.

This exact calculation is already recorded, with all units, in the retained course source `integral-monodromy-and-fundamental-group.md`, section 6, Lemma 6.1 and Theorem 6.2. It should be used as a checked derivation, not attributed to an independent authoritative theorem merely because it appears in that exposition. The underlying primary construction is Engel's original source, Proposition labelled `mum-cor`, with the two vanishing cycles identified in section 6 before Proposition labelled `fundamental-group`. C.3.8 independently supplies the required cusp deformation retraction.

The torus action lifts because its two fundamental loops die in the cusp body. The lifted regular action is free and transitive, since its stabilizer lattice is exactly the primitive kernel of the regular-fibre fundamental-group map. A lifted section trivializes the regular cover. Connectedness of H makes all deck transformations commute with it. Thus deck transformations are exactly the marked period multipliers; changing the affine origin does not change them.

## No essential-singularity assumption

The adjacent-component argument is sufficient and should remain explicit. At a general conductor point the stabilizer is the primitive connected toric one-parameter subgroup. Its two normal weights are +1 and -1, since these are the intrinsic primitive boundary weights on the two normalized toric surfaces. Compact-circle averaging gives these exact local linear coordinates. An invariant unit in q can be absorbed while preserving the weights, yielding q=ab.

Choose the two special origins `(q/b0,b0,z0)` and `(a0,q/a0,z0)`. The stabilizer element with parameter `a0 b0/q` sends one to the other. A path in that subgroup can keep both a and b in the fixed crossing chart: enlarge one modulus while decreasing the other, and adjust phase without changing either modulus. Thus the group element is computed by the local action itself, even though its parameter grows when q tends to zero. The origins' comparison is an integral cocharacter of q times a unit. Comparing either special origin with any chosen origin in its central component adds a holomorphic H-valued unit by the inverse-function theorem at the central free orbit. All of this holds on the full (q,s) bidisc.

Every component is reached by finitely many adjacent crossings, so its comparison with the initial origin is meromorphic with integral q-orders and nonvanishing holomorphic remaining factors. Different paths agree on the regular cover, hence give identical meromorphic characters. This proves, rather than assumes, the absence of an essential singularity.

At a triple point compact-torus averaging supplies eigen-coordinates with constant primitive characters. The invariant functions are functions of xyz and s; reducedness allows the invariant unit in q to be absorbed into a coordinate. A global character has a Laurent-monomial expression times a function of q,s. Evaluating on a section with two fixed nonzero coordinates, and using the preceding origin comparison, shows that this last function is a q-power times a holomorphic unit. The ray valuations and unimodularity then make every dual toric coordinate pull back to the corresponding actual coordinate times a unit. These are local biholomorphisms through every stratum.

## Uniformity and exact scope

There is no need to take an infimum of infinitely many chart radii. One compact central normalized dP6, together with finitely many overlaps for deck generators, has a finite chart cover. Choose one radius for that finite data. The global regular character map already exists on all H x Delta*. Its equivariance there forces the chart extensions to be equivariant through the centre. Deck translates of the finite collection cover the full central unfolding and use the identical reference period action. The maps are locally invertible and bijective on all labelled central orbits as well as on the regular locus, so they identify the actual and reference analytic cusp neighbourhoods.

No arbitrary quotient-smoothness assertion, infinite-radius inference, or inference from an abstract central surface alone is used. A global central threefold comparison still needs the finite-root and ordinary charts and their affine overlaps. Separation under arbitrary connected deformation chains additionally needs a justified propagation argument; closure under full punctured-disc specializations alone should not be called topological closedness without that argument.

# Independent referee: E.2.21

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026. Full source reviewed:
`sections/E_global_completion.tex`, with the prior E.1 comparison
and E.2.13/E.2.20 inputs.

**PASS at the stated one-sided-specialization scope.** The ordinary
Jacobian comparison is an identity of holomorphic period kernels, so
does not require an affine origin to extend. The finite affine action
has a fixed linear part and a locally constant integral cyclic norm
class; after matching it, finite averaging solves the vector-sheaf
cocycle. This is the cyclic H2 lattice class, not an unsupported claim
that every torus translation has a globally single-valued Lie lift.

On fixed annular collars, simply connected refinements admit the needed
Lie lifts. Their integral defects vary continuously in the parameter
and are therefore constant in the marked integral local system. The
finite and cusp identifications determine the allowed changes of lifts.
The actual cusp comparison supplied by E.2.20 is needed here; identifying
the abstract central surface would not suffice.

After these discrete classes agree, the remainder is exactly the
extension sheaf from E.1.2. Its quotient is O(-infinity), including for
the enlarged finite multiplicities, and both end terms have vanishing
H1. E.1.3 already proves the parameter-dependent version on a Stein
disc. The final Laurent/Cauchy and finite-averaging argument is therefore
valid holomorphically in the parameter. The residual automorphisms are
the connected scalar translation group.

The last scope remark is necessary and correct: openness plus closure
under full punctured-disc specializations does not alone establish
closedness in every analytic family. A separate generic-disc propagation
argument is still needed for arbitrary deformation chains.

# Independent referee: E.2.22 propagation on a disc

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026.
Full source read: `sections/E_disc_propagation.tex`, with E.2.1,
E.2.18--E.2.21 and the finite-chart and period-kernel arguments checked
against their stated hypotheses.

**PASS.** One expository precision was sent to the author: invoke
E.2.1 local miniversality at the beginning to obtain actual relative
known-family charts locally over the given open set U. This supplies
the locally constant marking used in the identity theorem and explains
why every geometric bad-set test is absent over U. It follows from an
already proved input; no additional closedness assumption is needed.

## Pencil and finite discriminant

The total space over the contractible disc has the homotopy type of
S6, so the exponential sequence gives the unique relative fractional
canonical line. The coherent direct image is torsion-free of rank two
and hence a bundle on the disc. The multiplication exact sequence gives
the claimed injection into each central section space. The common zero
locus of an evaluated frame is proper over the disc, so its proper
analytic image is a discrete exceptional set. Outside it the map has
pure two-dimensional fibres and is flat by the smooth-source/smooth-
target dimension criterion. The Stein factor is finite birational to
the normal target, giving connected fibres.

The relative critical image is finite over the remaining parameter
curve: every individual nonconstant proper map to P1 has finitely many
critical values, and the image is proper. Its isolated components,
branch values and singular values therefore give further discrete
analytic deletion layers. A finite etale labelling cover is legitimate;
each connected component meets the inverse image of U and proper images
of subsequent discrete sets remain discrete.

The surface-classification argument is sufficient. Ordinary fibres have
the topology of a two-torus, b1=4, and integral canonical class zero.
Even b1 makes them Kahler. Exceptional and ruled curves are incompatible
with that canonical class; a nonzero pluricanonical section has no zeros
by positivity against a Kahler form, so positive Kodaira dimension is
impossible. The remaining Kodaira-dimension-zero classification selects
the two-torus. No higher-dimensional deformation-invariance theorem for
complex tori is silently used.

## Finite root normalization

Every component of a critical Cartier divisor dominates the parameter
curve, since a vertical divisor would be an entire X_s and make its
pencil constant. Properness and the known irreducible fibre give one
total component, with the known multiplicity. Locally q=a^m u, so the
normalization of r^m=q is precisely the disjoint graphs
r=zeta a u^(1/m). These graphs are smooth copies of the actual total
space even if the reduced divisor has not yet been shown smooth. The
cyclic deck group permutes them freely. Only the root-map critical locus
remains to be removed; it is proper over the parameter curve and absent
over U. This provides an exact analytic test, not an assumed extension
of the known root model.

## Exact generic normal-crossing test

The total cusp is a reduced embedded hypersurface. Its finite
normalization is proper. Successive proper images of its singular
locus, parameter critical locus and relative derivative rank-drop locus
are discrete, since absent over U. After these removals it is a smooth
relative immersion.

For a finite map which is locally an embedding, the diagonal in its
fibre square is open as well as closed. Hence the loci of ordered,
pairwise distinct preimages in the two-, three- and four-fold fibre
products are closed analytic and proper over the parameter curve.
The relative normal-line rank tests on the first two and exclusion of
the last are therefore proper analytic tests. They ensure at most three
smooth local sheets with independent vertical conormals.

The essential final point is correct: the total divisor is an embedded
reduced hypersurface in a smooth ambient space, so its local principal
ideal is the product of the distinct local sheet equations. Independent
vertical conormals make these equations and the original parameter part
of a coordinate system. The result is exactly q=x, xy or xyz, up to a
unit. Thus reduced relative normal crossings follow without assuming
normalization/base-change compatibility or an unproved seminormality
criterion.

## Period continuation and completion

The normalized toric-pair argument, conductor equalizer, torus action
and integer characters use holomorphic identities which hold on a
nonempty open set; none intrinsically requires a whole punctured disc.
The constant marked peripheral representation preserves the invariant
lattice generated by w and delta. Its two-column determinant is tested
only at a single ordinary target point. Its zero set is an analytic
discrete subset of the parameter curve.

After deleting it, normalization at that one point extracts c(s).
Equality of the actual and reference holomorphic period-kernel maps
into a Grassmannian then propagates on the universal cover of the full
ordinary base times the parameter cover. This is stronger and safer
than assuming global independence of the two columns in advance. The
kernel equality itself proves their independence everywhere. The actual
compact tori enforce the admissible parameter domain. The already
proved toric reconstruction and additive comparison apply locally
throughout the current connected parameter domain; their discrete
affine and collar classes are transported from U.

There are only finitely many deletion layers. A layer is closed discrete
on the domain present when it is deleted, but later layers need not be
globally discrete on the original disc. Filling the last layer first
by E.2.21, then continuing in reverse order, handles exactly this issue.
At each stage a small punctured disc avoiding all earlier deleted layers
consists of known members. This proves the stated disc propagation and
does not appeal to an unjustified curve-selection principle for an
arbitrary open subset.

## Independent source check

Opened the original author-hosted Demailly manuscript,
`https://www-fourier.univ-grenoble-alpes.fr/~demailly/manuscripts/agbook.pdf`,
version 21 June 2012, on 15 November 2026. Chapter II Definition 7.9
explicitly makes normalization proper with finite fibres; Theorem 7.12
constructs it (printed pp. 114--115). Theorem 8.8 is Remmert's proper
mapping theorem (printed p. 118). Retrieval records: turn515view0,
turn516view0--turn516view2, turn517view0--turn517view1. These references
support the analytic tools used above; the finite normal-crossing test
itself is proved in the submitted source.

# Independent referee: E.2.23 arbitrary deformation bases

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026.
Full source read: `sections/E_arbitrary_deformation_bases.tex`.
Inputs E.2.22 and its source have been independently checked in
`D_E2222_referee.md`; E.1.2--E.1.3 were reread for the converse.

**PASS.** This is a valid consequence of the proved disc-propagation
statement, including for singular, reducible parameter spaces and
finite deformation chains.

On a connected Riemann surface, a nonempty known-pair locus is open
by local miniversality. A coordinate disc around any closure point
meets that locus in a nonempty open subset. E.2.22 applies directly,
so the locus is closed and hence is the whole Riemann surface.
The pulled-back proper smooth family has sphere topology throughout
by Ehresmann transport from a known fibre.

For a point b in the closure in an arbitrary reduced base, one of the
finitely many irreducible local branches T meets the known locus
arbitrarily near b. The finite local projection may be chosen with
rho^(-1)(0)={b}. For a known point a near b, the inverse image of the
complex line through rho(a) and zero has dimension exactly one:
the d-1 equations give the lower bound and finiteness gives the upper
bound. Any irreducible component C through a is closed and proper
over the connected line disc. Its one-dimensional analytic image is
therefore the whole line disc. It contains the unique point b over
zero. This is the decisive argument: it does not assume that an
arbitrary open known locus contains a punctured analytic curve
approaching b.

The normalization of C is a connected Riemann surface and has points
over both a and b. Smoothness and properness of the original family
survive its base change. Its known locus contains a nonempty open
neighbourhood over a, so the preceding Riemann-surface argument gives
the fibre over b. Thus the original known locus is closed. Connectedness
of the parameter base proves the first assertion. Passing to a
nonreduced base's reduction changes no fibre isomorphism class and
preserves smoothness, as claimed.

Every link of a finite deformation chain now preserves the ordered
pair. Conversely, the explicit E.1.2 congruences and E.1.3 family
comparisons put all sphere data with a fixed pair in the same connected
admissible period family, whose period subgroup is 2kl Z. This supplies
the converse without treating all discrete logarithmic data as equal
before applying the classification.

Source audit: the author's Demailly manuscript was independently
opened at
`https://www-fourier.univ-grenoble-alpes.fr/~demailly/manuscripts/agbook.pdf`.
Chapter II Theorem 4.19, printed pp. 95--96, supplies finite local
parametrization. Definition 7.9 and Theorem 7.12, pp. 114--115, supply
finite normalization; Corollary 7.7, p. 113, states the normal-curve
regularity used here. Retrieval records include turn515view0,
turn516view2 and turn517view0. The source's cited Theorem 7.6 also
implies that corollary.

# Independent referee: E.2.24 continuous complex structures

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026.
Full source read: `sections/E_continuous_deformations.tex`.

**PASS**, conditional only on the now independently accepted E.2.23.

The original published Meersseman article was opened independently:
`https://www.numdam.org/item/10.5802/jep.108.pdf`, retrievals
turn530view0--turn530view1. Theorem 3.4, printed p. 898, explicitly gives
a connected neighbourhood in the space of integrable structures, a
finite-dimensional Kuranishi subspace and a retraction, with inverse
representation by a diffeomorphism in Diff0. Remark 3.6, pp. 898--899,
explains the Sobolev construction and its restriction to smooth
operators. These are precisely the hypotheses needed here.

The continuous retract K is connected. The proper smooth Kuranishi
family may be restricted to its reduction without changing its fibres.
If the neighbourhood contains any known-pair structure, its Kuranishi
representative is biholomorphic to it. E.2.23 therefore identifies every
fibre over K, and the local Diff0 representation identifies every
structure in the neighbourhood with that pair. This argument applies
at initially unknown structures and proves the claimed clopen locus,
not merely openness near known members.

A continuous inverse image of a clopen set in a connected parameter
space is empty or the whole space. Saturation under Diff0 or Diff+
makes the image clopen for the quotient topology. Neither step assumes
that the fixed-pair locus itself is connected, and the stated limitation
on the number of marked components is appropriate.

# Primary analytic sources for E.2.22--23

Date opened: 15 November 2026. These are standard analytic inputs, not claimed as new results. The generic-disc propagation and its geometric comparison are supplied in the proof rather than attributed to an unrelated general separatedness theorem.

## Demailly

Demailly, J.-P. (2012). *Complex analytic and differential geometry* (version of 21 June 2012). Author-hosted manuscript: https://www-fourier.univ-grenoble-alpes.fr/~demailly/manuscripts/agbook.pdf.

The original 455-page author-hosted PDF was opened successfully. Its title page gives the 21 June 2012 version. Chapter II, Theorem 4.19, printed pp. 95--96, gives the finite local projection and branched covering description. Chapter II, Theorem 7.6, printed p. 113, says a normal point has singular locus of codimension at least two; this makes normal curves smooth. Definition 7.9 and Theorem 7.12, pp. 114--115, define and construct finite proper analytic normalization. Theorem 8.8, p. 118, is the proper analytic image theorem used for the exceptional loci. Each exact statement and its surrounding proof was opened.

The older source-files URL initially timed out; the successful manuscripts URL above is the actual opened source. An additional 25-page author-hosted manuscript, *Basic results on sheaves and analytic sets*, was opened at https://www-fourier.univ-grenoble-alpes.fr/~demailly/manuscripts/sheaves.pdf; its Theorem 4.8, p. 20, gives the same local parametrization statement. The main bibliography uses the fuller 2012 manuscript to avoid redundant references.

## Independent check of the curve construction

Lebl, J. (2026). *Hermitian forms meet several complex variables: Minicourse on CR geometry using Hermitian forms* (version of 19 May 2026). Author-hosted PDF: https://www.jirka.org/scv-mini/scv-mini.pdf.

The original author-hosted 62-page PDF and the author's book page were opened. Theorem 1.4.10, printed p. 17, states local parametrization; Exercise 1.4.3, printed p. 19, explicitly asks for the consequence that any sufficiently nearby point of an irreducible analytic germ lies on an irreducible curve through the distinguished point. E.2.23 proves this consequence using a finite projection and a line. The exercise is a comparison source, not an omitted proof. The general wording near Puiseux normalization is not imported beyond an irreducible curve germ; the proof instead uses finite normalization and normal-curve smoothness from Demailly.

## Exact scope of the bad-set construction

Every removal in E.2.22 is the proper image of a closed analytic locus on the parameter domain currently under consideration. Later exceptional sets may accumulate at parameters deleted earlier. Their union is not silently asserted to be discrete on the original disc. The proof fills the finite sequence of layers in reverse order, using the already established actual one-sided completion theorem.

The relative normal-crossing test works on a reduced embedded TOTAL hypersurface. A finite immersive normalization yields smooth local sheets; independent vertical normal covectors give relative normal-crossing coordinates. No assertion that normalization commutes with arbitrary base change, and no unsupported seminormality or non-Kähler good-reduction theorem, is used.

# Other-axis Tate interface: independent check and exact scope

Reviewer: boundary_d. 15 November 2026. This note checks the formulas
proposed by limits_ab for the other axis in B.4.10; it does not claim
an attachment to the actual two-period cusp or an origin cap.

## Cartier divisor and normal line

On the Kato fundamental domain define the divisor by
`pi_n^* w - s = 0`. The actual collar map is `sigma_s pi_n`, and
its second coordinate gives the exact identity

`w_new - s = a(s) (w_old - s)`.

It therefore glues to a relative Cartier divisor D. For s nonzero
the equation w=s avoids all blow-up centres, so it is the untouched
other Hopf axis. In its tangent coordinate the closing multiplier is
`lambda(s)=kappa(s) s^n`; in its normal coordinate it is `a(s)`.
Thus the generic pair is the marked elliptic curve E_lambda with the
degree-zero normal line of factor a.

At s=0 the total transform of w is reduced. Each successive blow-up
centre lies on the newest exceptional component and the strict x-axis,
but only the former belongs to div(w); the new exceptional coefficient
is therefore one at every step. The strict horizontal axis and the
n exceptional curves form the usual chain before collar gluing.
Identifying its end annuli gives the rational n-cycle. At each node
the ambient blow-up coordinates have `pi_n^*w=uv`, so D has equation
`uv=s`. Hence the total D is smooth and D to the s-disc is a semistable
I_n elliptic family. The normal line has the unit closing factor a(s)
and degree zero on every central component.

For the first period in the actual cusp, `q c1` is the Tate multiplier
and `c2` is the other-coordinate multiplier. After q=z^12, choosing
n=12, kappa=c1 and the contracting choice of c2 (or its inverse when
the transverse coordinate is inverted) matches this Tate curve and
its marked normal factor. This is an interface/normal-line match.

## A relative linear tubular neighbourhood is impossible

At a node the ambient Kato family has smooth coordinates (u,v,s)
and is a submersion to s. The divisor is `uv-s=0`. If e denotes its
defining normal coordinate, then

`e=uv-s`, equivalently `s=uv-e`.

In contrast, the total normal bundle of D, projected to the same base
through D, has local coordinates (u,v,e) and base function `s=uv`.
This map has a critical normal line u=v=0. The Kato family has no
such critical points, since the derivative of `uv-e` in e is -1.
Consequently there is no biholomorphic identification over s of these
two neighbourhoods, even though their normal lines agree exactly.

This also appears in the contracted germ

`(x,v) -> (kappa(s) x (a(s)v+s)^n, a(s)v)`.

The generic diagonalizing product uses v/s and is only a punctured-
parameter construction. Its existence does not linearize the total
neighbourhood across the I_n nodes.

## Explicit local non-toroidal plumbing

If an inverse normal coordinate f is introduced and the two normal
coordinates are plumbed by `ef=r`, elimination of e gives

`r=f(uv-s)`.

The total space is smooth in coordinates (u,v,f,s). The r-central
divisor is the union of the two smooth transverse hypersurfaces
`f=0` and `uv-s=0`, so it is SNC. For r nonzero the map to s is
submersive in this patch: rank failure of (s,r) would require
`fv=fu=uv-s=0`, which forces r=0. Thus this plumbing replaces the
local Tate node; it does not preserve a toric chart s=uv.

The equations provide a concrete local coupling which any attempted
attachment must respect. They do not establish the global second-
period action, the prescribed actual cusp strata, properness of an
origin cap, or the required global S6 filling. In particular they do
not evade the already proved obstruction to lifting a nontorsion
second elliptic translation to one fixed finite Hopf modification.

## Exceptional curves obstruct a naive whole-family attachment

For each fixed s nonzero the Kato fibre is obtained by n blow-ups of
the diagonal Hopf surface. The blow-up centres lie on its first axis
at the orbit v=-s, followed by infinitely near centres on that same
strict axis. The other axis D_s is v=0 with the first coordinate
nonzero. The two axes in a diagonal Hopf surface are disjoint, so every
curve of the exceptional configuration is disjoint from D_s.

A standard normal plumbing which leaves the complement of a small
neighbourhood of D_s unchanged therefore retains these compact rational
curves on each nearby r-nonzero surface over this fixed s. A complex
two-torus contains no rational curve: a holomorphic map P1 to it lifts
to its C2 universal cover and is constant. Consequently such a plumbing
of the entire Kato family cannot be biholomorphic, over the same
ordinary base, to the prescribed family of two-tori. This does not
exclude a construction which further deforms the exceptional region or
changes its relation to the original base. It identifies another exact
condition a global attachment must address.


## Restricted degree-twelve cone obstruction and its degree-one bypass

The linear neighbourhood of the persistent elliptic axis in the n=12
Kato model contracts to the cone of a degree-twelve line bundle on an
elliptic curve. Pinkham's original Theorem 7.5 (printed p.45), together
with the explicit elliptic-cone discussion in Section 9.2(a) (printed
pp.63–65), excludes a smoothing of this degree-twelve cone. Thus the
degree-eight contraction-and-quadric smoothing of B.4.11–12 cannot be
repeated merely by replacing S_8 by S_12. This is a restriction on that
specific contracted singularity, not an obstruction to every analytic
origin cap.

Source: Pinkham, H. C. (1974). *Deformations of algebraic varieties with
G_m action*. Astérisque, 20. Société mathématique de France. Original
full primary PDF opened at
https://www.numdam.org/item/AST_1974__20__1_0.pdf . The exact opened
sections were independently checked in this session; bibliography key
Pinkham1974Root already exists, so no duplicate entry was introduced.
Retrieval records: turn537view0 (metadata), turn537view1 (full PDF),
turn538view0 (§9), turn538view1 (§7), turn543view0 (Theorem 7.5).
Screenshot calls returned textual ImageDisplayed placeholders, so the
audit does not claim a separate visual reading of those screenshots.

The degree-one construction of B.4.21 bypasses precisely that restricted
obstruction. Start with n=1, make the base change q=s^12, and modify the
other-axis rational cycle. All its centres are disjoint from the
persistent elliptic axis. Consequently that axis retains its degree
minus one, while the other axis becomes the smooth I_12 Tate surface.
B.4.21 proves more than an equality of normal classes: its coordinate
H=h/q is holomorphic on an actual collar and its convergent product G
conjugates the closing to a linear Tate-line closing. This does not
construct or globalize a smoothing of the contracted degree-one axis,
and does not identify the whole modified Kato family with the prescribed
ordinary tori. The exceptional-curve obstruction above remains applicable
to a plumbing which leaves that entire complement unchanged.

## Detailed development record: E_B_general_completion_attempt.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# The actual origin: general analytic completion audit

Owner: classification_e. Date: 15 November 2026. This is an uninput main-question attempt, not a numbered completion or nonexistence theorem for the six-sphere family.

## 1. Exact problem and permitted completions

B.4.19 constructs the actual family after `w=r^6` over `(P1 × Δ_r) \ {(∞,0)}`. Its finite-place and cusp overlap maps are exact, including the scalar `K(t)=t^4/(t−1)^3` and component shifts `4,−3,−1`. Locally the missing base point is the origin of `B=Δ_q × Δ_r`. A desired completion may have compact three-dimensional components over this origin and need only be flat over `r`. Joint flatness over `B`, Kählerness, projectivity, or membership of all normalizations in Fujiki class C cannot be added. B.4.15 already supplies non-Kähler vertical components in the actual simple-torus family.

The present route asks whether a general analytic extension theorem supplies that missing cap, or whether its hypotheses produce a verifiable obstruction. It is independent of the explicit Kato-interface construction pursued by AB and D.

## 2. A general Hartogs completion principle is false, even with trivial monodromy

Here is an explicit instance of the obstruction treated by Nakayama. Set `U=B\{0}` and fix an elliptic curve `E=C/Λ`. Cover U by

`U_q=Δ*×Δ`, `U_r=Δ×Δ*`.

Glue the trivial E-bundles on these sets by translation by the E-valued holomorphic function `[1/(qr)]` on their intersection. This is a smooth proper holomorphic principal E-bundle `Y→U`; compactness of E and local triviality establish properness, with no global compactification assumed. It is locally projective and its integral local system is constant.

Its torsor class is nonzero and nontorsion. The base U retracts onto S3, so `H1(U,Λ)=H2(U,Λ)=0`. The sequence `0→Λ→O_U→E_U→0` consequently gives `H1(U,E_U)=H1(U,O_U)`. The displayed two-open cover is acyclic for O. A holomorphic function on U_q has no negative r powers, and one on U_r has no negative q powers. Thus the coefficient of `q^(−1)r^(−1)` survives in the Laurent-series quotient computing H1(O); it is nonzero after multiplication by every nonzero integer.

Nakayama's **Theorem 7.1.3**, in the original published paper on **p.604**, says that an LBP elliptic fibration over the complement of a codimension-at-least-two set in a polydisc, smooth outside the specified coordinate boundary, has a proper analytic elliptic extension (even up to bimeromorphic equivalence on that complement) exactly when it is globally BP. Here LBP means locally bimeromorphically projective and BP means bimeromorphically projective over the entire stated base. The preceding discussion of Ueno's problem on the same page identifies the smooth sectionless case above with a nontorsion class and gives its negative answer. Equivalently, the torsion characterization in Theorem 6.3.8 applies.

Crucially, this is **not** merely a theorem forbidding Kähler extensions. Step 3 of the proof, printed p.605, begins with an arbitrary putative proper extension and uses Hironaka flattening. It therefore accommodates nonflat fibres and vertical components over the missing origin before reaching the obstruction. The example disproves an automatic analytic completion theorem under the bare properties proper, smooth over U, compact fibres, and trivial cohomological monodromy. It does not by itself obstruct our two-dimensional torus fibres.

## 3. The hidden elliptic-quotient reduction fails for the actual periods

The precise failure can be proved directly from the actual period matrix, rather than inferred only from a generic-simplicity assertion. On the logarithmic cover of `(Δ_q*)×(Δ_r*)` use

`s=log(q)/(2πi)`, `v=log(r)/(2πi)`,

and the marked period columns

`γ=(6μ(q), C(q)−s−12v)`, `u=(τ(q), μ(q))`, `w=(1,0)`, `δ=(0,1)`.

The actual bounded units satisfy `τ=s+τ_hol(q)` and `μ(q)→μ0`, where `Im μ0≠0` (the specified unit has modulus `5+2√6`). The functions μ and C are holomorphic at q=0. These are the periods used in B.4.18–B.4.19, with all affine gauges retained separately.

Suppose two independent rational lattice vectors `z_i=(a_i,b_i,c_i,d_i)` had collinear complex periods throughout a nonempty open base set. An analytic family of elliptic subtori would supply exactly such a flat rank-two integral subsystem locally. On the connected logarithmic cover the determinant identity continues analytically. Write

`A_i=6a_i μ+b_i τ+c_i`,

`B_i=a_i(C−s−12v)+b_i μ+d_i`.

The coefficient of v in `A_1 B_2−A_2 B_1=0` is

`12[(a_1 b_2−a_2 b_1)τ+(a_1 c_2−a_2 c_1)]`.

Since τ is nonconstant, both rational coefficients vanish. If, say, `a_1≠0`, it follows that `a_1 z_2−a_2 z_1` is a pure δ vector. It is nonzero by independence. Collinearity would then force `A_1=0`. Let q tend to zero along the positive real ray. The unbounded τ term first forces `b_1=0`; the imaginary part of the remaining equality at μ0 forces `a_1=0`, a contradiction. Thus both a_i vanish.

In that remaining case the determinant identity is

`(b_1d_2−b_2d_1)τ+(c_1b_2−c_2b_1)μ+(c_1d_2−c_2d_1)=0`.

The same limit forces the τ coefficient to vanish. Because μ0 is nonreal and the other two coefficients are rational real numbers, both remaining coefficients vanish. All two-by-two minors of the vectors `(b_i,c_i,d_i)` are zero, contradicting independence.

Therefore the actual two-parameter torus family admits no analytic family of elliptic subtori over any nonempty open base set. It has no family of nonconstant elliptic quotients either: a holomorphic map from a complex two-torus to an elliptic curve is a translation of a homomorphism, whose connected kernel supplies the forbidden rank-two subsystem. A meromorphic map from a smooth two-torus to an elliptic curve is also holomorphic: near an isolated indeterminacy point, lift the map from the simply connected punctured ball to C and apply Hartogs. Thus replacing holomorphic quotient maps by meromorphic ones does not repair the reduction.

Finite base changes do not evade this argument. On a sufficiently small open subset where such a cover is unramified, it is a local analytic isomorphism, and the same rational period vectors and determinant identity apply. The result excludes the proposed passage from this actual family to Nakayama's relative-dimension-one theorem. It does **not** exclude an unrestricted proper origin cap.

The determinant coefficients are independently checked in `code/e_push_completion_diagnostics.py` and its output. The cusp-limit argument proves the statement for all rational vectors, not a finite list.

## 4. What the full weight-one variation does and does not supply

The actual linear period matrix has only logarithmic terms s and v, with bounded holomorphic remaining entries. Its two marked linear monodromies are commuting unipotent matrices: `u↦u+w`, `γ↦γ−δ` around q and `γ↦γ−12δ` around r. It therefore does not exhibit the elementary non-quasi-unipotent-monodromy obstruction to a proper analytic degeneration. Nor is its period matrix an essential-singularity map into the period Grassmannian. Such elementary natural-boundary tests do not decide this case.

The affine comparison between the two axial models is more data than that weight-one variation. In the positive-rotation convention B.4.18 it is represented by

`F(s,v)=(μ(q)(s+12v)/2, C(q)(s+12v)/12−(s+12v)^2/24+6v)`,

with `F(s+1,v)−F(s,v)=γ/12−δ/24` and `F(s,v+1)−F(s,v)=γ`.

Its quadratic logarithms are not a single-valued O-valued Čech cocycle on U. They cannot be identified with the additive class `1/(qr)` above, and their exponentials cannot be labelled an essential-singularity obstruction merely from their appearance. Integral-period changes and the singular group models on the axes must be retained. The actual fractional deck class, component rotation and reduced q-boundary are precisely the information missing from a test on the linear Gauss–Manin system alone.

### 4.1. The actual Jacobian pairing does extend as a log complex torus

This is stronger than merely observing unipotent monodromy. Put `Z=exp(2πiζ1)`, `W=exp(−2πiζ2)`, let X be their character lattice, and let `Y=Zu⊕Zγ`. Write `A=exp(2πi(τ−s))`, `a=exp(2πiμ)`, and `D=exp(−2πiC)`; all three are holomorphic units on the full q disc. The multiplicative period pairing, with rows `(Z,W)` and columns `(u,γ)`, is

```
             u              γ
 Z           q A            a^6
 W           a^(−1)         q r^12 D.
```

The base has the standard fine saturated log structure from qr=0. Its sharp monoid at the origin is N², written additively with generators q and r. The pairing modulo units is `diag(q,q+12r)`. Here is the complete face test in Kajiwara–Kato–Nakayama (2008a), Definition 1.2.3, printed pp.77–78. For the full face, a nonnegative functional `N(q)=h,N(r)=k` gives `diag(h,h+12k)`. If h>0 this is nondegenerate. If h=0<k, its kernel on both lattices is the first basis line, exactly the lattice associated with the face generated by q; the quotient pairing is multiplication by 12k. The zero functional gives zero-dimensional quotients. For the q face, both face lattices are the first basis line and the nontrivial quotient pairing is multiplication by h. For the r face and the zero face both face lattices vanish. These exhaust all faces and establish admissibility.

Definition 1.2.6, printed p.79, also requires nondegeneracy of the residual unit pairing. At q=0 no residual lattice remains. At r=0,q≠0 the residual lattices are `Ze1` and `Zu`, with pairing `log|qA(q)|≠0` after shrinking the q disc. At ordinary points the real logarithmic matrix has determinant

`log|qA| · log|q r^12 D| + 6(log|a|)^2 > 0`

after shrinking both discs so the first two logarithms are negative. Thus the stated pairing is nondegenerate everywhere on the bidisc. Definition 1.3.4, printed pp.81–82, therefore produces a log complex torus extending the actual Jacobian on the ordinary locus. No polarization is assumed or needed for this conclusion.

The precise existence inputs for proper models are Theorem 5.2.1, printed pp.171–172 (an admissible pairing admits a complete invariant fan with finitely many Y-orbits), and Theorem 5.3.2, printed pp.180–181 (the associated model is represented by a log-smooth fs log analytic space and is proper for a complete fan). Theorem 5.1.3 on p.170 summarizes these results; its unpolarized proper-model clause applies here. This establishes proper local models of the **Jacobian**, without identifying them with the two prescribed axial models of the actual affine family.

### 4.2. Full overlap, rejected short obstruction, and the remaining log-torsor test

The actual overlap must retain B.4.9's affine section σ and B.4.19's ordinary gauge. With `κ=κ0(q)`, one has `k=−s+12κ`, `σ1=6μv`, `σ2=−6v²+v(C−12κ)`. The exact identity is

`F−σ−kvδ = G(s)+6vδ`,

`G(s)=(μs/2, Cs/12−s²/24)`.

In particular the r-loop of the **full** overlap is only the integral period 6δ; its first coordinate has no log r term. A proposed obstruction obtained from the γ-valued r-loop of F alone is not an obstruction to this raw actual overlap. The analogous essential-orbit claim in `D_cusp_origin_attempts.md` was already withdrawn. No such analytic obstruction is asserted here. The polynomial cancellation is included in the exact diagnostic program.

There is a narrower log-torsor calculation still requiring an explicit check of the chosen axial origins. After q=Q^12, F by itself has Y-valued lift monodromies γ around Q and γ around r, since its additive differences are γ−6δ and γ. For the standard Jacobian log torus, a section on `Δ_Q×Δ_r*` has zero r-loop Y-monodromy: applying the q-ghost valuation to local multiplicative lifts sends that class by the injective map `Y→Z²`, `diag(12,12)`, while a section's transition coboundary has zero class. A section on `Δ_Q*×Δ_r` has zero r-loop Y-monodromy by contractibility of that loop in the base. Consequently F cannot be split into two sections of those standard axial log tori, and further finite Kummer covers do not kill this particular calculation.

To apply that calculation to the actual family one must establish both axial log-torsor identifications, with their origins and full descent: B.4.9's σ does give an actual section and fan-unit trivialization on `Δ_q×Δ_r*`; on the other axis the rotating quotient after q=Q^12 has period-valued twelfth deck, but its precise log-group origin and comparison still require verification. The ordinary gauge kvδ is not an extending section on the q-axis, so its cancellation cannot be silently treated as an allowed change of both log origins. Conversely, without proving the two identifications one cannot silently replace the actual overlap by F. This is a **working diagnostic**, not a claimed obstruction to the actual log torsor and still less to an unrestricted analytic cap.

### 4.3. Why arbitrary analytic caps do not automatically enter this category

Kajiwara–Kato–Nakayama's construction starts with a specified log torus and admissible pairing. It is not a theorem that an arbitrary two-torus torsor on this punctured bidisc extends across the corner, and it does not assert that every analytic cap with non-Kähler vertical components is such a log model. Flattening an unknown cap also does not produce that assertion.

Their separate *Analytic Log Picard Varieties* (2008b) makes the missing hypotheses explicit. Definition 7.1, printed pp.164–165, requires the log de Rham comparison, strict Hodge filtration properties, and an actual log Hodge structure (their term is “good for H^m”). The main comparison in §8 additionally assumes properness, separatedness, log smoothness, exactness, torsion-free characteristic cokernels, and the degree-one invariant-cycle isomorphism in Assumption 8.1, p.166. Section 9.3, pp.173–174, supplies goodness under locally projective or log-Kähler hypotheses; it does not supply it for an arbitrary non-Kähler filling. Section 9.3 explicitly says that log-Kählerness of every model of a log complex torus had not been proved there. Therefore that paper gives no automatic cap-to-log-torsor comparison under the permitted hypotheses here.

The exact unresolved step for this route is therefore an extension or obstruction theorem for the **full marked two-torus torsor and its two different axial models**, allowing vertical threefolds. No theorem with those hypotheses was located in the present searches. This is a search result and an exact failure of the proposed reductions, not a priority claim or a nonexistence proof for the actual cap.

## 5. Opened sources and access record

The original author manuscript https://www.kurims.kyoto-u.ac.jp/preprint/file/RIMS1322.pdf has readable text, with the corresponding theorem and seven-step proof on manuscript pp.140–144. The published numbering and actual flattening step were verified, rather than inferred from metadata. Original author manuscript opened at https://www.math.uni-duesseldorf.de/~schroeer/publications_pdf/kodaira.pdf. Section 6 supplies an instructive punctured-corner construction for its own positive-degree elliptic bundles; its Kodaira nearby surfaces are not the actual two-tori here. AB is independently investigating its explicit interface, so that construction is not duplicated in this note.

Original journal PDF opened through https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/41606?nt=1. Original journal PDF opened and downloaded: https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms150104.pdf. ## Detailed development record: D_exceptional_curve_closure_attempt.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Exact exceptional-curve nonclosure in the repaired degree-one Kato model

Owner: boundary_d. Working proof, 2026-11-15. This does not exclude an
arbitrary origin cap which changes the complementary region.

## Marked intersection section

In the n=1 family of B.4.10, at a parameter q not zero the unique
exceptional rational curve of the generic Hopf blow-up meets the
persistent elliptic axis transversely at [v=-q] in
E_{a(q)}=C*/a(q)^Z. This is the ordinary blow-up intersection, expressed
in the exact v=w-q marking used in that construction. After q=s^12
the section is p(s)=[-s^12]. The seventy-eight modifications of B.4.21
are all disjoint from the persistent axis, so they leave this entire
intersection germ unchanged.

Choose a holomorphic logarithm tau(s)=(2 pi i)^(-1) log a(s^12) on the
full small disc. The elliptic family is C/(Z+tau(s) Z). On a lifted
punctured disc the section has logarithm
z(s)=(2 pi i)^(-1) log(-s^12). Its continuation around a positive
s-loop changes z by 12, whereas tau returns unchanged. Therefore its
loop has class 12 times the primitive C* winding cycle in the integral
first homology of the smoothly trivial elliptic family. It cannot be
the boundary of a continuous section over the disc. In particular p
has no holomorphic or meromorphic extension. A finite base change of
degree d replaces 12 by 12d and does not remove this obstruction.

## No analytic divisor closure of the exceptional family

Suppose the union of generic exceptional P1s had a proper analytic
divisor closure Z in the repaired threefold. Take the irreducible
component which dominates the parameter disc. It cannot contain the
persistent axis family, since on every nonzero fibre their intersection
is exactly the one point p(s). Intersecting the two Cartier divisors
gives an analytic curve, proper over the disc. Its horizontal reduced
part has generic degree one. A proper irreducible analytic curve with
nonconstant map to a disc has finite fibres; after normalization its
degree-one map is a biholomorphism. Vertical components do not alter
this horizontal component. Thus the intersection extends p(s),
contradicting its nonzero homology winding. Hence no such analytic
divisor closure exists. The same reasoning applies to any further
finite proper modification if a putative closure is pushed forward
properly to the repaired model.

A relative proper holomorphic modification contracting the generic
exceptional P1s would supply precisely such a horizontal exceptional
divisor. A relative proper meromorphic contraction, interpreted via
an analytic graph and a proper resolution of that graph, would do the
same after proper pushforward. Thus these contractions cannot extend
through s=0 while retaining the given complementary Kato family. This
does not rule out a cap obtained by a new deformation or a replacement
which is not such a relative meromorphic comparison.

## Primary context and source access

Dloussky and Teleman explicitly study the known failure of properness
for exceptional curves in deformations of minimal GSS surfaces to
blown-up Hopf surfaces. Their Section 1.2 explains that the exceptional
classes have no compact effective limiting cycle; later sections study
limits on universal covers. The marked-intersection proof above is
self-contained and makes no novelty claim.

APA metadata independently checked on the publisher site:
Dloussky, G., & Teleman, A. (2012). Infinite bubbling in non-Kählerian
geometry. *Mathematische Annalen, 353*, 1283–1314.
https://doi.org/10.1007/s00208-011-0713-9

Opened original author preprint: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1012.5247
(version 2, 5 October 2011), Section 1.2, printed pp.3–4.
Opened publisher metadata: https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00208-011-0713-9 .
Search query: Dloussky Teleman infinite bubbling non Kahler exceptional
curves Hopf Enoki deformation. Retrieval: turn557academia22, then
turn558view0 (full primary preprint) and turn558view1 (publisher).
The subscription published full text was not obtained; no claim of
reading it is made. No new manuscript input or results CSV yet.


## Post-checkpoint full-source version

The complete marked nonclosure and general semistable persistence
proof is now in `B_exceptional_curve_obstruction.tex`, reserved B.4.23.
For the latter, the exact sequence
0 -> O(-1) -> N_{C/Y} -> O^2 -> 0 splits, H1(N)=0, and the induced
map on H0 to the two base directions is an isomorphism. Kodaira's
local embedded-deformation space consequently maps locally
biholomorphically to (Q,r), forcing a rational curve in each nearby
ordinary torus fibre. The conductor-disjointness assumption is
explicit and essential.

Kodaira1962 original DOI/publisher metadata were Opened; its full

Merkulov, S. A. (1995). Moduli spaces of compact complex submanifolds
of complex fibered manifolds. Mathematical Proceedings of the
Cambridge Philosophical Society, 118(1), 71–91.
https://doi.org/10.1017/S0305004100073473 .
Original Kodaira metadata: Annals of Mathematics, 75(1) (1962),
146–162; DOI10.2307/1970424 (turn565search0, turn568search0).
No full-original reading claim is made.


Root then independently opened Dloussky–Teleman Proposition 2.3 and

# B.4.24: exact logarithmic category obstruction

Owner: classification_e. 15 November 2026, 20:46 UTC. **Proved and integrated after B.4.23.** Root and boundary_d independently read the full source and opened the original KKN definitions and proper-model theorems. D's referee is `D_B4424_referee.md`.

The actual Jacobian has multiplicative pairing `[[q a0,a1^6],[a1^−1,q r^12 a2]]`. Every monoid-face test and the residual unit pairing satisfy KKN's nondegeneracy definition. Thus the unpolarized proper-model existence theorem applies to the Jacobian itself. This is an existence assertion in its exact source category, not a polarization assumption.

The actual affine gluing gives additional information. After q=Q^12, the actual cusp side has the extending origin σ from B.4.9, and the rotating side is its prequotient root fan with the ordinary zero origin. Passing from those explicit fan presentations to their associated log-torus sheaves, the transition is exactly F. Its multiplicative lift has Y-monodromy γ around r. Every extending log section on the q-axis has zero such monodromy because q-valuation induces the injective matrix diag(12,12) on Y. A section extending across r=0 has zero r-loop monodromy by contractibility. Hence the clutching cannot split, and multiplying it by any positive integer cannot split either. Finite coordinate-root covers preserve the obstruction.

The full raw overlap remains `F−σ−kvδ=G(s)+6vδ`. This exact cancellation invalidates an analytic nonextension argument from F alone. It does not invalidate the sheaf calculation using the two verified extending origins: kvδ does not extend as a log section at q=0. The difference between these two arguments is explicitly recorded in the proof and the earlier working notes.

The result excludes only a cap supplied by a locally trivial torsor under this fixed log complex torus with the given axial identifications. Proper fan models themselves are not group objects. Arbitrary analytic caps may contain vertical threefolds and need not define such a torsor. KKN's log Picard comparison explicitly assumes Hodge goodness and an invariant-cycle condition; neither is silently transferred to the present general cap problem. The unrestricted B.4 construction and B.5 invariants remain Open.

Files: `B_log_torsor_obstruction.tex`, `B_log_torsor_results.csv`, `B_log_torsor_references.bib`; exact checks in `code/e_push_completion_diagnostics.py/.out/.json`. The historical attempts and source scopes remain in `E_B_general_completion_attempt.md` and `E_B_log_torsor_working.md`. Search metadata is in `evidence/E_B_completion_search_log.json` and its addendum. Full downloaded articles remain outside the deliverable package.
