# B.4.30: noncompletion of the fixed partial filling

## Result and exact scope

The particular actual partial filling of B.4.19 cannot be completed properly over its missing origin, even after finite coordinate roots and even with a three-dimensional origin fibre. This result uses both prescribed punctured axial models. It does not on its own classify or exclude an arbitrary r-only filling whose r=0 axial model changes after resolving the marked map.

## Proof mechanism

The original analytic logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert theorem IKN2005 Theorem6.3 identifies relative log-de Rham H1 with the locally free nilpotent extension without a Kähler or projectivity hypothesis. A low-degree exact sequence and the absence of t-torsion in R1fO make the closed relative log1-form lattice saturated, so it equals the known normalized Hodge subbundle. Full-rank monodromy then forces all local meridian residues to be integral cocharacters. A convergent integer-weight calculation gives monomials times holomorphic units as local exponential primitives; a finite chain of charts proves logarithmic extension of the difference of two sections both extending into the same cap.

A direct excess-one coefficient-ideal argument, following the opened original Parusiński preprint, makes the cap equidimensional by a base modification supported at the origin. It does not require a full flattening theorem. Finite local slice surfaces are normalized and therefore finite-flat over the smooth surface base. Generic semistable reduction uses a simultaneous root whose degree is the product of the multiplicity common multiple and the finite-section splitting bound, followed by compatible height-one lattice subdivisions. Norms extend a common multiple of every trace difference. Hartogs for A-sections fills isolated crossing points: H1 of the punctured bidisc with constant Y coefficients is zero, so a logarithmic character lift exists. These transition differences glue an extending positive power of the actual torsor, contradicting B.4.24 and B.4.27.

## Source audit

IKN2005 original printed pp32,34 and the entire 2007 erratum were opened. The erratum concerns algebraic Theorem7.1, not the analytic theorem used. Parusiński MPI/90-58 Corollary1.2 and proof were opened directly; the published 1994 version was bibliographically verified but its author-hosted PDF failed to download, so its page numbers are not substituted. Enokizono–Hashizume arXiv2404.05124v3 Lemma2.14 was read for analytic toroidal gluing; their projective main theorem is not used. BM95AB Theorems1.6,1.10 were independently opened for analytic resolution near the compact exceptional set. The inaccessible Hironaka1975 full-text search remains in the working audit and is no longer a proof dependency. All full articles remain outside the deliverable.

## Review

Root and boundary_d independently passed the full source. Root requested the explicit coherent-localization sentence and simultaneous product-root correction; both were applied before integration. D supplied the convergent coefficient-ideal division and globally ordered star-subdivision precisions. His detailed notes are D_B4430_referee.md and D_B4430_equdimensionalization.md. The previous working note is retained as a superseded development record.

# B.4.32: global radial-loop defect

**Status:** proved and integrated after B.4.31. Root read the complete final TeX and passed it. Classification_e and limits_ab independently read the full working proof and passed the connecting-class definition, root normalization, actual affine-overlap audit, section comparison, trace normalization and fixed-space calculation.

The proof begins with an arbitrary proper analytic filling. Its reduced main component is normalized and resolved without altering the punctured family; B.4.4 then extends the intrinsic marked map. B.4.31 supplies the global constraint that all generic r-central meridians lie in the primitive δ direction. Local finite roots and explicit toric subdivisions suffice; no global nonprojective semistable-reduction theorem is invoked.

For a punctured Jacobian section, the positive r-loop defines a connecting class in Λ/(T_r−I)Λ and hence in Λ_Q/Qδ. An e-fold parameter root multiplies this class by e. Dividing by e makes the class of an extending cap section independent of all roots. The saturation and finite chart-chain arguments of B.4.30, with the B.4.31 residue vector (0,integer), show that any two extending sections have the same normalized class.

The origin comparisons are those of the actual B.4.19 construction. Ordinary logarithm changes contribute n log(r)·δ. The finite-root connector's entire r logarithm cancels. The cusp comparison is G(s)+6vδ with G independent of r. The Lie-frame maps are independent of r and fix δ. Thus the normalized class patches globally in the actual homology local system.

On Λ_Q/Qδ, the two peripheral fixed lines are Q(1,2,−4) and Q(1,3,−3), with zero intersection. Consequently the global class vanishes. This controls the γ radial-loop component required for the next cusp argument, while allowing essential translations with zero period defect.

The exact program code/d_push_global_loop_defect.py and its saved output/JSON pass the fixed-space, coherent root-transfer, finite connector and cusp-loop checks. Geometry is proved in the TeX. The working derivation is retained in sections/D_global_loop_defect_working.md. No new primary bibliography entry is needed: the analytic input is the original IKN2005E theorem already independently opened and cited in B.4.30; the actual comparison identities are the proved B.4.19 equations.

## Audit distinctions retained

The first warning that bounded pairwise differences do not identify the old affine origin was correct. The present proof does not remove that warning by assuming an origin extends. It controls only the period defect via global monodromy, which is enough for the separate B.4.33 argument if that final argument passes review.

The exact local marking change χ(r)=[(log r)/(2πi)·u] produces a nonzero u defect over an isolated ordinary base disc. It cannot patch over the entire marked base because u has infinite monodromy orbit. This is the appropriate local scope example; B.4.15's vertical Hopf insertion is not claimed to realize this particular generic defect.

# B.4.33: the unrestricted proper-filling contradiction

## Review and integration state

The complete proof in `B_global_noncompletion.tex` has passed root, boundary_d, and limits_ab full-source review. Root requested an explicit uniform local-root paragraph and a precise treatment of sigma under modifications; both were inserted before registration. The result is Proved, with its own CSV, and is input immediately after B.4.32. The author's adversarial audit is retained separately from the independent referee notes.

## Exact conclusion

No proper Hausdorff complex-analytic filling of the actual punctured six-sphere family exists after any finite parameter cover. This includes arbitrary singular central spaces and vertical three-dimensional fibres of the marked two-parameter map. Consequently no positive exponent N can provide the smooth, reduced normal-crossings filling requested in Part B. The assertion is stronger than B.4.30, which only excludes completing the particular B.4.19 axial models.

## Proof structure and the previously missing affine comparison

The revised B.4.4 extends the marked fibration after resolving the main component of any putative proper filling. Its proof needs smoothness of the dominating fourfold, not a reduced central divisor. B.4.31 is also valid with central multiplicities. The only semistable constructions are local one-parameter surface reductions near generic boundary divisors, retaining a transverse parameter.

After shrinking the cusp bidisc, excess fibre dimension can occur only at its origin: a three-dimensional fibre must contain a central divisor component mapped to a constant marked base value, and there are finitely many such components. B.4.30's direct coefficient-ideal construction then makes the marked map equidimensional after point blowups of that origin. A finite cover of K=the inverse image of the origin supplies finite local multisections. This covers the whole modified small bidisc after shrinking, including the case of no blowup, when K is a point.

Multiply the local traces to one common degree D. Their differences extend to the exact associated-log-torus sheaf on every boundary divisor: full-rank monodromy treats exceptional divisors and the actual q axis, while B.4.31 forces every angular residue on the unknown strict r axis to lie in the delta line. Thus the first character remains a unit and the second is a boundary monomial times a unit, exactly as required by the bounded character sheaf. Norms give a common multiplier; Hartogs for logarithmic sections fills isolated crossings. These differences glue a torsor Q on all of the modified base S. They do not assert that the cap itself is a log-group model.

B.4.27 gives H1(S,A)=0, so Q has a global origin h whose ordinary restriction is a section of the actual D-fold torsor. B.4.32 says h relative to the B.4.19 ordinary origin has zero radial class in Lambda_Q/Q delta. The actual ordinary-to-rotating comparison is G(s)+6v delta; its radial class is also zero in that quotient. Therefore h relative to the rotating origin has zero gamma-loop coefficient. This is only a loop statement: a common essential translation is still allowed.

On the actual q-axis model, sigma is a genuine extending origin, so h−D sigma is a section of the specified log torus on the whole q-axis region. The injective q-ghost period map diag(12m,12m) makes its gamma-loop coefficient zero as well. However, sigma minus the rotating origin is exactly the audited clutching section F, whose positive r-root loop has gamma coefficient n. Subtracting the two expressions for h therefore gives 0=D n, impossible for positive D and n.

## What this argument does not assume

It does not identify an unknown r-axis completion with the B.4.19 rotating model. It does not infer boundedness of a single translated origin from relative forms or Gauss–Manin data. It does not infer Hartogs extension for torsors; only sections are extended, after the actual trace differences have been obtained. It does not invoke a projective or Kähler semistable-reduction theorem for threefolds. The earlier essential-translation counterexample B.4.29 remains valid and is explicitly accommodated.

## Source and verification provenance

The final proof combines the already opened primary inputs recorded in B.4.24, B.4.27, and B.4.30, including analytic logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert comparison, the coefficient-ideal equidimensionalization argument, analytic resolution, and compatible toroidal subdivisions. No new general completion theorem is claimed. Exact period differences and the complete gauge cancellation are audited in `code/e_push_completion_diagnostics.py`; root-transfer and global fixed spaces are audited independently in `code/d_push_global_loop_defect.py` and `code/ab_push_global_meridians.py`. These computations verify identities, not the geometric nonexistence argument.

The finite trace normalization, strict-r residue comparison, global loop invariant, and final affine comparison have each received an independent agent referee. Those reviews are independent checks of one proof, not separate proofs or new published literature. Full downloaded articles remain outside the deliverable tree.

## Uniformity and comparison checks

A finite cover of the compact inverse image of the origin supplies finitely many local finite covers and trace degrees. Embedded resolution over a relatively compact neighbourhood supplies finitely many boundary multiplicities. One product of the multiplicity common multiple with lcm(1,...,d_max) therefore bounds the local transverse roots and norms at every generic boundary point. No global root along an entire boundary curve is claimed or needed; local norm extensions glue by uniqueness. Nonboundary branch curves and section-lift indeterminacy add only isolated points near that compact set, where Hartogs applies. Sigma can be compared directly through projection to its prescribed fan model and the explicit finite-character norm, without a global holomorphic lift through an arbitrary modification.

The untwisted Engel family is not contradicted. Its identity closing has both extending fan origins equal to zero and therefore has zero clutching section F. The actual twisted closing has the nonzero gamma loop calculated in B.4.24. The linear monodromy and zero fixed-space calculation alone do not distinguish these cases; the exact affine closing does.

## Literature status

No matching theorem excluding arbitrary proper analytic fillings was found in the sources checked for this task. The cited analytic logarithmic comparison, toroidal modification, and equidimensionalization results supply specific inputs; they are not cited as an existing general noncompletion theorem. This is a derivation for the actual family from its audited periods and affine transitions. No priority or novelty claim is made.

The indexed original-PDF extraction identifies printed p.553, Theorem1.1,
and retains the same equality converse as the preprint. This was obtained
by the exact domain query for the phrase `Equality holds`; reference
turn453search0. It is sufficient to identify the published statement,
but it is not recorded as a full published-PDF read. Direct web-open of
the PDF URL redirected to the landing page; a standard urllib request
returned HTTP403. The publisher preview on ResearchGate contains only
the first printed page and was not used to infer the second page's theorem.

**Independent root check:** root subsequently opened the original
retrieval reference turn453search0 directly and used `find` for
Theorem1.1. Its independent retrievals turn476view0 and turn476view1
expose the original publisher PDF's printed pp.552–553, with the
equality converse at lines41–48. Root confirmed the published statement
from that two-page indexed original-PDF excerpt. The distinction from
a complete published-PDF read remains in force.

The main proof of B.4.17 is independent of this paper. It is a direct
compact-base realization of the fully checked Cartier cocycle in B.4.7.

## Logical issue and what remains valid

The proof gives

`b1(F0) <= dim H1(F)^T <= b1(F)`.

Trivial T gives equality in the right-hand inequality. It provides no
equality in the left-hand inequality when F0 is reducible. Our compact
principal-elliptic example has values `0 < 1 = 1`. No Kähler hypothesis
is imposed in the source theorem, and our compact smooth threefold
satisfies all its actual stated hypotheses.

The topological inequality itself and the forward implication from
equality of the endpoint Betti numbers to trivial monodromy are not
refuted. The irreducible-fibre Corollary3.7 is also unaffected: the relevant
top homology target has rank one and its fibre-class image is nonzero.
The exact compact counterexample has two components.

## Additional preprint issue, not needed for B.4.17

The version1 proof of Theorem3.5 also equates trivial monodromy around
every singular value with trivial global monodromy for an arbitrary
base curve. That implication fails for positive-genus bases. For example,
take elliptic curves E1,E2, a nonzero two-torsion point a in E1, and the
free involution `(x,y) -> (x+a,-y)` of E1 times E2. Its quotient is a smooth
projective surface fibred over E1/<a>, all fibres E2. Its Euler number is
zero, equal to the product of the two fibre/base Euler numbers, whereas
one global monodromy acts as minus identity on H1(E2). This separate
explicit example does not require any classification theorem. I have not
checked the complete published proof of Theorem3.5, and do not attribute
its precise proof wording to that unavailable full published version.

No claim of novelty is made for either elementary construction or the
underlying Cartier logarithmic-transformation method.

# Independent referee of A.4.8

Date: 2026-11-15. Source: `sections/ab_push_all_fan_homology.tex`. Verdict: **passes in the explicitly specified class of finite toroidal outputs of A.2.4**. This is not a statement about arbitrary analytic fillings.

The primary source check was made directly against Abramovich–Karu–Matsuki–Włodarczyk (2002), Theorems 1.6.1 and 2.7.1, the proof on page 552, and analytic Section 5.5 on page 565, together with Matsuki's corrected Section 2. The proof of Theorem 2.7.1 works with the finite polyhedral complex, its integral barycentric embedding, a fan cobordism, and nonsingular star subdivisions. Section 5.5 supplies the analytic realization of those modifications. The displayed complete-analytic formulation in Section 5.6 is not being misquoted as a relative theorem. The manuscript explicitly constructs the finite complex and its analytic stars, which is the needed additional argument.

Deepest-strata-first barycentric modification is valid for the normal-crossing analytic boundary with self-identifications. Deeper intersections are separated before taking a stratum closure as the next smooth centre. Temporary pure-q centres are allowed; this is essential because strictification need not preserve the punctured r-family. The gluing uses monomial ideals, so analytic bundle units do not obstruct its descent.

The centre classification has no hidden period quotient in the mixed or pure-bottom case. A stabilizer power fixes each primitive ray. A positive q-height ray then forces first the G1 exponent and then the G2 exponent to vanish. Only a pure-top two-cone retains G1, and its global plumbing extension is birational to the rational surface S. Pure-bottom centres are smooth toric surfaces or curves proper over the r-disc; the primitive character gives connected generic fibre P1 or a point, with rational-chain or point central support. Thus all the listed centres have free even integral cohomology, and precisely the compact ones have the top cohomology needed to contribute to degree six.

The ordinary integral blow-up splitting applies to these noncompact closed centres. Tubular excision, integral Thom and exceptional Gysin maps, the projective-bundle theorem, and proper blow-down pushforward via Borel–Moore duality require neither compactness of the ambient space nor a Kähler hypothesis. The claimed b2 and b6 increments follow in every complex codimension.

The reference normalization calculation is consistent with A.4.1. Eliminating the intermediate identity blocks leaves the original difference map and the functional a·(m−F), which is already an integral combination of its primitive coordinates. The rank-seven kernel and all primitive-image claims therefore survive for arbitrary n; the finite Smith computations are supplementary checks, not the general argument. The final Euler reconstruction of b4 and the monodromy substitution I+nN are correct.

Primary sources independently opened:

* Abramovich, D., Karu, K., Matsuki, K., & Włodarczyk, J. (2002). Torification and factorization of birational maps. *Journal of the American Mathematical Society, 15*(3), 531–572. <https://personal.math.ubc.ca/~karu/papers/semist.pdf>. DOI: 10.1090/S0894-0347-02-00396-X.
* Matsuki, K. (2000). Erratum to the paper “A note on the factorization theorem of toric birational maps after Morelli and its toroidal extension.” <https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0006038>. Section 2 is the corrected weak-factorization statement; the invalid strong-factorization algorithm is not used.

No substantive correction was identified. This referee does not certify any unrestricted non-toroidal extension or infer a homology formula outside A.2.4.

# Independent referee: B.4.12

Referee: boundary_d. Date: 2026-11-15. Outcome: PASS at the stated proper-family and normal-line scope.

The two distinct degree-two line systems give an injective immersion of the marked elliptic curve into P1×P1: a shared identified pair or shared branch point would equate the line systems. Taking a degree-four square root of N inverse then makes the anticanonical normal exactly N inverse.

The anticanonical section ring of Q is normal and generated in degree one. The restriction sequence has vanishing H1 in every degree, including d=0 because H1(O(-2,-2))=0. Its quotient by the section is therefore exactly the elliptic section ring, without an overlooked degree-zero term. The cone map is flat, and its nonzero fibre is Q minus E.

The vertex blow-up is Tot(K_Q), with parameter xi·e. Its central components are Q and Tot(N), each reduced, with equation xi·z=t near E. B.4.11 globalizes this particular local component, so the compact strict transform is exactly S8 and the other component exactly Q. Properness and smooth total space follow as stated. The normals K_Q and O_S(-E), and the three pairwise products including one triple-curve factor, have the correct signs and degrees.

The integral Thom sequence gives H1(Q minus E)=Z/2, H2=Z^3 and H3=0: H2(Q)→H0(E) is (a,b)↦2a+2b, while H4(Q)→H2(E) is degree one. The local Euler number is four. The compact nearby surface has Euler number twelve, not two.

The scope limitation is necessary and correctly explicit: a proper family over an open disc is not itself an absolute compact threefold, and the line-bundle identities do not construct a global compact NC threefold or the prescribed punctured S6 comparison. No such stronger inference is used.

## Detailed development record: D_B4415_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent referee: B.4.15 regular-place Hopf insertion

Referee: boundary_d. 15 November 2026, 18:36 UTC.

**Verdict: PASS at the exact local, two-parameter scope of the working statement.**
I read `sections/ab_push_regular_cap_working.md` in full and inspected the
exact assertions in `code/ab_push_regular_cap.py`, its output and its JSON.
The argument gives a proper replacement over the bidisc, isomorphic to the
actual prescribed model off the base origin, with smooth total fourfold and
reduced normal-crossings divisor over the degeneration parameter. It does
not assert joint flatness, properness over the noncompact parameter disc
alone, a one-parameter compact-surface degeneration, or a global S6 filling.
Those qualifications are necessary and are correctly retained.

## 1. Actual semiabelian action and the closing period

The rank-three quotient is the analytic group

\[
G_z=\mathbb C^2/(\mathbb Z u(z)+\mathbb Z w+\mathbb Z\delta),
\quad u(z)=(\tau(z),\mu(z)),\quad w=(1,0),\quad\delta=(0,1).
\]

It is the actual degree-zero punctured line bundle over the actual elliptic
curve. The formula

\[
\chi_z(t)=\left[\frac{\log t}{2\pi i}u(z)\right]
\]

is a single-valued holomorphic group homomorphism because a logarithm
change adds an integral multiple of the actual period u. No algebraicity
of this homomorphism, closedness of its image, splitting of G, or torsion
of the degree-zero line bundle is required. In particular, its existence
does not contradict simplicity of a generic compact two-torus.

Here is a useful explicit version of the equivariance check. Before
quotienting by the closing period, a translation represented by (a,b)
acts in additive/multiplicative coordinates by

\[
(\xi,W)\longmapsto(\xi+a,e^{-2\pi i b}W).
\]

This descends through the three fixed periods. The closing period is
another translation of this same commutative group, with its scalar
coordinate containing the factor r^12. Thus the two actions commute
exactly, not just on the elliptic quotient. Each unfolded compactification
is G times, over the scalar C* subgroup, P1. The zero and infinity sections
are preserved. At a node the two fibre directions are paired dually; their
factors cancel in xy=r. The toric subdivision of the W/r direction is
equivariant under scalar multiplication. Consequently the action extends
to the entire twelve-cycle resolution and keeps each component label.
This is precisely the action needed for the later twisting and ideal
blow-ups; an action on the smooth generic fibres alone would not suffice.

## 2. The affine-to-Jacobian comparison is valid locally

For B=Delta times Delta*, the actual lattice system Lambda has H2(B,Lambda)=0:
B has the homotopy type of a circle, also for these local coefficients.
The relative Lie algebra is a coherent vector bundle. The exponential
sequence of sheaves of local holomorphic sections

\[
0\longrightarrow\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathcal V
\longrightarrow\mathcal T\longrightarrow0
\]

and Cartan B on this Stein base give H1(B,T)=0. The actual affine torus
family is a torsor under its Jacobian and therefore has a holomorphic
section on this punctured local base. This provides the required actual
comparison. Nothing in this argument extends that section across r=0 or
asserts that an affine torsor is globally split over the elliptic base.

## 3. The ideal and both component calculations

In the base-blow-up chart r=zs, the pullback node is xy=zs. A central
component of the original smooth total space is Cartier. Hence its
pulled-back ideal and O(-F) are honest invertible ideals, and their sum is
globally meaningful. With the even branch labelled x=0, this sum is
(x,z). It is invariant under G because the component and the base
exceptional divisor are invariant. Its non-Cartier locus is exactly
x=y=z=s=0, with the elliptic coordinate free. The twelve such loci are
disjoint. Thus the six even-component prescriptions do not introduce an
overlap choice.

The blow-up of (x,z), after the requisite strict-transform cancellation,
has the two smooth charts

\[
z=xQ,\quad y=Qs,\quad r=xQs;
\qquad
x=zX,\quad s=Xy,\quad r=zXy.
\]

All displayed factors occur once. In the other base chart z=r zeta, the
ideal is (x,r)=(x,xy)=(x), so the operation is an isomorphism. Away from
the node loci it is likewise Cartier and has no effect.

For the vertical even branch V_x, the two induced maps to its original
normalization with coordinates (y,s) are

\[
(Q,s)\mapsto(Qs,s),\qquad (X,y)\mapsto(y,Xy).
\]

These are exactly the blow-up charts of (y,s). The untouched elliptic
coordinate makes the centre an elliptic curve. V_y is unchanged. The
horizontal odd branch H_y has the corresponding blow-up, whereas H_x
is unchanged. Applying this at the two distinct conductor sections of
each component gives two disjoint elliptic-curve blow-ups on every even
vertical component and none on an odd vertical component. The claimed
horizontal parity is the opposite. This agrees with the exact twelve-edge
incidence calculation in the code.

## 4. Cartier twisting, separation and properness

The transition functions of O(F) are holomorphic units on their overlaps.
Applying the holomorphic homomorphism chi gives actual automorphisms of
the resolved model satisfying an exact cocycle. Invariance of the ideals
proves that resolving and twisting commute. The gluing is a locally
trivial form of the given proper model over each base open set. Two points
over the same base point are separated in one such chart, and points over
different base points are separated by the base. Thus the result is
Hausdorff. Properness is local on the target and is preserved under
composition with the proper base blow-up.

The canonical section of O(F) is nowhere zero off F and therefore
trivializes the twisting there. The small resolutions are also
isomorphisms there. This supplies an actual specified holomorphic
isomorphism with the original model off the base origin, in particular
over every r nonzero. It is stronger than just an isomorphism of generic
fibres or equality of their periods.

The total space is smooth, r is a nonzero divisor, and a torsion-free
module over the one-dimensional regular local parameter ring is flat.
This proves the asserted flatness over r. The vertical threefolds over a
point of the bidisc correctly preclude an assertion of joint flatness.

## 5. Compact vertical normalization and its topology

On F, O(F) restricts to O_P1(-1). With the usual associated-bundle
convention, the elliptic quotient is

\[
H=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^1}(-1)^\times/\ker(\chi\colon\mathbb C^*\to E).
\]

Dividing the elliptic additive coordinate by tau0 changes its lattice to
Z+eta Z, eta=-1/tau0 in the upper half-plane. The projected homomorphism
then has kernel a'^Z, a'=exp(2 pi i eta), with absolute value less than one.
Thus H is exactly the scalar Hopf surface

\[
(\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\})/\langle a'\operatorname{id}\rangle.
\]

The associated G-bundle over P1 maps to H with free scalar C* fibre.
Compactifying this principal C* bundle produces the actual projective
bundle P_H(O plus M_H), with both conductor sections globally defined.
This argument retains the original degree-zero line bundle on each
elliptic fibre and does not replace it by a product. The two curve centres
are the two conductor sections over the one distinguished point of F,
hence disjoint compact elliptic curves.

The universal cover of H is C2 minus zero, so pi1(H)=Z. A P1 bundle has
the same fundamental group as its base, and blow-up along a smooth
complex codimension-two centre preserves fundamental group. Therefore
every final vertical normalization has b1=1 and fails the ddbar lemma.

## Scope and source use

This is a genuine local counterexample to an attempted exclusion of
non-ddbar central components based only on generic simplicity in this
two-parameter setting. It preserves the actual punctured torus family.
It leaves the original component permutations unchanged, and therefore
does not evade B.3.1. The explicit analytic gluing proves the needed
extension of the Cartier construction; no unsupported enlargement of
Fujimoto's original principal-elliptic hypotheses is being used. No new
global compactification or uniqueness theorem is imported in this
referee report.

# Independent referee of B.4.16

15 November 2026. **PASS after the stated coefficient-system correction.**

I checked `sections/ab_push_regular_cap_dual.tex` against the two analytic
charts already independently verified in B.4.15, and inspected the exact
incidence/collapse code `code/ab_push_regular_cap_dual.py`.

The two triples at an even/odd node are exactly `(V_i,V_j,H_j)` and
`(V_i,H_i,H_j)`. Their diagonal is `V_i H_j`. Over the twelve nodes these
give the indicated cylinder. Each top edge is free at the first stage;
each diagonal is free after the top triangles have been removed; each
remaining new vertex then has just its vertical edge. Thus all 36
elementary collapses are valid relative to the old horizontal cycle.
The chain retraction and the primitive angular cocycle in the code agree
with this actual collapse, rather than merely reproducing Betti numbers.

When local horizontal branches acquire global identifications, the new
space is still the mapping cylinder attached along the existing local
cycle map. Its deformation retraction onto the original dual complex
does not require that attaching map to be injective. A connected
holomorphic group preserves the finite sets of component and connected
stratum labels; toroidal subdivisions preserve the underlying
polyhedral homotopy type. Consequently the result applies successively
to precisely the operations listed, with no assertion about arbitrary
analytic replacements.

The first version of the final spectral-sequence paragraph needed a
coefficient-system qualification for self-crossings. Ordered branch
tuples must be taken with the alternating/sign summand; unordered branch
strata carry the corresponding orientation local system. Ordinary
constant coefficients on unordered strata are not a substitute when
branches permute. The revised proof correctly uses complex coefficients
and alternating direct summands, so averaging over the finite
permutation group is available and the augmented local simplex complex
is exact. Its `E2^(1,0)` is the first cohomology of the dual complex,
and no higher differential enters or leaves that position. This gives
the needed injection into H1 of the central space. A nonzero integral
first cohomology class remains nonzero over C because first integral
cohomology of a finite complex is torsion-free. The combinatorial
collapse certificate itself remains integral.

Thus the resulting obstruction to a simply connected semistable centre
is valid. It is an obstruction only for the specified generated class of
insertions, connected-action twists and toroidal subdivisions; it does
not settle the unrestricted analytic filling question.

# Independent referee: local corner resolution candidate B.4.22

Referee: boundary_d. Date: 2026-11-15, 20:33 UTC.
Source read in full: `ab_push_corner_resolution_working.tex`.
Saved exact certificate output read: `../code/ab_push_corner_resolution.out`.
Verdict: PASS at the explicitly local scope. No global origin cap is proved.

For n=1, the q chart of Bl(u,q) gives u=qU and g=fUv; the pullback
ideal (f,g) is already principal. Its u chart gives q=uQ and fv=gQ;
the f and g charts of the second blow-up give respectively v=GQ,
g=fG and Q=Fv, f=gF. These are closures of the dense torus, not the
naive total transform with extraneous components. All three are
smooth and have the displayed product equations for r.

After q=s^n, the only remaining singular factors are uQ=s^n and
uvF=s^n. The height-one unit triangulation of n Delta_2 is unimodular
and covers the cone; its F-invertible edge is i+j=n. The identification
Q=vF matches exactly the minimal A_(n-1) resolution used on the
second chart. The second-to-first overlap has Q nonzero, where that
resolution is an isomorphism. Thus the local proper modifications
glue, and there is no unresolved fan compatibility condition.

On the third resolved chart, g is independent and F is a submersion
above F=1, so r=g(1-F) has two transverse reduced branches. Above
F=0 the other factor is a unit. The analogous second-chart product
f(G-1) and the first-chart product f(Uv-1) verify r normal crossings
everywhere. For n>1, s=0 is the reduced semistable divisor, while
the original q=s^n divisor has multiplicity n. The source's cyclic
base-change language is compatible with this; I requested that the
distinction be made explicit before any q-deck descent.

The collar comparison is exact: h=uv-q, H=h/q, Hf=r give
fuv=(f+r)q. Near H=0, putting F=(1+H)^(-1), g=f(1+H) identifies
the repaired collar with the third chart near F=1. The additional
F=0 charts are proposed local completion charts; this calculation
does not attach them to the complementary global Kato region.

The original period action remains a separate issue. The proven
first collar action is H -> aH, f -> a^(-1)f and is locally conjugated
by the product G. No identity with the full second period, no proper
global cap, no extension across all zeros of that conjugating product,
and no removal of the surviving exceptional-curve obstruction are
inferred. The conditional Mobius formula is explicitly conditional
on a test coordinate, not an asserted marked-period obstruction.

# Independent referee of B.4.26

Referee: boundary_d. Full source read: `sections/B_origin_fibre_dimension.tex`, 2026-11-15, 20:56 UTC.

**Verdict: PASS at the stated scope.** The conclusion concerns a smooth fourfold completion carrying the fixed holomorphic map to the bidisc and the prescribed partial model over its puncture. It does not assume that an arbitrary analytic cap is a log torsor; the finite multisection is mapped into the already prescribed axial fan models, where the associated log-torus trace is constructed.

## Points checked

1. After the normalized finite base change, the period lattice in the associated log-torus quotient is the constant lattice generated by the two exponentiated nonvanishing periods. Local lifts of a map to a prescribed fan quotient retain precisely this integral-period ambiguity. No group operation on the compact fan model is asserted.
2. A finite normal surface over the smooth bidisc is Cohen–Macaulay, and hence the finite map is flat. Thus its holomorphic trace, constant degree, and multiplicity-weighted sum are legitimate across ordinary branch points. Alternatively the field trace extends holomorphically over the normal base. The source may add the first sentence explicitly; it is not an additional hypothesis.
3. Away from the origin, every remaining branch component is disjoint from either punctured axis after shrinking. A connected normal finite cover of an axis polydisc is consequently the root cover of its normal coordinate. The displayed multiplicative norm of `t^k u` has integral base order `k`, including negative `k`, and a nonzero holomorphic unit factor.
4. The norm preserves the axial boundedness conditions: zero first-character order at the r-axis, with the second unrestricted, and both character orders unrestricted at the Q-axis. A change of lift contributes an integral norm period, which vanishes in the associated quotient. Agreement on the dense ordinary locus extends as an equality of meromorphic characters up to a constant period.
5. The two resulting traces differ by `dF`, not by `F` and not by a fractional multiple. Its r-loop lattice class is `d gamma`, nonzero for every positive degree. B.4.24 therefore excludes every such finite multisection.
6. At a point with local origin-fibre dimension two, two generic linear sections of the normal fourfold isolate the fibre germ. The resulting two-dimensional germ is finite over the smooth two-dimensional base. A horizontal irreducible component has full-dimensional finite image and admits a finite representative over a smaller bidisc; normalization preserves finiteness. This supplies the forbidden multisection without requiring global projectivity or a global multisection theorem.
7. The fibre-dimension lower bound is two. Flatness over r bounds origin components above by three. Exclusion of dimension two thus gives precisely pure dimension three, while retaining the vertical-threefold possibility allowed by the main problem.

No implication is made for a filling whose q-coordinate has not been extended holomorphically. Resolving the graph of a merely meromorphic q-map is a separate geometric operation whose exceptional components cannot be discarded.

## Detailed development record: D_B4427_referee.md

# Independent referee of B.4.27

Referee: boundary_d. Full source read: `sections/B_log_base_modification.tex`, 2026-11-15, approximately 21:04 UTC.

**Verdict: PASS in the stated category of usual-analytic-topology torsors under the specified associated logarithmic torus.**

The bounded character sheaf is correctly computed, rather than replaced by the full logarithmic torus. At the strict R-axis, the first character has zero normal order, because its bounding period is q. The second period q r^12 has positive order there and on every other boundary component. At a crossing, a character with nonzero second coefficient is bounded above and below by suitable signed multiples of that period; if its second coefficient is zero, exactly the first-character support condition remains. This gives the two displayed divisor-unit sheaves, also after blowups at non-toric points of the exceptional boundary.

The exceptional curves form a tree of rational curves. Proper base change computes H2 of the blown-up bidisc by restriction to those curves, with H1, H3, and H4 zero. Higher coherent cohomology vanishes under point blowups; the given local Laurent splitting is consistent with the usual calculation. The exponential sequence therefore identifies Picard with the integral exceptional lattice and kills H2 and H3 of O*. The strict-transform exceptional divisor classes form an integral basis because their intersection matrix is unimodular.

The divisor-order sequence has the direct sum of constant sheaves on individual components, so it introduces no additional graph-cohomology term. Its H1 is zero; its H2 consists exactly of the exceptional-curve summands. Both support divisors contain every exceptional curve, making the order-to-Picard map surjective. Thus each divisor-unit sheaf has H1 zero and H2 equal to one copy of Z per exceptional curve, via the actual order map.

In these coordinates the period map on H2 has, at each exceptional curve, the diagonal block with entries 12m ord(Q) and 12m ord(Q)+12n ord(R). Both are positive integers. Unit factors have no hidden contribution because the H2 order map is an isomorphism. The resulting integral injection gives H1 of the associated logarithmic torus zero by its exact quotient sequence. This includes the unblown bidisc as the zero-rank case.

The final restriction contradiction is valid: a torsor extending to the blown-up base would be trivial there, and hence trivial on the original punctured bidisc, contradicting B.4.24. No claim that an arbitrary proper analytic cap or its flattening supplies such a torsor has been made. Local multisections after flattening are not silently promoted to a global finite multisection over the exceptional tree.

## Detailed development record: D_B4430_equdimensionalization.md

# Proper equidimensionalization in the excess-one bidisc situation

**Status: proved auxiliary lemma for the B.4.30 referee, 15 November 2026.** This file is an independent proof note, not an additional numbered paper result. It replaces the invocation of a general flattening theorem in the preliminary working source. The source need not become flat.

## Precise statement

Let B be a sufficiently small smooth complex bidisc with centre 0. Let f:Z→B be proper and surjective, with Z reduced and pure of dimension four and every irreducible component under consideration dominating B. Suppose every fibre away from 0 is pure of dimension two, and every local fibre dimension at 0 is at most three. Then, after shrinking B, there is a proper modification S→B, which is an isomorphism off 0 and is dominated by a finite sequence of point blow-ups of B, such that the dominating strict transform Z_S→S is proper and all its local fibre dimensions are exactly two.

The conclusion is equidimensionality, not flatness. Every point of S nevertheless has a neighbourhood carrying a positive-degree finite multisection. After normalization, each such multisection is finite and flat over the smooth surface base.

In the intended application, the central r-divisor of a putative filling bounds the local origin fibre dimension by three. The prescribed partial family away from the origin has two-dimensional fibres. Reduction, retention of the dominating component, and proper resolution do not alter the prescribed smooth ordinary locus; the lemma is applied before any subsequent source resolution that might create excess fibres.

## 1. A finite local projection and its actual image

Fix z in the compact origin fibre where the local fibre dimension is three. Choose three sufficiently general holomorphic linear functions p=(p_1,p_2,p_3) in a local embedding of Z. The germ

    (f,p):(Z,z)→(B×C³,(0,0))

is finite. Take finite representatives. Its reduced image V has pure dimension four in the smooth five-dimensional target, and therefore has a local reduced hypersurface equation F(y,x)=0. All local image components are included in this equation; none is added merely to obtain a vanishing function. In a product representative write

    F(y,x)=Σ_β a_β(y)x^β.

Let I⊂O_B be the ideal generated by all coefficient germs a_β. The Noetherian property makes this a finitely generated coherent ideal after shrinking the base. Crucially, the vanishing set of I is supported at 0. Indeed, if every coefficient vanished at some y≠0 sufficiently near 0, then F(y,·) would be identically zero on the vertical polydisc. Thus V_y would contain an open three-dimensional set. The finite, surjective map onto the actual image would then have a three-dimensional fibre over y, contradicting the assumed two-dimensional fibres of f away from 0.

Using an arbitrary equation that merely vanishes on V would not prove this support assertion: such an equation could acquire an extraneous vertical hypersurface. This is why the reduced hypersurface equation of the finite image is chosen.

## 2. Convergence after dividing out the coefficient ideal

There is no formal-convergence gap in the coefficient operation. In the convergent local ring R=O_{B×C³,(0,0)}, the function F lies in I R. To see this, complete R/IR in the ideal (x_1,x_2,x_3). Every coefficient of the image of F is zero, so its image in this completion is zero. The Krull intersection theorem, or faithful flatness of the corresponding completion, makes the original map into the completion injective. Thus F is already zero in R/IR.

Consequently, after a modification on which I is invertible with local generator a, the quotient

    F'=F/a

is a convergent holomorphic function on the pulled-back product. Its Taylor coefficients generate the unit ideal at each base point: the pulled-back coefficient ideal is exactly (a). Therefore the restriction F'_s to any vertical C³ fibre is never identically zero. Its zero set has fibre dimension at most two.

The strict transform of V is contained in {F'=0}. The base change of the original finite map remains finite; its dominating strict transform remains finite onto its image in that strict-transform hypersurface. Hence the dominating strict transform of the chosen source neighbourhood has all fibre dimensions at most two.

## 3. One modification for the compact origin fibre

The locus of points whose local fibre dimension exceeds two is closed. Properness and compactness of the origin fibre let us cover the relevant locus by finitely many finite-projection neighbourhoods as above, producing finitely many coherent ideals I_1,…,I_m. At points whose original fibre dimension is already two, the base-changed fibre cannot increase its dimension: it is a subspace of the original fibre times a fixed base point.

Blow up the product I_1⋯I_m. This modification is supported at 0. Each factor becomes invertible. Indeed, in any local domain, if a product of nonzero ideals is invertible, each factor is invertible as a fractional ideal: if IJ=(a), then I·(J/a)=R. An invertible ideal in a local ring is principal. Thus all the preceding divisions work on the same modification.

Every point of the new origin fibre maps to a point in one of the finitely many original neighbourhoods; the finite local-projection estimates apply there. Resolve and, if necessary, normalize the modified surface base, and dominate it by a finite sequence of point blow-ups of the original smooth bidisc. Such further base change cannot increase a fibre dimension, and passage to a dominating strict transform only takes a closed subspace of a fibre.

The resulting dominating source is pure of dimension four, while the base is smooth of dimension two. The analytic dimension inequality gives local fibre dimension at least two at every point. Combined with the established upper bound, this gives equality everywhere. Properness is preserved under the modifications and strict-transform closure. The entire construction is the identity away from 0.

## 4. Finite multisections without flatness of the source

At any point of an equidimensional fourfold-to-surface map, a sufficiently general local analytic slice of codimension two has an isolated fibre over the given base point. Take a finite representative of a dominating slice component. Its image has dimension two and therefore contains a neighbourhood of the base point; the finite map has positive generic degree.

Normalize this two-dimensional slice. Analytic normalization is finite, so the composite remains a finite surjective map to the smooth surface base. A normal complex surface is Cohen–Macaulay: Serre's S_2 condition is exactly depth two at its two-dimensional local rings. A finite module of dimension two over a regular local ring of dimension two, with depth two, is free by the Auslander–Buchsbaum formula. Thus the normalized finite slice is finite-flat, of constant positive degree.

These are the finite-flat covers used to take traces in B.4.30. Flatness of the full fourfold is neither asserted nor needed. Later source resolutions may introduce excess fibres, but the finite multisections have already been chosen; their proper meromorphic lifts are used on the generic parts of exceptional base curves.

## Primary provenance and exact access record

The proof follows the coefficient-ideal mechanism in Parusiński's original proof of Corollary 1.2, specialized to the excess-one situation where a finite local image is a hypersurface. The compactness/product-ideal and unit-off-origin points are written above for the actual proper bidisc application.

Parusiński, A. (1990). *Subanalytic functions* (MPI/90-58). Max-Planck-Institut für Mathematik. Original full preprint, Corollary 1.2 and proof, printed pp. 2–5 / PDF pp. 4–7:
https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/1622/1/preprint_1990_58.pdf .
Opened and read independently by boundary_d and root. Archive metadata:
https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/1622/ .
The retrieval identifiers for the latest independent reopens are turn678view0 and turn678view1; these are audit identifiers, not bibliographic substitutes.

Published version: Parusiński, A. (1994). Subanalytic functions. *Transactions of the American Mathematical Society, 344*(2), 583–595. https://doi.org/10.2307/2154496 . Published metadata was checked against the author's publication record; the complete original text directly read for the proof was the 1990 preprint.

The inaccessible original Hironaka flattening article is not claimed as opened and is not needed for this lemma.

# Independent full-source referee of the two-section bridge

**Reviewer:** boundary_d. **Date:** 15 November 2026, after checkpoint 09.

**Source reviewed:** sections/B_two_section_bridge_working.md, with the explicit replacements and precisions recorded below.

**Verdict:** PASS for the restricted noncompletion theorem concerning the fixed partial family B.4.19, after replacing the preliminary flattening paragraph by the proved coefficient-ideal equidimensionalization lemma in sections/D_B4430_equdimensionalization.md. This does not prove nonexistence of every r-only filling of the original six-sphere family.

## 1. Analytic log-de Rham input and saturation

I independently opened the complete original Illusie–Kato–Nakayama 2005 paper and its 2007 erratum. The exact applicable result is Theorem 6.3, with Theorem 6.2's hypotheses, on printed pp. 32–34. It concerns proper separated log-smooth fs analytic maps, with log-smooth base or exact map and torsion-free characteristic-group cokernels. A reduced semistable map satisfies the condition because the characteristic homomorphism sends 1 to (1,…,1). No Kähler or projectivity hypothesis occurs. The ordinary relative log-de Rham cohomology is the canonical nilpotent Gauss–Manin extension.

The 2007 erratum explicitly repairs the algebraic Theorem 7.1, not the analytic comparison used here. No Hodge-to-de Rham degeneration is imported.

The low-degree exact sequence

    0→F→H→R¹f_*O_Z

is correct, with F the kernel of the pushforward relative differential on log one-forms. The preceding boundary map vanishes because f_*O_Z=O_base and relative differentials annihilate base functions. Connected reduced compact central fibres imply f_*O_D=O_V; the multiplication-by-t sequence then gives no t-torsion in R¹f_*O_Z. Hence F is t-saturated.

The known Jacobian's normalized F_A is a genuine subbundle of the same canonical extension: subtracting the explicit logarithmic monodromy terms leaves holomorphic bounded unit-period columns and the w,δ columns remain the identity. Since F and F_A agree after inverting t and both are t-saturated, they agree as coherent subsheaves, including with the transverse parameter present. This does not assume local freeness or base change for the central H¹(O).

## 2. Integral residues and an actual convergent primitive

Local semistable angular generators are invariant under monodromy. At an exceptional base divisor with q=t^a times a unit and r=t^b times a unit, a,b>0, the actual H¹ monodromy has rank two and primitive invariant lattice Zw⊕Zδ. Therefore the two normalized angular periods are constant integers, uniformly in the transverse parameter.

The proposed power-series primitive is convergent. In xyz=t, a monomial x^i y^j z^k has relative weight (i−k,j−k). Closedness makes its coefficient vector proportional to that weight. A nonzero integral weight has an entry of absolute value at least one; division cannot create a convergence problem. The zero-weight terms are powers of t. Their coefficients are precisely the angular periods, so all nonconstant resonant terms vanish. Ordinary transverse coordinates are handled first by the holomorphic Poincaré homotopy. Thus the exponentiated primitive is an actual monomial times a holomorphic unit, not a formal or merely fibrewise construction.

## 3. A finite chart chain compares two extending sections

A compact connected NC central fibre is connected through finitely many semistable charts. Every nonempty open overlap meeting that fibre contains a smooth central point; a transverse holomorphic section through that point exists. These auxiliary sections and the finite chain persist after shrinking the transverse parameter disc. No extra root is required for honest sections at this stage.

For each section, the pulled-back product of the coordinate branches is t. Hence each coordinate is a power of t times a unit. Ratios of the local exponentiated primitives are therefore genuine logarithmic characters. Multiplication along the chart chain gives the difference of the two sections in the specified Jacobian, modulo its actual integral periods. Full positive monodromy valuations make every resulting integral ghost bounded. The construction is holomorphic jointly in both base variables.

This comparison is unaffected by a common essential affine translation. It does not contradict B.4.29, whose reference origin does not extend in the changed marking.

## 4. Equidimensionalization, finite traces, and uniform generic reduction

The full proof no longer needs a general flattening theorem. The separate auxiliary note proves the exact replacement:

* finite local projection of every excess-one point gives the actual reduced hypersurface image;
* its Taylor-coefficient ideal is a unit off the origin, because an identically zero noncentral fibre equation would force a three-dimensional fibre;
* a finite product of those ideals, using compactness of the origin fibre, is principalized by one proper base modification;
* division is convergent, and the strict transform has fibre dimension at most two;
* the dominant pure four-dimensional source over a smooth surface has fibre dimension at least two;
* normalized finite local slices are Cohen–Macaulay surfaces, hence finite-flat over the smooth base.

This yields all finite positive-degree traces used later without asserting flatness of the whole source.

For the subsequent generic semistable reduction, the strata must be the **closed intersections of globally marked SNC divisors**, not nonproper open strata. Resolve to simple NC first. Each closed stratum is proper over the compact exceptional base curve. Its nondominating image and the image of its critical locus are finite after discarding the curves that do not dominate. Removing these finitely many values gives simultaneous coordinates

    t=unit·∏x_i^(m_i),  u=u,  k≤3.

A common root degree divisible by the finitely many m_i gives toric height-one vertices. Use successive star subdivisions at all height-one lattice points in one global order on the finite marked cone complex. Shared faces then receive exactly the same subdivision, including any quadrilateral diagonal choices; independently triangulating different polygons without this common ordering would not suffice. All remaining two-dimensional lattice triangles are empty and hence unimodular. The monomial blow-up ideals are invariant under the unit changes of defining equations and glue. This gives proper smooth semistable local models uniformly in u, preserving the ordinary locus.

The normalized finite covers split after a cyclic root at a generic point of each exceptional curve. There are finitely many degrees and ramification indices, so a common finite root/norm multiplier can be chosen. Proper lifts of their sections through source modifications are meromorphic on smooth surface bases and are holomorphic away from isolated points; only finitely many such points meet the compact exceptional curves under consideration.

The explicit cyclic norm of τ^k times a unit is (−1)^(k(e−1))t^k times a unit. Changing a lift by a period changes its norm by an integral period. Thus norm descent is an actual character calculation and does not silently presume Kummer descent of the quotient sheaf.

## 5. Hartogs, the period ambiguity, and the multiplied cocycle

For a punctured smooth bidisc, H¹ with coefficients in the constant lattice Y=Z² vanishes. Thus an A-section lifts to the exact bounded character sheaf. Divide each character by its fixed boundary monomial; the resulting function and its inverse both extend by Hartogs and remain reciprocal. The first character retains zero order on the strict r-boundary, as required by B.4.27.

Uniqueness is correctly proved after retaining the Y ambiguity: the difference of two character lifts is one constant integral period on the connected puncture. Subtract it and apply the identity theorem. Consequently all cocycle identities extend.

After a common degree and norm multiplier, finite multisection traces provide local origins of a positive contracted power of the actual punctured torsor. Their differences extend generically along every exceptional curve and then across the remaining finite set by Hartogs. These differences glue an A-torsor over the entire modified base. B.4.27 makes it trivial, whereas B.4.24 excludes every positive multiple of the actual r-loop γ class. This is the required contradiction.

## 6. Exact scope

The conclusion applies to the fixed partial family B.4.19, including its prescribed rank-one axial completion. It allows proper origin fibres of dimension three and arbitrary finite point modifications of the base; the coefficient-ideal argument then manufactures an equidimensional dominating strict transform.

An arbitrary r-only filling can change the r=0, q≠0 model after resolving the graph of the intrinsic q-map. Rank-one monodromy has an additional elliptic invariant direction, so the local argument alone does not force the residues needed on that axis. A separate global-monodromy argument is being investigated; it is not part of this PASS.

## Primary records independently opened

Illusie, L., Kato, K., & Nakayama, C. (2005). Quasi-unipotent logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert correspondences. *Journal of Mathematical Sciences, the University of Tokyo, 12*, 1–66. Theorems 6.2–6.3, printed pp. 32–34. https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms120101.pdf . Opened; latest independent retrieval turn678view2.

Illusie, L., Kato, K., & Nakayama, C. (2007). Erratum to “Quasi-unipotent logarithmic Riemann–Hilbert correspondences.” *Journal of Mathematical Sciences, the University of Tokyo, 14*, 113–116. https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms140105.pdf . Opened in full; latest retrieval turn678view3. Scope is the correction to Theorem 7.1.

Parusiński, A. (1990). *Subanalytic functions* (MPI/90-58). Max-Planck-Institut für Mathematik. Corollary 1.2 and full proof, original PDF pp. 4–7. https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/1622/1/preprint_1990_58.pdf . Opened and independently read, latest retrieval turn678view0. Published metadata: *Transactions of the American Mathematical Society, 344*(2), 583–595 (1994), DOI 10.2307/2154496.

Enokizono–Hashizume's analytic toroidal-modification Lemma 2.14 was independently opened by E and root; the present review checks the finite star-subdivision construction directly. Their projective main theorem is not used. The inaccessible original Hironaka flattening article is not claimed as read and is no longer a necessary dependency.

# Independent referee: global central meridians

Reviewer: boundary_d. Date: 15 November 2026.

Source: sections/ab_push_global_meridians.tex.

**Verdict: full-source PASS at the stated scope.**

The graph modification and resolution can be supported on r=0 because the marked map is already holomorphic and the total space smooth on the punctured parameter family. Central multiplicities after resolution are explicitly retained.

The finite exceptional set is obtained from proper maps of the closed normalized branch-intersection spaces. This is the necessary distinction from nonproper open strata. Nonconstant maps from these smooth compact spaces to P1 have a proper analytic critical-value subset; constant components and deeper zero-dimensional strata contribute finitely many further values. Proper submersions of the double-branch surfaces give finite local systems of connected compact curve components.

On a double stratum, the displayed primitive angular circle for r=x^a y^b is correct. Moving its base point within a connected curve with finitely many triple points removed transports the actual normal circle. Normal-frame changes can rotate its parameter, and ordered-branch reversal can reverse its orientation, but neither operation adds a tangential H1 cycle. Based-path changes only conjugate the loop. The finitely many monomial-fibre components account for the remaining ambiguity.

There is therefore a finite set of actual H1 classes, not merely abstract stratum labels, permuted by q-monodromy. Compact generator paths give a common small nonzero r on which all the transports take place. The analogous finite local r-orbit is sufficient: for the actual unipotent T_r=I−mE, a periodic vector satisfies E v=0, since the lattice is torsion-free. No global reduced semistable model or additional r-root is assumed.

On ker E/Zδ, the matrices are exactly

    M1 = [[0,1],[-1,-1]],  M2 = [[0,-1],[1,0]].

Their product and its conjugate have distinct rational fixed lines. A finite orbit is fixed by positive powers of both unipotents, so its vector is zero. The primitive integral δ line then gives the claimed integral, rather than merely rational, conclusion.

At a higher crossing, the rational angular kernel is generated by the pairwise angular vectors. Each is represented by an actual open double-stratum circle after keeping the other branch coordinates small and nonzero. The q-coordinate is retained because it is a submersion on the closed triple stratum away from the deleted values. This proves the same conclusion for the whole local angular torus. Ordered branch spaces correctly handle self-crossing.

The final scope limitation is necessary: the result constrains vanishing periods and hence differences of extending sections, but does not identify an affine origin in an arbitrary cap with the chosen origin of B.4.19. A common nonextending translation cancels from all pairwise differences. No unrestricted Part B noncompletion follows from this proposition alone.

# Independent full-source referee: arbitrary proper noncompletion

Reviewer: boundary_d. Date: 15 November 2026.

Source reviewed in full: sections/B_global_noncompletion.tex, reserved B.4.33.

**Verdict: PASS.** The argument excludes any proper Hausdorff complex-analytic filling of the actual punctured six-sphere family after a finite parameter cover. It no longer assumes the unknown r-axis completion is B.4.19. The crucial added input is the zero global radial-loop class in B.4.32, and only its invariant γ evaluation is used in the final comparison.

## 1. Arbitrary analytic source and the intrinsic marked map

The smooth connected punctured family is irreducible, so its closure gives the unique reduced main component. Normalization and resolution are proper and can be chosen to preserve that smooth punctured locus. The resulting smooth fourfold has nonzero parameter function; its map to the disc is flat without a reducedness assumption on the central divisor.

B.4.4 applies in exactly this scope. Its relative bi-anticanonical bundle exists on the smooth fourfold, its proper direct image is torsion-free of rank two, and the three labelled critical values give the meromorphic target normalization. Resolving the marked graph therefore gives the actual q-map, not a freely chosen or essentially varying projective coordinate. Further resolution retains central multiplicities.

The finite parameter cover convention is valid. An arbitrary positive power N is dominated by w=R^(6N), which is the B.4.19 parameter r followed by r=R^N. A general finite disc-germ map becomes a coordinate power after absorbing its nonvanishing unit.

## 2. Excess fibres and finite traces

There are finitely many central divisor components. A nonconstant holomorphic map from any irreducible three-dimensional component to the marked curve has level sets of pure dimension two. Thus three-dimensional fibres occur only at the finitely many constant component images. The cusp disc can be shrunk to retain only its own centre among them and among the additional finite values of B.4.31.

The coefficient-ideal equidimensionalization from B.4.30 is applicable: the dominant total space is pure four-dimensional and the possible origin fibre has dimension at most three because it lies in the nonzero-parameter divisor. No projective flattening theorem is required. Finite base changes and normalizations do not increase fibre dimensions. The resulting normalized finite slice surfaces are Cohen–Macaulay and finite-flat over the smooth modified base, so their positive degrees and traces are legitimate.

The finite cover of a neighbourhood of the compact inverse image K of the origin can cover the entire modified base after shrinking the original bidisc. This follows from properness of the base modification: the image of the closed complement of that neighbourhood avoids the origin. It justifies one finite collection of trace degrees and norm multipliers. When no blow-up is required, K is a point and the same argument applies.

A trace of points in a torsor is intrinsically a section of the corresponding contracted power. It does not require selecting a global origin. The ordinary trace section is holomorphic even at ramification, because locally the symmetric sum is invariant under branch permutations. The proof uses this standard finite-flat construction only on the ordinary group locus.

## 3. Every generic divisor gives the correct bounded differences

At an exceptional base component both Q and R have positive order, so the actual monodromy is full rank two with primitive invariant lattice Zw⊕Zδ. The B.4.30 saturation and explicit monomial primitive apply.

At the strict Q-axis the monodromy is likewise full rank two, and the underlying r≠0 family is the known actual cusp. The normalized q-root may introduce toric modifications, but these preserve the period pairing and the section σ through the central toric open orbit.

At the strict R-axis the proof does not falsely replace a rank-three invariant kernel by rank two. B.4.31 proves that the actual local angular images are all integral multiples of δ. The first normalized form consequently has zero residue, and the second has integral residue. The exponentiated primitives lie exactly in O*×M_R^gp. In particular the first character is a unit across R=0, with zero radial winding, rather than a general logarithmic character.

New angular cycles after the local root and toric refinement have rational images generated by the old angular cycles; integral images in the primitive δ line are integral multiples of δ. Thus this residue constraint survives the reductions.

The finite chart-chain comparison connects arbitrary extending sections because the proper normal map to the normal surface base has trivial finite Stein factor: its generic torus fibres are connected. This remains true after retaining the normalized main component under a root. The common root/norm argument is local in the coordinate along a boundary curve and retains that coordinate holomorphically. It does not use a global nonprojective semistable-reduction theorem.

## 4. The derived torsor, rather than an assumed cap identification

After a common positive norm multiplier, all trace differences extend on the generic parts of the boundary. The character sheaf is precisely

    M_(D_Q+E)^gp × M_(D_Q+D_R+E)^gp.

The Hartogs proof retains the constant period lattice Y. On a punctured bidisc H¹(Y)=0 gives a character lift. Dividing by the fixed boundary monomials produces a holomorphic unit and its inverse; both extend. This preserves the forbidden first-character order along D_R. Uniqueness modulo one constant period preserves the cocycle identities.

The resulting torsor Q is defined on all of the modified base S and its ordinary restriction is the actual contracted power P^D by the explicit trace identifications. This construction never identifies its unknown R-axis model with the rotating model. B.4.27 gives H¹(S,A_S)=0 and hence a global section h.

This is the point at which the earlier affine-origin warning is genuinely resolved: not by claiming that an old origin extends, but by constructing a new origin and controlling only its radial loop.

## 5. The two loop evaluations are independent and correctly normalized

Near a generic strict R point, h differs from a multiplied cap trace by an A_S-section. A local character lift has first component a unit and second component R^k times a unit, so this difference changes the radial label only by δ. B.4.32 then gives ν_R(h−D o_ord)=0.

The rotating origin and the ordinary origin are not literally identical. The source correctly inserts the exact B.4.19.5 comparison G(s)+6vδ, whose radial contribution is purely δ. This transfers zero γ evaluation to h−D o, with o the rotating origin. Only after this step is the exact relation σ−o=F used.

On the strict Q-axis, σ is an actual extending section of the known cusp, including its normalized root fan. Its differences with cap traces extend by the same two-section argument, with the finite norm multipliers incorporated in D. Hence Dσ is a section of Q on B_r and h−Dσ is an actual A(B_r)-section.

The Q-ghost map sends u to (12m,0) and γ̂ to (0,12m). It is injective on the period lattice. The B.4.24 cocycle argument therefore kills the radial Y-connecting class of h−Dσ. Passing from the multiplicative period lattice to full H1 can add w and δ windings, but both are annihilated by the γ covector. Thus the asserted second zero is exactly what follows and exactly what is needed.

## 6. The contradiction and its scope

The exact ordinary identity is

    (h−D o) − (h−Dσ) = D F.

The positive R-loop corresponds to v↦v+n and the actual explicit difference is

    F(s,v+n)−F(s,v)=nγ̂−6n(n−1)δ

as an additive period label at the initial parameter. Its γ evaluation is n>0. Both terms on the left have zero γ radial evaluation, while the right has Dn>0. Every root, trace degree and norm multiplier is positive, so no auxiliary operation kills this integer.

The proof allows arbitrary additional central components and multiplicities and assumes neither Kählerness nor a log structure on the unknown cap. It derives the associated log torsor through extendible pairwise differences and uses global monodromy only for the affine loop class. Essential translations with zero loop defect remain allowed and do not affect the contradiction.

No assertion about an unknown central fibre's integral topology is inferred. If B.4.33 is accepted and integrated, the requested central-topology computation for an actual proper filling is vacuous because such a filling does not exist.

## Related independent records

The exact equidimensionalization and B.4.30 referee are sections/D_B4430_equdimensionalization.md and sections/D_B4430_referee.md. The global meridian referee is sections/D_B4431_referee.md. B.4.32 is proved in sections/B_global_loop_defect.tex, with an independent AB referee and an independent E referee, and exact checks in code/d_push_global_loop_defect.py/.out/.json.

# Stress test against the proper untwisted Engel filling

**Purpose:** root requested an explicit comparison with the known proper untwisted filling in Part A, to test B.4.33 against an actual positive construction. This note records the check; it does not introduce another theorem or numerical certificate.

**Conclusion:** the linear monodromy and the trace/Hartogs arguments are compatible with the untwisted filling. The nonzero affine radial clutching class used in the last step of B.4.33 is absent for that family.

## 1. The ordinary period matrices coincide

Use the R8 comparison and the common parameter cover u=r^12, c=−12v with v=(2πi)^−1 log r. On the ordinary marked base the same Jacobian period matrix is

    Π_c = [[6μ, τ, 1, 0],
           [β_part+c, μ, 0, 1]].

The finite logarithmic transformations change affine gluing and the fillings at the finite places; they do not change this ordinary linear period family. In particular the homology matrices A1,A2 in B.4.31 are the same, and their quotient matrices on Λ_Q/Qδ remain

    Ā1=[[1,0,0],[6,0,1],[−6,−1,−1]],
    Ā2=[[1,0,0],[0,0,−1],[−6,1,0]].

Their common invariant space is zero in both calculations. Thus the distinction is not that the fixed-space computation fails for the untwisted family.

## 2. The actual parameter closings are different affine maps

The genuine untwisted Engel family is constructed by varying the scalar linearization u itself. Around u=r^12, the additive parameter changes c↦c−12 and the period lattice is the same, since its γ column changes by −12δ. The actual closing on the additive universal cover is the identity:

    b_untwisted=0.

In homogeneous affine Lie coordinates (ζ1,ζ2,1), its closing matrix is I3. Its lattice basis still has the unipotent return I−12δ⊗γ; identity on the Lie space must not be confused with identity on the marked homology.

For the twisted six-sphere family, C.3.5 and B.4.19 give instead

    b_twisted = Π_c γ̂ − (k+6)e ≡ −k e modulo periods,
    e=(0,1),   k=(2πi)^−1 log K,
    K(x)=x^4/(x−1)^3.

Its scalar affine representative has homogeneous matrix

    [[1,0,0],[0,1,−k],[0,0,1]].

This is the actual difference in the parameter family, not a change in a matrix convention.

The finite places explain why the scalar K cannot simply be discarded. The twisted root-to-ordinary connector is σ_j,c=w_jΠ_c v_j, and its c-shift contributes −12ε_j w_j e. An identity comparison on the ordinary core would require the opposite logarithmic correction on the smooth root torus family. Its nonzero δ winding around the root-disc centre cannot extend as a comparison of smooth torus families over that contractible disc. The finite scalar representatives K s_1^(−12) and K s_2^12 are precisely the units that cancel this defect in the actual coherent closing. The untwisted family has no such logarithmic-transform connectors; its identity closing is the one in its direct u-linearization construction.

## 3. The local logarithmic Jacobian is the same, but its affine torsor is not

Both ordinary cusp Jacobians have the multiplicative period pairing

    [[q a0, a1^6],
     [a1^(−1), q r^12 a2]],

with the same holomorphic units. The plain two-parameter fan in Part A is a model of this group family. On both punctured axes the identity (Z,W)=(1,1) is an extending origin. The identity cocharacter lies in the relevant base-face cones, so this statement is also visible directly in the displayed toric charts.

For this untwisted family the actual cusp origin can be taken to be

    σ_untwisted=0,

and the transition between the two axial identity origins is

    F_untwisted=0.

It has zero γ radial-loop evaluation. Fan subdivisions may change the proper model but do not change this associated group origin or period pairing.

For the twisted family, the cusp unit representative of the actual closing is

    b_twisted=(6μ, C−12v−12κ0−6).

It is the exact v-difference of

    σ_twisted=(6μv, −6v²+v(C−12κ0)).

The rotating r-axis origin and this actual cusp origin differ by the explicit F in B.4.24, whose positive radial difference is γ̂ (up to δ). This nonzero affine class is the indispensable final premise of B.4.33.

## 4. Consequence for the proof audit

Applying the cap-trace construction to the known untwisted proper model gives an associated A-torsor with a global origin, consistently with H¹(S,A)=0. Its global normalized loop defect is zero, consistently with the same fixed-space calculation. Both axial comparisons then have γ radial value zero. The last equation is 0=0, not 0=Dn.

The positive Part A filling therefore does not violate any reduction, saturation, primitive, trace, norm, or Hartogs step. It fails the specific nonzero actual affine-clutching premise (B.4.33.14), because its affine closing is the identity. No general noncompletion theorem for the untwisted family, for arbitrary two-parameter torus families, or for all logarithmic-torus models is being claimed.
