# Independent referee of B.4.7

Referee: boundary_d. Date: 15 November 2026. Status: PASS at the stated parabolic-model scope.

The Cartier logarithm cocycle is single-valued modulo the elliptic lattice and satisfies the triple relation. Its Chern vector is `(1+i)L−iA`, giving restrictions `1,i` on the two central projective lines and `1+i` on the generic line. The total space is an actual proper principal elliptic bundle on the blown-up base, not a formal gluing of central surfaces. Local triviality above the base node gives precisely `xy=u`, so smoothness, flatness, properness and semistability follow in the analytic category without a Kähler assumption.

For a primitive vector c the quotient of O(1)^× by the kernel of `c log(z)/(2πi)` is correctly identified with a scalar Hopf quotient after fibrewise inversion to O(−1)^×. The choices `(1,i)`, `(i,−1)`, `(1+i,i)` yield the claimed multipliers `exp(−2π), exp(−2π), −exp(−π)`. The exceptional divisor line has its canonical trivialization on the punctured base, so the claimed punctured product is explicit.

The fibre inclusion on fundamental groups is the quotient of Z² by the Chern vector. Thus the two central inclusions have independent kernels, van Kampen gives trivial central fundamental group, and the normalization Mayer–Vietoris map in degree one is the displayed unimodular matrix. The stated integral homology follows, including rank three in degree three. The coherent degree-one restrictions are isomorphisms because translations act trivially on H1(E,O), giving the claimed one-dimensional central H1(O). Both normal lines are genuinely trivial, not merely degree zero.

The nonextension of a proper meromorphic comparison follows from the actual total-space fundamental groups, using the semistable retraction and proper-modification invariance already proved elsewhere. It does not assume that an arbitrary punctured analytic comparison automatically extends meromorphically. The extra comparison that the two scalar Hopf surfaces are not bimeromorphic is also valid: lifts extend by Hartogs, their derivatives cannot intertwine the two scalar generators, and both surfaces contain no rational curves.

Finally, the generic simplicity calculation was checked independently. The coefficient of beta in the determinant is `(Ab−aB)tau+(Ac−aC)`. If it vanishes with one of a,A nonzero, proportionality of the first three rational coordinates reduces the determinant to the remaining fourth-coordinate minor. If a=A=0, the coefficients of `1,tau,mu` are exactly the three remaining minors. Rational independence excludes an identically zero determinant for any independent integral pair. Countability then gives simplicity for all but countably many beta values at the chosen ordinary base point.

This is a concrete parabolic analytic degeneration and a counterexample to an automatic meromorphic-comparison inference. Its generic fundamental group and Euler number explicitly prevent its being identified with the requested S6 filling. The normal-degree and generic-simplicity limitations on insertion into the actual cusp are accurately retained.

# Independent referee of B.4.6

Date: 2026-11-15. Source: `sections/ab_push_kato_caps.tex`. **Verdict: passes with exactly its stated cap and overlap restrictions.**

The grouped closed-cover resolution is valid locally: each block ideal is a product of a disjoint set of normal-crossing coordinate factors. On each monomial the alternating complex is the augmented simplex on those block ideals which do not kill the monomial. A coefficientwise contraction with coefficients zero or plus/minus one preserves convergence, so the argument is valid for convergent analytic power series as well as polynomials. The same stalk argument works for constants. All compact reduced intersections have only constant holomorphic functions on connected components.

The spectral-sequence comparison correctly uses surjectivity on block degree-one cohomology and injectivity on pairwise overlaps. These imply surjectivity on the E2 kernel in bidegree (0,1); the isomorphism in bidegree (2,0) then preserves d2-closedness. The five-term sequences finish the argument. The grouped horizontal pinching is necessary and is correctly used: the conductor itself would not have the required injective comparison, whereas 1−sigma* is invertible on its degree-one groups and the pinched block's surviving class comes from the identical constant-function difference map on both sides.

I independently opened the published primary PDF of Istrati, Otiman, Pontecorvo, and Ruggiero (2022), *Toric Kato manifolds*, Journal de l'École polytechnique — Mathématiques 9, 1347–1395, DOI 10.5802/jep.208, <https://www.numdam.org/item/JEP_2022__9__1347_0.pdf>. Proposition 8.4 and Theorem 8.5 supply h10=0 and h01=1 in the asserted hyperbolic case. The proof of Theorem 8.8 on page 1380 explicitly identifies the normalized invariant divisor components as compact toric varieties or hyperbolic toric Kato manifolds. Theorem 5.2 gives rational invariant curves in that case. These support precisely the normalized-overlap calculation in the manuscript.

The exact sequence from locally exact holomorphic one-forms makes the comparison H1(C)→H1(O) injective when there are no global holomorphic one-forms; combined with the source dimensions it is an isomorphism for the hyperbolic Kato blocks and surfaces. The ordered branch resolution then gives the required injectivity for their specified overlaps. No assumption that a component's fundamental-group generator survives in the union is made, and no parabolic or unrestricted cusp case is claimed.

# Independent referee of C.3.14

Date: 2026-11-15. Referee: boundary_d. Source reviewed: `sections/C_characteristic_cap.tex`, as present during the draft11 build. **Verdict: the proposition is proved in its stated scope.** In particular, it supplies a specified characteristic-number target for the actual six-loop monodromy; it does not evaluate that number or resolve the comparison of orientation conventions.

## Actual cap comparison

The equivariant linearization uses the known smooth orbit pair, the trivial scalar normal rank-two complex bundle over the fixed sphere, and classification of the free principal circle bundle by its signed primitive Euler class. The boundary gauge has a real logarithm because the common boundary has vanishing first integral cohomology; its extension with a collar cutoff makes the two isomorphisms agree. This is sufficient and does not use a false general assertion that arbitrary orbit diffeomorphisms lift smoothly at fixed points.

The cap is the surgery manifold of the actual orbit knot. Each of the two orbit six-discs, with its standard fixed three-disc neighbourhood removed, gives `S2 × D4` with distinct inner and outer `S2 × D3` faces. Gluing the outer faces by the actual relative diffeomorphism gives the knot exterior. Surgery attaches a third such piece along its inner faces. Combining that third piece with either original piece along the inner product face yields another `S2 × D4`; the remaining gluing is the extension of the actual relative diffeomorphism by the identity on the other half. Thus the displayed double is the required cap with a specified framing, rather than an abstract six-manifold with matching homology.

The Mayer–Vietoris, van Kampen, spin, and twisted spin-index arguments are valid. In particular, restriction in degree two is injective over both the integers and the field with two elements. The characteristic integer is

\[
J=(\langle p_1x,[M]\rangle-4\langle x^3,[M]\rangle)/24,
\]

and integrality follows directly from the index theorem, independently of six-manifold classification.

## Primary-source formula check

Wall's original Theorem 4 on page 360 was inspected visually, not merely through OCR. It states

\[
\mu(e_i,e_i,e_i)=6\varphi_i+\beta'_i,
\qquad p_1(e_i)=4\beta'_i.
\]

The proof on page 361 identifies the stable **tangent** bundle class and explains the signs. Therefore the subtraction defining `J` gives `J = −φ` exactly in that convention. The 1967 corrigendum changes Theorem 14, not Theorem 4. The source's retained fixed sign for comparison with the Davis/Eells–Kuiper generator is appropriate; this referee has not computed that sign.

* Wall, C. T. C. (1966). Classification problems in differential topology. V. On certain 6-manifolds. *Inventiones Mathematicae, 1*, 355–374. Original PDF: <https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/wclass5.pdf>.
* Wall, C. T. C. (1967). On certain 6-manifolds: Corrigendum. *Inventiones Mathematicae, 2*, 306. Primary PDF: <https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/BF01425407.pdf>.
* Local visual audit: `external_sources/C_independent_primary/Wall1966V_ocr/page_06.png` and `page_07.png` (original pages 360–361); the text was compared with the image because the OCR corrupts several symbols.

## Framing and scope

The pure framing generator is represented on the relative orbit complement by a map to `SO(3)`. Its lift to `SU(2)` exists on the three-disc with prescribed identity near the boundary. Left quaternionic multiplication then gives an equivariant lift commuting with the right complex circle and extending over the seven-disc. Thus that direction maps to zero in `Theta_7`, as asserted.

The formula relating the actual six-loop clutching sphere to `J` is consequently valid with the stated fixed orientation sign. Nothing in this verification computes `J`, `k`, or a numerical monodromy class. The classical target-knot self-linking 12 and local normal Euler value −2 remain insufficient to do so without the actual global gluing calculation.

One optional terminology clarification, requiring no mathematical correction: the phrase “first Pontryagin number is six” in the discussion of the Milnor sphere refers to the evaluation of the associated oriented rank-four bundle's first Pontryagin class on `S4`, not to a Pontryagin number of a seven-manifold.

# Independent referee of C.3.20

Date: 2026-11-15. Source: `sections/C_cubic_transgression.tex`. **Verdict: passes at its stated nonnumerical relative-square scope.**

The marked identity section in C.3.17 avoids the fixed sphere. Its product circle coordinate therefore sends that compact sphere into a proper arc, which can be compressed inside the cusp while leaving the boundary marking fixed. A suitably rotated page then avoids the arc at all twelve critical-knot intersections. This produces the asserted actual four-manifold inside the free complement P.

The primitive B with dB=E squared exists because H4(P)=0 and can be made zero near the boundary because E squared is zero there and H3(boundary P)=0. The boundary-trivialization ambiguity is in the connecting image of H1(boundary M), which lies in the radical of the relative intersection form; hence the relative Euler square does not depend on that gauge.

The one-form eta patches as claimed. Its outer closed class is the actual linking generator from C.3.16, and its inner class extends over the actual product of C.3.17. Exact collar corrections and a correction near boundary P are permitted. The mapping-cylinder curvature is closed and its endpoint discrepancy can be corrected because H1(DP)=0. The cut-and-reglue trace uses the same bundle lift on all faces, which is the needed condition for the characteristic bordism.

The cubic expansion has coefficient three. Both E wedge Delta and Delta squared vanish on the support of Delta. Stokes reduces the integral to three times Delta wedge B. In the displayed orbit orientation dg du dw dr da, the permutation dr da dg du dw is even; since chi decreases from one to zero, the result is minus the gamma-u-w period of B, which equals s_delta under the stated boundary convention.

The closed three-form alpha wedge E minus B has normalized integral one on the normal Hopf three-sphere and evaluates on the actual marked delta=0 lift as the same relative square. This establishes an actual linking coefficient, not a numerical assignment from an auxiliary surface.

# Independent referee: C.3.21, actual delta-line square

**Result:** pass, with a suggested expansion of the final local Weierstrass checks. Referee: boundary_d. The proof concerns the actual line on the actual Lefschetz four-manifold, not the auxiliary normalization of Part B.

## Actual geometric comparison

The weighted-rescaling formulas identify the actual page interior with `b^3=a^4+1` and its marked map with `t_epsilon=(a^4+1)/(1+epsilon a^4)`. The change from the cover ramified over the original cusp to the cover with twelve unramified cusp preimages is retained explicitly. The added point is over the ordinary value `1/epsilon`, not over the cusp. The genus-three compactification has one point at infinity.

Keeping the cusp product modification inside `r0<epsilon` makes its intersection with the page exactly twelve nodal neighbourhoods. The remainder is the original marked torus quotient. On that remainder the explicit eta coordinate gives the varying Picard section P, including its mixed base--fibre class. A smooth translation origin exists because the punctured page retracts onto a graph. The argument uses smooth dependence in the base and fibrewise holomorphic lines; it does not assert holomorphic dependence of the origin or of the affine torsor.

## Excision and extension

For a full I1 neighbourhood E, Poincare--Lefschetz duality gives `H^2(E,boundary E;Z)=H_2(E;Z)=Z`. The primitive generator is the class of the reduced irreducible fibre. Its image in the page surface is the pullback of a point on a surface with boundary, hence zero. Therefore restriction of absolute H2 cohomology to the untouched exterior is injective. This excludes an additional absolute Chern class in the twelve nodal clutching regions.

The remaining regular boundary has an actual trivial marked line. It can be capped with the product extension whose Picard section is P near the added point. Boundary gauge changes lie in the radical of the relative intersection pairing, as proved in C.3.20, so this choice cannot alter the square. No global diffeomorphism of the actual surface with its Jacobian is assumed.

## Integral Leray comparison

For both surfaces the local invariant-cycle calculation gives `R^1 pi_* Z=j_* Lambda`, `R^2 pi_* Z=Z`, with the same integral fibre intersection pairing. The filtration-one mixed term is `E_infty^(1,1)=H^1(C,j_*Lambda)`: there is no possible incoming or outgoing differential in this bidegree. The map to the smooth-locus group is injective by the low-degree Leray sequence for the open immersion j. Thus the exterior Picard calculation identifies the actual mixed class with the Jacobian Poincare mixed class, up to dualization.

In total degree four the only nonzero graded term is `E_infty^(2,2)=H^2(C,Z)=Z`. Multiplicativity identifies the square with the cup square of the mixed term, using the fibre pairing. Filtration-two base twists contribute no square or cross term, because filtration three is zero on this base. Integral differentials elsewhere in Leray do not affect this argument. Dualization changes the sign of the mixed class and preserves its square.

## Explicit final local checks

The original model, obtained directly by reversing the substitutions in B.3.4, is

`y^2=x^3-3 t^3(t-1)x+2 t^4(t-1)^2`,

with `P=(0,-sqrt(2)t^2(t-1))` and discriminant `1728 t^8(t-1)^3`.

* At `t=s^3`, use `x=s^4 X`, `y=s^6 Y`. The new fibre is good; P is `(0,-sqrt(2)(s^3-1))` and is away from O.
* At `t=1+s^4`, use `x=s^2 X`, `y=s^3 Y`. The new fibre is good; P is `(0,-sqrt(2)s(1+s^4)^2)` and is away from O, including its limit `(0,0)` on the smooth fibre `Y^2=X^3-3X`.
* At `q=1/t`, use `x0=q^2 x`, `y0=q^3 y`. The I1 model has P `(0,-sqrt(2)(1-q))`, which limits to a smooth nonidentity point of the nodal cubic. It is disjoint from O.

The units in the actual `t_epsilon` cover preserve these local conclusions. The added ramification point lies over a good fibre, so introduces no new intersection. All twelve bad fibres of the minimal Jacobian are I1, and the two finite bad fibres become good. Consequently its Euler number is 12, its holomorphic Euler characteristic is one, and P and O are disjoint sections of square minus one. Thus `(P-O)^2=-2`, with no component correction. The actual relative Euler square is therefore minus two.

## Scope

This audit validates C.3.21. It does not by itself identify a one-loop monodromy class. Combining it with the independently calibrated common-sign lemma C.3.22 gives the six-loop cap integer zero. Passing from that six-loop integer to the original one-loop mapping class still requires the integer-level additivity and relative-normalization argument.

Primary height/intersection reference used in the containing manuscript is Shioda (1990), Theorems 8.4 and 8.6; the local disjointness checks above avoid inferring the needed intersections from an auxiliary numerical height alone.

# Independent referee: C.3.23, integer additivity

**Result:** pass. Referee: boundary_d. This argument closes the gap between the integer invariant of the six-loop cap and the original one-loop ordinary mapping class. It does not infer the latter from an equation only in the group of order 28.

## Normalization

An equivariant normal derivative for the fixed scalar circle representation on C2 is complex linear. Its real determinant is positive. Thus an orientation-preserving equivariant map of the six-sphere restricts to an orientation-preserving map on its fixed S2. Smale's connectedness result permits removal of that restriction by an equivariant tubular isotopy.

Once the fixed sphere is pointwise fixed, the normal derivative is a map from S2 to GL2(C). Polar decomposition and pi2(U2)=0 give a homotopy to the identity. It is realized by local equivariant vector fields and an invariant cutoff. The remaining germ can be rescaled to its derivative; the tangent-normal mixed derivative vanishes by the absence of equivariant linear maps between the trivial and scalar representations. The rescaled germ is smooth, and the resulting local isotopy extends equivariantly. Consequently every component has a representative equal to the identity on a fixed tube.

## Independence

Equivariantly isotopic clutching maps give equivariantly diffeomorphic clutched pairs. Variable unitary normal tubes along the isotopy supply compatible smooth Hopf charts. This uses an adapted smoothing near the fixed set, rather than the false assertion that every equivariant map induces a smooth map in a preassigned raw quotient atlas. Equivalently, interpolate the normal metrics and use positive linear gauge changes near the fixed set with a tubular cutoff; the resulting comparison is unitary between the chosen endpoint metrics.

The ambiguity in identifying an orbit homotopy six-sphere with S6 does not alter the knot integer. An orientation-preserving diffeomorphism can be isotoped to fix the germ of a point. Its compact support in the complementary stereographic chart can then be conjugated, through diffeomorphisms isotopic to the identity, into a ball disjoint from the specified knot. The representative fixes that knot, while the isotopy gives the required ambient isotopy of its images.

## Addition

After normalization, choose a fixed point outside the supports. The stereographic action is scalar on the C2 summand of R2 plus C2. Positive dilations and translations in the fixed R2 commute with the circle and extend smoothly to the one-point compactification. Conjugating the two compact supports places them in disjoint invariant balls centred on the fixed plane. This does not change their equivariant components.

The composition with disjoint supports is the equivariant connected sum of the two clutching constructions. The separating collar is the identity both upstairs and on the orbit. The fixed discs are joined by their ordinary boundary connected sum, so the orbit knots are connected-summed with the stated orientations. The Haefliger integer is therefore additive as an integer, before any reduction modulo 28.

## Passage to the ordinary mapping class

For tube-relative representatives, C.3.14 identifies this integer with Wall's unframed coordinate, minus J. The remaining framing generator has an explicit SU2 lift extending over the seven-disc; it contributes nothing to the ordinary homotopy-sphere group. The Davis generator comparison then gives the fixed signed reduction modulo 28.

Thus q(phi^6)=0 implies 6q(phi)=0 in Z, hence q(phi)=0 and the ordinary class vanishes. The application must retain the fact that the actual six-loop representative is related to phi^6 by equivariant isotopies. This holds for the transports, finite/cusp corrections, and the invariant logarithmic gauge isotopy of C.3.6/C.3.10, but should be stated in the final corollary.

The required primary references are those opened and cited in the containing proposition: Smale (1959), Theorem A; Haefliger (1966), the connected-sum knot group; Davis (1982), Example 7.14 and Proposition 7.15. The proof supplies the actual group-operation comparison instead of inferring it from a numerical coincidence.

# Independent referee: C.3.24, actual smooth monodromy

**Result:** pass. Referee: boundary_d. This audit uses the actual geometric proofs C.3.18--C.3.23, not just their numerical outputs.

## Integer cancellation

C.3.18 gives the actual affine-framed relative Pontryagin number minus 24. C.3.21 identifies the actual delta-line mixed class and proves its relative square is minus two. The connection normalization in C.3.22 makes the same cap-orientation sign occur in both pairings. Therefore the cap integer is

`epsilon_cap * (-24 - 12*(-2))/24 = 0`

as an integer. No unknown orientation or Wall--Davis generator sign affects zero. The earlier wrong-sign diagnostic was resolved by the inverse between a boundary-frame change and a coordinate clutch, checked directly with connections.

## Equivariant sixth-power comparison

For a positive w loop, c decreases by two. After six loops the actual path runs from c0 to c0 minus 12 and closes by the positive shift F12 at c0 minus 12. Six positive-shift F2 maps compose in ascending c order from that endpoint. Since the gamma column changes by h times e, direct substitution in the displayed b formula gives `b_(h,c)+b_(k,c+h)=b_(h+k,c)`. Thus these six maps give exactly F12 with the required source and target.

A circle-invariant connection on the descended w-family gives the same equivariant return map on every repetition of the loop. Its sixfold return is the sixth power. Interpolation with the invariant connection used for C.3.10 is an equivariant isotopy. The finite-root transport, cusp unit multiplication, global real C-star flow, and logarithmic gauge comparison in C.3.6 are all equivariant. Changing the coherent closings to the originally stipulated R2 closings is also equivariant, since the scalar difference belongs to the connected C-star action centralizing delta.

Therefore the actual annular lift and the sixth power agree in the equivariant component group, not merely after forgetting the circle action.

## Consequences

C.3.23 makes the orbit-knot coordinate an integer homomorphism. The preceding calculation gives `6 q(Phi)=0` in Z, so q(Phi)=0. Its fixed signed reduction modulo 28 is the ordinary monodromy class, hence that class is zero. This avoids dividing by six in Z/28 and removes its possible order-two ambiguity.

An ordinary isotopy of the return map to the identity gives the oriented product mapping torus. Positive powers, hence all finite cyclic base changes, remain trivial. The twist-data consequence is restricted exactly to the family comparison proved in C.4.1.

This referee record makes no assertion about an analytic extension of the complex family. Trivial smooth monodromy is compatible with the analytic nonextension and degeneration obstructions elsewhere in the manuscript.

**Result:** pass. Referee: boundary_d. This is a second group-theoretic bridge, independent of the Haefliger integer, Wall's framed-surgery formula, and the Davis generator comparison.

## Integrality and composition

The cap is spin with the stated cohomology by its actual two-piece construction. Its twisted spin Dirac index is the negative of lambda, so lambda is an integer without invoking knot classification.

For tube-relative orbit maps, the actual line-bundle lifts extend by the identity over the other half of Y=S2 times S3. Their composition is the specified lift of the composed map. The Y-bundle over an oriented pair of pants therefore carries an actual line bundle, not merely an invariant cohomology class. Its seven-dimensional total space is a characteristic bordism between the two incoming mapping tori and their outgoing composition. The stable tangent bundle and line bundle restrict correctly, so both p1x and x cubed add.

The common cap/mapping-torus cut-and-reglue bordism then gives the same additivity for the caps. Its one fixed orientation sign is the same for all maps, and the identity cap and identity mapping torus have both numbers zero. This proves integer additivity directly by characteristic bordism.

## Normalization independence

The equivariant fixed-tube normalization and independence of the smooth orbit pair use only tubular geometry. Different induced normal framings of its oriented fixed S3 differ by a map to SO3. The difference of the two framed surgeries is characteristic-bordant to the total S2-bundle over S4 clutched by that map. The meridian lines glue because they agree in Chern class on the common S2 times S3 boundary, and its H1 is zero, so any gauge discrepancy has a logarithm.

For this S2-bundle Z, H2(Z)=Z with generator x of fibre evaluation one. The vertical tangent Euler class is 2x. Since its stabilization is the pullback of the rank-three clutch bundle, p1(TZ)=4x squared; the base S4 contributes no p1. Thus p1x minus four x cubed is zero directly. This calculation does not require a generator or sign convention for pi3(SO3). It proves lambda is unchanged by normalization and framing choices.

## Scope and orientation

Use the same meridian line convention as C.3.25. Switching the associated line to its dual reverses p, k, and lambda simultaneously, leaving the zero conclusion unchanged. The actual six-loop comparison remains equivariant by the independently checked closing cocycle and invariant transports.

Combined with the explicit C.3.25 coboundary, lambda gives the Eells--Kuiper invariant by a direct spin-c index calculation. This supplies an independent conversion and integer additivity argument, while sharing the actual geometric characteristic-number inputs from C.3.18--C.3.22. It does not construct the analytic filling from Part B.

For the assumed extended relative map, the critical-value image is proper analytic. The nonconstant central map from a smooth manifold to a curve has finitely many critical values, so there is no vertical one-dimensional discriminant component. The three sections on the punctured disc are individually marked by distinct linear monodromy orders. Their finite degree-one normalized closures extend across zero, even if their values collide. The cross-ratio gauge is therefore meromorphic.

A constant target value outside the central discriminant yields an actual proper smooth surface family over the entire disc. Its first-homology monodromy is identity. The original marked base point is a meromorphic function of the disc parameter, so its small loop is null-homotopic or peripheral to finite order. A zero of order d in z contributes the independent shear S_{−h}, h=2kl d. Its cancellation with a finite order-three/four matrix is impossible after taking that finite power. At the cusp, the u-to-w coefficient of nN−hE forces n=0, after which h=0. This is an integral argument and does not use an approximate period matrix.

The maximum-modulus argument excludes a limit on the outer circle. The source correctly concludes only that the period function has an interior nonzero limit. It does not identify the actual central complex manifold with the reference period member, and does not infer separatedness or arbitrary-chain invariance.

## Detailed development record: D_E228_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent referee: E.2.8

Referee: boundary_d. Date: 2026-11-15. Outcome: PASS at the stated necessary-condition scope.

Alexander duality and the graph-base Leray sequence give r=s−1+dim(H1(F)^mon), with the invariant subspace retained. When b1(F)=1, r≥s forces equality, reduced fibre irreducibility, and trivial H1 monodromy. Adding a regular value if necessary handles an empty original discriminant.

A horizontal prime intersects every fibre and cannot contain its irreducible reduced support. Its defining section is a non-zero-divisor even at local self-branches: vanishing on one branch would pull back to vanishing on an open set of the irreducible normalization and hence identically. Its pullback to a Kähler resolution is therefore a nonzero effective divisor, although its integral Chern class is zero by H2(S6,Z)=0. Positivity gives the contradiction. Passing first to the reduced support handles nonreduced fibres.

For D<0, the canonical-root identity gives a nonzero effective antipluricanonical power with c1 zero. A positive pluricanonical section would multiply with it to a nonzero constant possessing zeros. Thus the general fibre has Kodaira dimension minus infinity and is non-Kähler; the stated surface-classification consequence b1=1 is appropriate. The result does not exclude non-Kähler singular-fibre normalizations and does not close arbitrary deformation-chain invariance.

The tempting stronger argument that an elliptic fibre of a resolved horizontal divisor must remain elliptic on the fibre normalization is not justified: exceptional elliptic curves may be contracted. No such assertion appears in the accepted source.

## Detailed development record: D_Kato_axis_audit.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent axis and normal-line audit for the new Kato family candidate

Owner: boundary_d. Date: 15 November 2026. This records exact local calculations for AB's proposed family; it is not a claim to have constructed the S6 filling. AB owns the global annulus domains and the eventual numbered proposition.

## Two normal frames must be distinguished

The last blow-up chart is pi_n(z,w)=(z w^n,w), and sigma_s(z,w)=(kappa z,a w+(1−a)s), with 0<|a|<1. The actual Kato boundary identification is sigma_s composed with pi_n. Write v=w−s on the strict axis z=0. In the strict-transform normal frame z it gives

`v' = a v, z' = kappa (v+s)^n z`.

On an annulus avoiding w=0, use instead the blowdown normal frame x=z(v+s)^n. Then

`v' = a v, x' = kappa (a v+s)^n x`.

Thus AB's multiplier g_s(v)=kappa(a v+s)^n is correct in the blowdown frame. The two expressions differ by the explicit unit x=(v+s)^n z. On a fixed annulus |a|R<|v|<R and its gluing collars, this unit is nonzero for sufficiently small s, including s=0. This prevents a false sign or shifted-centre discrepancy between pi sigma and sigma pi.

The contraction pi_n sigma_s has fixed point (0,s), or v=0. The original blow-up centre w=0 corresponds to v=−s, not to the contraction fixed point. For s≠0 the germ can be diagonalized without a resonance hypothesis: the convergent product

`psi_s(v)=product_{k>=1}(1+a^k v/s)^n`

is nonzero near v=0 and satisfies psi_s(av)/psi_s(v)=(1+av/s)^(−n). The coordinate x_new=psi_s(v)x makes the normal multiplier kappa s^n and leaves v unchanged. This identifies the local Hopf marking exactly.

## Constant marked elliptic curve and normal family

The axis quotient has the fixed marking [v] in E_a=C*/a^Z, since v maps to av under the gluing. The point v=0 lies in the removed contraction ball, so the annulus marking is defined. Its central elliptic curve is the same marked E_a.

For the blowdown normal multiplier, the product

`H_s(v)=product_{k>=0}(1+a^k s/v)^n`

converges uniformly on the fixed annulus and its gluing collars. If, for example, |s|<|a|^2 R/2 after fixing sufficiently narrow collars, it is a nonzero holomorphic unit there. Its exact ratio is

`H_s(av)/H_s(v)=(1+s/(av))^n=g_s(v)/g_0(v)`.

This gives a genuine family isomorphism of the annulus-defined normal line with its central line. The fact that the product has zeros elsewhere in C* is immaterial: those points lie outside the chosen fundamental annulus and its gluing collars; one must not pretend g_s is a globally invertible automorphy factor on all of C*.

The central multiplier is kappa a^n v^n, of degree −n. One can check its relation with the blown-up Hopf description by a theta identity. With theta_a(av)=−v^(−1)theta_a(v), the divisor line O([p]) has multiplier −p/v. Hence, for p=−s,

`L_{kappa s^n} tensor O(−n[−s])`

has multiplier `kappa s^n (v/s)^n = kappa v^n`. This is equivalent to kappa a^n v^n by the nowhere-zero gauge v^n. The moving degree-zero Hopf normal factor and the moving exceptional-point divisor therefore cancel exactly in the marked normal class.

## The exceptional intersection divisor does not extend

The proposed punctured intersection divisor n[−s] on E_a has no limit in Sym^n(E_a). For any two distinct classes [v_1],[v_2], the sequences s_j=−a^j v_i tend to zero and give the distinct constant divisor limits n[v_i]. Consequently no analytic Cartier divisor on E_a times the disc can restrict to n[−s] on the punctured disc, even if a vertical component is allowed: remove its finite multiplicity along E_a times {0}; the remaining proper divisor would be finite of degree n over the disc and would define a continuous, indeed holomorphic, map to Sym^n(E_a), contradicting those limits.

This is precisely compatible with extension of the curve and its normal bundle. It forbids extension of the individual marked intersection divisor, not extension of its tensor product with the Hopf normal factor.

## Full auxiliary-family referee, 17:41 UTC

AB's completed working note `ab_push_parabolic_insertion_working.md`, §§2–5, was then read independently. The full auxiliary compact family and generic Hopf blowdown pass at that stated scope. Its later threefold attachment remains explicitly unconstructed.

For the uniform Kato domains, choose |a|≤A<rho<1 and |kappa|≤K. The bound |aw+(1−a)s|≤rho R and K²(rho R)^(2n)+rho²<1 place pi sigma of the closed ball strictly inside the original ball. The image sigma is compact in the last blow-up chart as well, since its two chart coordinates are bounded; hence it is compactly inside the modified ball. The outer collar avoids all exceptional curves, so sigma pi is an actual biholomorphism of collars. The parameter-preserving collar maps are holomorphic in s. Uniformly disjoint collars yield a smooth Hausdorff complex total space, and compact fundamental regions uniformly over compact parameter subsets give properness of the submersion.

The axis marking has a direct global check even with the original balls centred at w=0. In the coordinate v=w−s, its outer disc D_s contains zero and its inner disc is aD_s, compactly contained in D_s. The union of a^(−j)D_s is C and the intersection of a^jD_s is {0}. Thus D_s minus aD_s is a fundamental annulus for C*/a^Z and the marking [v] is exact. One may alternatively use balls centred at w=s and view the blow-up centre v=−s as a moving holomorphic section; then the v-annulus is literally round. The normal calculations concern actual line-neighbourhood gluing, since every transition is linear in the normal variable, not merely its first derivative.

For s≠0, the entire product G_s(v)=product_{j>=1}(1+a^j v/s)^n gives Psi_s(x,v)=(xG_s(v),v). Its identity Psi_s f_s=diag(kappa s^n,a) Psi_s is exact. The composite with pi_n on the actual fundamental region never hits the target origin: that would require v=x=0, the removed fixed point. It descends by the exact boundary equivariance to a holomorphic map of the compact Kato surface to the diagonal Hopf surface. Off the orbit v=−s times a^Z it is locally invertible and degree one. Its contracted curves all lie over that one orbit. At v=−s the product is nonzero, namely product(1−a^j)^n, so the map has the actual local n-fold blow-up model pi_n followed by an invertible coordinate change. This establishes the claimed generic marked blowdown, rather than relying on a classification analogy.

The exceptional correction in the normal-bundle identity is the weighted total transform and equals n[−s]. The reduced exceptional locus meets the final strict axis at a single point [−s]. Both distinctions are retained in AB's note.

## Detailed development record: D_generic_disc_geometry_working.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Generic analytic-disc geometry: finite bad sets

Owner: boundary_d. 15 November 2026. Working lemma supplied to E for
the generic-propagation argument. No additional result number or
arbitrary-chain conclusion is assigned here.

## Setup

Let X be a smooth complex fourfold, proper and smooth over a connected
parameter curve S, with compact fibres diffeomorphic to S6. Suppose an
intrinsic pencil has already been constructed as a proper map
g:X -> P1 x S on the current parameter domain, and that its distinct
critical multisections and their multiplicities have been separated
after deleting a discrete analytic set and, locally, a finite base
change. Assume there is a nonempty parameter open set on which this is
the prescribed family. All assertions below concern this current
parameter domain; later bad sets need not be discrete at previously
removed points. Fill the layers in reverse order using E.2.21.

## A total reduced cusp hypersurface becomes relatively NC generically

Let D be the total cusp divisor. It is reduced. A divisor component
contained over one parameter value would be the whole threefold fibre
of X -> S, contrary to nonconstancy of its pencil. Thus every divisor
component dominates S. Its multiplicity equals one on the known open
set, and hence everywhere. A hypersurface in a smooth space has no
embedded components, so these facts imply total reducedness.

Take the finite analytic normalization nu:Dtilde -> D -> X. The
normalization is proper over S. Delete the images of its singular locus
and, on the smooth part, the critical locus of Dtilde -> S. These are
proper analytic images and miss the known relative-NC open set; they
are therefore discrete analytic subsets of S. After this deletion
Dtilde is smooth over S.

Now delete the image of the rank-drop locus of the relative derivative

T_(Dtilde/S) -> nu* T_(X/S).

This too is a closed analytic locus proper over S, absent on the known
open. The resulting nu is a holomorphic immersion. It is finite onto
its image, so it is unramified: its diagonal is both open and closed in
Dtilde x_X Dtilde.

For k=2,3,4 let R_k be the locus of ordered, pairwise distinct preimages
in the k-fold fibre product of Dtilde over X. Partial diagonals are
open and closed by the preceding unramifiedness, so R_k is a closed
analytic subspace of that proper fibre product and is proper over S.
At each of its points the k local sheets have relative normal covector
lines in the same rank-three bundle T*_(X/S). On R_2 and R_3 remove the
images of the rank-drop loci of these k lines. Remove also the image of
R_4. All are proper analytic images, and all are empty over the known
relative-NC open set, hence discrete on S.

At a remaining point the total normalization has at most three local
sheets. Each is embedded as a smooth hypersurface in X, submersive over
S, and their vertical normal covectors are independent. Since the
TOTAL D is a reduced embedded hypersurface, its local ideal is exactly
the product of the distinct local equations of these sheets. There is
no possible extra finite-subring or seminormality datum. The independent
vertical covectors make those equations, the original parameter on S,
and the remaining coordinates a holomorphic coordinate system. Thus
q is a unit times x, xy, or xyz; the unit is absorbed into one branch
coordinate. These are the required relative NC equations.

This total-space argument also proves reducedness of each remaining
cusp fibre. It does not assume normalization commutes with base change.
An earlier optional suggestion to delete nonflatness of the quotient
nu_*O_Dtilde/O_D is unnecessary once one works with the total reduced
embedded hypersurface in this way.

## Finite root charts

Let q be a transverse coordinate to a finite critical section and let
m be its multiplicity on the known open. On the total smooth X its
divisor is mD: all its components dominate S and the multiplicity is
constant there; a whole parameter fibre cannot be a component because
the pencil on that fibre is nonconstant.

Locally q=a^m u, with a reduced equation of D and a unit u. After a
local holomorphic choice of u^(1/m), normalization of r^m=q gives the
disjoint graphs

r=zeta a u^(1/m),  zeta^m=1.

Each graph is a copy of X. Thus this cyclic root cover is smooth and
etale over X and its cyclic deck action is free, without first knowing
that the finite reduced fibre is smooth. The only remaining issue is
submersivity of its map to the root disc times S. On r!=0 this follows
from the propagated ordinary-locus condition. Its critical locus over
r=0 is closed and proper over S, and is absent on the known open.
Deleting its discrete image gives the required smooth root torus
families. Their compact fibres have the same topology as the known
root tori; surface classification and the inherited integral canonical
class identify the ordinary surfaces as complex tori.

## The period identity should be imposed on kernels

At one fixed ordinary target point t0, the invariant integral cycles
w and delta give two holomorphic period vectors. Their complex wedge
has a marking-independent zero set on S; since it is nonzero on the
known open, this set is discrete. Normalize the Lie frame there and
extract c(s) from the gamma column. The identities for the other columns
at t0 extend by the one-variable holomorphic identity theorem.

On the universal cover of the entire ordinary target times this
parameter domain, compare the actual holomorphic Hodge kernel
subbundle of Lambda_C with ker Pi_ref(t,c(s)). They coincide on the
product with the known open set and hence everywhere. This formulation
does not assume that normalization by w,delta was already possible at
every ordinary t, and does not improperly project a determinant-zero
set from a noncompact punctured target. The kernel identity proves that
normalization is possible everywhere afterwards.

These are analytic finite-locus constructions and holomorphic identity
arguments. They replace an unsupported generic topological
stratification assertion. The final assembly and reverse-order filling
of parameter exceptions are to be supplied in E's propagation theorem.

# D.4 global level classification: primary-source audit

Date of search and reading: 15 November 2026. No novelty or priority claim is made.

The second search engine was queried for `elliptic Green function critical points varying modulus zero Weierstrass p theta norm` and `"Weierstrass" "zero" "Green function" modular critical`. The stronger search engine was then queried for `"Green" "wp" "zero" "moduli" "tori" critical points` and `"Simple zero property" "moduli space of tori" Chen Lin`, with a ten-year recency window. Related older searches are retained in D_push_report.md.

Primary documents actually opened and read:

1. Lin, C.-S., & Wang, C.-L. (2010). Elliptic functions, Green functions and the mean field equations on tori. *Annals of Mathematics, 172*(2), 911–954. https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2010.172.911 . The previously read Theorem 1.3 counts critical points in the elliptic variable for each fixed modulus. The journal landing page was reopened at https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2010/172-2/p03 . This does not determine zeros of both factors in D.4.4 along wp_tau(mu(tau))=0.
2. Chen, Z., Kuo, T.-J., & Lin, C.-S. (2019). Simple zero property of some holomorphic functions on the moduli space of tori. *Science China Mathematics, 62*(11), 2089–2102. https://doi.org/10.1007/s11425-018-9355-0 . Opened publisher PDF: https://files.sciengine.com/pdfs/1674-7283/D32F7CA22FD34AC2AB4CEDD492E051EB.pdf . Read Theorems 1.1–1.4, their definitions (1.2)–(1.3), and Appendix A, equations (A.1)–(A.3), Propositions A.1–A.2. Their eta1 equals our E=pi²E2/3. Their classical derivative formulas agree exactly with our independently derived wp_tau and g2_tau. Their holomorphic functions f_{k,C} and the multiple-Green Hessian curves are different from our real-analytic factor F=3ZP−2g2 on the zero section of wp. No theorem from that paper was substituted for the finite interval proof.
3. Chen, Z., Fu, E., & Lin, C.-S. (2025a). *Generic non-degeneracy of critical points of multiple Green functions on torus and applications to curvature equations* [Preprint, version 1, 10 March 2025]. arXiv. https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2503.06935 . Opened https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06935 and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.06935 ; read introduction and Theorem 1.1. That theorem gives a measure-zero exceptional set for degeneracy of critical points of G_n at fixed modulus. It does not exclude all critical moduli for the specific composite height function.
4. Chen, Z., Fu, E., & Lin, C.-S. (2025b). *Green functions, Hitchin's formula and curvature equations on tori* [Preprint, version 2, 20 October 2025]. arXiv. https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2508.17604 . Opened https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17604 and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.17604 ; read Theorems A and B, Theorem 1.2, its proof, and the nearby definitions. Theorem 1.2 proves nondegeneracy of the nontrivial critical pair of a fixed torus Green function when that pair exists. It neither counts intersections with wp=0 as the modulus varies nor handles the second modulus-gradient factor.

The arXiv HTML endpoints for 2508.17604v1 and 2503.06935v2 did not open; the successful abstract and PDF URLs above are the sources used. Searches did not locate the exact D.4.8 classification. This absence is a bounded literature-search result, not a claim that the statement is absent from all literature.

The global conclusion is proved here by the independent local/cusp certificate D.4.7, the exact compact certificate d_push_global_arb.py and d_push_global_refine.py, the cover audit d_push_global_cover_audit.py, and the C1 level-transport argument. The preliminary complex128 scan is exploratory evidence only. The exact normalization at the equianharmonic point is proved analytically in D.4.5, rather than inferred from any numerical height comparison.

## Detailed development record: D_global_loop_defect_working.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# The global radial-loop defect of an arbitrary cap

**Status: full working proof for reserved B.4.32; not yet input or registered.** Boundary_d, 15 November 2026. Root and AB independently proposed or checked parts of the mechanism. This file does not itself assert the final noncompletion theorem reserved for B.4.33.

## Statement to be proved

Assume any proper analytic filling of a finite parameter cover of the actual six-sphere family exists. Retain its reduced main component, normalize and resolve it, and then resolve the graph of the intrinsic marked map and the central support as in the strengthened B.4.31. B.4.4 applies to the smooth connected dominating fourfold without any hypothesis on the central fibre. After deleting finitely many values of the marked P1 base, the following invariant is defined.

Use the local ordinary origins of the proved partial family B.4.19 as reference. Any local cap section, after any necessary finite root of r, has a radial-loop defect in

    V = (Λ⊗Q)/(Qδ).

Divide the defect by the root degree. This normalized class is independent of the extending cap section, and of all source modifications and further roots used to obtain it. It patches, in the actual homology marking, to a flat section of V over the remaining ordinary base. This section is zero.

Consequently, if a finite cap multisection of degree d has trace t, then the r-loop class of t−d o, measured in V, is zero. Here o is the B.4.19 ordinary local origin. The same conclusion applies to any origin obtained from these traces by differences that are bounded logarithmic sections on the strict r-axis.

Only the loop class is controlled. The holomorphic difference t−d o need not itself be bounded or extend at r=0.

## 1. The loop class without choosing a meromorphic lift

Over a simply connected ordinary base chart U, the Jacobian of the actual family has its marked period presentation. Its r-monodromy is

    T = I−mE,   E=δ⊗γ,   m>0,   E²=0.

The Lie bundle has the ordinary holomorphic frame used in the period matrix. For a holomorphic Jacobian section a on U×Δ_r*, take a holomorphic lift on the universal cover of the r puncture. After one positive loop, the difference between its endpoint and its initial value is an actual period. Let v(a)∈Λ be its period label at the chosen base point. Changing the lift by a period changes this label by an element of

    (T−I)Λ ⊂ Zδ.

Thus a determines a well-defined class

    ν_r(a)∈Λ_Q/Qδ.

Equivalently, this is its connecting class in H¹(Δ_r*,Λ), followed by the quotient to Λ_Q/Qδ. No single-valued additive lift on the punctured disc is assumed. A varying holomorphic section gives a locally constant such class as q varies, because the integral period label varies continuously.

For r=R^e, the pullback monodromy is T^e. Restriction of the connecting class is

    v ↦ (I+T+⋯+T^(e−1))v.

Modulo Qδ this is e v. Therefore define the normalized defect of an extending section b on the root cover by

    λ(b;o) = (1/e) ν_R(b−o(R^e)) ∈ V.

It is unchanged by further roots. This definition also makes the transfer/trace normalization exact; no arbitrary choice of a logarithm of r is being concealed.

## 2. Exact ordinary affine-overlap audit

The reference origins are the actual ordinary origins in B.4.19, not arbitrary zeros of abstract isomorphic tori. Recall v=(2πi)^−1 log r, c=−12v, and

    ζ̃_i = ζ + k_i v δ,   k_i=(2πi)^−1 log K,
    K(x)=x⁴/(x−1)³.

Their local zero is ζ̃_i=0. On an ordinary overlap k_j−k_i=n_ij∈Z, so the extra translation is n_ij vδ. Its positive r-loop period is n_ijδ and is zero in V.

The linear Lie-frame maps are independent of c and r. For the two generating finite peripheral changes they are exactly

    R1=[[-1/τ,0],[(1−μ)/τ,1]],
    R2=[[ 1/τ,0],[    −μ/τ,1]],

and satisfy R_jδ=δ and R_jΠ_c=Π'_c A_j. Compositions preserve these properties. Thus a change of period frame applies the actual lattice matrix A_j to a loop class; it does not add an unrecorded r-dependent Lie-frame contribution.

The only potentially dangerous nonconstant connector is the finite-root-to-ordinary translation. B.4.19.4 computes it exactly:

    σ_j,c = w_j Π_c v_j,
    k−k_j = 12 ε_j w_j,   γ(v_j)=ε_j,
    σ_j,c + (k−k_j)vδ = w_j Π_part v_j.

Since c=−12v, the entire r logarithm cancels. The last expression is independent of r and contributes zero radial-loop defect. The remaining finite deck maps contain the factors r⁴ and r^(−3) in their multiplicative coordinate; these add only integral δ periods to the loop. Passing to a sector or changing its period representative adds (T−I)ℓ, also in Qδ.

On a punctured cusp sector, B.4.19.5 gives the additional comparison

    G(s)+6vδ,   G(s)=(μs/2, Cs/12−s²/24),

where G is independent of r. Its radial-loop defect is again purely δ. A full twelve-component shift must not be called the identity: it can include translation of the elliptic base by 6P. As an actual period representative, however, its radial-loop change is (T−I)γ, which is in the same δ line.

Therefore all reference origins have zero relative radial-loop defect in V on their overlaps. They define a globally consistent reference **loop class**, even though they need not form a single global affine origin. Changing the coherent closing by a global scalar C* gauge also changes a radial loop only by an integral δ winding and leaves this assertion unchanged.

AB independently checked every displayed overlap, including the finite connector cancellation and the cusp comparison. The proof uses the actual source-to-target period covariance, not merely a statement that a translation happens to look like a second coordinate.

## 3. Any two extending cap sections have the same normalized class

Resolve the marked graph and central support globally. Use the closed normalized branch-intersection spaces, proper over P1, to delete the finitely many critical or nondominating values as in B.4.31. On a sufficiently small ordinary q disc, a finite root of r and the finite toric subdivisions used in B.4.30 make the map semistable with the q parameter retained. On an overlap take a common further root and common refinement to compare the constructions. No global semistable model over the whole punctured P1 is asserted or required.

The nearby torus fibres are connected. The proper normal map to the normal two-dimensional base has trivial finite Stein factor because its generic fibres are connected. Hence the central surface fibres are connected too.

The saturation argument of B.4.30 applies without the rank-two assumption: it only needs the known normalized rank-two form subbundle of the canonical log-de Rham extension. Over an ordinary q chart, the r-logarithmic period term is solely the δ⊗γ term, so the same explicit subbundle extends. What rank two supplied there was the residue calculation.

Here B.4.31 supplies the stronger required residue statement directly. Every local angular class is an integral multiple of δ. The first normalized form has period zero on δ, and the second has integral period one (or minus one with the W character convention). Thus the local logarithmic primitives have residue vector

    (0, integer).

Their exponentials consequently lie in

    O* × M_r^gp,

which is exactly the bounded character sheaf along the strict r-axis. In particular the first character has zero r-order.

Given two sections extending into this same semistable cap, the finite chart-chain proof of B.4.30 compares them by these characters. The first character has zero radial winding; the second can wind only by an integer δ period. Hence

    ν_R(b−b')=0 in V.

The connectedness just established makes this true even when the two sections meet different components of the central fibre. For sections requiring different roots, pass to a common root and divide by its degree. Their normalized defects relative to the reference origin therefore agree.

Smooth points of the reduced central fibre have transverse holomorphic sections. Thus the common defect exists locally around every remaining ordinary q. A finite multisection gives the same class: after a common cyclic root it splits into sections, all with this normalized class; the connecting class of its trace is the sum of their classes. Dividing by its degree gives the same invariant. Proper lifts through modifications and further roots do not change it, by the preceding comparison and the restriction formula in §1.

## 4. Global continuation and the exact fixed-space calculation

The invariant λ(q) is locally constant in the actual homology local system: on one q chart choose one extending section; its integral connecting label is constant. On overlaps, independence of the cap section and the zero reference-overlap defect in §2 make these local definitions agree under the actual lattice transport. Thus λ is a globally defined flat section of V. Alternatively, retaining finite multisection branches gives a finite monodromy orbit; the argument below also excludes that possibility.

In the ordered quotient basis (γ̂,û,ŵ), the two generating matrices are

    Ā1 = [[1,0,0],[6,0,1],[−6,−1,−1]],
    Ā2 = [[1,0,0],[0,0,−1],[−6,1,0]].

Their rational fixed lines are

    Fix(Ā1)=Q(1,2,−4),
    Fix(Ā2)=Q(1,3,−3).

They have zero intersection. Removing additional ordinary points from P1 does not remove either peripheral monodromy from the image: the induced map to π1(P1 minus {0,1,∞}) is surjective. Hence a global flat section of V is zero.

For completeness V has no nonzero finite orbit either. The γ coefficient is invariant under the whole group. Averaging a finite orbit gives an invariant vector with the same γ coefficient, so that coefficient must be zero. The orbit then lies in the elliptic two-dimensional subspace, where the two unipotents of B.4.31 have different fixed lines and no nonzero finite orbit.

It follows that λ(q)=0. In particular every finite trace t of degree d has

    ν_r(t−d o)=0 in V.

If an origin h is obtained from such traces by differences that are bounded logarithmic sections on the strict r-axis, those differences also have defect in Qδ. Therefore ν_r(h−d o)=0 as well. Applying the globally invariant covector γ gives zero. This is the only component needed for the intended B.4.33 cusp contradiction.

## 5. Scope retained

The proof does not assert that h−d o is meromorphic, bounded, or holomorphically extendible. A common essential translation with zero period defect remains possible. Its irrelevance to the final comparison must be proved using only the γ component of the loop class.

The proof uses the global marked P1 base and the actual peripheral matrices. An arbitrary rank-one degeneration over a small isolated q disc need not satisfy this conclusion. For an exact example, change the punctured marking of a standard local cap by the translation χ(r)=[(log r)/(2πi)·u]. It is single-valued as a torus translation because u is an actual r-invariant period. The same proper cap now has defect u modulo Qδ relative to the old origin, while every difference of two extending sections remains unchanged. This marking cannot patch over the full ordinary base because u has infinite Γ-orbit. The Hopf insertion B.4.15 is not asserted to furnish this particular nonzero generic defect.

The current hypotheses allow an arbitrary proper analytic filling. Resolution of its reduced main component gives the smooth fourfold needed by B.4.4 and B.4.31 without changing the punctured family. Its central divisor may have multiplicities. Only the local two-parameter semistable reductions proved directly in B.4.30 are used; a global nonprojective semistable-reduction theorem is not assumed.

## Detailed development record: D_local_CY_torus_cap_attempt.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# A local torsion-canonical finite-monodromy caution

Working note, 15 November 2026. No numbered result or S6 comparison is
claimed here. This checks the scope of a possible local good-reduction
argument in the remaining interior-limit problem.

There are explicit local proper torus degenerations with trivial
canonical bundle, finite degree-one monodromy, and non-Kähler central
components. Their Euler number remains zero because a compact elliptic
action survives. Thus the global topology or the actual extended
delta action is essential; a local torsion-canonical hypothesis alone
must not be treated as a good-reduction theorem.

Take the compact Weierstrass cubic family over a small t-disc with
affine equation y²=x³+tx and resolve its unique total-space singular
point. Setting t'=t+x² makes that singularity y²=xt', an A1 singularity.
In a chart of the minimal resolution,

`x=rho, y=rho s, t'=rho s², t=rho(s²−rho)`.

The central fibre consists of two smooth rational curves C1 and C2
meeting with tangency order two: rho=0 and rho=s². Their intersection
is two and, since C1+C2 is a fibre divisor, their self-intersections
are both minus two. The original cuspidal cubic has rational
normalization, so the strict component also completes to P1. This is
the usual KodairaIII local model. The form dt wedge dx/y pulls back
to a nonzero constant multiple of d rho wedge ds and extends across
the resolution. The local elliptic surface therefore has trivial
canonical bundle. Its j-invariant is constantly1728 and its smooth
elliptic fibres have order-four linear monodromy.

Let E=C/(Z+iZ), and use L=O(C1) to form a principal E bundle over this
resolved surface by the transition cocycle [log(l_ab)/(2 pi i)].
This is the explicit Cartier logarithmic-transform cocycle used in
B.4.7; no classification of a new surface is necessary. Its total
space is smooth and proper over the t-disc, and is flat there.
The relative canonical bundle of a principal elliptic bundle is
trivial, so its total canonical bundle is trivial as well.

Outside the central fibre L has its canonical trivialization. The
punctured family is consequently the product of E with the original
elliptic family, and its H1 monodromy is identity on H1(E) and the
order-four elliptic operator on the second summand. The restrictions
of L to C1,C2 have degrees minus two and plus two. Each corresponding
principal E bundle over P1 is a secondary scalar Hopf surface: its
fundamental group is Z plus Z/2 and its first Betti number is one.
The two surfaces meet along an elliptic curve with tangency, so the
centre is not a normal-crossings surface in this model.

The compact elliptic group E acts freely on the whole total space.
Every fibre therefore has Euler number zero. This construction is
fully consistent with the new global E.2.14 obstruction on S6. It
does not identify this local model with an actual prescribed finite
root torus, does not construct a compact S6 fibration, and supplies
no uniqueness failure at the marked six-sphere interior limit.

For context, I opened Nishiguchi's original MPI88-48 preprint,
*Degeneration of K3 surfaces, II*:
<https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/3390/1/preprint_1988_48.pdf>.
Printed pp.7–12 distinguish torsion/trivial-canonical non-Kähler
types from the algebraic Kulikov classification. Printed p.12,
Conjecture1.9, explicitly leaves the corresponding nilpotent-monodromy
classification conjectural in that source. It therefore cannot be
cited as a general finite-monodromy good-reduction theorem. Retrieval
reference: turn465view0. No later resolution of that conjecture is
asserted or needed for the explicit check above.

# Boundary agent: ninth-hour cross-part work

Owner: boundary_d. Updated 2026-11-15, approximately 21:25 UTC. Part D itself remains complete; its proofs and exact certificates are unchanged.

## Integrated authored results

- **B.4.21:** the degree-one Kato repair, after q=s^12, gives a genuine linear Tate-line collar, not merely a matching normal bundle. Six point blowups and 72 curve blowups make H=h/q holomorphic, yield the simultaneous A11 product charts, and permit the uniformly convergent product conjugacy. The persistent elliptic axis retains degree −1. Root and limits_ab independently passed all charts and exact code.
- **B.4.23:** the generic exceptional curves have no proper analytic closure compatible with their marked elliptic intersections, and no extending proper relative contraction. A smooth semistable cap retaining an ordinary exceptional curve away from its conductor, with the specified holomorphic base map, forces nearby rational curves by the exact normal sequence and the relative Douady theorem. The changed-base/vertical-component and conductor exceptions remain explicit.
- **B.4.25:** a diagonal Hopf family with one contraction multiplier tending to zero and the other to a nonzero contraction has no proper analytic completion, even with singular/nonreduced central geometry and after finite base change. The proof is exact: accumulating anticanonical resonances, quantitative Rouché estimates, direct-image coherence, and resolution preserving the punctured smooth family. Root and limits_ab independently passed. The classical Oda degeneration drives both eigenvalues to zero and is not contradicted.

Each result has its own TeX source and results CSV. B.4.21 has exact executable chart and lattice checks. B.4.25 is a complete analytic proof and does not rely on a numerical test.

## Independent referees completed

- B.4.22: the toric local corner resolution and its exact repaired collar; no full period action or global attachment is silently supplied.
- B.4.24: associated-log-torus torsor nonsplitting in the actual two axial origins; finite coordinate roots do not kill the r-loop period class. The ordinary logarithmic gauge does not invalidate the fixed axial-origin obstruction.
- B.4.26: every origin component of a smooth completion carrying the fixed holomorphic bidisc map must be three-dimensional. The finite local slice, finite-flat trace, explicit root-cover norm and nonzero d-gamma class are all checked.
- B.4.27: the specified associated log-torsor cannot extend after finitely many point blowups of the base. The exact bounded-character sheaf, exceptional Picard basis and degree-two valuation blocks give H1 of the log-torus sheaf equal to zero. This is not an arbitrary analytic-cap theorem.
- B.4.28: the initial central fundamental-group free-product step was rejected because conductor-cycle paths carry nontrivial component-group elements. Root's replacement was independently checked: a marked hyperbolic Inoue cusp-link torus has zero rational H2 image in the nearby T4 but nonzero holomorphic two-form period. The revised proof passes, retaining its extra trivial-central-dualizing hypothesis. Original Oda Section15 and Fujiki–Pontecorvo were opened for the exact angular marking. The rejected argument is preserved as rejected provenance.
- B.4.29: the Tate-product small resolution with an essential translation preserves all relative invariant forms, Gauss–Manin data and an absolute canonical form, yet the marked automorphism has no meromorphic extension. Both charts, the proper-graph restriction and the two central-limit sequences pass. This does not refute a theorem about differences of two sections that already extend into the same cap.

Separate referee records are in the D_B4422, D_B4424, D_B4426, D_B4427, D_B4428 and D_B4429 Markdown files. The B.4.23 and B.4.25 source audits record the directly opened primary references and the exact scope.

## Current constructive gap

The main Part B cap remains unconstructed. A changed-base cap must be genuinely vertical for original q; writing q=r^k h(s) is only a local description and does not identify the internal Kato parameter with the original q. A smooth S_n bundle over the exceptional P1 has a product persistent elliptic family: its degree −n normal line forces the elliptic torsor class to be torsion in the torsion-free group H1(P1,E)=H2(P1,Lambda), hence zero. The actual two-period Chern comparison needed to exploit this constraint has not been constructed.

The combined-period Hopf quotient has two multipliers vanishing with orders k and k+12, so B.4.25 does not exclude it. Its remaining-period action and conductor gluing are unresolved. These are working attempts, not additional proved cap claims. Details are saved in D_vertical_kato_base_attempts.md.

No novelty or priority claim is made. All failed or superseded comparisons are retained with their corrected scope, particularly the earlier wrong-sign cusp gauge and the rejected central free-product inference.
