## Detailed development record: D_referee_AB_C.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent referee note: A.3.1–A.3.2 and C.1.1

Reviewed 2026-11-15 by the Part D agent. This note concerns the stated analytic smoothing invariant, the normalization calculation, and the clutching sign. It does not independently certify the global collar construction in A.1.2 or the previously established Euler number of Engel's fibres.

## Verdict

The virtual Euler obstruction is valid for arbitrary proper flat holomorphic smoothings of the specified reduced lci space. Neither projectivity nor the Kähler condition is needed. The calculation for Q gives zero. I found no contrary example or missing geometric hypothesis in A.3.1–A.3.2. Two proof passages should be made more precise, as recorded below. C.1.1 has the stated positive sphere summand with its stated seam and orientation conventions.

## A.3.1: analytic invariance

A flat deformation over the smooth disc of an lci fibre is lci near that fibre: choose local relative embeddings, lift the central regular sequence, use flatness to identify the ideal modulo the parameter, and apply Nakayama. The lifted sequence is regular, either by the nonzerodivisor criterion inductively or by the Cohen–Macaulay dimension criterion in the regular relative ambient local ring. Properness permits one common disc after the local construction. Consequently the relative cotangent complex is perfect, and its derived dual has a natural topological complex K-class. Its restriction to a fibre is the derived tangent class; this is the actual tangent bundle on a smooth fibre.

The reference to a global analytic vector-bundle resolution would be unsafe: such resolutions need not exist on arbitrary complex spaces. The manuscript instead uses the topological class. Levy (2008), Lemmas 3.1–3.2 and §3.1, pp. 588–590, supplies precisely the stabilization and gluing of local complexes into a global continuous complex. It is enough to perform this construction on a compact inverse image of a segment, where a finite covering suffices. This also avoids any uniform rank issue on a noncompact total space.

For the homology assertion, the following gives a precise replacement for the informal lower-strata sentence. Fix a small positive real ε and set K=π⁻¹([0,ε]) and S=Sing(Z). Flatness and regularity of each smooth fibre imply that π is a submersion at every point of K outside S. Thus K minus S is an oriented real (2n+1)-manifold with boundary Xε disjoint union minus Zreg, with orientation dt followed by the complex fibre orientation. Since Z is reduced and pure dimensional, dim_R S is at most 2n−2. Compatible triangulation of K relative to S, Z and Xε now gives an integral chain by summing all oriented top-dimensional simplices. All interior faces cancel. The only 2n-dimensional boundary faces lie in Xε or Zreg; none can lie in S. At a smooth central point the parameter is a submersion, so the central coefficient is exactly one. Its boundary is [Xε]−[Z]. This proves equality in integral singular homology of K and hence of the total space. Pairing c_n of the topological K-class, and applying smooth Gauss–Bonnet, proves the asserted equality.

Compatible triangulation is the standard analytic-space form of semianalytic triangulation; Łojasiewicz (1964), §3, Theorems 1–2, pp. 463–464, is the primary local/ambient theorem. Compact analytic spaces are triangulable using their finite analytic atlases. No Whitney a_f theorem, projective specialization theorem, or global ambient complex embedding is being assumed here.

## A.3.2: normalization and K-theory

The notation ν* must mean pullback of a perfect K-class, equivalently derived pullback. It must not mean ordinary pullback of a possibly non-locally-free tangent sheaf.

For a single branch with normal line A in a smooth local ambient space and conductor D, the hypersurface normal line restricts to A(D). Hence the virtual difference from the branch tangent bundle is A−A(D)=−i_*[A(D)|D]. The latter line on D is the product of the two branch normals, namely T¹_Q. The intrinsic differential comparison makes these local identities compatible. On the normalization's infinity section, the line is technically pulled back by the conductor identification. It agrees with the same J because J=π*O_B(1) and the translation preserves B. Thus the two negative terms displayed in A.3.2 are correct.

Put v=d0+d∞=c1(T_R/S). Since J extends to R, each i_*J is represented by [J]−[J(−Di)]. The Chern correction factor is exactly 1−v+jv: expand the displayed ratio using j²=0 and d0d∞=0. Write c=c1(S), C=c2(S). Then c1(R)=c+v, c2(R)=C+cv, c3(R)=Cv. The degree-three corrected class is

    Cv − (C+cv)v + jv(c+v) = cjv + (j−c)v².

Here j=c=F and F²=0, so the entire pulled-back c3 class is zero, not only its integral. Since ν_*[R]=[Q], the virtual Euler number is zero. This agrees with the manuscript's independent numerical intersection calculation 24−24+0. An independent symbolic check is saved as code/d_push_referee.py with output code/d_push_referee.out.

Conditional only on the separately proved input e(X(u))=2, A.3.1 now excludes every proper flat holomorphic smoothing of Q with those fibres. The argument does not require a map to B, a prescribed smoothing section, a smooth total space, or an algebraic family.

## C.1.1: clutching orientation

At the seam of the stated mapping torus, write the lower coordinate as x and the upper coordinate as φ(x), because (x,1) is identified with (φ(x),0). The chart on the inserted block therefore uses x below and φ⁻¹ of the original coordinate above. Relative to the original product complement, the boundary attaching map is φ on the upper cap and the identity on the lower cap and side collar. For dt wedge o(D6), the upper cap has positive boundary orientation o(D6). The regluing is consequently the sphere D7_+ union_φ (−D7_-), with positive sign. Hence the stated oriented connected-sum formula has Σφ, not its orientation reverse or inverse clutching class. Reversing the seam convention would reverse this sign. The orientation dθ wedge o(fibre) also agrees with the outward boundary orientation of a positively oriented complex base disc.

## Primary references opened and checked

Levy, R. N. (2008). Riemann–Roch theorem for higher bivariant K-functors. *Annales de l’Institut Fourier, 58*(2), 571–601. https://doi.org/10.5802/aif.2361. Full primary PDF: https://www.numdam.org/article/AIF_2008__58_2_571_0.pdf. Read introduction, Lemmas 3.1–3.2 and §3.1, especially pp. 588–590. Search/open records: turn50view0, turn52view3, turn55view0, turn55view1.

Łojasiewicz, S. (1964). Triangulation of semi-analytic sets. *Annali della Scuola Normale Superiore di Pisa, Classe di Scienze, Serie III, 18*(4), 449–474. https://www.numdam.org/item/ASNSP_1964_3_18_4_449_0/. Full primary PDF: https://www.numdam.org/item/ASNSP_1964_3_18_4_449_0.pdf. Read §3, Theorems 1–2, pp. 463–464, and metadata. Search/open records: turn50search1, turn52view0, turn53view1, turn53view2.

The algebraic comparison FG10 in the manuscript is not used to supply the analytic specialization step.


## Additional checks: A.1.2, A.2.1–A.2.2, and Lead 5

The global core/node plumbing in A.1.2 is correctly typed: a belongs to L_x, b to L^−1_{Tx}, and a tensor b=uφ_x. Hence the infinity collar coordinate b^−1 lies in L_{Tx}; its contracting inverse covers T^−1, as required. The two collars are disjoint under the displayed uniform norm bound. The smaller closed core and node sets cover the quotient, establishing properness without a polarization assumption. The local total node ring is Cohen–Macaulay and the joint fibres have pure dimension two, so the regular-sequence flatness argument applies even over the nonreduced Kodaira fibres.

In A.2 the exclusion of new rays under joint flatness and reducedness checks: a mixed positive-height ray would give a three-dimensional component over the origin of a two-dimensional base; a new bottom ray changes the retained triangulation; a remaining primitive ray has h_q=0, h_u=1 and is already a rho ray. On each regular chart of either retained subdivision, q and u are monomials in disjoint sets of affine coordinates because all rays have pure q-height or pure u-height. This gives a particularly direct proof of the required pure-dimensional fibres and joint flatness. The singular-centre blow-up charts X=zvA and zv=XB, followed by the stated conifold resolution, are correct. The strict transform of the section is A=0 in the first chart and misses the conifold in the second chart, so it stays isomorphic to S and its normal bundle acquires exactly O_S(−S∞).

I also checked the component-cover obstruction subsequently found by the AB agent for the literal Lead 5 gluing. Over B° the prescribed r=0 fibre is a cycle of 12 ruled components; normalization and its Stein factor give a degree-12 étale component cover, and incidence restricts transition permutations to the dihedral group D12. The finite models impose rotations +4 and −3. The plain cusp fan has global labels j modulo 12, preserved by both periods, so its peripheral permutation is identity. Even allowing independent incidence-preserving relabellings changes the two rotations only to ±4 and ±3, none of whose sums is zero modulo 12. Thus those prescribed pieces cannot be the restrictions of one global family. This refutes the literal gluing, not all possible corrected cusp models. In common orientation the missing cusp rotation is −1. It agrees with the simple cusp pole found independently in D_monodromy_order_note.md.
## Further independent referee check: B.3.4 trace obstruction

The current B.3.4 proof was read in full after the trace calculation was added. Its stated scope is the split elliptic normalization over the component cover; a genus-one torsor is not covered. No gap was found within that scope.

Independently recomputed:

- The substitution `x=a^2 b^4 X`, `y_old=a^3 b^6 Y` changes the original elliptic equation to `Y^2=X^3-3bX+2a^2`, whose discriminant is the nonzero constant 1728 on `b^3=a^4+1`.
- At infinity, `z=a/b` and `c=z^3 a=z^4 b` satisfy `c^3(1-c)=z^12`. The integral equation has discriminant of order twelve and unit `c_4`, hence the unique singular fibre is `I_12` and the fundamental line bundle is `O_C(infinity)`.
- The local expansions of the sections with `k=omega^j` give `U=-sqrt(3) k^2 z^8/6+O(z^12)` and `V=2 sqrt(3) k z^4+O(z^8)`. Thus the three orbit sections meet the same component (index 4, or 8 after reversal), at distinct points.
- The affine intersections are `P.O=0`, `R.O=R'.O=1`, `P.R=P.R'=2`, and `R.R'=1`; infinity adds none. These give the displayed positive height matrix `2 direct_sum ((4,-2),(-2,4))/3`, determinant `8/3`.
- `chi=1`, `g(C)=3` and `e=12` give `q=3`, `p_g=3`, `b_2=22`, `h^{1,1}=16`. The equality `q=g(C)` can also be obtained directly from the Leray sequence and `R^1 pi_*O=O_C(-infinity)`, whose global sections vanish.
- The trivial lattice has rank 13; the three independent sections saturate the Hodge upper bound on the Picard rank. The integral rank-16 sublattice has determinant `-32`, so its index in the Neron--Severi lattice, equivalently the Mordell--Weil index, is 1, 2 or 4.
- Height zero would require `j(12-j)=24`, with no integer solution; this excludes torsion. The two pairing congruences force both quarter coefficients to be even. The component homomorphism then forces the component of a hypothetical trace-half section to be even. Its geometric height lies in `(1/3)Z`, whereas its lattice height lies in `1/2+(1/3)Z`, a contradiction.
- The same argument excludes every odd half-integral invariant coefficient, so the full trace image is `12Z P`, not merely the exclusion of `6P`.

This check is algebraic and geometric; it does not claim a second construction of a global filling or any extension of the obstruction to non-split torsors. The exact programs and their outputs remain AB-owned.

## Detailed development record: D_referee_B35_B36.md

# Independent referee note: B.3.5–B.3.6

Read the current proofs in `AB_push.tex` in full. No substantive gap was found within their expressly stated scope.

For B.3.5, the pole orders `3i+4j`, with `0<=j<=2`, are pairwise distinct. The spaces of functions with poles at infinity of order at most two and three are therefore exactly the displayed spaces. A section avoiding the zero section has Weierstrass coordinates in those spaces; substitution leaves only `P` and `-P`. Every other nonzero section has height at least one. Once B.3.4 makes the invariant coefficient integral, any proper residual overlattice of index 2 or 4 contains one of the three nonzero half classes; their common height is `1/3`, giving the asserted contradiction.

For B.3.6, the following steps were checked independently:

1. The Hochschild–Serre transgression is onto because `H^2(K,E)=0` over the complex function field. The independent cohomological-dimension explanation in the proof is valid: the quotient of the geometric point group by its torsion subgroup is a rational vector space, and averaging on finite quotients kills its positive continuous cohomology.
2. At the ramified places the orders are exactly 3, 4 and 12, and `6P` lies in the identity component of the original Néron model. On that group, multiplication by each order is onto: solve first on the residue additive or multiplicative group over `C`, then use the étale multiplication map and Hensel's lemma. A division point defined already over the smaller local field is invariant and has norm `6P`.
3. Naturality of local Hochschild–Serre now makes the local torsor classes descend. Their finite support follows by spreading the torsor over an open subset and using local sections over strictly henselian smooth fibres.
4. The exact Ogg–Shafarevich localization statement needed here is applicable to the original nonconstant Jacobian (`j=1728t`). Subtracting a global class with the selected finitely many local descents keeps the transgression and makes the restricted class locally trivial over the cover. Compatibility at conjugate points follows from its Galois invariance.
5. A locally trivial genus-one torsor has the stated minimal regular model and the same local fibres. The generic affine lift extends: in a common resolution a terminal exceptional curve has square `-1`; if it were not contracted by the other projection its vertical rational image would have square at least `-1`, whereas the available vertical rational curves all have square `-2`. Repeated contraction of common exceptional curves proves extension and its inverse.
6. The twelfth power of the affine lift is a translation. Conjugation by the lift makes that section invariant. Its cyclic carry factor set represents the transgression, so its coefficient is `6 modulo 12`; translating the lift by a suitable integral multiple of `P` gives the exact equality `A^12=t_(6P)`. A possible global sign convention for the transgression is harmless for this order-two class, since `6=-6 modulo 12`.

The result is an existence theorem for an unramified torsor and its affine lift, not a threefold gluing or a smoothing theorem. In particular no characteristic numbers for the desired filling follow from it alone.

## Sources independently opened

- Cossec, F., Dolgachev, I., & Liedtke, C. (2024). *Enriques surfaces I*, author manuscript dated April 19, 2024. https://sites.lsa.umich.edu/idolga/wp-content/uploads/sites/1334/2024/08/EnriquesOne.pdf . Opened and read Lemma 4.7.8, exact sequence (4.7.4), and Theorem 4.7.9 with the characteristic-zero portion of its proof, printed pp. 445–447 (PDF pp. 460–462, zero-based numbering). These are exactly the vanishing and localization statements used above.
- Xiao, G. (1991). *Elliptic surfaces with fixed jacobian* (MPI/91-30). Max-Planck-Institut für Mathematik. https://archive.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/id/eprint/2953/1/preprint_1991_30.pdf . Opened the introduction, the local fibre-twist construction, and §4, equation (4); the latter independently states the needed surjectivity in the nontrivial-Jacobian case.

These URLs and complete bibliographic metadata are already retained by AB in `AB_push_references.bib`; no duplicate keys are required.

## Detailed development record: E_A4_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent referee note on A.4.1–A.4.4

**Verdict.** No gap was found in the integral topology or the monodromy block
of A.4.1–A.4.4, conditional on the actual families and normalization morphisms
constructed in A.2.2. In particular, the node generization is unimodular, the
Thom and Mayer–Vietoris images have the claimed integral saturation, and the
nonzero monodromy Smith factors are exactly `1,1,1,1,1,11`. This review does
not extend its verdict to other parts of A or B. No changes were made to
`AB_push.tex`.

The check below uses the displayed local charts and integral intersection
data directly. It does not accept the rank computation in the AB program as
a substitute for the geometric identifications. The independent program
`code/e_push_A4_referee.py` uses the odd unimodular blow-up lattice of rank ten,
rather than the E8 basis used by the AB program. Its output is retained as
`.out` and `.json`.

## 1. Exceptional fibres and their node generization

Write the base normalization coordinates as `(X,z,v)` and blow up `(X,zv)`.
The two charts are `X=zvA` and `zv=XB`. In the second chart the chosen small
resolution has charts

\[
 (z,a,B):\quad X=za,\quad v=aB,
 \qquad
 (X,d,v):\quad z=Xd,\quad B=dv,
 \quad ad=1.
\]

Over the base node the fibre is a chain `C0 ∪ Cs`. In the `(z,a,B)` chart,
`C0` is `z=a=0` with coordinate `B`; it is completed by the original `A`
chart. The small exceptional curve `Cs` is `z=B=0` with coordinate `a`; it
is completed by the `d` chart. They meet once, at `z=a=B=0`.

On the branch `v=0, z=t≠0`, the generic exceptional fibre has `a=0`.
Its closure over `t=0` is `C0`, with multiplicity one. On the branch
`z=0, v=t≠0`, its closure has equation `aB=t`. Its central fibre is
`C0+Cs`, again with both multiplicities one. Consequently a cohomology class
whose degrees on `(C0,Cs)` are `(r,s)` has generic degrees `(r,r+s)` on the
two branches. This proves the displayed generization matrix

\[
 \begin{pmatrix}1&0\\1&1\end{pmatrix}
\]

by actual specializations of fibres. The other small-resolution choice
interchanges the branches and gives the same integral conclusion.

The stalks of `R²h_*Z` are therefore one copy of Z on the smooth part of
the nodal curve and two copies at its node; the displayed generization is an
integral isomorphism to the two branch values. Complex orientation makes the
rank-one local system on the smooth part constant. Thus this sheaf is the
normalization pushforward `η_*Z_(P1)`, not a constant sheaf on the nodal curve.
This distinction is essential: replacing it by the latter would create a
spurious odd cohomology group.

Proper base change, or the local toric retractions in the displayed charts,
identifies these stalks with fibre cohomology. Every fibre is connected and
has no positive cohomology other than H², so `R⁰h_*Z=Z` and there are no other
positive direct-image rows. The two Leray rows are in even degrees and free.
There are no possible differentials or torsion extensions. This yields

\[
 b(R^\pm)=(1,0,12,0,12,0,1).
\]

For the fundamental group, a direct general-position argument supplements
the local retractions. The nodal centre has real dimension two in the
six-manifold R, so loops and their null-homotopies avoid it. Hence `R\C` is
simply connected. Every loop in the smooth `R^±` can avoid its exceptional
divisor, whose real dimension is four. The complement is `R\C`; thus the
whole normalization is simply connected as well.

## 2. Primitive integral lattices

Let `L=H₂(S;Z)` with its unimodular intersection pairing. The data used are

\[
 F^2=Fm=Fv=0,\quad m^2=-2,\quad v^2=-6,\quad mv=-1,
 \quad OF=Om=1,\quad Ov=0,\quad O^2=-1.
\]

The plane generated by `(F,O)` has determinant −1, so it splits integrally.
Put `n=m−F`. Then `n,v` belong to its orthogonal complement, a negative E8
lattice, and their Gram matrix is

\[
 \begin{pmatrix}-2&-1\\-1&-6\end{pmatrix},\qquad \det=11.
\]

The span `A=<n,v>` is primitive: the square of a nontrivial saturation index
would divide 11. Therefore `<F,m,v>=ZF⊕A` is primitive in L. Unimodularity
and the dual of the free quotient show that

\[
 L\longrightarrow\mathbb Z^3,\qquad a\longmapsto(aF,av,am)
\]

is onto, with no hidden finite index. This also shows that `<F,m>`, `<F,v>`
and the indicated rank-one quotient images are primitive.

The section intersection data are consistent with the elliptic-surface
input: `O·6P=2` and `P·6P=2` give `Ov=0`, `v²=−6`, and `mv=−1` for
`v=6P−O−3F`. The translation formula sends O to `6P`, fixes F and the
seven reducible-fibre root classes, and preserves the pairing. It gives
`T*m=m−F`, as used below.

## 3. Thom maps and the complement Z

Use integral degree-two cohomology coordinates `(α,h,a)` on `R^+`, where
H restricts to zero on the first section and to `−m` on the second, and A
is the exceptional divisor. Its restriction is F on the modified first
section and zero on the second. Thus the combined restriction is exactly

\[
 (\alpha,h,a)\longmapsto(\alpha+aF,\alpha-hm).
\]

Its cokernel is `L/<F,m>`, obtained by taking the difference of the two
components. Since `<F,m>` is primitive, this map has primitive image of
rank 12. These restrictions are the Poincaré duals of the degree-four Thom
maps for the removed sections. They give `H₄(Z)=0` and
`H₃(Z)=L/<F,m>≅Z⁸`.

There is also a useful explicit description of the degree-two Thom map.
The two conductor divisor classes are

\[
 D'_0=H+m-A,\qquad D_\infty=H.
\]

Write a degree-two homology class in `R^+` as `(b,λ,η)`, dual to the stated
cohomology coordinates. Its two section intersections are

\[
 (bm+\lambda-\eta,\lambda).
\]

This map is onto `Z²`; its kernel is

\[
 H_2(Z)=\{(b,0,bm):b\in L\}\simeq L.
\]

This description supplies an integral splitting without relying merely on
the rank of the sequence. The degree-six Thom map is the primitive diagonal
`Z→Z²`, giving `H₅(Z)=Z`. The remaining asserted groups follow immediately
from the same exact sequence.

## 4. Boundary Gysin and Mayer–Vietoris maps

The first normal circle bundle `π:E→S` has Euler class `n=m−F`, a primitive
class. The integral Gysin sequence gives

\[
 H_2(E)=n^\perp,\quad H_3(E)=L/\mathbb Zn,
 \quad H_1(E)=H_4(E)=0,\quad H_5(E)=\mathbb Z.
\]

All identifications here use the complex normal orientation. The plumbing
identifies the second boundary using T on S and reverses this circle
orientation, exactly as required by `xy=u`.

For `a∈n^⊥`, put `(s,t,h)=(aF,av,am)`, so `h=s`. In `H₂(R^+)` the
normalization difference is

\[
 ((I-T)a,\ h-s,\ s).
\]

It belongs to the displayed `H₂(Z)` kernel since
`m·(I−T)a=aF`. Under `H₂(Z)≅L`, the actual degree-two Mayer–Vietoris map
is therefore

\[
 a\longmapsto(I-T)a=-s v+(t-3s)F.
\]

Surjectivity of the three intersection coordinates shows that `(s,t)` is
arbitrary on `n^⊥`. Its image is exactly the primitive span `<F,v>`, and
its kernel is `K={a:aF=av=am=0}`. This verifies the claimed saturation
geometrically, rather than deriving it from an abstract matrix of the same
rank.

For H₃, a tube from the first boundary maps to `a mod<F,m>`; one from the
second boundary maps with the opposite sign in the cokernel of the combined
restriction. The gluing reverses the normal circle and covers T. Consequently
the difference map has image `(T−I)L mod<F,m>=Zv` up to the harmless global
sign set by the boundary convention. This image is primitive, because
`<F,m,v>` is primitive. Thus the H₃ exact sequence in A.4.2 is an integral
exact sequence of free groups.

The H₅ difference is zero: the normal-circle reversal cancels the fact that
the two boundary fundamental classes sum to zero in Z. The H₀ difference
is also zero. The remaining terms now give

\[
 b(X(u))=(1,1,8,14,8,1,1),
\]

and all groups are torsion free. In particular the Euler number is 2.

## 5. The coefficient of the tube map and the factor 11

The geometric coefficient in the monodromy map is one. To see this without
assuming a Picard–Lefschetz normalization, the trace of the free oriented
circle action on E is the composite

\[
 H_2(E)\xrightarrow{\pi_*}H_2(S)
             \xrightarrow{\pi^!}H_3(E).
\]

Locally this is the product of the projected two-cycle with a circle making
one complete turn. The orientation-preserving circle action patches these
local products, proving the identity integrally. Under the Gysin descriptions
it sends `a∈n^⊥` to `a mod Zn`. A positive circuit of u in `xy=u` makes
exactly one such turn. Hence the neck variation on the quotient K is

\[
 a\longmapsto a\bmod\langle F,m,v\rangle,
\]

with the sign convention stated in A.4.2. It vanishes on the left-hand tube
subgroup. No multiple arising from a normal-bundle Euler class is present.
The same trace description is zero in other degrees, by the Gysin groups
already computed. It also makes `N²=0` immediate.

Finally split `L=<F,O>⊕E8(−1)`. Then

\[
 K=\mathbb ZF\oplus A^\perp,\qquad
 L/\langle F,m,v\rangle=\mathbb Z[O]\oplus(E_8(-1)/A).
\]

N kills the F summand and is the natural inclusion
`A^⊥→E8(−1)/A` on the other six generators. Since A is primitive in a
unimodular lattice, `E8(−1)/(A+A^⊥)` has order `|det A|=11`. It is cyclic
because 11 is prime. This proves the rank-six map has nonzero Smith factors
`1,1,1,1,1,11`, independently of any choice of E8 basis. The separate exact
program verifies the same statement in the integer lattice `I_(1,9)` and
records every basis and quotient matrix it uses.

## 6. Nearby Euler function and the non-Kähler conclusion

At a smooth point of the zero divisor, the fixed nonzero-u equation `xy=uz`
is a graph. Its sufficiently small local fibre contracts to its origin by
scaling x,y and the transverse coordinate, with z scaling quadratically.
At the old node, `xy=uzv` is a quadratic cone for fixed u, and its intersection
with a sufficiently small ball contracts by simultaneous radial scaling.
These facts justify the pointwise Euler value one at both kinds of zero,
rather than only the integrated contribution of their strata. Away from
the zero divisor the annulus has Euler number zero. Since the smooth part
of the nodal curve is `C*`, its integral contribution is zero even though
its pointwise value is one. The node contributes one.

For the semistable model the transverse equation is `xy=u` at every point
of the double locus, so its local nearby Euler value is zero everywhere
there. This yields `χ(R^±)−2χ(S)=26−24=2`. These counts agree with the
independent normalization and exceptional-fibre stratifications.

A global section of `η_*Z_(P1)` has equal values on the two branches at the
node. By the verified generization, its degree on `Cs` is zero. Pullbacks
from R also have degree zero on `Cs`, and Leray exhausts H². The small curve
is therefore null in torsion-free integral H₂. This independently proves
that `R^±` cannot be Kähler. The smooth fibres are not Kähler because `b₁=1`.
Thus the paper correctly declines to apply the classical Kähler/projective
Clemens–Schmid comparison. The stated filtration of the computed rational
monodromy representation has ranks `6,2,6`; it is explicitly not asserted to
be a polarized limiting mixed Hodge structure.

## 7. Scope of the verdict

The review verifies A.4.1, A.4.2, A.4.3 and A.4.4 using the actual A.2.2
normalization and semistable plumbing. It does not infer the existence of
that global model merely from the local rank computations. The existence
and proper gluing of A.2.2 are premises of this focused referee check. The
statement about the sign of N is read with the explicit neck and boundary
orientation conventions in A.4.2; changing them changes an overall sign,
not any group, rank, or Smith invariant.

# Independent referee: B.4.17

15 November 2026. Source read in full: `sections/B_monodromy_source_counterexample.tex`. Verdict: **PASS** at its stated scope.

The logarithmic transition formula defines a genuine holomorphic principal elliptic bundle: logarithm changes lie in the period lattice, and the line-bundle cocycle identities give the elliptic cocycle identity. The compact blow-up base and compact fibre make the total space compact; the local product construction makes it smooth and Hausdorff. The map to the second projective line has only the one stated reduced crossing fibre.

The Chern vectors on the strict transform, exceptional curve, and ordinary ruling are respectively 1, i, and 1+i. The last yields the multiplier exp(2πi·i/(1+i))=−exp(−π); the first two give exp(−2π), after reversing the cyclic generator in the second description. The canonical section of the exceptional line trivializes it on the entire complement of the zero fibre. Consequently the family there is a holomorphic product, not merely a differentiable bundle with identity linear monodromy.

The two fundamental-group maps from the crossing elliptic curve are the quotient maps by Z and by Zi. Their pushout is trivial. The degree-one Mayer–Vietoris map has determinant one, giving H1=H2=0; the degree-two elliptic class gives the additional H3 summand, so H3=Z³ and H4=Z². The total-space check b2=1 is also correct: the two independent Chern vectors kill two of the three base H2 classes, and the fibre H2 term has injective d2. No higher differential changes total degree two.

The original arXivv1 PDF was independently opened at https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.14420, especially equation (3.9) and §3.3, Corollary 3.7. The two successive inequalities do not make equality in the second imply equality in the first. The counterexample directly disproves that converse under the compact smooth hypotheses. The irreducible-fibre proof remains separate and valid. This referee did not independently obtain the complete published PDF; the source note appropriately distinguishes the indexed published excerpt from the version read in full.

No conclusion about a prescribed S6 completion follows from this example, and no such conclusion is asserted in the reviewed proposition.

## Detailed development record: E_B43_degree_one_referee.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Independent referee of the degree-one normal-crossing Hodge obstruction

**Verdict: the proposed stronger lemma is valid for proper reduced analytic normal-crossing spaces, including self-crossings.** If every connected component of the smooth normalization satisfies the partial-partial-bar lemma, the natural map `H¹(D,C) -> H¹(D,O_D)` is surjective. No Hodge assumption on the double or higher branch intersections is required in this degree.

## Branch resolutions

Use the smooth proper spaces D^[p] of ordered (p+1)-tuples of distinct **local** branches through a point, with their common intersection and finite projection to D. This differs from indexing only by distinct global irreducible components: that latter indexing would miss an irreducible self-crossing. The normal-crossing coordinate models make the ordered branch spaces smooth. Distinct local branches cannot merge into one local branch at a limit point in a normal-crossing space; the finite branch construction is proper. The symmetric group acts by permuting labels. Over C, the alternating projector is available.

The augmented alternating complexes of pushed-forward constant sheaves and of holomorphic function sheaves resolve C_D and O_D, respectively. This is a stalkwise assertion. At a coordinate-crossing point, the constant complex is the augmented simplex complex on the local branches. For functions, it is the exact alternating restriction complex for the union of coordinate hyperplanes and their intersections. This is the usual coordinate-ideal inclusion-exclusion resolution, and can also be proved inductively from `0 -> O_(A union B) -> O_A direct-sum O_B -> O_(A intersection B) -> 0`. These local calculations are compatible with permutations, so no global ordering or simplicity of the crossings is assumed.

Resolve the branch terms by their smooth Dolbeault and complex de Rham complexes. Their pushed-forward form sheaves are fine, using partitions of unity pulled back from the compact analytic base. Hypercohomology is therefore computed by the corresponding total complexes of global forms. Projection of a complex differential form to its (0,*) part realizes the natural map from the constant to the coherent resolution.

## Degree-one cocycle calculation

With the sign convention `delta alpha = bar-partial f`, a Dolbeault total degree-one cocycle is a pair alpha in A^(0,1)(D^[0]) and alternating f in A^(0,0)(D^[1]), satisfying `bar-partial alpha=0`, `delta alpha=bar-partial f`, and `delta f=0`.

On each normalization component, partial alpha is partial-exact and bar-partial-closed. The partial-partial-bar lemma supplies a smooth function h with `partial alpha=partial bar-partial h`. Subtracting the total coboundary of h replaces alpha by `alpha'=alpha-bar-partial h` and f by `f'=f-delta h`. Then alpha' is a d-closed pure (0,1)-form and `delta alpha'=bar-partial f'`.

Applying partial gives `partial bar-partial f'=0`, since holomorphic face pullbacks commute with partial and partial alpha'=0. On every compact connected smooth component of D^[1], a complex-valued pluriharmonic function is constant: apply the maximum principle to its real and imaginary parts in local holomorphic coordinates. This also covers the zero-dimensional components. Consequently `d f'=0` and `delta alpha'=0`, while `delta f'=0` remains true.

The same pair (alpha',f') is now a cocycle of total degree one in the complex de Rham branch resolution, and its (0,*) projection is the original coherent class after the stated coboundary. This proves surjectivity with no unresolved differential or spectral-sequence degeneration claim.

## Application and scope

Combining this lemma with B.4.2 and upper semicontinuity gives the proposed obstruction: a semistable six-sphere centre has H¹(D,C)=0 but h¹(D,O_D)>=1, so at least one smooth normalization component fails the partial-partial-bar lemma. In particular an all-Moishezon normalization is impossible, since smooth compact Moishezon manifolds satisfy that lemma. This application does not establish that the unknown vertical components are Moishezon. It strengthens the previous SNC-only all-strata condition precisely in total degree one.

The proof is elementary once the explicitly described normal-crossing resolutions are fixed; it does not apply to arbitrary singularities, pinch points, or nonreduced centres without further arguments.

# Author's adversarial audit of B.4.33

**Verdict: the full proof passes the checks below.** This is classification_e's own audit of the assembled argument, not an additional independent method or independent referee. The separate root, boundary_d, and limits_ab reviews provide the independent source checks.

## 1. Scope of the hypothetical filling

An arbitrary proper Hausdorff analytic filling has a unique main component containing the smooth connected punctured family. Its reduction, normalization, and resolution remain proper and unchanged on that puncture. The parameter is a nonzero divisor on the resulting smooth dominating fourfold. The revised B.4.4 marked-map proof uses no reducedness of its centre, and the revised B.4.31 retains all central multiplicities. Thus the initial reduction does not assume the desired semistable filling exists, and no higher-dimensional semistable-reduction theorem enters.

After resolving the marked graph, every three-dimensional fibre of the map to P1×Delta must contain a whole central divisor component. Its marked base value is therefore one of finitely many constants. Shrinking near the cusp excludes all such values except the cusp. The coefficient-ideal argument then applies with modification centres only over the origin. Finite q-root base change and normalization preserve fibre dimension.

## 2. Uniform finite traces on the entire modified base

Equidimensionality supplies finite local surface slices. Their normalizations are Cohen–Macaulay and finite-flat over the smooth base. The compact set K=beta^{-1}(0) includes the no-blowup case, and a finite neighbourhood cover of K covers the whole inverse image of a smaller bidisc by properness. This is why one finite collection of trace degrees suffices.

Resolving the full boundary over a relatively compact neighbourhood of K gives finitely many divisor multiplicities. A product of their common multiple with lcm(1,...,d_max) uniformly bounds local cyclic splitting and semistable roots. These roots are local transverse covers; no global root along the exceptional tree is needed. Other branch curves meet a boundary curve in isolated points unless equal to it. Section lifts through a proper modification are meromorphic from smooth surfaces and have only codimension-two indeterminacy. After shrinking the relevant compact neighbourhood, the exceptional points are finite and are handled by the separately proved Hartogs extension of A-sections.

## 3. The exact logarithmic sheaf, including the unknown r axis

Full-rank exceptional and q-axis monodromy gives the primitive cocharacter kernel <w,delta>, so the saturated closed logarithmic forms have integral local residues. At the strict r axis, the kernel is larger; the proof does not silently replace it by <w,delta>. B.4.31 instead forces every cap angular meridian into the primitive delta line. The first exponential primitive is therefore a unit and the second a boundary monomial times a unit. This is precisely M_(Dq+E)^gp × M_(Dq+Dr+E)^gp, rather than the larger unrestricted character sheaf. Norms preserve zero first-character order at Dr.

Only pairwise differences of cap-extending traces are extended. They glue a locally trivial associated-log-torus torsor Q on all of S; the cap itself is not claimed to be a log-group model. H1(S,A)=0 then yields a global origin h whose ordinary restriction belongs to the actual D-fold torsor.

## 4. The two different origin comparisons

Near Dr, h differs from a multiplied cap trace by an A-section whose radial loop is delta-only. B.4.32 therefore gives zero projected radial defect for h relative to the B.4.19 ordinary origin. The actual ordinary-to-rotating coordinate change is G(s)+6v delta, also delta-only in the radial direction. Only after this correction does the proof use the rotating origin o and the exact equality sigma−o=F.

Along Dq, sigma is an actual q-unit origin of the prescribed cusp. The finite norm argument can be performed by projecting the modified cap to that fan presentation; it does not require a global holomorphic lift of sigma through every modification. Thus D sigma is an actual section of Q on Br. The q-ghost injection diag(12m,12m) kills the radial Y-connecting class of h−D sigma. Although the w and delta cocharacter loops are discarded on exponentiation, the gamma evaluation used in the contradiction annihilates them, so no relevant information is lost.

## 5. Final integer and finite-cover normalization

The equality (h−D o)−(h−D sigma)=D F is an equality in the actual common Jacobian. A positive R loop with r=R^n gives F(s,v+n)−F(s,v)=n gamma−6n(n−1)delta. The invariant gamma covector therefore evaluates the right side to Dn, while both left-side terms evaluate to zero. All trace, root, and norm degrees are positive and cannot annihilate this integer. No division in a torsion group or unproved cancellation of essential translations occurs.

The existing exact programs for period differences and global loop defects were rerun and returned PASS. They validate algebraic identities only; the geometric construction is proved in the source.

## 6. Stress test and source limits

The untwisted Engel family has the same relevant linear local-system calculations but identity affine closing, zero axial origins, and F=0. Hence the contradiction's nonzero affine-clutching premise is absent, and its known filling is consistent with the argument. This is an essential test that the proof does not derive noncompletion from linear monodromy alone.

All outside inputs are the specifically opened primary theorems recorded in the preceding sections. No source was found that already states this arbitrary noncompletion conclusion for the actual family; no priority claim is made. The explicit essential-translation counterexample B.4.29 is retained because it explains why the global loop invariant is necessary.

# Independent referee note on B.4.4

**Verdict: the final written proof in `B_marked_map_extension.tex` is valid in its stated meromorphic scope.** It proves extension of the marked map and a proper projective graph modification. It does not prove extension of any punctured comparison with an auxiliary threefold model.

The relative line bundle restricts to K⁻² on each smooth fibre. The audited canonical formula makes this f*O(1), and connected fibres give exactly two global sections. Constant h⁰ on the punctured disc supplies Grauert base change there. The direct image across zero is coherent and torsion-free, hence locally free of rank two; a frame gives two holomorphic evaluation sections. This does not require base change at the central fibre.

After removing the common divisorial factor in the regular local ring, the graph incidence equation in a neighbourhood U times the whole P¹ has a unique dominating component, the closure of its graph on the dense base-point-free open subset of U. It is locally a hypersurface. It can have more than one local analytic branch at a singular graph point; the proof does not require local irreducibility there. The stated maximal-Jacobian-minor construction detects exactly the nonsubmersion locus, including singular graph points, because it is applied to the hypersurface equation together with the two target coordinates in a smooth ambient space.

Proper direct image of the coherent structure sheaf of this bad locus gives its analytic image. The constant section 1 shows equality of support and image, without assuming cohomology-and-base-change for that sheaf. After removing vertical curve components, the remaining curve is proper and quasi-finite over the disc, hence finite. Intrinsic labels (multiplicities three and four and the reduced cusp fibre) prevent permutation of the three punctured branches. Their normalizations therefore have degree one and extend each critical value meromorphically through zero, even when values collide.

The cross-ratio projective transformation consequently has meromorphic coefficients and restores precisely the original marked fibration. Clearing powers of the parameter constructs its graph by the same two-section method. Its projection is proper projective and is an isomorphism over the punctured disc. No resolution, Kähler hypothesis, central base-change isomorphism, or Moishezon assertion is used. No remaining gap was found.

# Independent referee of B.4.9

## Verdict

PASS for the exact scope of `ab_push_torus_log_cocycles.tex`: fixed-cocharacter logarithmic cocycles, their zero ordinary torsor class, the full punctured cusp torsor group, and the actual affine section. No arbitrary compactification or classification of all descent data follows, and the source correctly makes neither claim.

## Positive-loop sign and actual section

The period convention is Pi(c+h)=Pi(c) S_h, where S_h=I+h delta tensor gamma. A translation F_h,c from X_c to X_(c+h) has derivative the identity in the covering vector coordinates and acts on marked lattice columns by S_h inverse. After w=r^6, a positive r-loop changes c by -12. The descent deck on the logarithmic cover is therefore F_(-12,c), whereas the return identification after transport is F_(12,c-12). The latter gives the actual homology monodromy S_(-12)=I-12 delta tensor gamma. This distinguishes the deck identification from the closing return and validates the displayed N_r sign.

Substitute Pi_c gamma=(6mu,-s+C0+c) and ell=-s/12+kappa0 in C.3.5. The s terms cancel exactly, giving the bounded cusp logarithm

    bar_b(h,c)=(-h*mu/2, -h*(c+D)/12 - h*h/24),
    D=C0-12*kappa0.

The proposed section

    sigma(c)=(-c*mu/2, -c*c/24 - c*D/12)

satisfies sigma(c+h)-sigma(c)=bar_b(h,c) as an exact equality of vector coordinates. In particular its positive-loop difference is (6mu,c+D-6), precisely F_(-12,c). All coefficients are single-valued holomorphic functions of q, including at q=0. The original cusp's affine q defect is zero. Thus the q and r affine lifts commute already before quotienting by periods, and sigma is an actual holomorphic section over the punctured parameter product. Its exponential gives extending toric units for each nonzero r. Quadratic logarithmic dependence on r is retained, so no extension across r=0 is inferred.

## Linear and sheaf computations

A holomorphic group homomorphism C* to a varying complex torus lifts to t times a vector in the relative Lie bundle. Periodicity makes the vector a lattice period; its integral coefficients are locally constant. The common invariant lattice for the actual q and r monodromies is exactly Z*w plus Z*delta. Exponentiation gives the two asserted monomials. Extending torus-unit multiplication preserves every fan orbit and component, so it cannot change the previously computed component permutation.

The product of two punctured discs retracts to T2. The last cellular differential for the commuting local system has image N_q Lambda+N_r Lambda. Since N_q already has primitive image Z*w plus Z*delta, its quotient is free on gamma,u and is Z2. The analytic exponential sequence and Cartan theorem B on this Stein base give the asserted H1 of the torus-section sheaf. Naturality sends a Cartier Chern class times either invariant cycle into zero in this quotient. This proves only the fixed-cocharacter construction's zero class. The independent explicit sigma proves the actual affine family's zero class.

## Source and verification scope

The local polynomial calculation is checked separately in `ab_push_hopf_winding.py`. This referee uses the actual closing formula C.3.5 and the integral monodromy conventions, not an inference from linear monodromy alone. Fujimoto's one-dimensional formula is used as the stated model; the proof itself supplies the higher-dimensional group-homomorphism argument and retains the non-polarized case.

# Independent referee note on B.4.3

**Verdict: the stated obstruction and its carefully restricted scope follow from the cited primary statements.** This review does not extend the result to arbitrary self-crossing analytic central fibres with merely prescribed normalization strata, and does not assert that every unknown vertical component is Moishezon.

## Primary-source checks

Chen, K.-W. (2026), *On the Hodge Structures of Global Smoothings of Normal Crossing Varieties*, arXiv:2404.19229v2, was opened at the version-specific arXiv HTML URL. The PDF fetch failed in this browser tool; the complete version-specific primary HTML was accessible. Definition 2.3 imposes K1 on every nonempty intersection of irreducible components, including individual components. In Theorem 2.4 the K1 qualifier appears explicitly between parts (2) and (3), and applies to both parts (3) and (4). Part (4) asserts E1 degeneration for the nearby ordinary Hodge–de Rham sequence, after shrinking the parameter disc. Neither the stronger K2 hypothesis nor a local invariant-cycle hypothesis is needed for this particular conclusion. Those extra conditions concern stronger Hodge-theoretic conclusions elsewhere in the source.

Graf, P., & Kovács, S. J. (2014), *Potentially Du Bois spaces*, Journal of Singularities, 8, 117–134, DOI 10.5427/jsing.2014.8i, was opened in the original author-hosted journal PDF. Section 1.D, printed page 120, states that proper Du Bois varieties have the canonical surjection from singular to coherent cohomology and lists normal-crossings singularities among Du Bois singularities. The statement uses properness; projectivity of the central fibre or total space is unnecessary. The conclusion applies to self-intersections of an algebraic component as well, since the Du Bois property is local and the singularities are normal crossings.

Raw search/open records are retained in `evidence/E_C_search17.json` through `E_C_search20.json`. The existing root bibliography keys and metadata were independently checked against the primary results.

## Mathematical derivation

Conditional on the proved specialization-surjection statement B.4.2, simple connectivity of the smooth six-sphere fibre gives H¹(D;C)=0. The root-curve direct images O⊕O(−1) give h¹(X,O)=1 by the Leray edge sequence, because the weighted projective line has no H¹ or H² of O and O(−1) has no section. Proper flat upper semicontinuity therefore gives h¹(D,O_D)≥1. Flatness follows directly from the reduced normal-crossings local equation of the central divisor in the smooth total space.

The independent lower-bound argument also checks: H² and H⁴ of S⁶ vanish, so c₁=c₂=0 and analytic Riemann–Roch gives χ(O)=0. A holomorphic top form is closed, exact by H³(S⁶;C)=0, and has zero L² norm by Stokes; hence h⁰(K)=h³(O)=0. Consequently h¹(O)=1+h²(O)≥1. This avoids any inference from tangent-bundle cohomology.

The algebraic contradiction follows from the proper Du Bois surjection in degree one. For a simple-NC central fibre satisfying K1, Chen's Theorem 2.4(4) gives b₁=h⁰(Ω¹)+h¹(O) on a nearby fibre, contradicting b₁=0 and h¹(O)≥1.

If all components are Kähler, their closed smooth strata are Kähler. If all are Moishezon, take a projective modification of a component; an inverse-image component dominating a closed stratum is projective, and general relative hyperplane sections give a generically finite projective cover of the stratum. Meromorphic function fields then have the same transcendence degree, proving that the stratum is Moishezon. Smooth Moishezon manifolds satisfy the ddbar lemma; Chen §2.1 explicitly recalls the dominance implication. Thus the two component hypotheses really imply K1.

Finite base change leaves the nearby fibre biholomorphism type unchanged. Every contradiction therefore applies for all positive exponents. No gap was found in this stated scope.

## Detailed development record: E_B_log_torsor_working.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Actual axial origins and the restricted log-torsor obstruction

Owner: classification_e. 15 November 2026, 20:36 UTC. **Historical working proof, superseded at 20:46 UTC by the independently refereed B.4.24 in `B_log_torsor_obstruction.tex`.** Root and boundary_d opened the original KKN definitions and passed the full source. The final version explicitly works with the associated log-torus sheaves; a chosen fan model itself need not be a group object or torsor. This note retains the conditional stages of the audit. The result concerns only locally trivial torsors under the specific KKN log complex torus, including after finite Kummer base changes. It does not exclude arbitrary proper analytic caps, modifications of the base, or non-Kähler vertical threefolds.

## 1. The log group and the exact overlap

Let q=Q^12, let `B=Δ_Q×Δ_r`, `B_q=Δ_Q*×Δ_r`, `B_r=Δ_Q×Δ_r*`, and `U=B_q∩B_r`. The Jacobian log torus A has multiplicative pairing

`[[qA(q), a(q)^6], [a(q)^−1, q r^12 D(q)]]`

in characters `(Z,W)` and period lattice `Y=Zu⊕Zγ`. Its nondegenerate/admissible nature and proper models are checked in `E_B_general_completion_attempt.md`, using the original KKN Definitions 1.2.3, 1.2.6, 1.3.4 and Theorems 5.2.1, 5.3.2. The names A(q),a(q),D(q) in this matrix denote holomorphic units, not the log group itself.

The auxiliary translation is

`F=(μ(s+12v)/2, C(s+12v)/12−(s+12v)^2/24+6v)`,

where `s=log q/(2πi)`, `v=log r/(2πi)`. Its exact differences are

`F(s+12,v)−F(s,v)=γ−6δ`,

`F(s,v+1)−F(s,v)=γ`.

Thus on U it is a well-defined ordinary A-section. For the multiplicative presentation `0→Y→T→A→0`, where `T=(C*)²` on U, the connecting class of this section has Y-monodromy γ around both Q and r. The δ term disappears in T, as it must.

It is essential to distinguish this from the raw actual overlap. B.4.19 proves

`F−σ−kvδ=G(s)+6vδ`,

with `k=−s+12κ0` and `G=(μs/2,Cs/12−s²/24)`. Consequently the raw comparison after the ordinary gauge has only 6δ r-monodromy. Studying F as though it were that raw comparison is invalid. The following use of F is justified only if the two **extending log-group origins** in §3 are actually established.

## 2. A precise ghost-valuation lemma

On the underlying analytic site, KKN's definition gives the exact sequence

`0→Y→T_log^(Y)→A→0`,

where the middle sheaf consists of bounded multiplicative log characters. Along `D_Q={Q=0}⊂B_r`, evaluation of the characteristic monoid gives a homomorphism

`ord_Q:T_log^(Y)→i_*Z²`.

The composite with Y is `u↦(12,0)`, `γ↦(0,12)`. In particular it is injective. These are the actual valuations of the displayed pairing; bounded holomorphic units do not affect them.

If h is a section of A on B_r, choose local multiplicative log lifts. Their overlap differences are Y-periods representing `∂h∈H¹(B_r,Y)`. Applying ord_Q makes this cocycle a coboundary, the difference of the local integral ghost vectors. The restriction `H¹(B_r,Y)→H¹(D_Q,Y)` is an isomorphism, since B_r retracts onto D_Q, and the induced map to `H¹(D_Q,Z²)` is multiplication by diag(12,12). Hence `∂h=0`. In particular every such h has zero Y-monodromy around the r loop.

For a section h on B_q, its class in `H¹(B_q,Y)` also has zero r-loop component, because that loop contracts in B_q. It follows that F cannot be expressed on U as the difference of one A-section from B_q and one from B_r.

Consequently the A-torsor obtained by gluing **trivial A-torsors** on B_q and B_r by F is nontrivial on B minus the origin, and it has no extension which is an A-torsor locally trivial for the usual analytic topology near the origin. Such an extension would admit a local section; after shrinking the bidisc, that section would provide exactly the forbidden splitting. This conclusion does not require any computation of the full H¹ group.

The same calculation survives `q=Q^(12m), r=R^n` with positive m,n. The r-loop class becomes nγ, while the Q-ghost map on Y is diag(12m,12m), still injective. Thus finite Kummer covers do not trivialize this particular torsor obstruction. This statement is about finite Kummer covers of the fixed bidisc; no assertion about arbitrary log modifications is included.

## 3. The axial-origin application requiring independent verification

The candidate application to the actual family is based on the following two explicit identifications, rather than on F alone.

1. **Actual cusp axis B_r.** B.4.9 proves that σ is a global section of the actual affine family for r≠0, including q=0, and that exponentiating σ is a torus-unit automorphism of every cusp fan chart. Its coefficients are holomorphic at q=0. Therefore `ζ↦ζ−σ` should identify this axial log model with the group model of A, with the origin at the section σ. Normalized toric base change q=Q^12 retains that extending section and the same multiplicative period pairing.

2. **Rotating r-axis B_q.** B.4.18 constructs the rotating model as a finite free quotient of its q-root toric model. On the root cover the twelfth affine deck is the actual period γ−6δ; its multiplicative action is precisely the second lattice period. Normalized pullback by q=Q^12 should therefore recover the original root fan model. Its point `(Z,W)=(1,1)` extends in the toric chart over r=0 and supplies the group origin. This assertion is about the normalized pullback; the unnormalized fibre product at Q=0 must not be mistaken for it.

If both identifications are verified as log-group identifications with the stated global sections, the actual comparison `ζ↦ζ−σ+F` becomes translation by F between these origins. Section 2 then applies to the actual axial **log torsor**. Any other extending choice of origin differs by a section on its own axis, so cannot change the tested r-loop class. The ordinary gauge kvδ is not a section extending at q=0, and hence is not an allowed simultaneous change of both origins in this argument.

The two origin identifications have been sent to root and D for independent challenge. Until that verification is complete, this note is an exact conditional log-category calculation. Even if accepted, it would establish no unrestricted analytic nonexistence result: a proper cap with vertical components need not be a locally trivial log-A torsor, and no theorem supplies that implication under the allowed non-Kähler hypotheses.

## 4. Provenance and checks

- Kajiwara, T., Kato, K., & Nakayama, C. (2008). *Logarithmic abelian varieties, Part I: Complex analytic theory*. Journal of Mathematical Sciences, the University of Tokyo, 15, 69–193. Original PDF: https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/journal/pdf/jms150104.pdf. The quotient-sheaf definition is §1.3.1–1.3.4, printed pp.81–82. No general torsor-extension theorem from this source is invoked.
- Actual sections and overlap: `ab_push_torus_log_cocycles.tex` (B.4.9), `ab_push_corrected_cusp_overlap_working.md` (B.4.18), and `ab_push_global_partial.tex` (B.4.19). These were read in full for this check.
- `code/e_push_completion_diagnostics.py` checks the exact F differences, the full σ and kv cancellation, and the Jacobian valuation/unit matrices. It does not verify the sheaf geometry or assert an origin cap.

# Independent referee of C.3.12

**Verdict: the stated Hausdorff-target collapse theorem is proved by the written argument.** This is a theorem about the literal regular-fibre translational leaves. It does not prohibit a cusp quotient obtained after changing those leaves, and it does not affect the genuine smooth delta-circle quotient used in C.3.10.

The period formula is exact: the u column of `Z=sB0+C(q)` is `(s+C12(q), mu(q))`, so real translation by r u multiplies the two toric characters by `q^r exp(2πir C12(q))` and `exp(2πir mu(q))`. The cone over the edge joining 0 to e1 has character coordinates `x=z1`, `y=q/z1`, `z=z2`, giving `xy=q`, with z invertible. Thus the claimed local chart is the actual toric chart, with the period units retained.

For fixed x,z nonzero and 0<r<1, a path `(x,q/x,z)` approaching the dense component point P(x,z) is translated to a path approaching the double-curve point `Q(exp(2πir mu0)z)`. Equality of the h-images on each regular fibre, continuity, and uniqueness of limits in a Hausdorff space give the stated limiting equality. Comparing two r values yields invariance under `z -> exp(2πia mu0)z` for all real a, first for |a|<1 and then by iteration. Delta-circle translations supply arbitrary phases. Since Im(mu0) is nonzero, the combined multipliers exhaust C*. Hence the dense component image, and by density the entire irreducible W, is constant.

The separate variable-r argument for independence of x is valid: q_n=e^(-n), r_n=-lambda/n gives q_n^(r_n)=e^lambda, while both holomorphic-unit powers converge to one. The w direction supplies phases. In fact, the fixed-r limiting equality already proves independence of x, but retaining the separate argument creates no gap.

The displayed cusp value satisfies sin²(πmu0)=3 and is nonreal. The proof only needs its nonzero imaginary part, not the selected logarithm branch or numerical value. The raw-coordinate comparison at the end is also correct: `(Im Z)^(-1)(0,rT)=(-r,0)+O(T^(-1))`, whereas all paths converge to the same toric edge point.

## Provenance and correction of the attempt report

The supplied background already records nonextension of the fixed raw character coordinate: `background/notes/A1_notes.md`, candidate A1-1, and `background/S6_SURVEY_20261112.md`, item F9, give distinct limiting character values at one triple point. Those background notes are not treated as authoritative proofs; the local toric calculations above were independently checked. C.3.12 has the stronger stated scope of every Hausdorff-valued continuous map retaining the regular translational leaves. No novelty claim is made.

My earlier attempt report asserted an actual S6-to-S3 rank-three quotient and thereby identified a marked (3,4) attaching knot. That assertion is superseded and withdrawn. The abstract finite Seifert multiplicities and the algebraic (3,4) Milnor cover do not identify a marked global cusp knot or the annular twist's framing. The genuine delta quotient and C.3.10 remain valid.

Referee completed 15 November 2026, independently of the source's informal quotient assertion.

# Independent referee of C.3.13

**Verdict: the final written corrected Seifert comparison is valid, with the stated modification of the cusp leaves and no six-dimensional framing conclusion.**

For the weighted map, the numerator z2³ is R^12 t exp(24πiTheta), while z1⁴ is R^12(t−1) exp(24πiTheta). Therefore the displayed target map gives p=t exactly. The root-generator changes give cancelling phases: at the multiplicity-three centre, the change of F is cancelled by Theta increasing by 1/3; at the multiplicity-four centre, the change of G and Theta decreasing by 1/4 cancel in both coordinates. The omitted radial term would have been a genuine error; here it is retained as A_j and cancelled by d=−A_j. The finite root expressions thus extend smoothly with the actual affine deck rotations.

The cusp boundary extension uses the exact integral character. The q-meridian bounds the toric section z1=z2=1 and has character zero, so the extension class has no hidden meridian shift. An averaged integral-period closed one-form represents that class. Its contraction with the circle generator is constant by Cartan's formula and zero at a fixed point. An averaged primitive and a collar cutoff correct the boundary discrepancy without changing the class. Integration modulo Z yields a smooth invariant map on the actual smooth cusp body, equal to the original character on the specified outer collar. This argument makes no unjustified smoothness assumption about arbitrary functions in raw Hopf quotient coordinates.

The cusp algebra also checks: R_t=|q|^(1/12)S(q), and the q phases cancel precisely after setting vartheta=Theta−Re(s)/12. The resulting target solid-torus chart has the displayed smooth inverse; hence it is an actual marked chart, not a multiplicity-only identification. Changing the original Mumford coordinate by a holomorphic base unit leaves the chosen bounding toric section and its zero character unchanged.

Finally, z2³−z1⁴ along a nearby regular weighted orbit has argument degree +12. Its zero is a simple complex-normal zero along the positive (3,4) torus knot, so that argument represents the positive meridian class. This proves the three-dimensional linking claim with its sign. It does not identify this target push-off with the normal framing of the six-dimensional orbit trace. The nonzero Euler correction in C.3.11 and the main monodromy value remain unresolved.

The relative homotopy uniqueness statement is also valid: both boundary and cusp quotient are connected, and restriction on integral H¹ is injective on the two marked period generators; hence H¹(Vbar,boundary Vbar;Z)=0. The statement concerns maps to S¹, not framed embeddings or ambient isotopies.

# Independent referee: C.3.16 and C.3.17

Reviewed on 2026-11-15 against the displayed sources, including the final explicit weighted Hopf overlap calculation in C.3.17.

## C.3.16: actual cusp linking — accepted

The central orbit cycle is compact and locally contractible, with integral top cohomology Z and multiplicity one at a generic smooth point. Alexander duality therefore identifies the complement's H1 with Z, generated by that point's positive normal meridian. The globally defined continuous function h = G2^3 - G1^4 has precisely that zero set. Its cusp expression is q times a nowhere-zero factor, so h/|h| evaluates to +1 on the local meridian and hence is the integral linking class. Evaluating the actual boundary expression gives the row (12, 0, 1) on (gamma, u, q). No smoothness of the descended map at fixed points is required. The distinction between this globally defined function and the merely meromorphic coordinate q is necessary and correctly retained.

## C.3.17: marked product cusp quotient — accepted

The fixed-fan deformation changes only bounded period units. Uniform invertibility, free deck translation on the fan, the finite fundamental collection of charts and compact central normalization give an actual proper smooth family after a uniform shrink. Its outer-collar transport fixes real marked period coordinates. A disjoint fixed tubular neighbourhood can independently carry unitary normal transport. Patching invariant horizontal fields preserves the parameter derivative and the boundary marking.

The zero-unit fibre is the specified small resolution of the product of two nodal Tate families. In the conifold chart u=xA, y=Av, quotient coordinates (x, Av, (|A|^2-|v|^2)/2) make projection to the first nodal family a submersion. The second chart gives the complementary check. The two fixed preimages of the base node are distinct, so they do not require a spurious identification of fixed charts. Away from those charts, the normal coordinate changes are diagonal units pulled from the projection base. The added Hermitian metric calculation makes their weighted Hopf real coordinates agree and makes the quotient overlaps smooth. This is a chosen valid Hopf smoothing, not a use of a generally false functorial smoothness statement for arbitrary equivariant maps.

The second Tate identity section avoids the node and lifts through the small resolution; it trivializes the oriented circle bundle. A positive vertical length form, normalized on each compact fibre, extends the exact marked boundary translation action. The cutoff of this action produces the actual annular map as an ambient isotopy endpoint, but the isotopy moves the fixed sphere. Thus it does not prove a relative isotopy or a vanishing monodromy class.

Finally the regular first elliptic fibre is a primitive generator of H2 of the nodal elliptic neighbourhood. Its product with the marked circle is the primitive H3 class. The gamma-u-w torus consequently does not bound inside the cusp quotient. This specifically prevents using an unproved local bounding chain to discard its stable framing contribution.

## Remaining scope

Neither proposition evaluates the cubic or Pontryagin characteristic number of the actual cap in C.3.14. The extension gives a loop of embedded fixed spheres and a concrete immersed trace, but an actual framed compression or an equivalent characteristic-class transgression is still required.

# Independent referee: C.3.18–C.3.19

Reviewed on 2026-11-15. Both statements pass at their expressly stated scopes.

## C.3.18: the actual bounding manifold and affine-frame defect

The product circle coordinate selected by the second Tate identity section agrees exactly with the marked gamma character on the outer collar. The rebuilt cusp map therefore glues to the actual exterior two-torus quotient. Its only critical model is (x,y,theta) -> (xy,theta), and the finite root charts remain submersions. The Milnor page of the nearby weighted knot meets the critical knot in twelve transverse positive points. Its inverse image is consequently an actual embedded positive genus-one Lefschetz four-manifold, with Euler characteristic twelve and the specified torus boundary.

The relative determinant calculation does not assume that the whole four-manifold is algebraic or globally complex. A compatible fibrewise elliptic complex structure and positive nodal charts provide a Hodge line on the base. Evaluation of the elliptic differential is nowhere zero in the relative dualizing line; in a node chart the identity dt wedge dx/x = -dx wedge dy explicitly extends this identification. The complex determinant, including its chosen boundary determinant frame, is thus pulled back from the base. Its relative square is zero. One boundary complex vector is outward pointing, so the relative top Chern number is the Euler characteristic. Hence the relative p1 evaluation is -24.

The final comparison retains the actual normal framing: along the boundary the page normal has q-angle equal to a constant minus twelve times gamma. Adding the hypersurface normal gives one SO(2) block of winding -12. This is null after stabilization, since its image in pi1(SO(5)) is the even class. Horizontal changes are contractible shears. The boundary orientation is (gamma,w,u), opposite the ordered marked (gamma,u,w) torus. Thus the stated defect and the equivalent reversed-orientation transgression are consistent. This does not yet compute a closed-cap characteristic pairing by itself.

## C.3.19: the actual cap Pontryagin pairing

The chosen stable frame on S2 times D4 and a global stable orbit frame have homotopic restrictions to P, since P retracts to S2 and pi2(SO)=0. In the actual affine nilmanifold frame the derivative is a shear, contractible relative both ends. The full change of frame remains in the clutching map A(rho_chi p)^(-1) A(p). Flattening the radial dependence of A preserves the identity at both ends.

The map Gamma on S1 times L is identity at the chosen circle basepoint. The relative H3 class is therefore precisely its H2(L) slant summand; surjectivity of H2(S1 times L) -> H2(L) removes any extra relative-lift ambiguity. Applying the primitive suspension of the actual universal p1 class gives the gamma-translation trace, with the sign fixed by the displayed inverse. This avoids silently replacing p1 suspension by a primitive generator, which would risk a factor of two.

The positive-q coefficient deck is (g,u,w,d,a) -> (g,u,w-u,d+g,a+1). Its invariant connection dd-a dg has curvature dg wedge da. Pairing the relative trace with that actual Euler class selects exactly the gamma-u-w period of the frame comparison; all other H3(L) components vanish in the pairing. Consequently its absolute value is the actual defect 24 from C.3.18, and Wall's formula gives absolute framing coordinate six.

## Remaining scope

The absolute-value statement does not by itself fix the sign relative to the cubic pairing. A common cut-and-reglue orientation must be used for both. Nor does it identify the actual delta line on the Lefschetz four-manifold with a Poincare line on a holomorphic Jacobian or torsor. Those comparisons are separate and are not assumed in either accepted result.

# Independent referee: the explicit eight-dimensional and direct-index method

## Verdict

C.3.25 and C.3.26 pass at their stated smooth, oriented, spin-c scope. The method supplies an actual coboundary and an integer-additive invariant independently of the Wall three-knot formula and the Davis generator comparison. It shares the actual cap and the separately proved numerical inputs p and k with the first method. It does not construct the specific analytic filling of Part B.

## Actual boundary and four-handle

The six-dimensional surgery cap is the actual fixed-knot exterior with the specified S2 times D4 piece attached. Its meridian line restricts to O(1) times D4. Its unit boundary contains the product Hopf S3 times D4, so attaching the product four-handle replaces that free piece by D4 times S3, exactly the actual fixed tube. The free bundle on the common exterior is identified by its Euler class. The remaining gauge mismatch on S3 times S2 has a global logarithm because H1 is zero, and the logarithm extends through a collar. Thus the construction identifies the actual boundary, rather than merely a sphere with a proposed invariant. The orientation is fixed by the actual circle-bundle identification; reversing the preceding meridian convention reverses p, k and the index together and does not affect the zero evaluation.

The added four-cycle is CP2, with its explicitly product-framed normal D4. It is not a four-sphere. This distinction gives p1[b]=3 and c^2[b]=1. The old four-cycle is PD(x) in the zero section, with two normal copies of the line; therefore a^2=k. Its intersection with CP2 is the meridian pairing one. The four-handle sequence and H3 of the cap equal to zero show that a,b form an integral basis. Hence Q=[[k,1],[1,0]], determinant -1, signature zero. The degree-two handle isomorphism gives the determinant extension c and proves w2=c mod2. The boundary has its unique spin structure.

## Characteristic arithmetic

On a, the tangent splitting gives p1=p+k and c^2=k. On b, the CP2 Euler sequence and product normal give p1=3 and c^2=1. Pairing the covectors with Q inverse gives p1^2=6p-3k, p1*c^2=p+k and c^4=k. The spin-c expression already derived in C.2.1 then simplifies exactly to (p-4k)/672. This can be checked without assigning a knot generator. At the actual six-loop values (-24 epsilon,-6 epsilon), the three terms are (-126 epsilon)/896, +(30 epsilon)/192 and (-6 epsilon)/384, whose sum is zero.

## Actual characteristic bordism and additivity

The cut-and-reglue construction has been expanded in C.3.20 and C.3.26. Take the Y-bundle over an annulus with monodromy the doubled relative diffeomorphism. Attach two N times I pieces over opposite outer boundary arcs, with opposite interval parametrizations. The remaining outer arcs give the twisted and identity doubles; the inner boundary is the mapping torus. The specified line lift extends over the annular bundle and agrees with the pullback lines on the attached pieces. The tangent bundle is the tangent bundle of this actual seven-manifold. Thus both x^3 and p1*x are compared, with the same map-independent orientation sign.

For two relative maps, the Y-bundle over a pair of pants exists because the surface fundamental group is free. The two specified line lifts define the total line bundle, and composition defines its outgoing lift. Bordism invariance makes x^3 and p1*x individually additive on the mapping tori. The annular bordism transfers this to the caps. The identity cap and identity mapping torus both have zero pairings. This proves more than additivity of their index combination and does not use an abstract three-knot composition law.

## Normalization independence

Equivariant isotopies yield diffeomorphic actual clutched pairs. The ambiguity in the inherited normal framing of the fixed S3 is a map to SO(3). Cutting out the common exterior compares the two surgery caps with the sphere bundle S(eta) over S4. Its vertical Euler class is 2x. The stable vertical identity gives p1=4x^2, so its contribution to p1*x-4x^3 is exactly zero. This proves invariance under the relevant normalizations without computing a generator of the framed-knot group. The twisted spin Dirac index supplies integrality. No division by six in Z/28 is used.

## Exact remaining limitation

The direct second method is independent in conversion to the boundary invariant and in the integer-level one-loop argument. Its p,k inputs are shared geometric inputs and are explicitly disclosed as such. The analytic B.4 filling, its c1 and c2, and its specified intersection data remain unconstructed.

# Independent signed-cap audit

The separate signed comparison, subsequently numbered C.3.22, passes independently of any proposed value of the actual delta-line square.

## Calibration and corrected diagnostic

An intermediate sign audit incorrectly treated a boundary frame as the same transition as a coordinate clutch. That produced a possible J=2 instead of the anticipated cancellation. This was a useful diagnostic, but the two transitions are inverses; no orientation may be selected merely to obtain a desired value.

The correction was checked directly with connections. Write the boundary frame as f=t B in a global frame. The connection flat in f has matrix -dB B^(-1). With p1(F)=-tr(F wedge F)/(8 pi^2), the corresponding relative boundary transgression is minus tr(B^(-1)dB)^3/(24 pi^2). By contrast, a mapping-torus coordinate clutch K has endpoint connection K^(-1)dK. Interpolating the connection t K^(-1)dK on an interval gives the positive transgression tr(K^(-1)dK)^3/(24 pi^2). The elementary line-bundle calibration agrees: the boundary frame exp(i n theta) on a disc has relative c1=n, while the same coordinate clutch on a mapping torus oriented with the interval first gives -n.

In D's convention t=f_aff A, the frame matrix is B=A^(-1). Since the four-manifold boundary is negative the ordered gamma-u-w torus, its relative Pontryagin number is -a0, where a0 is the ordered-torus period of the coordinate-clutch suspension of A. The annular derivative clutch is A(psi p)^(-1)A(p). Its relative trace is -dchi wedge beta. Pairing with the actual Euler curvature dg wedge da, with chi decreasing outward, also gives -a0. Thus the mapping-torus Pontryagin pairing equals the relative frame defect, with no independent sign choice.

The possible additional line-curvature term vanishes: it pairs the closed degree-three clutching class with the exact two-form Delta=deta on the closed double DP. The same geometric cut-and-reglue bordism carries both the stable tangent bundle and the actual line bundle. Consequently its one overall boundary sign multiplies both p1*x and x^3. The resulting formula is

J = epsilon_cap * (p_rel - 12*s_delta)/24.

This audit proves the sign relation only. The identification and evaluation of s_delta require the separate actual-line argument and are not used anywhere in the calibration.
