# Independent full-source referee: E.2.17

15 November 2026. Read `sections/E_cusp_normal_crossings.tex` in full, and independently opened the original Lazzeri article at https://numdam.org/item/AST_1973__7-8__269_0.pdf. Verdict: **PASS**.

The corollary proof on printed pp.274–275 explicitly obtains a strict inequality of summed Milnor numbers when one deformed fibre has several singularities. This is stronger than an unspecified proper submodule statement and rules out a finite-index ambiguity. The local critical scheme in the reviewed proof is finite flat over the parameter disc because the two gradient equations form a regular sequence and the central fibre is zero-dimensional. Thus its degree conserves all critical multiplicities, regardless of values. The actual fibration supplies the additional fact that every such critical value is zero.

At a free-circle point, the relative holomorphic flow box yields a reduced plane-curve germ. Its isolated critical point can neither split nor disappear under these conditions, and the nearby known singularity is a node. The parameter Morse lemma then gives the stated relative double-crossing equation.

At the fixed curve, the two weight lines give the invariant T=xy and the coefficient section Q in O(2). Reducedness excludes Q identically zero. A hypothetical double zero yields a reduced isolated plane singularity T g(T,z), while its two nearby simple coefficient zeros give two singularities in the same zero fibre. Other critical points, including those away from T=0, also have value zero by their actual lifts. Conservation and the strict Lazzeri inequality contradict the splitting. The two simple zeros consequently give the two relative triple sections and the exact coordinate changes written in the proof.

The local normalizations separate branches and are smooth over the original parameter in each displayed chart. Finiteness of normalization and properness of the cusp give a simultaneous smooth proper normalization. This supplies relative normal-crossing geometry but does not by itself prove uniqueness of the analytic Mumford completion; the reviewed scope keeps that distinction explicit.

# Independent referee: E.2.19

Reviewer: classification_e. Date: 15 November 2026. Verdict: PASS at the exact stated scope.

The vertical logarithmic bundle is locally free, and its restriction to a normalized branch is canonically the toric logarithmic tangent bundle. The displayed double- and triple-crossing bases verify that restriction without an unproved extension across a stratum. In the relative normalized frame of E.2.18, conductor descent matrices are holomorphic in the parameter and constant along each compact conductor curve. Since the full normalized two-dimensional section space descends for every noncentral parameter, these matrices are the identity there and centrally. The normal-crossing equalizer provides all remaining descent conditions, including triple points.

Proper constant-cohomology base change applies to the locally free bundle over the flat bidisc family. Fibrewise evaluation is an isomorphism, so Nakayama gives the actual global frame. The bracket vanishes on the dense ordinary torus locus. Compact invariant fibres justify complete complex flows, with holomorphic dependence on parameters.

The implicit-function argument is made at a central free open-orbit point, where its vertical derivative is invertible. Extended periods fixing that point act as the identity on every ordinary torus, hence everywhere. Their classes in the ordinary integral local system are flat. Nonprimitivity would produce a fixed nontrivial finite-order torus element acting identically on the dense ordinary locus and therefore on the faithful central open orbit. Thus the marked rank-two lattice is primitive in the full torus lattice and equals the full monodromy-invariant lattice. A constant integral basis change gives exactly w and delta.

The triple-point characters are discrete representations of the compact torus, hence constant along each fixed section. The exact ray calculation supplies the stated unimodular triples. No assertion that arbitrary quotients are smooth or that the entire analytic completion is already classified is imported.

# Source audit for the continuous deformation conclusion

## Opened primary source

Meersseman, L. (2019). The Teichmüller and Riemann moduli stacks. *Journal de l'École polytechnique — Mathématiques, 6*, 879–945. https://doi.org/10.5802/jep.108

The original published PDF was opened at https://www.numdam.org/item/10.5802/jep.108.pdf on 15 November 2026. The journal-hosted endpoint returned HTTP 403; the Numdam copy is the published 68-page PDF, including its cover page, and was read successfully. The source is cited under `Meersseman2019E`.

The exact inputs are Section 2.1, pp.883–884, specifying the topology of smooth convergence on integrable complex operators; Section 3.2, Theorem 3.4, p.898, giving the classical Kuranishi product description on a connected neighbourhood; Remark 3.6, pp.898–899, explaining that elliptic regularity restricts the Sobolev construction to smooth structures; and the actual retraction in equation (3.11), p.899. The inverse comparison is explicitly by a diffeomorphism in the identity component. The local theorem has no global bounded-automorphism hypothesis. The latter belongs to different global stack results in the paper and is not imported here.

The author's arXiv version `1311.4170v4`, revised 17 June 2018, was opened before the published PDF. Its corresponding results are Theorem 3.2 and Remark 3.4, manuscript pp.11–12. E.2.24 uses the published numbering, not the earlier numbering. The published metadata and DOI were also checked on the Numdam article record.

## Use in the proof

The Kuranishi space is a continuous retract of the connected local domain and is therefore connected. Its proper smooth holomorphic deformation family falls within E.2.23 after reduction of the analytic base. If any smooth operator in the domain is a known member of a given pair, all Kuranishi fibres, and hence all operators in that domain, have that pair. This proves that the fixed-pair locus is clopen. Saturation gives the same separation in the diffeomorphism quotients. The proof does not assert that a fixed-pair marked locus is connected, does not identify Teichmüller space with a Kuranishi space, and does not assume that the quotient is Hausdorff.

## Access distinctions and verification

Attempts to open Kuranishi's 1962 Annals PDF directly through JSTOR and a guessed Annals endpoint failed. No claim is made that the original 1962 paper was read. The result is identified as classical Kuranishi theory through the opened primary research article's precise statement and explanation. Attempts to open the 2011 Meersseman Numdam PDF and a Douady Bourbaki endpoint also failed and are not used as proof sources.

The parent and boundary_d independently opened the published 2019 PDF and passed the complete E.2.24 proof. Their review is corroboration of this argument, not an additional proof method. Retrieval references and source metadata, without reproduced article bodies, are saved in `evidence/E_Kuranishi_topology_search_log.json`.

# E.2.20 working proof: recovering the analytic cusp from its actual torus action

Owner: root. Full-source review required before integration. This is a proposed completion-identification theorem, conditional on the fully proved E.2.16--19 geometry and action; it is not yet counted as a proved result.

## Exact setup

Normalize the three distinct critical sections to 0,1,infinity, and write q=1/t near the cusp. Let N be the restriction of the actual smooth fourfold to a small (q,s)-bidisc. Its cusp divisor W=(q=0) is relative normal crossings, irreducible on every s fibre, with normalized pair the toric dP6 hexagon and unchanged opposite-side identifications. E.2.19 supplies an actual holomorphic H=(C*)² action on N over the bidisc. Its constant period lattice is the primitive marked vanishing lattice Zw+Zdelta. The parameter c(s) extends holomorphically to the interior. The goal is an actual biholomorphism of N with the marked Mumford cusp at parameter c(s), after shrinking the bidisc.

## 1. Topology and the cover, with the comparison made explicit

The relative NC coordinates q=x,xy,xyz provide local smooth lifts of the two real coordinate directions of s which annihilate dq and are tangent to all cusp strata. Away from W the map to (q,s) is a submersion, so such lifts exist there too. A smooth partition of unity preserves these linear conditions. On the inverse image of a closed smaller q-disc the lifts integrate along a small s-path: the q-coordinate is constant, and properness prevents escape. Their flows give a q-preserving diffeomorphism of the cusp neighbourhoods and their strata. Thus the actual N has the same fundamental group and the same regular-fibre inclusion map as a prescribed punctured member.

In the known Mumford cusp, pi1(N_s)=Z². The map from a regular torus's Z⁴ kills exactly the primitive lattice Zw+Zdelta and is onto. Root opened and checked the original AC26 PDF, Proposition4.7 and Corollary4.8 on printed p30. An independent elementary proof is as follows. Each maximal toric chart restricted to the cusp disc is {|xyz|<epsilon}, which is star-shaped and simply connected. All chart intersections contain the same connected dense open torus (C*)²×Δ* and are connected; intersections with finite chart unions have the same property. Finite van Kampen and compactness of loops prove that the entire toric cover is simply connected. Its free properly discontinuous deck group is the marked quotient Λ/(Zw+Zdelta). The regular fibre of this cover is C²/(Zw+Zdelta)=(C*)². The q-loop dies through the explicit smooth section (x,y,z)=(q,1,1). Its central components form the planar hexagon unfolding. These descriptions transfer by the q-preserving diffeomorphism just constructed.

Take the universal cover Ntilde→N. It has deck group Z² and central normalized components indexed by the planar hexagon tiling. The H action lifts to Ntilde: its fundamental loops at a central open orbit lie in one normalized dP6 component, hence are null-homotopic in N. Uniqueness of the identity lift gives the action law. Pick a holomorphic (q,s)-section p through a smooth point of one central component, and lift it. On q≠0 its H orbit map is an isomorphism H×(Δq*×Δs)→Ntilde_reg: its stabilizer is precisely the marked vanishing lattice already divided out. Thus the two H characters and q are globally defined on the open cover.

The deck transformations commute with H. On this open cover they therefore have the form h→P_i(q,s)h, where the two P_i are exactly the actual marked period multipliers. The choice of p only changes the affine origin; it does not change these multipliers. They are the same q-monomials times holomorphic units as in the reference Mumford model, because the full marked period matrix is the one at c(s).

## 2. Why the character comparison has no essential singularities

For any central component D_v, choose a holomorphic base section a_v through its dense H orbit. The H orbit map gives an open chart H×(Δq×Δs), containing that dense central orbit and the entire regular cover. Indeed it is injective on each fibre, is a local biholomorphism along the central orbit, and is an isomorphism on q≠0. Two such charts differ on q≠0 by a holomorphic translation h_vw(q,s)∈H. At this point h_vw is only known on the punctured q-disc; its meromorphic extension must be proved.

If two origins lie in the same central component, their ratio extends as an H-valued holomorphic unit over q=0. At the central parameter their points are in the same free H orbit; the holomorphic inverse-function theorem for the orbit map supplies the extension in both q and s.

For adjacent components, use a relative crossing chart q=ab and an orbit coordinate z≠0. The primitive connected stabilizer along their common open conductor is a C* subgroup of H. Compact-circle averaging linearizes it with opposite normal characters, while a complementary H direction supplies z. The full characters are unimodular, as on every punctured s fibre. Choose origins in this chart by a=q/b0,b=b0,z=z0 on one side and a=a0,b=q/a0,z=z0 on the other, with a0,b0,z0 fixed small nonzero values. The normal stabilizer translates the former to the latter by the cocharacter evaluated at a0*b0/q; tangential changes are holomorphic units. The orbit path may be kept within the crossing chart by increasing |a| while decreasing |b| with their product fixed. Thus the comparison is exactly a fixed integral power q^v times a holomorphic H-valued unit. Comparing these special origins to the chosen origins adds only holomorphic units, by the same-component argument.

Every central component is reached by a finite adjacent path. The translation from the initial origin is therefore q^v u_v(q,s), with v∈Z² and u_v a holomorphic H-valued unit. There is no essential singularity. Different paths give the same meromorphic character functions because they agree on the dense regular cover. The integers v are locally constant in s: each crossing calculation holds on the full bidisc with a nonvanishing unit.

## 3. The fan and the analytic charts

At a triple point the whole H fixes the point. Average local coordinates over its maximal compact torus. The resulting three coordinates are H eigenfunctions with the same integer characters as on a punctured s fibre. Their only primitive positive invariant relation is their product; reducedness and the NC equation make q a holomorphic unit times that product. Absorb the invariant unit, which is a holomorphic function of the product and s, into one eigen-coordinate. Thus the chart has the exact form q=xyz with diagonal H action.

The valuations of the two global characters along D_v, together with ord_Dv(q)=1, give rays (v,1). At a triple point the three incident rays are a unimodular cone. In that chart the global characters are Laurent monomials in x,y,z times holomorphic units in q,s; this follows from the origin comparisons above, or by evaluating a character on a section holding two of x,y,z fixed nonzero and solving the third from q. Conversely the toric coordinates of that cone pull back to x,y,z times holomorphic units. They therefore extend holomorphically and give a biholomorphism near the triple point. The same assertion on a double or smooth stratum follows from its crossing or open-orbit chart.

The component incidence and all the integer characters are constant over s. For any s≠0 they are the prescribed A2 triangulation. Hence the resulting fan on the central fibre is exactly that fan, not an arbitrary smooth refinement. The character maps on different charts agree on the dense open torus and therefore glue. This reconstructs the analytic toric universal-cover model.

Uniformity over the infinite cover is handled by deck equivariance, not by an infimum of infinitely many radii. Choose finitely many charts covering one compact central dP6 component and finitely many overlaps for the two deck generators. Shrink the bidisc once for these charts and comparisons. On the regular cover the reconstructed map intertwines the actual deck translations with the multipliers P_i(q,s) in the reference model. Both sides extend holomorphically in the charts just constructed, so the equality extends to their central strata. Translate the finite chart collection by the actual deck group. The reference period multipliers act on the prescribed invariant fan, and these translated charts cover the entire central unfolding. This gives one common bidisc for the quotient comparison.

The reconstructed map is a local biholomorphism at every central stratum, bijective on each labelled torus orbit there, and an isomorphism on the whole regular cover. Its inverse is supplied by the same monomial-coordinate charts. Quotienting by the identical properly discontinuous deck actions yields the required biholomorphism of the actual cusp neighbourhood with the prescribed Mumford neighbourhood over the bidisc.

## 4. What must still be checked before promotion

The known-cover topology is now derived above and checked against original AC26 Proposition4.7/Corollary4.8. The crossing character argument must retain the full two-parameter unit and primitive stabilizer when converted to final TeX. The finite fundamental-core/deck argument must also remain explicit. Both classification_e and boundary_d passed the full cusp-neighbourhood reconstruction. The final global central-X identification still uses the finite-root charts, ordinary marked periods, and E.1's affine collar classification. No arbitrary-chain conclusion is asserted until that application and an analytic propagation argument are proved; ordinary curve selection does not apply to the boundary of an arbitrary open known-member locus.

# Final dependency and scope audit

**Verdict: PASS.** Classification_e, 15 November 2026. This audit covers the integrated B.4.24, B.4.27, B.4.30–33, their strengthened B.4.4 input, and the closing B.4.1/B.5.1/C.5.1 statements. The current source and registry rows were read. No shared master or main report was edited.

## 1. The proof dependencies are acyclic

| Result | Mathematical inputs actually used | Scope check |
|---|---|---|
| B.4.4 | R1's intrinsic bi-anticanonical pencil; Grauert coherence; the standard analytic base-change input audited in E.3.1; explicit proper graph and labelled critical-value argument | Requires a smooth dominating fourfold, not a reduced or semistable central divisor. E.3.1's base-change theorem is independent of the later deformation-closure and noncompletion results. |
| B.4.24 | The explicit closing C.3.5, affine section B.4.9, axial presentations B.4.18–19, and the specified KKN definitions/exact sequence | Constructs the fixed associated log torus and its nontrivial axial clutching. Does not assume that an arbitrary cap is a log torsor. |
| B.4.27 | B.4.24's period pairing; direct blowup/Picard/ghost cohomology; KKN base change | Proves H1(S,A)=0 for the specified sheaf after any permitted point blowups. Does not classify arbitrary caps. |
| B.4.30 | B.4.24/26/27; analytic log de Rham comparison; direct coefficient-ideal equidimensionalization; explicit local roots, subdivisions, norms and Hartogs | Its conclusion remains restricted to the prescribed partial filling. Its extending-section lemmas and constructions are proved before being reused in B.4.32–33; the final noncompletion conclusion is not assumed in those arguments. |
| B.4.31 | B.4.4, analytic resolution, compact branch-stratum transport, and the actual peripheral matrices | Starts from a hypothetical arbitrary proper filling and retains central multiplicities. Uses no B.4.30 or B.4.33 conclusion and no semistable-reduction theorem. |
| B.4.32 | B.4.31's integral meridians; the local saturation/primitive/chart-chain arguments of B.4.30; B.4.19's exact reference-origin overlaps; the quotient fixed-space calculation | Controls a normalized radial-loop class only. Does not assume bounded comparison with the old origin or invoke B.4.33. |
| B.4.33 | B.4.4/24/27/30/31/32 | Constructs the cap-derived torsor from multiplied trace differences, obtains a global origin, and derives the positive-integer contradiction. No existence or topology assertion in B.4.1, B.5.1 or C.5.1 is an input. |

The closing references from the scope paragraphs of B.4.24, B.4.27 and B.4.31 to the later results are explanatory forward references, not premises of their proofs. Likewise B.4.1 is deliberately stated before B.4.33 and proved by that later theorem. The dependency is one-way. C.3.5's “cusp filling” means the q-cusp neighbourhood inside an already constructed nonzero-parameter member; its explicit closing calculation does not assume an r-parameter filling.

## 2. No relative-dimension-three semistable reduction is hidden

An arbitrary proper filling is first reduced to its main component, normalized and resolved, unchanged over the puncture. Its smooth dominating fourfold is flat over the parameter because the parameter is a nonzero divisor. B.4.4 then extends the marked map, and its graph and central support are resolved with multiplicities retained.

B.4.31 directly treats the local equation r=x^a y^b and its higher-crossing analogues. The only semistable constructions in B.4.30, B.4.32 and B.4.33 occur near a generic boundary curve of a **two-dimensional base**, with a transverse parameter retained. Their transverse fibres are surfaces. After deleting the proper images of closed bad strata, the charts have t=product(x_i^m_i), with at most three factors. The height-one slice therefore has dimension at most two, where the stated lattice subdivision gives unimodular triangles. This is not a claimed semistable-reduction theorem for arbitrary threefold degenerations.

The external analytic input is IKN2005 Theorems 6.2–6.3, not its algebraic Hodge-degeneration Theorem 7.1. The original text and the erratum were checked; no Kähler or projectivity assumption is dropped. The equidimensionalization uses the reproduced Parusiński preprint argument. Enokizono–Hashizume Lemma 2.14 supplies analytic toroidal gluing; their projective main theorem is not used.

## 3. Roots, traces, and the whole modified base

The compact set K=beta^{-1}(0) includes the no-blowup case. Properness makes a finite neighbourhood cover of K cover the entire inverse image of a smaller bidisc. This supplies finitely many trace degrees. Resolution over a relatively compact neighbourhood supplies finitely many boundary multiplicities. A product of their common multiple with lcm(1,...,d_max) works for the **local transverse** covers; splitting is done before the final toric subdivision. No global root along an exceptional curve or the full marked P1 base is asserted.

Norms return multiplied differences to the original base. Other branch-curve intersections and meromorphic-lift indeterminacy occur at isolated points near the compact set. The Hartogs argument extends A-sections, using H1 of a punctured bidisc with constant Y coefficients equal to zero; it does not assert Hartogs extension of torsors. Unique section extensions preserve cocycles. Thus Q is constructed on all of S, rather than only near a generic exceptional divisor.

At the unknown strict r axis, B.4.31 supplies delta-only angular meridians. This gives exactly the bounded sheaf whose first character has zero r-order. The proof never replaces the rank-one monodromy kernel by a rank-two kernel. Along the actual q axis, the sigma comparison uses projection to the known fan and the finite-character norm, so an unproved global holomorphic lift of sigma is unnecessary.

The ordinary-to-rotating correction G(s)+6v delta is explicitly inserted before using sigma−o_rot=F. B.4.32 kills only the loop defect; common essential translations remain allowed. The final gamma evaluation is 0=Dn with D,n positive. All finite covers and norms preserve the nonzero sign-independent integer obstruction. The untwisted identity-closing family has F=0 and is therefore not excluded.

## 4. Closing statements and registries

B.4.24, B.4.27 and B.4.30–33 remain **Proved** with their respective scopes. The strengthened B.4.4 source and CSV both cover resolution of an arbitrary proper filling. B.4.1 and B.5.1 are correctly **Refuted** as proposed-object premises: no positive least filling exponent exists, and the requested central topology and nearby-cycle data are undefined, not zero. Their registry rows agree with the source.

C.5.1 is also correctly **Refuted** for the specified analytic filling premise. It refuses to assign c1, c2, p1, relative lifts, intersection form or characteristic numbers to that nonexistent analytic object. It retains the proved actual smooth monodromy [Phi]=0 and the two stated determinations through C.3.24 and C.3.25–27. Those smooth arguments do not depend on B.4.33 or on the existence of an analytic parameter filling. Their shared annular characteristic inputs and distinct boundary-invariant arguments are stated accurately.

Finally, C.5.1 explicitly distinguishes the constructed smooth spin-c eight-dimensional coboundary from the nonexistent analytic W. It neither asserts an almost-complex structure on that smooth coboundary nor identifies its determinant class with the first Chern class of an almost-complex tangent bundle. No scope error or circularity was found in these closing statements.

## Detailed development record: E_global_completion_working.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# E.2.21 working proof: global identification of a one-sided smooth completion

Owner: classification_e. This is a full working argument for review, not an integrated result or an arbitrary-chain conclusion. It assumes the actual cusp identification E.2.20 once that source has been finalized and refereed.

## Proposed exact statement

Let pi: Xcal -> Delta be a proper holomorphic submersion with fibres diffeomorphic to S6, and suppose every noncentral fibre is a member of the fixed coprime pair (3k,4l) in E.2.1. Then its central fibre is a member of that same pair. Its normalized isomorphism parameter is the interior value z(0) proved in E.2.7. After choosing a logarithm c(s), the actual family is locally over the parameter disc isomorphic to the prescribed family at c(s), up to the connected translation automorphisms already computed in E.1.

The last family assertion can be omitted if only the central identification is required. It must be justified by the parameter-dependent version of the same cocycle solution, not by pointwise isomorphisms alone.

## 1. What is already known about the actual completion

E.2.10 extends the intrinsic pencil to a proper flat map g: Xcal -> P1 x Delta. E.2.7 supplies a holomorphic interior nonzero parameter z(s); a logarithm on the disc gives c(s). E.2.12 excludes additional critical values, and E.2.14--15 exclude target collapse. The three labelled sections therefore extend distinctly and are sent to 0,1,infinity by a holomorphic PGL2 normalization on the whole parameter disc.

On the ordinary locus, the actual marked torus Jacobian is the one with period matrix Pi_(c(s))(t). This is equality of holomorphic period subbundles with their integral markings: it holds for s nonzero by the given family comparison, and extends to s=0 by the identity theorem. Translation automorphisms of a fixed marked member act trivially on H1, as proved in E.1. Consequently the local choices introduce no additional homology return. The identity monodromy around s=0 also follows from the actual smooth family at any fixed ordinary t.

E.2.13 gives the actual normalized finite root covers, of degrees 3k and 4l. They are smooth proper torus families, with the same marked Jacobians and free deck actions. E.2.16--19 give the actual relative normal-crossing cusp, normalized toric pair, and marked torus action. E.2.20 then identifies the actual cusp neighbourhood with the prescribed Mumford neighbourhood over the full (q,s) bidisc, including its period multipliers.

## 2. The finite affine data really persist

Choose local holomorphic origins on the smooth finite root torus families. A deck map is affine on each compact torus fibre. Its linear part is the fixed marked finite monodromy; subtracting this linear part leaves a holomorphic translation section. Over a simply connected small root-parameter chart, lift that translation to the Lie bundle. The finite-order power relation has the form that the sum of its successive linear translates is an integral period. This integral vector is locally constant in s, because both the lifted translations and the period map are continuous and the lattice is discrete. Changing the lift changes that vector by exactly the integral coboundary already allowed in E.1.

Thus the actual finite deck cocycle has the same integral class at s=0 as for s nonzero. Its varying holomorphic part is removed by the explicit finite averaging used in E.1: the difference of two cocycles with identical lattice class is a cocycle in the complex vector sheaf, and multiplication by the finite group order is invertible there. This produces holomorphic local comparisons in the finite root charts. No assertion that a smooth reduced finite fibre is itself a torus is needed; it is the free finite quotient of the root torus.

One may work with a finite cover of the root disc and its deck translates rather than impose a global origin. The same averaging solves the resulting equivariant comparison. All terms depend holomorphically on s, so the solution does as well.

## 3. Integral collar data and the global affine comparison

Choose a finite cover of the t-sphere by the two finite-root neighbourhoods, the cusp neighbourhood, and ordinary simply connected charts, with fixed annular collars. Choose local holomorphic origins on ordinary torus charts. On overlaps, every comparison is fibrewise affine, because every biholomorphism of compact complex tori is affine. The common integral marking makes the linear parts the prescribed ones, so the remaining maps are translation sections.

Lift these translation sections on simply connected refinements of the collars to the corresponding Lie bundles. Their differences on deck translates, and the sums on triple overlaps, are integral periods. Each resulting integral vector is constant in the parameter s. This statement uses fixed finite collars away from the singular centres, where the actual and reference torus families are smooth; no logarithm is evaluated at a singular point.

For s nonzero the family is a prescribed member, so these integral classes are exactly the classes of its logarithmic-transform data, modulo the changes of origins already allowed in E.1. The same is true at s=0. Equivalently, one can normalize the discrete classes once over the connected parameter disc; their local constancy prevents a new central class. This includes the cusp winding class. The cusp chart comparison in E.2.20 has the actual marked period action, so no unaccounted translation or fan change enters there.

After this discrete normalization, the remaining comparison is the additive holomorphic cocycle in the extension sheaf V used in E.1. Its root extension conditions are those of the already identified finite root charts, and its cusp extension condition is the actual toric collar condition, now supplied by E.2.20. In particular it fits the exact sequence

`0 -> O_(P1) delta -> V -> O_(P1)(-infinity) -> 0`.

Both end sheaves have vanishing first cohomology. E.1 gives the explicit analytic solution, by finite averaging and splitting the collar Laurent series into the two convergent parts. This kills the remaining cocycle and glues the local comparisons to a biholomorphism of the actual central threefold with the prescribed member at c(0).

For a family comparison, perform precisely the same splitting with coefficients holomorphic in s on a fixed finite collar cover. Cauchy coefficient formulas preserve holomorphic dependence, and the finite averaging is holomorphic in s. The resulting comparisons therefore glue over a smaller parameter disc. Their ambiguity is the connected scalar translation group from E.1; this does not change the central isomorphism class.

## 4. What this does not yet imply

The known-pair locus is open by E.2.2 and is stable under full punctured-disc smooth specializations by the proposed statement. Those two facts alone do not prove that it is topologically closed in an arbitrary reduced analytic base. An arbitrary open set can have a real boundary through which no holomorphic disc has its entire punctured image in that open set. Analytic curve selection is unavailable until the known locus has a suitable analytic or constructible description.

The following separate propagation route is under investigation. On any smooth disc carrying a smooth family and containing a nonempty known open set, the unique fractional canonical root L exists on the whole total space because H1 and H2 with integer coefficients vanish. Its direct image is a free rank-two sheaf, since rank two holds on that nonempty open set. The evaluation base locus is a closed analytic subset of the proper total space, so its image is a closed analytic subset of the disc. It misses the known open set and is therefore discrete. Thus the actual relative pencil exists off a discrete analytic set, without first assuming the whole punctured disc consists of known members.

The critical image, finite root covers, cusp normalization and conductor must then be controlled by finitely many proper analytic exceptional sets. If this yields the original local charts off a discrete set, the ordinary holomorphic periods can be continued from the known open set and the above global comparison would identify the generic disc fibres. Full punctured-disc closure would then fill the remaining isolated points. This generic propagation step is not asserted here until its finite normalization/conductor argument is written and reviewed. In particular E.2.4 remains Open while it is missing.

## Detailed development record: E_hopf_case_audit.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Negative-slope specialization: Hopf-case source and obstruction audit

Status at 15 November 2026, after checkpoint05: **the negative-slope fixed-divisor obstruction is resolved by E.2.9–E.2.10**. The full exclusion of arbitrary holomorphic Hopf-surface algebraic reductions on a complex six-sphere has not been established and is not needed for that conclusion. The parent and boundary-D agents independently refereed the complete E.2.8–E.2.10 sources and passed them. Sections1–9 below retain the source audit and unsuccessful classification routes in their historical scope; Section10 records the subsequent vanishing proof that supersedes their fixed-divisor gap.

## 1. The exact question remaining after E.2.5–7

In a smooth one-sided specialization of known members, write A=12kl and D=kl−3k−4l. The unique relative fractional canonical line L satisfies L^D=K_rel^A. Its two limiting sections have a holomorphic ratio, by the valid non-holomorphic-map theorem, but may have a common divisor E0. For D>0, E.2.6 removes this divisor and the possible target degree. For D<0, a horizontal component of E0 would restrict to a nonzero effective antipluricanonical divisor on a smooth general fibre F of the central algebraic reduction. Because c1(KF)=0, this makes F non-Kähler with Kodaira dimension −infinity, hence class VII with b1=1. Also c2(F)=0, since TF⊕O is TX restricted to F and H4(X,Z)=0; thus b2(F)=0. The known classification then leaves Hopf surfaces once the presence of a curve excludes the Inoue case. The proof of E.2.8 only needs b1=1 and deliberately avoids choosing a primary cover or excluding secondary Hopf surfaces by an unproved integral monodromy argument.

## 2. The proved component-count repair and normalization obstruction

For a finite nonempty set Sigma containing all critical values, let s=|Sigma| and r be the total number of irreducible components of the reduced inverse image. Alexander duality gives b1(X minus A,Q)=r. The correct low-degree Leray sequence gives r=s−1+dim H1(F,Q)^mon, with the invariant subspace retained. If b1(F)=1, then r≤s, whereas each fibre is nonempty so r≥s. Equality forces irreducibility of every reduced fibre and trivial rational degree-one monodromy.

A horizontal prime H maps onto the base and meets each fibre S. Irreducibility prevents S from being contained in H. Its canonical section therefore gives a nonzero effective Cartier divisor on the reduced support S. At a local self-intersection, nonvanishing on every branch follows by pulling to the irreducible normalization and applying analytic continuation; it is not an inference from global irreducibility to local irreducibility. Pullback to a Kähler resolution of the normalization would give a nonzero effective divisor in a line with c1=0, contradicting positive Kähler area. Nonreduced fibre multiplicities cause no difficulty because the argument first passes to S_red.

This proves E.2.8, including its application to the negative-slope horizontal fixed divisor. It does not exclude fibre normalizations outside Fujiki class C.

The final source also includes the following companion, accepted independently by the root: without a rank-one assumption, each irreducible component of a reducible fibre meets another component by connectedness. The line of that other prime divisor restricts to a nonzero effective Cartier divisor with zero Chern class. The same positivity argument therefore puts the normalization of every component of a reducible fibre outside class C. This rules out ordinary projective blowup components as a proposed explanation of a collapsed-target smooth limit, but not the non-Kähler alternatives.

## 3. What was and was not recovered from CDP2020

The original publisher PDF was opened and Sections 4, 6 and 7 were read directly:

Campana, F., Demailly, J.-P., & Peternell, T. (2020). The algebraic dimension of compact complex threefolds with vanishing second Betti number. *Compositio Mathematica, 156*, 679–696. https://doi.org/10.1112/S0010437X19007802

Publisher full text: https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/CDF2D04CFB35CEE105E10B188D97D68A/S0010437X19007802a.pdf/the-algebraic-dimension-of-compact-complex-threefolds-with-vanishing-second-betti-number.pdf

Theorem2.1, p.679, concerns a non-holomorphic nonconstant meromorphic map to P1. It is the theorem used in E.2.5, with the independent LRS proof also recorded there. The different holomorphic-fibration assertion, Theorem2.2, and Corollary2.3 are not imported. Lemma4.2, p.684, replaces the monodromy invariant space by the full H1 group; E.2.8 repairs only the rational rank-one consequence by the explicit inequality above. It does not recover the claimed unrestricted direct-sum decomposition or integral torsion-freeness.

The singular-fibre reduction on p.692 goes through the cotangent sequence and then attempts to reduce to torsion-free differentials on the fibre. For a nonnormal fibre the torsion in its differential sheaf can support the missing sections. Irreducibility by itself does not remove that issue. Thus neither the Hopf-general-fibre subcase nor the Picard/cohomology assertions of Sections6–7 are promoted wholesale. Possible rank-one repairs of R1f_*O and the restriction map still do not prove the needed cotangent vanishing on arbitrary nonnormal fibres.

## 4. A concrete Hopf degeneration does not fit the canonical condition

Opened original publisher PDF:

Kerr, M., Laza, R., & Saito, M. (2022). Deformation of rational singularities and Hodge structure. *Algebraic Geometry, 9*(4), 476–501. https://doi.org/10.14231/AG-2022-014

https://content.algebraicgeometry.nl/2022-4/2022-4-014.pdf

Proposition1, p.478, has a projective central-fibre hypothesis. It cannot be silently changed to an arbitrary Moishezon analytic centre. Remark2.4, pp.493–494, discusses Kodaira's degeneration of Hopf surfaces with smooth total space and a central nonnormal rational surface. Its normalization is the first Hirzebruch surface, with two disjoint sections of self-intersections −1 and +1 identified. Its rational Betti numbers are (1,1,1,0,1), while those of a smooth Hopf surface are (1,1,0,1,1). Thus h2(O)=0 or rational normalization alone does not prohibit a singular Hopf limit.

For that particular model, however, the pullback of the dualizing line is K_F1+C_minus+C_plus=−2F, where F is a ruling fibre. This integral Chern class is nonzero. Such a reduced Cartier fibre cannot occur in the present six-sphere setting, where adjunction makes its dualizing Chern class zero (the fibre normal line is torsion in the multiple-fibre case). E.2.8 also excludes it when a horizontal divisor is present. This is an exclusion of the stated model, not a classification of all irreducible Hopf completions.

## 5. Isotriviality is suggestive but does not solve the completion problem

Opened author primary version:

Dloussky, G. (2006). On surfaces of class VII_0^+ with numerically anticanonical divisor. *American Journal of Mathematics, 128*(3), 639–670. https://doi.org/10.1353/ajm.2006.0021

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0406387 (version3, 13 April 2005), Section1.3, manuscript pp.12–13.

For primary Hopf surfaces the contraction trace and determinant are bounded holomorphic moduli and extend across parameter punctures. Over a punctured compact base curve their extensions are constant, and the determinant remains nonzero. This suggests generic isotriviality, allowing the known diagonal/resonant distinction. Constructing the appropriate relative primary covers for a family of secondary Hopf surfaces is a further step; it is not supplied by the rational rank-one calculation.

Even a proved isotrivial punctured Hopf family does not give a meromorphic comparison with every proper analytic completion. B.4.7 supplies an isotrivial Hopf family with a reducible centre. Its two components are excluded by the rank-one component count here, but its existence blocks any unrestricted compactification-uniqueness claim. The Kato family audited in B.4.10 has blown-up Hopf general fibres with b2>0 and a contraction eigenvalue tending to zero; it is not a counterexample to the narrower irreducible, minimal, fixed-nonzero-eigenvalue question. Neither example proves that narrow question.

## 6. The attempted elliptic-curve/Enoki route has a specific gap

The normalization of a horizontal divisor is itself outside class C: pull back f^*O(1), whose Chern class is zero on X, to any supposed Kähler resolution and use a nonempty fibre divisor. Since curves on the general Hopf fibre are elliptic, the resolved horizontal divisor has a general elliptic fibration after Stein factorization. One might try to force an elliptic component in every intersection with a singular fibre, then exclude a flat effective divisor supported on the rational cycle of an Enoki normalization.

The source statements about Enoki surfaces are precise. Teleman (2010), p.1758, recalls Enoki's characterization via an effective divisor with torsion Chern class. Toma (2008), pp.249–250, identifies the rational cycle as the only homologically trivial effective reduced divisor in the relevant Enoki case. Their original PDFs were opened:

- Teleman, A. (2010). Instantons and curves on class VII surfaces. *Annals of Mathematics, 172*(3), 1749–1804. https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2010.172.1749 ; https://annals.math.princeton.edu/wp-content/uploads/annals-v172-n3-p07-p.pdf
- Toma, M. (2008). On the Kähler rank of compact complex surfaces. *Bulletin de la Société Mathématique de France, 136*(2), 243–260. https://doi.org/10.24033/bsmf.2556 ; https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/bsmf.2556.pdf

The attempted application fails without an extra argument: an elliptic component in a fibre of the resolved horizontal divisor can be contracted on the map to the possibly singular divisor H. Exceptional elliptic curves can occur in resolutions of Gorenstein surface singularities. The remaining image in H intersect S can therefore consist of rational curves. It is not legitimate to assert survival of an elliptic component merely because the general curve is elliptic. Both the E and D agents identified and retained this gap.

## 7. Additional restrictions and failed shortcuts

- A nonzero effective antipluricanonical divisor on a surface with torsion canonical line is impossible; thus the horizontal obstruction lies in the non-torsion canonical case.
- The higher-degree local invariant-cycle assertion is unavailable in this non-Kähler setting. The Kodaira example above already illustrates a failed higher-degree comparison, so it cannot be used to force normalization cohomology.
- In diagonal nonresonant Hopf coordinates, multiplicities of the two anticanonical curves can impose divisibility conditions on the fractional root. Resonant non-diagonal cases have variable multiplicity and defeat that simple arithmetic exclusion. No uniform arithmetic obstruction is asserted.
- Standard classifications of semistable degenerations of two-dimensional tori impose Kähler or related hypotheses on the total degeneration. The actual limiting pencil divisor lies in a non-Kähler total family; no such hypothesis has been proved. Those classifications are not applied.
- A globally paired self-pinching conductor model retains a dual-circle fundamental-group quotient. That rules out that particular way of killing the Hopf fundamental group, but it is not a classification of arbitrary irreducible nonnormal fibres.

## 8. Exact primary inputs for E.2.8

Fujino, O. (2021). Minimal model theory for log surfaces in Fujiki's class C. *Nagoya Mathematical Journal, 244*, 256–282. https://doi.org/10.1017/nmj.2020.14

Opened publisher full text: Definition3.1, Remark3.2, Lemma3.3 and Corollary3.4. These identify compact normal class-C surfaces with those having a Kähler resolution; they also include Moishezon surfaces. The main minimal-model theorems are not used.

Barth, W. P., Hulek, K., Peters, C. A. M., & Van de Ven, A. (2004). *Compact complex surfaces* (2nd ed.). Springer. https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-57739-0

Opened publisher metadata and the official two-page ChapterVI preview, pp.243–244, including Theorem1.1 and Table10: https://page-one.springer.com/pdf/preview/10.1007/978-3-642-57739-0_7 . These suffice for the non-Kähler Kodaira-dimension-minus-infinity implication b1=1. The remainder of the book was not represented as opened. Peters (2021), Theorem2.2, p.843, was independently opened as another published formulation of that classification table, but is not an extra theorem needed by E.2.8.

## 9. Search record and remaining goal

The searches on 15 November 2026 used both search engines and included: CDP2020 Hopf case and nonnormal differential torsion; Hopf degenerations with irreducible centre; fixed contraction trace and determinant; isotrivial Hopf completions; Kähler and Moishezon central-fibre hypotheses; non-Kähler local invariant cycles; effective flat divisors on class VII and Enoki surfaces; and degenerations of tori without a Kähler total-space hypothesis. Access metadata, primary URLs, and failed openings are retained in evidence/E_hopf_search_log.json. No claim of exhaustive literature coverage is made.

At the end of the classification attempts, the unresolved step was exclusion or classification of an irreducible singular fibre with non-Kähler normalization in the presence of the actual horizontal fixed divisor. Section10 resolves the fixed-divisor problem without that classification. Uniqueness of an interior smooth period completion and loss of fractional canonical sections on a different deformation branch remain independent E.2.4 gaps.

## 10. Subsequent resolution by horizontal twisting and Picard variation

E.2.9 observes that a horizontal divisor meeting every irreducible reduced fibre supplies a uniform vanishing argument. Negative twists kill sections of any vector bundle on each reduced fibre by descending finite-dimensional spaces, Krull intersection and analytic continuation. The finite O_S(−jS) filtration handles every thickened fibre mS. Compactness of P1 makes the fibrewise bound uniform; Gorenstein duality and constant-zero base change then give R2f_*B(NH)=0. A positive twist from P1 kills global H2 and H3. A separate negative H twist kills H0 on X. Because Pic(X)=H1(O_X) additively, a holomorphic affine line through these two Picard classes makes all three vanishings simultaneous outside finitely many proper analytic jumping loci. For B=TX this contradicts HRR chi(TX)=c3/2=1.

The proof uses the exact proper finite-complex and base-change statements of Bănică–Stănăşilă already opened in E.3.1, and the compact complete-intersection duality of Ramis–Ruget already audited there. It neither imports CDP2020 Theorem2.2 nor relies on the corrected generic exceptional-locus assertion of Grauert1960. The exact HRR polynomial check is saved in code/e_push_horizontal_vanishing.py, with its .out and .json; that computation verifies only the polynomial, not the analytic proof.

For the negative-slope fixed divisor, E.2.8 first forces irreducibility, and E.2.9 then excludes the hypothetical horizontal component. The connected-divisor-limit argument of E.2.6 now applies for either sign. E.2.10 therefore proves E0=0, target degree1, a proper flat relative intrinsic pencil, a general central torus fibre, an interior limiting period, and exclusion of a common smooth limit for two distinct multiplicity pairs. Interior-completion uniqueness and passage through a new deformation branch remain Open. The independent D referee is sections/D_E229_E2210_referee.md; the parent also read and passed the complete source.

# Interior smooth limits after E.2.10: exact reductions and open steps

Working note, 15 November 2026. This file is not an additional proved result registry and is not input in the paper. E.2.9–E.2.12 are integrated separately. The former extra-central-critical-value gap is now resolved by the explicit good-Jacobian argument of E.2.11–E.2.12. The remaining objective is uniqueness of a smooth completion at an interior period, or persistence on every deformation branch through such a completion. No such conclusion is claimed here.

## 1. Setup supplied by the proved results

For a one-sided smooth specialization of fixed multiplicities (3k,4l), E.2.10 gives an actual proper flat map g:mathcal X→P1×Delta. Its central restriction is the intrinsic algebraic reduction. The line L is the actual pullback of O(1), and K_rel^A=L^D with A=12kl and D=kl−3k−4l. E.2.7 gives an interior limiting normalized period. After choosing its local logarithm, c(s) extends holomorphically to s=0.

The three punctured critical sections have intrinsically distinguished finite orders3,4 and cusp monodromy. Their closures are degree-one branches over the disc, hence define holomorphic P1-valued functions. The corresponding normalization A_s∈PGL2 is meromorphic in s. It can have a degenerate limit; interiority of c does not itself exclude that.

## 2. Cartier multiplicity preservation

Let Gamma_j be the graph of a labelled critical section in P1×Delta. The pullback g^*Gamma_j is an effective Cartier divisor on the smooth total space. On the punctured family it equals m_j Z_j, where m_1=3k and m_2=4l and Z_j is the reduced multiple-fibre family. Its irreducible closure is a prime Cartier divisor. There can be no extra component equal to X0: g0 is nonconstant, so it does not map the whole central threefold into the single point Gamma_j(0). Consequently g^*Gamma_j=m_j overline Z_j as actual divisors on the total space.

Restriction to X0 gives g0^*(p_j)=m_j(overline Z_j|X0). If the two finite critical sections collide, every coefficient of the resulting central fibre is divisible by lcm(3k,4l)=12kl. The argument allows the restricted divisor to be reducible or nonreduced; it makes no claim about its normalization or singularities.

## 3. What a degenerate target normalization would imply on regular fibres

A meromorphic PGL2 matrix with degenerate limit maps a generic fixed central target point, in the original normalized source coordinate, toward one constant point t*. For a fixed target point at which the central fibre is regular, the actual torus family extends smoothly over the parameter disc. Its degree-one integral monodromy in s is the identity. Since the period logarithm c has no winding, the calculation of E.2.7 leaves only the peripheral monodromy associated with t*.

Approach to the cusp with nonzero order is impossible: the rank-two cusp nilpotent has nonzero u→w part and no parameter shift is present to cancel it. At a finite source value, the approach order must be divisible by3 or4, respectively. After taking the corresponding local root, the reference torus family has a smooth limit. Comparison of the actual and reference integral local systems gives the same limiting marked torus. This uses separation for marked complex tori, not separation for the entire threefold moduli problem.

Thus in the collapsed-target scenario the central smooth regular torus fibres are all isomorphic. If t* is 0 or1, this is the finite root torus at c(0), an abelian surface: the finite linear automorphism has one complex eigenvalue1 and one primitive cube or fourth root, and its rational idempotent decomposition splits the torus up to isogeny into two elliptic factors. If t* is ordinary, it is the ordinary torus at (c(0),t*), which need not be abelian. An earlier shorthand incorrectly omitted this ordinary case; the integrated E.2.12 explicitly distinguishes the two. Neither case supplies a global polarization on the total central threefold.

## 4. The former extra-critical-value gap, now resolved

It is not justified to say that the central map has at most two critical values solely from the collision of the three punctured critical sections. The proper analytic critical image can also have isolated points supported at s=0. Smoothness of the total space and flatness alone do not give purity of the discriminant for arbitrary families of surfaces. A general purity assertion would need proof in this particular torus setting.

The gap has now been closed under the actual period hypotheses, without invoking a general purity theorem. Near a hypothetical extra value, E.2.12 constructs the smooth Jacobian over a bidisc from the ordinary or finite-root period model. The finite-root order divides the parameter approach order by the actual identity H1 return, and the remaining unit has a holomorphic root. The punctured bidisc is simply connected, so its actual marked period subbundle agrees with that reference Jacobian. E.2.11 then extends the torus action by Hartogs and proves smoothness at the allegedly exceptional value. Therefore the central critical values are exactly the labelled limits.

If the image is the nontrivial group generated by A1 or A2, its invariant H1 has dimension2. Alexander duality and the correct invariant-space formula give r=s+1, where r counts the components of the actual critical fibres. In the degenerate-target case E.2.12 now proves s=2 in this nontrivial-monodromy case, so one fibre has two components and the other is irreducible. If all punctured critical sections cluster together, their product has trivial monodromy; then s=1 and r=s+3=4. The count now has the needed purity justification. It does not classify the singularities or normalizations of these fibres.

## 5. Restrictions from the six-sphere and the canonical root

E.2.8, with its final companion, proves that every component of a reducible fibre has normalization outside Fujiki class C. Therefore ordinary blowups of a projective good model, which introduce rational or ruled Kähler components, do not provide the required central map on a complex S6.

The identity K_X0^A=g0^*O(D) is also stronger than merely having tori on the smooth locus. Every singular-fibre neighbourhood has torsion canonical bundle. Fujimoto's isotrivial examples below do not automatically satisfy this extra condition. In particular their components of positive Kodaira dimension cannot simply be transferred into a proposed torsion-canonical model without checking adjunction and the normal line.

There is a further exact conditional Chern calculation. If a component has a smooth normalization nu:S→X0 that is a holomorphic immersion, then

0→TS→nu^*TX0→N→0

is an exact sequence of bundles. Since c1(TX0)=c2(TX0)=0, it gives c1(N)=−c1(S) and c2(S)=c1(S)^2. For a non-Kähler surface, chi(O_S)=0, so Noether gives c1(S)^2+c2(S)=0; both Chern numbers are therefore zero. In particular an immersed class-VII normalization cannot have positive b2. The smooth-branch or normal-crossing hypotheses are essential to this immediate argument. Divisorial ramification of a normalization, cusp-type conductors, or exceptional curves in a resolution alter the differential sequence; no positivity assertion for their correction terms has been proved here.

## 6. Why ordinary good reduction does not finish the argument

Fujimoto's original 1988 journal article was independently opened, printed pp.98–99, Example3.1 Step4 and Example3.4(1):

Fujimoto, Y. (1988). Logarithmic transformations on elliptic fiber spaces. *Journal of Mathematics of Kyoto University, 28*(1), 91–110.

Primary article scan, mirror host: https://scispace.com/pdf/logarithmic-transformations-on-elliptic-fiber-spaces-1uinrvp8jj.pdf

The punctured family in the construction is a product. Starting from a fixed elliptic product or an appropriate finite-order extension gives an abelian-surface example, while logarithmic transformations produce non-Kähler central components. Thus finite linear monodromy, constant complex period, and an available reference good model do not alone identify every proper analytic completion with that model. The example does not itself give the present six-sphere topology, the fixed canonical root, or the required component count.

The standard torus/abelian degeneration classifications examined in the searches impose Kähler, projective, or polarization-extension hypotheses. None was applied without those hypotheses. The needed comparison remains an actual theorem for this non-Kähler, torsion-canonical, marked completion, rather than a general assertion that finite monodromy gives a unique proper filling.

## 7. New-branch obstruction remains separate

The cohomology complex of the canonical pencil can have a determinant whose zeros persist under small deformations. That does not preserve a two-dimensional section space: a double kernel can split into distinct simple effective classes. The familiar loss of an elliptic Hopf pencil under nonresonant deformation illustrates the logical failure of that inference. An S6-specific argument would be required to prevent it. E.2.10 excludes common limits of two known pairs, but does not prove that every small branch at an unconstructed limit retains the two canonical-root sections.

The exact remaining tasks are therefore to control the possible one- or two-critical-value isotrivial completion, prove uniqueness for a noncollapsed interior completion, or find an invariant that survives every new deformation branch. The former central-only critical fibres have been excluded. No completion theorem or full deformation-chain separation is inferred in this note.

# Source and scope audit for E.2.18

15 November 2026. The normalization argument contracts only three disjoint (-1)-curves on the central compact complex surface. It does not invoke a relative contraction theorem or assume that the central surface is projective before applying the analytic contraction.

Grauert, H. (1962). Über Modifikationen und exzeptionelle analytische Mengen. *Mathematische Annalen, 146*, 331–368. https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01441136

Opened the original article transcription at https://de.scribd.com/document/864372889/grauert1962, specifically §4, no.8(a), printed pp.364–365. This treats a projective space with normal O(-1) in a complex manifold and explicitly proves that its contraction is a smooth point. With dimension two this is the analytic (-1)-curve contraction used here. The additional projectivity assertion in the source is conditional; it is not used. Publisher metadata was independently opened and confirmed title, author, year, volume, pages, and DOI. The publisher PDF redirected to a subscription landing page; no complete publisher-PDF access is claimed. The EuDML request timed out. A four-page English translation was opened but contains only the introduction and was not used as proof of the contraction statement.

The compact-surface classification input is the already opened official BHPV2004 Chapter VI preview, Theorem1.1 and Table10, recorded in E_hopf_normalization_references.bib and its source audit. The proof establishes κ=-infinity directly from a nef rational curve C with C²=1 and K.C=-3, then uses b1=0 to obtain rationality. It needs no separate Kähler criterion or projectivity theorem.

The relative toric logarithmic frame follows from constant-cohomology base change, in the exact version already read and audited in E.3.1, and fibrewise evaluation. It does not require a relative blowdown or an unproved simultaneous projective-coordinate normalization.

# Independent referee of B.4.23 and B.4.25

Reviewer: limits_ab. Full source read and mathematical PASS communicated to root and boundary_d before integration. This record was saved on 2026-11-15 after the eighth checkpoint. It does not add a numbered result.

## B.4.23: exact scope passes

The retained elliptic axis is disjoint from every modification in B.4.21. Thus its intersection with the generic exceptional curve is exactly `[-s^12]` in the smooth family `C*/a(s^12)^Z`. A holomorphic logarithm of the nonzero contraction gives a smooth marking of that elliptic family. The section has integral loop class `(12,0)`, multiplied by the positive degree of any finite base change. A section extending over the disc would null-homotope that loop, which is impossible.

For a hypothetical proper analytic divisor closure of the exceptional curves, its intersection with the elliptic axis is an analytic curve. The horizontal component has generic degree one. After normalization, properness and one-dimensionality make it finite of degree one over the nonsingular disc, hence an isomorphism. Vertical components cannot change this horizontal extension. Proper images under further finite modifications therefore cannot produce the missing closure. The same reasoning applies to a proper analytic graph of a generic blowdown after resolving that graph.

For the deformation assertion, the hypotheses on both the original base coordinate and the absence of conductor intersections are essential. They give the exact sequence

`0 -> O_P1(-1) -> N_(C/Y) -> O_P1^2 -> 0`.

Its extension class vanishes, and its map on global sections is an isomorphism. The unobstructed two-dimensional embedded deformation space therefore maps locally biholomorphically to the two original parameters. Every nearby fixed pair contains a rational curve, contradicting its being an ordinary complex two-torus. If an additional conductor is introduced along the persistent elliptic axis, the curve meets that conductor and the normal sequence changes. The proposition explicitly leaves that escape route open.

## B.4.25: exact scope passes

The equivariance of an anticanonical bivector is `f(alpha z,beta w)=alpha beta f(z,w)`. Under `0<|alpha|<|beta|<1`, the sole generic monomial is `zw`; the only extra monomial is `w^(N+1)` when `alpha=beta^N`, with at most one integer `N>=2` at each parameter. No Picard-sign convention enters this computation.

For `alpha=q^m kappa(q)` and a nonzero contracting limit `beta(0)`, the Rouché radius in the proof yields `m` nonzero resonant roots for every sufficiently large `N`. Different `N` give distinct roots. Nonresonant parameters also occur arbitrarily near zero because the resonance locus is a countable union of discrete sets. Hence `h^0(-K)` has infinitely many isolated upward jumps accumulating at zero.

On any smooth proper completion, `pi_* K_X^-1` is a torsion-free coherent sheaf on the disc and therefore a vector bundle. The torsion of `R^1 pi_* K_X^-1` has no punctured support after shrinking. The displayed multiplication-by-`q-a` exact sequence consequently forces `h^0(-K)` to be constant at every sufficiently small nonzero parameter. This is the required contradiction. Passing to the reduced unique dominating component and resolving it is legitimate: the resolution is an isomorphism over the entire smooth punctured family and remains proper. Singular or nonreduced central structure therefore cannot evade the argument. A finite base change preserves the two hypotheses on the multipliers.

The elementary basis conversion in the Oda comparison checks algebraically: if `v1=n`, `v2=-n+a n'`, then `a n'=v1+v2`, so `lambda=t^a` acts with both eigenvalues tending to zero. I did not independently reopen Oda's original pages; the primary-source verification of that comparison belongs to boundary_d. The main resonance/no-completion proof does not depend on the Oda comparison.

These results concern the specified auxiliary Kato/Hopf replacements. Neither is an unrestricted nonexistence theorem for the actual torus completion.

# Independent full-source referee: B.4.33

**Reviewer:** limits_ab. **Date:** 15 November 2026. **Source reviewed:** `sections/B_global_noncompletion.tex`, complete current candidate, including the clarified phrase “rank-two subbundle of normalized invariant one-forms.” **Verdict: PASS.** The conclusion is nonexistence of a proper complex-analytic filling of the specified actual punctured family after any finite parameter cover. The theorem does not assume that an unknown cap is a logarithmic torsor or that a punctured isomorphism extends meromorphically.

This referee also reread the complete sources of B.4.24, B.4.27, B.4.30, B.4.31, and B.4.32. The independent B.4.32 source-to-target period and affine-overlap checks are recorded in `ab_push_global_loop_referee.md` and `code/ab_push_affine_loop.py/.out/.json`.

## 1. Reduction from an arbitrary proper filling

The punctured total space is smooth and connected, so only one irreducible component dominates it. Retaining its reduced closure, normalization, and proper resolution preserves the prescribed punctured family. The smooth dominating fourfold has a nonzero parameter, which is a nonzero divisor. B.4.4 therefore applies without a hypothesis on central multiplicities; its coherent bi-anticanonical pencil and three distinguished critical-value branches extend the actual marked map. Resolving its graph and the central support changes only the parameter-zero locus.

On any dominating central threefold component, a nonconstant map to the marked base curve has fibres of dimension two. A three-dimensional fibre thus requires a whole central component with constant marked value. There are finitely many such values. Shrinking near the cusp removes all the others, and gives equidimensionality away from the single base origin. The explicit coefficient-ideal argument of B.4.30 applies to the remaining excess-one fibre and permits finite point blowups of the surface base. Neither projectivity of the cap nor a general analytic semistable-reduction theorem is being assumed.

Finite local multisections exist for the resulting equidimensional map by transverse analytic slicing. Their normalized two-dimensional sources are Cohen--Macaulay, hence finite flat over the smooth surface base. The compact inverse image of the origin admits a finite cover of such constructions; a smaller bidisc has its full inverse image in their union by properness. This justifies a single common positive degree and a single finite set of later root/norm choices.

## 2. The cap-derived differences extend in the exact sheaf

The proof correctly separates three types of boundary divisor.

* At each exceptional divisor over the origin, both base valuations are positive. The actual monodromy has primitive invariant lattice generated by w and delta. The B.4.30 logarithmic-form, integral-residue, and chart-chain proof applies.
* At the strict q axis, the same full-rank condition holds, and the completion away from the missing origin is the specified actual cusp. Proper modifications over the origin do not change that fact.
* At the strict r axis, the monodromy kernel is larger. The proof does not replace it by the cocharacter lattice. Instead B.4.31 shows that all actual local angular classes lie in the primitive delta line. Hence the first normalized form has zero residue and the second has integer residue. The resulting character sheaf is precisely `O* x M_r^gp`, not the full log sheaf in both coordinates.

Under a local cyclic root and toric subdivision, a new angular class maps rationally into the span of the old local angular classes. Its actual nearby-fibre homology remains integral, so primitivity of delta gives the same integral conclusion after these operations. The simultaneous root accounts for both central multiplicities and splitting of the finitely many multisections before subdivision. Multiplicative norms multiply the difference by a positive integer and preserve the zero first-character order along the strict r axis.

The proper normal dominating models have trivial finite Stein factor over the normal surface base, since ordinary torus fibres are connected. This supplies connected central fibres for the finite chart-chain comparison, including sections with limits on different components. Hartogs is applied to sections, not to torsors. On a punctured small bidisc the constant period lattice has zero H1; a lift to the exact bounded-character sheaf exists. Divide boundary monomials, extend each unit and its inverse, and restore the same monomials. Uniqueness modulo the constant period lattice preserves the cocycles.

The glued object is therefore a torsor under the specific associated sheaf `A_S`. It is built from differences of extending local traces. No log-group presentation of the unknown proper space is used. Its ordinary restriction is the actual positive contracted power of the original torsor, with the actual trace sections as identifications.

## 3. Primary analytic input checked independently

Opened the original publisher PDF of Illusie--Kato--Nakayama (2005), Theorem 6.2, printed p.32, and Theorem 6.3 with proof, printed p.34. These are analytic statements for proper separated log-smooth maps; the log-smooth base and torsion-free characteristic-group cokernel hypotheses hold in the reduced semistable charts used here. Theorem 6.3 gives ordinary log-de Rham local freeness and the nilpotent Gauss--Manin extension. No Kähler assumption is present. Also opened the complete 2007 erratum: it concerns Theorem 7.1, not this analytic comparison. The proof here does not invoke Hodge spectral-sequence degeneration. Bibliography keys already present are `IKN2005E` and `IKN2007ErratumE`; no duplicate entries were added.

The saturation argument is essential: connected reduced central fibres eliminate parameter torsion in `R1 f_*O`, while the explicitly known invariant-form span is a subbundle of the canonical extension. Equality off the parameter-zero divisor then gives equality of the coherent saturated subsheaves. This is not an essential-singularity extension assertion for arbitrary holomorphic sections.

## 4. The two distinct origin comparisons

B.4.27 gives `H1(S,A_S)=0`, so the trace-derived torsor has a global section h. This h need not correspond to a holomorphic section of the original unknown cap. The proof only uses that its difference from a multiplied trace is an associated-log-torus section.

Along the strict r axis, B.4.32 gives zero normalized loop class for a multiplied trace relative to the actual ordinary B.4.19 origin. An associated section there changes the radial label only by delta. The rotating reference origin has the same loop class because its actual comparison is `G(s)+6v delta`. Thus the gamma evaluation of `h-D o` is zero. No claim of boundedness or meromorphic extension of this difference is needed.

Along the strict q axis, the known actual sigma origin extends through the central open orbit of its root fan. Comparison with traces uses the same two-extending-sections argument and can be included in the finite common norm multiplier. Hence `h-D sigma` is an associated section on the whole punctured q-axis neighbourhood. The q-order map on the constant period lattice is diagonal `(12m,12m)` and injective. The ghost-cocycle argument then makes its radial Y-connecting class zero. Projecting the additive homology connecting class to Y kills w and delta but does not alter its gamma evaluation. This gives the second zero.

These two controls are different. The first uses the full global marked base and its peripheral monodromy; the second uses the actual cusp and its injective ghost valuation. Neither identifies the unknown central model with B.4.19.

## 5. Exact contradiction and finite-cover scope

The coordinate change from the actual sigma-based chart to the rotating chart is `zeta -> zeta - sigma + F`. Therefore, as torsor points, `sigma-o=F`; the sign in the displayed difference equation is correct. For every positive parameter-cover degree n,

`F(s,v+n)-F(s,v) = n gamma - 6n(n-1) delta`.

Its gamma label is n. After the common positive trace and norm degree D, the two zero evaluations would imply `0 = Dn`, impossible. The independent exact code verifies this symbolic identity for arbitrary positive n and D. All extra roots and trace multipliers have positive degree and cannot annihilate the integer obstruction.

The proof consequently covers every initial finite parameter cover, arbitrary singular centres, additional vertical components, and central multiplicities. Retaining the main component at the start means none of those additions evades the argument. Its use of the actual global monodromy and exact affine clutching is explicit. I found no remaining substantive gap in the reviewed source.

# A corrected cusp over the punctured bidisc

Owner: limits_ab. 15November2026,19:00UTC. **Working proof; independent review requested. No completion over the origin is claimed.**

The intended contribution is an explicit comparison of the rotating central model with the prescribed punctured cusp, not another claim that a multiple cusp already fills the actual family.

## 1. Exact affine data

On the logarithmic cover of U=Δq*×Δr* put

s=log(q)/(2πi), t=log(r)/(2πi), C(q)=βpart(q)+s.

The actual cusp functions μ(q) and C(q) are single-valued and holomorphic at q=0. After the B.4.9 affine gauge, the family is its Jacobian with period

γ(s,t)=(6μ(q), C(q)−s−12t), δ=(0,1).

The other two periods are u=(τ,μ), w=(1,0), with τ increasing by1 under s→s+1. The monodromies send γ toγ−δ under a q loop and toγ−12δ under an r loop. The ambient additive C² coordinate is unchanged; these are integral changes of the period basis.

Define the affine q-deck translation

b(s,t)=γ(s,t)/12−δ/24.

Its twelvefold norm is

sum(k=0..11)b(s+k,t)=γ(s,t)−6δ,

an actual integral period. Further b(s,t+1)−b(s,t)=−δ. Thus on the q-root cover q=z^12 it gives a genuine order12 affine deck action, well-defined around the r loop. Taking −b supplies the opposite component rotation. The half-integral scalar correction is retained; a bare γ/12 would have the wrong twelfth iterate.

The explicit logarithmic-cover function

f(s,t)=( μ(q)(s+12t)/2,
         C(q)(s+12t)/12−(s+12t)^2/24+6t )

satisfies the exact identities

f(s+1,t)−f(s,t)=b(s,t),
f(s,t+1)−f(s,t)=γ(s,t).

The second difference is an integral period. Therefore translation by f gives a holomorphic trivialization of this affine torus torsor on U. Translation by −f treats the desired opposite rotation. Composing with the already proved B.4.9 gauge identifies it with the actual retained affine cusp family, not merely with its unmarked fibrewise periods.

## 2. The rotating model with the wrong q-boundary

For completeness, its compactification can be constructed on the root cover before quotient. In multiplicative coordinates, the periods have valuation shears

G1:(y1,y2,hz,hr)→(y1+12hz,y2,hz,hr),
G2:(y1,y2,hz,hr)→(y1,y2+12hz+12hr,hz,hr).

The fractional affine deck has monomial shear

H:(y1,y2,hz,hr)→(y1,y2+hz+hr,hz,hr),

and rotates z by exp(2πi/12). It has H^12=G2 and commutes with G1. Its unit factors are single-valued in q. In the convention W=exp(−2πiζ2), the positive translation b multiplies W by

r z exp(−2πi C(q)/12) exp(πi/12)

and the first multiplicative coordinate by exp(πi μ(q)). In the twelfth iterate, the z-rotation contributes exp(2πi·66/12)=−1 and the scalar correction contributes exp(πi)=−1; their product is1, so the twelfth iterate is exactly the actual second period, including its unit.

Use the usual unit-square cusp triangulation, with twelve consecutive bottom cells in the y1 direction, and the same bridge choice in every cell. It is invariant under G1 and H, hence under G2. Each of its unit prisms has the three determinant-one cones of A.2.1. The A.1.2 compact-core construction on the I12 elliptic root model, or its explicitly unfolded toric charts, gives the proper rank-one model on the root cover. The finite H action extends because it is an actual torus-unit map preserving the fan. It is free on the r-central divisor: its nontrivial powers rotate the twelve r-component labels, and at most two adjacent r-branches meet. It is also free on the q-central divisor away from r=0, by the nonzero translation of the q-component grid. The same r-branch argument covers their common locus. Off those divisors it is free because the root-base action is free. Consequently its quotient is smooth, proper over (q,r), flat over r, and has reduced NC r-centre.

The component monodromy around q is +1 for b and −1 for −b, in the common convention. Its q-fibre for r≠0 has multiplicity12, exactly as in B.3.3. This is the wrong q-boundary, and is not substituted for the actual Mumford fibre.

## 3. A proper family over the punctured bidisc

Let B^×=(Δq×Δr)\{(0,0)}. On the open subset q≠0 use the preceding rotating model, with the sign giving component shift−1. On the open subset r≠0 use the actual prescribed reduced Mumford cusp family. On their intersection U, glue by translation−f composed with the B.4.9 actual gauge. These are specified holomorphic isomorphisms of the actual period quotients. There is only one overlap, so no additional triple cocycle is being omitted.

The resulting analytic space is Hausdorff and proper over B^×: both properties follow locally over the two base open sets, which are proper Hausdorff models there. It has smooth total space and is flat over r. Its r-central fibre over q≠0 has the required shift−1, whereas its q-fibre over each r≠0 is exactly the prescribed reduced Mumford cusp. This is a partial filling over a punctured bidisc, with the missing set reduced to the single base origin.

The remaining task is a proper analytic completion over that origin with smooth total space and reduced NC r-centre (after any permitted finite base change), and a compatibility proof with the global finite-place data. None is supplied by the exact affine identities. The comparison contains quadratic logarithms, so neither Remmert graph closure nor a meromorphic common modification may be assumed. Indeed a proper birational comparison between the two full q-boundaries for fixed r≠0 is excluded by their multiplicity12 versus reduced multiplicity1, as already proved in B.3.7.

## Verification record

`code/ab_push_corrected_cusp_overlap.py` verifies the two finite differences, twelvefold norm, scalar phase, commuting shears and determinant-one unit cones exactly. The global root-cover fan/gluing and free-action arguments above require independent review before this partial-family proposition is promoted to a numbered result. The earlier288 q-cover Euler calculation is already established in the parent package and is not claimed here as a new result. The auxiliary B.3.7 plumbing's twelvefold circle cover has the same local12-by12 cusp combinatorics, but no global isomorphism of that cover with the actual q-basechanged family is inferred from the numerical match.
