# Independent referee of B.4.21

Owner: limits_ab. Full source read: `B_degree_one_tate_repair.tex`.

**PASS in its stated collar scope.** I independently checked the six ambient point blow-up charts, including the final conic; the reduced I12 configuration; coefficients2min(i,12−i); degree vector−4 atC0,+4 atC6; and the72 smooth curve blow-ups. The u-chart gives total-transform powers2j+2 and2j at the adjacent vertical divisors, so the exact induction h=e x^zi y^zj and s=xy is valid. Each curve centre remains Cartier in D, and its blow-up raises exactly the corresponding h exponent. This proves holomorphicity of the actual function H=h/q with divisor D, not merely an abstract isomorphism of normal lines.

The subsequent character argument is sufficient: near D the u,V valuations are forced by their product s12 and their known restrictions; the adjacent toric character ratios have no divisorial poles and restrict to coordinates on D. Together with H they are local coordinates. The product G(q,H) is uniformly convergent, nonzero and holomorphic for |a|≤A<1, |H|<η<1. Its exact functional equation linearizes the closing. Multiplication by G and its inverse extends on the toric node charts, so the comparison includes all twelve nodes.

The original nodal component C0 has two local end branches in the old node chart. The local valuations0 and12 belong to those distinct local pieces; this does not contradict the global compact curve centre used by the blow-ups. All centres are disjoint from the persistent elliptic curve, so its degree−1 normal is unchanged. Properness follows from the finite sequence of proper blow-ups; smoothness and DVR flatness are correctly stated. The source expressly does not claim a submersion over s0, a global second-period action or an actual S6 cap.

# Exact two-period action on the repaired collar: working calculation

Status: exact identities proved; a global complementary cap and proper period quotient are unconstructed. This note is not a numbered result.

Use the B.4.21 convention `a=exp(-2*pi*i*mu)`, with `0<|a|<1`, and

`G_a(H)=product_(j>=1)(1+a^j H)`.

On a sufficiently small fixed H-disc this converges uniformly, is nonzero, and obeys `G_a(aH)=G_a(H)/(1+aH)`. In the actual repaired coordinates `Z=x G_a(H)`, the first period is

`A:(Z,H) -> (kappa(q) q Z, a(q) H)`.

The desired second period in these linear collar coordinates is

`B:(Z,H) -> (c(q) Z, b(q,r) H)`,

where `c=a^-6` is the retained translation by `6P` in this convention and `b=q r^12 D(q)` for the actual holomorphic unit `D`. It therefore has the exact expression

`B:(x,H) -> (c x G_a(H)/G_a(bH), bH)`.

This commutes with A wherever both expressions are defined. The functional identity proves commutation before any analytic continuation. It gives the actual local second-period action, but it does not extend it automatically through the full Kato complement.

For fixed `a`, `G_a` extends to an entire function of H, with simple zeros `H=-a^-j`, `j>=1`. Hence the formula defines a meromorphic conjugacy on larger charts; it need not define a holomorphic automorphism there. In particular it cannot be used to assert a quotient is Hausdorff or proper, nor can all of its zero divisors simply be retained in ordinary torus fibres.

## The F=0 corner

In the B.4.22 third chart,

`q=u v F`, `H=(1-F)/F`, `r=g(1-F)`.

The first Kato period becomes

`F' = F/(a+(1-a)F)`,

`u' = kappa u^2 v (a+(1-a)F)`,

`v' = 1/(kappa u)`.

It preserves q exactly. With `g'=r/(1-F')` it also preserves r on the relevant overlap. Thus this is an explicit meromorphic expression, not a globally regular automorphism of the local resolution. Its leading expression along F=0 is parabolic and may motivate another quotient construction; none is claimed here.

The apparent denominator in `bH` cancels:

`bH = r^12 D(q) u v (1-F)`.

Consequently `G_a(bH)` is holomorphic near F=0 on bounded small-coordinate regions, while the numerator `G_a(H)` still has its accumulating zero pattern at the omitted end. This cancellation is a useful exact compatibility target. It does not resolve the numerator, the exceptional-curve persistence, the original q-deck descent, or properness at the missing base origin.

The expression `W_test=1+H=1+r/f` should not be confused with the marked multiplicative normal coordinate H. If one separately requires `W_test -> b W_test`, then `f -> r f/((b-1)f+b r)`. That Möbius identity is conditional and is not the actual first-period action, which scales H.

Finite symbolic identities are verified by `code/ab_push_full_period.py`; its output is retained beside the script.

# Independent referee: global affine loop defect B.4.32

**Reviewer:** limits_ab. **Date:** 15 November 2026. **Verdict:** PASS for the complete source `sections/B_global_loop_defect.tex`, read in full, and for its current working precursor `sections/D_global_loop_defect_working.md`. This does not prejudge the final noncompletion argument reserved for B.4.33.

## Definition and finite-cover normalization

The connecting class of a holomorphic Jacobian section is canonically in `H1(Delta*, Lambda) = Lambda/(T-I)Lambda`. It does not require a single-valued additive lift. Since `T-I` has image in the primitive delta line, its image in `V = Lambda_Q/Q delta` is unambiguous. Under a degree-e parameter root, the restriction is the norm `I+T+...+T^(e-1)`, equal modulo delta to `e I`. Dividing by e gives the claimed normalization. Additivity of the connecting map and finite transfer give the trace formula with its stated degree. The independent symbolic-induction check is `code/ab_push_affine_loop.py`, with saved stdout and JSON; no finite sample is used to infer the all-degree formula.

## Actual affine changes of origin

I checked B.4.19.1--B.4.19.5 against the finite-place source-to-target matrices in B.1.2 and the exact checked program `ab_push_finite_decks.py`.

* On ordinary overlaps, the logarithm difference is an integer n, so the affine term is `n v delta`. Its positive radial-loop label lies in `Z delta`.
* The two actual Lie-frame matrices are independent of the degeneration parameter and fix the last coordinate. The exact covariance is `R_j Pi_c = Pi'_c A_j`, with target tau, mu, and beta retained. Thus lattice transport is the actual `A_j`; no omitted moving Lie frame contributes to the loop invariant.
* The root-to-ordinary connector is `w_j Pi_c v_j`. Its c-dependent part is `-12 epsilon_j w_j v delta`; the difference between scalar closing gauges is the opposite term. The full vector connector after both gauges is independent of r, not merely trivial after projecting its first coordinate.
* The finite root deck factors `r^4` and `r^-3` have only delta winding. Period-representative changes alter a loop label by `(T-I)ell`, also delta.
* The corrected cusp comparison is exactly `G(s)+6v delta`. The r-independent G contributes no radial-loop label. The remaining label is `6 delta`.
* A shift of all twelve central labels is not the identity: it can translate the elliptic base by `6P`. The proof correctly retains that fact. Its change under an r loop remains `(T-I)gamma`, which is in the delta line.

Consequently the reference origins define a global reference loop class, although they need not define a global affine zero section. This is the precise assertion needed for patching lambda.

## Geometric independence of the extending section

The reduction of an arbitrary proper analytic filling is legitimate at the stated scope. Retain the unique reduced main component, normalize, resolve, and apply the strengthened B.4.4. Its direct-image and marked-critical-value proof uses only a smooth connected dominating fourfold, with no assumption on central multiplicities. Principalization gives the model used by B.4.31 without changing the punctured family.

Over a sufficiently small ordinary q disc outside the finite bad set, the local simultaneous root and toric subdivision of B.4.30 apply with q retained as a smooth tangential parameter. A common further root and refinement compare any two such choices; a global nonprojective semistable-reduction theorem is not invoked.

The finite Stein factor of the proper normal dominating model is generically degree one and hence is the normal base. Central surface fibres are therefore connected. This supplies the finite chain of charts comparing sections limiting to different central components.

The saturation argument for normalized closed logarithmic forms in B.4.30 is independent of full-rank monodromy. Its rank-one application here uses the explicitly retained period frame and the same analytic IKN comparison theorem. B.4.31 supplies the missing integral-residue assertion: every local angular class is in `Z delta`. Thus the two exponentiated primitives have the precise form `O* x M_r^gp`; the first has no r order, and the second contributes only delta winding. The chart-chain comparison therefore proves zero difference in V for any two extending sections. This comparison does not choose a bounded origin relative to the old model.

Proper transforms of section germs are holomorphic away from isolated points of their two-dimensional base. Comparing on this dense open set suffices, because the normalized connecting classes are locally constant. Smooth points of a reduced local central fibre provide local transverse sections. Finite traces and further roots then give the statement for arbitrary finite multisections.

## Global monodromy and scope

Section independence removes the choice of cap section, while the exact affine audit removes the reference-origin ambiguity. These are separate steps, and together they produce a global flat section of the actual quotient local system V.

The quotient matrices have fixed lines `Q(1,2,-4)` and `Q(1,3,-3)`, with zero intersection. An explicit common fixed-equation minor is 6. Removing further ordinary points does not remove the two peripheral monodromies from the image. The independent exact program also checks the two distinct elliptic unipotent fixed lines. Averaging a finite orbit first kills its gamma coefficient, so the finite-orbit variant is valid as well.

The conclusion controls only a loop class. An essential translation with zero loop class is not asserted to extend. The corrected local caution is exact: changing the punctured marking of a standard local cap by `chi(r)=[log(r) u/(2 pi i)]` gives nonzero local class u modulo delta, while all differences of extending sections are unchanged. It cannot patch over the global base because u has an infinite monodromy orbit. B.4.15 is correctly not claimed to establish that particular local example.

The current theorem applies to arbitrary proper analytic fillings after finite parameter covers. It does not by itself prove nonexistence. The further cusp comparison and torsor argument belong to B.4.33 and require their own full-source review.

# B.4.31: global control of every r-central meridian

The initial statement was strengthened, with root's explicit approval, to every proper analytic filling after a finite parameter cover. Retaining the reduced main component, normalizing and resolving gives a smooth connected dominating fourfold and therefore r-flatness. The B.4.4 proof only used a nonzero divisor to make its direct image torsion-free; its central reduced-NC assumption was unnecessary and has been removed with that explanation. Graph resolution and principalization then produce exactly the normal-crossings support with multiplicities already treated in the proof. No semistable-reduction theorem is used. No unrestricted nonexistence conclusion is included without the separately audited affine-clutching bridge.

The intrinsic marked fibration extends by B.4.4, and graph resolution gives a smooth global model over P1 with normal-crossings r-central support, possibly with multiplicities. After excluding finitely many base values, compact ordered double-branch strata form proper smooth families of curves. Their connected fibre components, branch orderings and monomial-fibre choices have finite monodromy. Moving a normal meridian along one connected punctured double curve changes its based loop only by conjugation, so its actual H1 class has a finite q-monodromy orbit.

The local monomial equation r=x^a y^b also shows that its r-monodromy orbit is finite. Actual r-monodromy is unipotent I-m(delta tensor gamma), so the angular class belongs to ker(gamma). Its projection to the residual elliptic lattice has finite orbit. The exact finite-place quotient matrices generate the lower and upper elementary unipotents; a vector fixed by positive powers of both is zero. Primitivity of delta upgrades the rational conclusion to an integral multiple of delta. Higher-crossing angular homology is spanned rationally by the pairwise double-branch circles.

Root and boundary_d independently passed the complete source, including the multiplicity and self-crossing treatment. The referee is sections/D_B4431_referee.md. The result is integrated immediately after B.4.30. Exact source and output are code/ab_push_global_meridians.py/.out/.json. An initial sign-only expectation for a selected minor was corrected from +k*l to -k*l; the exact initial script and failed assertion remain saved. The nonvanishing argument was unchanged.

This global theorem is stronger than the corresponding local rank-one information, but does not itself determine an affine origin. The next candidate argument uses a normalized r-loop class of local trace origins in the coinvariants Lambda_Q/Q delta. That separate argument is being audited by root, boundary_d and classification_e; no unrestricted nonexistence conclusion is included in B.4.31.

# Independent Kato geometry and tangent-weight audit

Owner: limits_ab. Date:15November2026,18:08UTC. This note audits D's B.4.11 and the proposed next relative-bundle construction. The weight result below is proved; no analytic globalization theorem for the threefold bundle is inferred merely from the weight calculation.

## B.4.11 referee

The exact linear neighbourhood in B.4.10 identifies a neighbourhood of the elliptic axis with the negative line bundle N. For degree−8 its contraction is the normally generated degree-eight elliptic cone. The maximal-ideal blow-up recovers the line bundle, so the flow/functorial-blow-up argument identifies π_*T_S(−log E) with Θ_Y. The invariant dlog two-form has poles exactly E plus the rational cycle C and gives K_S=−E−C. The resulting injection Ω¹(log E)⊗K_S→Ω¹_S proves H²TlogE=0 because b1(S)=1 forces H⁰Ω¹_S=0. The Atiyah extension on E has nonzero class c1(N); H¹At(N)→H¹T_E is an isomorphism. Every higher formal graded piece has positive degree and zero H¹. Finally varying a=e^(2πiτ) changes the marked elliptic period with nonzero Kodaira–Spencer class, even at extra-automorphism elliptic curves. This makes the Leray boundary map vanish. I found no gap in these Kato/formal-neighbourhood steps. D/root separately checked the analytic local-to-global criterion against its primary source.

## Complete global-vector-field calculation

Let n≥1 and write the central contracted Kato germ as

F(x,v)=(K x v^n,av), with K=κa^n and 0<|a|<1.

A global holomorphic vector field on S_n restricts to the outer spherical annulus of the Kato construction. In the blowdown coordinates its two coefficients extend to the ball by Hartogs. They agree with the pushed-down field on the connected unmodified part of the fundamental region by the identity principle. The collar gluing therefore imposes

V(F)=DF·V.

Write V=P(x,v)∂x+Q(x,v)∂v and expand in x. The coefficient Q_i(v) satisfies

K^i v^(ni) Q_i(av)=a Q_i(v).

For i>0, comparison of the smallest nonzero v-order is impossible, so Q_i=0. For i=0, the power-series coefficients give Q_0(v)=b v, since a is not a root of unity. In the first component the x⁰ coefficient vanishes by the same lowest-order comparison. For the x¹ coefficient P_1(v), the equation is

P_1(av)=P_1(v)+nb.

Evaluation at v=0 gives b=0, and then P_1 is constant. For every i>1 the equation is

P_i(v)=K^(i−1)v^(n(i−1))P_i(av),

which again forces P_i=0. Thus H⁰(S_n,Θ)=C·x∂x. The Euler field is tangent to E, so H⁰(S_n,Flog)=C with weight zero for Flog=T_S(−log E).

The Hartogs extension is used only to establish this exact germ functional equation; no finite Taylor truncation is used to classify the vector fields.

## Fixed locus and tangent weights

The action λ·(x,v)=(λx,v) fixes E pointwise. Outside E and the rational cycle, an equality with a nontrivial Kato deck iterate would require a^k v=v, hence k=0; then λ=1. On each rational curve in the cycle the action is nontrivial and fixes its two endpoints. The quotient cycle has n nodes, counting the self-node when n=1, so for λ≠1 the fixed locus is E and these n points.

Adjacent two-dimensional toric rays are (j,1),(j+1,1). Their dual characters evaluate on the x-scaling cocharacter (1,0) as −1,+1. Thus the tangent weights at every cycle node are λ^−1,λ. The logarithmic tangent sheaf is the ordinary tangent sheaf there. On E its Atiyah extension has both fibre weights zero and Chern character2; the normal line has degree−n and weightλ.

## Exact character computation

Apply the holomorphic Lefschetz formula to any root of unity λ≠1. It requires only a compact complex manifold, an equivariant holomorphic vector bundle, and a finite group action. The original theorem is Atiyah–Singer1968III, Theorem(4.6), printedp566; existing bibliography key AtiyahSinger1968IIIE is reused. The contribution of E is

2nλ/(λ−1)²,

and each node contributes

−(λ²+1)/(λ−1)².

Their sum is identically−n. Since H²Flog=0 and H⁰Flog is one weight-zero Euler field,

H¹(S_n,Flog)=(n+1) copies of the trivial representation.

This equality for infinitely many roots of unity determines the finite-dimensional C* character. The natural surjection H¹Flog→H¹(E,T_E)=C has weight zero; the Leray kernel therefore gives

H¹(Y,Θ_Y)=C^n with trivial C* action, and H⁰(Y,Θ_Y)=C with trivial action.

Consequently for the associated Y-bundle over P1 formed with any line bundle and this genuine C* action, the direct images of the relative tangent sheaf are O in degree0, O^n in degree1, and zero in degree2. Leray then proves H² of the relative tangent sheaf is zero. Analytic globalization of an assigned local deformation along the whole singular section requires its own precise relative deformation argument; this note does not replace that step.

Exact symbolic checks, adjacent-ray inversion and the vector-field defect for n=1,...,12 are saved in code/ab_push_kato_weights.py/.out/.json.

## Opened primary sources and scope

Opened Cathelineau1978 original Numdam PDF, https://www.numdam.org/article/ASENS_1978_4_11_3_391_0.pdf, Definitionp391 and Theorem1p392. Its group acts over the deformation base, so it does not provide the required moving-base weight-one action. Opened Rim1980 original journal scan, https://scispace.com/pdf/equivariant-g-structure-on-versal-deformations-3i8iymesgm.pdf, pp217–221: its construction is formal and algebraic. Neither is silently promoted to a convergent moving-base theorem for singular compact Y.

Opened Atiyah–SingerIII at https://math.uchicago.edu/~shmuel/tom-readings/AS%20III.pdf and Atiyah–SegalII at https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/as2.pdf. Their scanned PDFs initially exposed only metadata; targeted primary-source search returned the complete relevant text of III Theorem(4.6), printedp566, and II Theorem(2.12), printedp540. The former is sufficient using finite cyclic subgroups, as above. An attempted opening of Doan2026 arXiv2606.09199 returned DisabledError; no theorem from it is used. The smooth-manifold-only Doan2022 Kuranishi result is likewise not applied to singular Y.

# Parabolic Kato insertion: retained constructive working note

2026-11-15, 17:37 UTC. **Unintegrated working note.** The explicit auxiliary compact family and its elliptic-axis calculations are developed below. Root and boundary_d have received the formulas; boundary_d has independently checked the normal factors, theta/Picard calculation, and failure of the marked exceptional divisor to extend. The complete attachment to the prescribed S6 family, a third central component, proper global gluing, and smoothing remain **unconstructed**. No numerical S6 invariant is inferred from this note.

## 1. What ordinary retained blow-ups cannot do

Let C* be the good-reduction punctured elliptic interface of B.4.8, with normal L_m of degree zero and nonzero mixed winding m. If a modification on one branch consists of point blow-ups on this interface, the strict transform is again the same elliptic curve and

`N_new = L_m ⊗ O_C(−D_exc)`.

Here D_exc is the **weighted total exceptional correction**, namely the sum of the restrictions of the total transforms of the exceptional divisors at every step. For n successive infinitely-near blow-ups on the strict elliptic curve at one point p it is n[p], although the reduced exceptional locus meets the final strict transform only once.

If both N_new and this Cartier correction extend on a proper semistable completion of C*, then their tensor product extends L_m, contradicting B.4.8. This remains true after arbitrary finite base changes. Negative degree and a drifting translation cannot remove the obstruction while retaining that correction divisor. A different operation must lose its Cartier extension.

## 2. Explicit Kato data

Fix n≥1, holomorphic units a(s), κ(s) on a small disc, with 0<|a(s)|<1. Initially one can take both constant. Let B_R⊂C² be a small ball. Blow up n times successively at the intersection of the newest exceptional curve with the strict transform of the axis x=0. In the last chart the composite map is

`π_n(z,w)=(z w^n,w)`.

Define embeddings into this chart by

`σ_s(z,w)=(κ(s) z, a(s)w+(1−a(s))s)`.

These are holomorphic embeddings because a and κ are units. They can be chosen on a neighborhood of the closed ball with image compactly contained in the modified ball, uniformly for small s. Indeed choose A<ρ<1 with |a|≤A and K≥|κ|. Shrink the parameter disc so that |a w+(1−a)s|≤ρR on B_R. Then

`|π_n σ_s(z,w)|² ≤ K²R²(ρR)^(2n)+ρ²R² < R²`

after shrinking R. This proves the required compact containment without requiring κ to represent a special Picard class.

Remove σ_s(B_R) from the modified ball and identify the outer and inner collars by σ_s∘π_n. The outer collar avoids the exceptional locus, so this map is a biholomorphism of collars. The same gluing in the product with the parameter disc is holomorphic. Uniformly disjoint collars give a Hausdorff smooth threefold; every fibre has a compact fundamental region, uniformly over compact parameter subsets. The projection is therefore a proper holomorphic submersion. This is the standard Kato annulus construction, with explicit moving embedding; see Istrati–Otiman–Pontecorvo–Ruggiero (2022), §2, printed1351–1353. It is established machinery, not a novelty assertion.

The contracted map f_s=π_nσ_s has the fixed point (0,s). In coordinates v=w−s it is

`f_s(x,v)=(κ(s) x(a(s)v+s)^n, a(s)v)`.

For s=0 this is the parabolic monomial germ with matrix [[1,n],[0,1]]. The indicated n-fold modification is exactly the one in Istrati et al. (2022), Example9.10. The central surface is a parabolic Inoue surface; its elliptic curve has normal degree −n and is disjoint from its rational cycle. Its fundamental group is Z and its second Betti number is n. These are auxiliary surface data, not the topology of a proposed S6 centre.

## 3. Exact elliptic marking and full tubular line

The strict axis is preserved by gluing. In the coordinate v=w−s its deck map is v↦a(s)v, so it extends as the smooth proper elliptic family

`E_s=C*/a(s)^Z`.

Choose a fixed fundamental annulus avoiding v=0, then take |s| small enough that both v+s and a(s)v+s are nonzero there. There are two useful normal frames, which must not be confused:

* in the literal last blow-up chart, σ_sπ_n gives normal factor `g_z(v)=κ(v+s)^n`;
* in the blowdown normal coordinate `x=z(v+s)^n`, it gives `g_x(v)=κ(av+s)^n`.

The two differ by the nonzero gauge x=(v+s)^n z on this annulus. Both extend over s=0. The central blowdown-frame factor is κa^n v^n; the degree of this line on E_a is −n (Istrati et al., Proposition5.6 gives precisely the factor λ v^n and Chern class −n).

There is an explicit regular gauge. On the fixed annulus set

`H_s(v)=∏_{j≥0}(1+a(s)^j s/v)^n`.

It converges locally uniformly in (v,s), is nonzero for sufficiently small |s|, and satisfies

`H_s(av)/H_s(v)=(1+s/(av))^n`.

Replacing x by x/H_s(v) changes g_x to κa^n v^n. Thus for constant a,κ the normal line is the constant family of a fixed degree−n line; for varying units it is the evident holomorphic family of such lines. The gluing is linear in the normal coordinate, so it describes an actual tubular line neighbourhood, not just its first infinitesimal normal bundle.

## 4. Exact generic Hopf blowdown

For fixed s≠0 define the entire function of v

`G_s(v)=∏_{j≥1}(1+a(s)^j v/s)^n`.

The product converges locally uniformly on C_v and obeys

`G_s(av)=G_s(v)/(1+av/s)^n`.

Consequently the holomorphic map

`Ψ_s(x,v)=(xG_s(v),v)`

satisfies `Ψ_s f_s = diag(κs^n,a) Ψ_s`. Composing Ψ_s with π_n on a Kato fundamental region and using this identity gives a holomorphic map from the compact fibre to the diagonal Hopf surface

`H_(κs^n,a)=(C²\{0})/〈(X,V)↦(κs^n X,aV)〉`.

The origin of this target is excluded: its preimage would be the attracting fixed point (0,s), which lies inside the removed embedded ball. The only contracted axis orbit is [v=−s]. The zeros of G_s are v=−s/a^j, j≥1, all in that orbit; π_n itself contracts its exceptional locus at v=−s. Near v=−s, G_s is nonzero (its factors are 1−a^j), so the local map is precisely π_n followed by a holomorphic coordinate unit. Thus the map is n successive blow-ups at that point and the indicated infinitely-near strict-axis points. This paragraph's global quotient/blowdown realization is the current additional referee target; the functional equation and local model are exact.

In particular the original Hopf axis has normal L_(κs^n), while the strict transform has

`N_s = L_(κs^n) ⊗ O_E(−n[−s])`.

This agrees with the preceding annulus calculation. With θ_a(av)=−v^−1θ_a(v), the bundle O([p]) has factor −p/v. At p=−s the displayed tensor product therefore has factor κv^n, equivalent to κa^n v^n by the gauge v^n. Boundary_d checked this Picard calculation independently.

## 5. The exceptional correction really does not extend

The effective degree-n divisor n[−s] on E_a has no limit in Sym^n(E_a). For constant a choose inequivalent classes [v_1] and [v_2] and sequences s_j=−a^j v_i→0. The divisors along the two sequences are constantly n[v_i], giving different limits. More invariantly, the Abel–Jacobi image of n[−s] has monodromy n times a primitive lattice cycle; a section extending across a disc would have zero winding. The latter proof also covers varying a(s) and every finite base change.

Adding a vertical E×{0} component cannot produce a Cartier closure: remove that vertical component, and the remaining proper degree-n horizontal divisor would be finite over the disc and yield a continuous Sym^n limit. Thus the positive normal extension does not contradict B.4.8 or §1. The normal was changed by a divisor whose Cartier extension is genuinely lost.

## 6. Elliptic complete-intersection attachment: exact first-order data

Let E be a smooth (2,2) complete intersection in P3. Put L=O_E(2), of degree8. Then N_(E/P3)=L⊕L, and the exceptional divisor D of Bl_E P3 is E×P1, with

`N_(D/Bl_E P3)=L ⊠ O_P1(−1)`

in the lines convention for projectivization. In particular the E-degree +8 must be retained; merely quoting the ruling degree−1 loses essential data.

For an unmodified Hopf-product cap, its divisor E×P1 has normal M⊠O, where deg M=0. The required triple-divisor line would be `(L⊗M)^−1 ⊠ O(1)`, whose E-degree is−8. It has no nonzero section, by the product formula for H0. Hence this bare cap cannot match even after an arbitrary effective triple divisor is supplied.

A parabolic Inoue cap with n=8 instead has an elliptic axis normal N of degree−8. Its parameter κ changes the flat factor, so, after fixing the elliptic identification, N can be chosen isomorphic to L^−1. Any class admits a sufficiently small unit representative modulo a^Z if needed for an embedding. The product cap S8×P1 then has normal L^−1⊠O on D, and the triple section E×{p} supplies O⊠O(1). The product of the two normal lines and that triple-divisor line is exactly trivial.

This is **first-order normal matching only**. A third component must meet the two existing components along surfaces whose common curve is E, and all its own normal and higher-overlap data must agree. For example a quadric containing E becomes a surface Q≅P1×P1 in Bl_E P3 with trivial normal (since O_Q(2) is cancelled by E of bidegree(2,2)); it meets D in a section. On the parabolic product side the corresponding surface is S8×{p}, also with trivial normal. Producing a compact third threefold with these two divisors and the required neighborhoods is still missing. D-semistability alone does not supply that threefold or a smoothing.

If the original toric component has other conductor divisors meeting E, their induced vertical triple fibres on D must also be included. Positive E-degree contributions must be balanced explicitly; they cannot be discarded. Likewise the q=s^n ramification used in the auxiliary Kato family is not automatically a permitted global change of the original base. These are current construction gaps.

## 7. Primary-source audit

Opened the published primary PDF `https://www.numdam.org/item/JEP_2022__9__1347_0.pdf` and the coauthor PDF `https://webusers.imj-prg.fr/~matteo.ruggiero/pub/pdf/istrati-otiman-pontecorvo-torickato.pdf`. ## Detailed development record: ab_push_rank_one_axis_attempt.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Rank-one axial comparison: current attempt and exact missing lemma

This is an unproved working route, not a numbered result. It addresses the residual gap after the marked-graph audit in ab_push_marked_graph_scope.md.

Fix an ordinary q value. The actual two-torus periods may be written u=(tau,mu), w=(1,0), delta=(0,1), and gamma=(6mu,C-12v), with v=log(r)/(2pi i). The monodromy image is the line spanned by delta; its kernel is spanned by u,w,delta. The residual elliptic torus is Gr^W_1, with lattice spanned by u,w after quotienting delta.

There is no holomorphic projection from the ordinary simple two-torus to this residual elliptic torus: zeta_1 changes by 6mu under gamma, and 6P is not a lattice point. A putative residual quotient of an arbitrary cap must therefore be constructed from its limiting geometry; it cannot be the restriction of a generic torus quotient.

The current full-rank two-section route is being developed by classification_e and boundary_d. It does not automatically cover this rank-one axis. A local semistable meridian lies in the specialization kernel, and in particular in the monodromy-invariant lattice; this alone leaves the two residual elliptic directions. The missing statement is either that these meridians lie in im(N) over Q, or another analytic mechanism bounding the residual elliptic part of differences of two sections extending into the same cap.

One intermediate route to check is the following. Suppose the normalized relative one-form alpha=d zeta_1 has already been shown meromorphic as a section of Omega^1(log D)/O dlog r on a semistable surface degeneration. Its periods on all flat cycles are bounded. If alpha had a minimal pole r^(-m), m>0, then r^m alpha would have zero limiting periods on compact loops in every normalization component and zero residues on conductor meridians. The restrictions would consequently be holomorphic one-forms with zero periods on compact surfaces, hence vanish. Vanishing on the reduced entire D would make this locally free logarithmic section divisible by r, contradicting minimality. This suggests removal of poles without a Kahler hypothesis.

Two gaps must not be concealed. First, the initial meromorphic extension of the specifically period-normalized alpha has not been derived merely from coherence of the direct image; its coefficients in a coherent frame could in principle have essential singularities unless their periods are controlled. Second, even a holomorphic logarithmic extension of alpha need not have zero residues when m=0. A local meridian with a residual elliptic component would have a finite nonzero alpha period. Thus the pole-removal argument does not prove the required logarithmic Jacobian comparison.

The generic simplicity supplies an additional exact constraint: if two independent rational lattice vectors span a decomposable two-torus class, their period against the holomorphic volume form is nonzero. Otherwise their complex periods would be collinear, giving an elliptic subtorus. It is not yet proved that a forbidden residual meridian produces such a two-torus on which the volume period must vanish. The possible failure of invariant-cycle equality in non-Kahler degenerations must be retained; one cannot replace this missing construction by an unqualified Clemens--Schmid argument.

The B.4.15 regular-place insertion is not a counterexample to a one-parameter rank-one two-section theorem: its Hopf normalization components are vertical threefolds over a two-dimensional base origin, and a generic one-dimensional slice has a cycle of elliptic ruled surfaces. Conversely, its existence shows that a classification of transverse one-parameter surface limits alone cannot identify every vertical fourfold cap.

Current conclusion: the intrinsic marked q-map extends, but a comparison between an arbitrary rank-one axial completion and the prescribed log Jacobian remains unproved.

## Detailed development record: ab_push_regular_cap_working.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Actual regular-place torus germ: a central Hopf insertion

Owner: limits_ab. 15November2026,18:29UTC. **Working proof for reserved B.4.15; not yet integrated or entered in the result registry.** This construction preserves the actual punctured local family at a regular point of the elliptic base. It does not repair the global component-cover obstruction B.3.1. In particular it is not a completed S6 filling.

## Precise proposed statement

Choose a small simply connected disc Δ_z centred at a regular point q0 of the elliptic base, avoiding its three special values. After the already specified cover w=r^6, let X→Δ_z×Δ_r be its standard twelve-cycle rank-one semistable model. Its restriction over r≠0 is the prescribed local family of two-tori, including the actual affine family after its local holomorphic origin is chosen.

There is another proper model X_new→Δ_z×Δ_r, isomorphic to X away from the base point (z,r)=(0,0), with smooth total fourfold and reduced normal-crossings divisor r=0. The composite map to Δ_r is flat. The map to the bidisc is deliberately not asserted flat: its fibre over (0,0) contains new compact threefold components.

Each of its twelve new vertical normalization components is either a P1-bundle over one scalar Hopf surface or the blow-up of that P1-bundle along two disjoint elliptic curves. In particular every new vertical normalization has b1=1 and fails the ddbar lemma. The generic punctured two-tori are exactly those of X, so generic simplicity is preserved. Thus generic simplicity does not exclude such non-ddbar components in a two-parameter local degeneration. This does not assert an analogous result for a one-parameter degeneration of compact surfaces.

## 1. Actual translation group and its cocharacter

On the chosen disc write the fixed rank-three period subsystem as

u(z)=(τ(z),μ(z)), w=(1,0), δ=(0,1).

Its quotient

G_z=C²/(Z u(z)+Z w+Z δ)

is the semiabelian complex Lie group extending the elliptic curve E_z=C/(Z+τ(z)Z) by C*_δ. In multiplicative coordinates it is the punctured degree-zero line bundle determined by the actual period μ. No product splitting and no torsion assumption on that line is made.

The map

χ_z:C*→G_z,  χ_z(t)=[u(z) Log(t)/(2πi)]

is single-valued: changing the logarithm adds the actual lattice period u(z). It varies holomorphically with z. The r-monodromy fixes u, so no r-branch ambiguity occurs. Its projection to E_z is [τ(z)Log(t)/(2πi)]. The map into a generic compact two-torus may have nonclosed image; no closed elliptic subgroup is used.

The group G acts on the whole standard rank-one twelve-cycle model. To verify rather than assume this, each normalization component is the equivariant compactification

R_z=G_z×^(C*_δ)P1=P_Ez(O⊕L_z).

Translation in G preserves its zero and infinity sections. The smoothing glues adjacent line directions by their natural dual pairing, with local equation xy=r. The closing translation is a chosen element of G over 6P multiplied by the scalar r^12; all G translations commute with it. Hence the action descends to the generic quotient and extends to all twelve components and their plumbing charts. On a node chart it preserves the two branches; a defining function x of either branch is carried to a holomorphic unit times x, with the elliptic coordinate translated. This is the same semiabelian construction already used in B.1; no elliptic-product model is substituted.

The local actual affine family can be compared with its Jacobian before making this construction. Over Δ_z×Δ_r*, the lattice local system has cohomological dimension one and H²=0. The relative Lie sheaf is coherent and the base is Stein, so the exponential sequence and CartanB give H¹ of the torus-section sheaf equal to zero. Thus the actual affine torsor admits a holomorphic section on this local punctured base. Choosing it gives the required holomorphic comparison. This is only a punctured local comparison; it is not asserted to extend over r=0.

## 2. Blow up the base and resolve the actual node equations

Let β:B~→Δ_z×Δ_r be the blow-up at (0,0), with exceptional curve F≅P1. Pull back X. Near a double curve of the twelve-cycle, the only new singularity occurs in the chart r=zs and has equation

xy=zs.

There is one further smooth elliptic coordinate, suppressed in this display. Label the twelve original central components cyclically by i mod12. At every node choose x=0 to be its even-indexed branch. Blow up the ideal (x,z). Globally this is the sum of the pulled-back invertible ideal of that central component and the invertible ideal O_B~(−F). It is G-invariant because G preserves the component and acts over the base. Distinct even components give disjoint non-Cartier loci, so the six small resolutions are compatible.

The two charts are

z=xQ, y=Qs, r=xQs,

and

x=zX, s=Xy, r=zXy.

They are smooth and make r a product of three distinct coordinates. On the other base chart z=rζ, the pulled-back equation is xy=r and the same ideal is (x,r)=(x), so no modification occurs there. Away from the node loci the ideal is likewise invertible. Consequently the resulting total fourfold is smooth and r=0 is reduced normal crossings, including at F∩strict(r=0).

For later identification retain the component chart calculation. Before the small resolution, the four branches over r=0 are

V_x={x=z=0}, V_y={y=z=0}, H_x={x=s=0}, H_y={y=s=0}.

Here V denotes vertical over F and H denotes the strict horizontal branch. In the resolution, V_x is the blow-up of its former normalization at the elliptic curve over y=s=0; V_y is unchanged. H_y is the corresponding curve blow-up, and H_x is unchanged. This follows explicitly from (Q,s)→(y=Qs,s) and (X,y)→(y,s=Xy) on V_x; these are the two blow-up charts of (y,s). The same calculation applies at both neighbours of each even component.

Therefore vertical even components undergo two disjoint elliptic-curve blow-ups; vertical odd components undergo none. Horizontal odd components undergo the corresponding two curve blow-ups, and horizontal even components undergo none. This is an identification, not an inference from Betti numbers.

## 3. Cartier twisting and its properness

Take the line bundle M=O_B~(F). Choose a local trivializing cover with transition units g_ij. Glue the resolved pulled-back models over that cover by the automorphisms χ_z(g_ij). They satisfy the cocycle identity because χ_z is a group homomorphism. This is Fujimoto's Definition1.1 Cartier-logarithm construction, applied to an actual analytic semiabelian translation action; the displayed formulas prove its applicability here without requiring his principal-elliptic hypothesis.

The action lifts to the small resolutions because each ideal used above is invariant. Alternatively one can twist first and then blow up the descended ideals; the results agree. The glued analytic space is Hausdorff: over every base chart it is the original Hausdorff proper model, while points over distinct base points are separated in the base. Properness is local on the target and therefore follows from properness of the resolved pullbacks. Composition with the proper base blow-up makes X_new→Δ_z×Δ_r proper.

The canonical divisor section of O(F) is nonvanishing on B~\F and trivializes the twist there. Together with the isomorphism β off (0,0), it gives a specified holomorphic identification with the original model on the complement of that point, including every r≠0 fibre. The local action is needed only near F; the same trivialization allows this operation to be inserted into any larger model already standard at q0, without extending u around the special points of the elliptic base.

All charts still have r as a product of distinct coordinates, so r=0 is reduced SNC. The map to the r-disc is flat: the smooth local rings are torsion-free modules over the one-dimensional regular local base, equivalently r is a nonzero divisor. Properness over the r-disc itself follows only when this local replacement is inserted into a model whose q-base is compact. Locally we assert properness over the bidisc, as in the statement.

## 4. Identify the new compact components

Before the small resolutions, the normalization of the preimage of F consists of twelve copies of R_(q0)×F. After twisting, each becomes

V_i^0=O_P1(−1)^××^(C*)R_(q0),

because O(F)|F=O_P1(−1). The structure homomorphism is χ_(q0). Passing to the elliptic quotient gives a principal E_(q0)-bundle H→P1 whose Chern cycle is primitive ±u.

This surface is explicitly a scalar Hopf surface. Put τ0=τ(q0), η=−1/τ0 and a'=exp(2πiη), so |a'|<1. After dividing the elliptic additive coordinate by τ0, the projection of χ is [Log(t)/(2πi)] in C/(Z+ηZ). Its kernel is (a')^Z. Hence

H=O_P1(−1)^×/(a')^Z=(C²\{0})/〈a'·id〉.

The principal G-bundle O(−1)^××^(C*)G maps to H with fibre C*_δ. Associated compactification of this C* fibre by P1 shows that V_i^0 is an actual P1-bundle over H, with two global conductor sections. It can equivalently be written P_H(O⊕M_H) for the line bundle determined by that principal C* bundle. Its restriction to each elliptic fibre of H retains the original nontrivial degree-zero semiabelian line L_(q0).

The two conductor sections intersect the fibre over the distinguished point F∩strict(r=0) in two disjoint elliptic curves. By the charts in §2, the final vertical even components are exactly the blow-ups of V_i^0 along those two curves; the final odd components equal V_i^0. Since π1(P1)=0, each V_i^0 has π1(H)=Z. Blowing up smooth curves in a smooth threefold preserves π1 by the already proved modification argument, so every final vertical normalization has b1=1. A compact ddbar manifold has even b1; these components therefore fail ddbar.

All translations used in twisting preserve the twelve component labels. Thus this construction leaves the rotations +4,−3,0 of the original attempted global assembly unchanged. It does not solve B.3.1 and cannot be inserted as an unexplained correction there. Its positive result is a fully specified local replacement of the actual regular-place punctured torus germ with non-ddbar central components, even when those punctured tori are generically simple.

## Verification still requested

Independent referee should check the global G action on the rank-one plumbing (especially the closing translation), the local affine-Jacobian comparison over Δ×Δ*, and the component chart identifications above. No new primary theorem beyond the already opened semiabelian construction, CartanB and Fujimoto's explicit cocycle is being imported. Exact chart and primitive-Chern calculations will be saved separately.

## Detailed development record: ab_push_untwisted_sanity.md

Status language in this record is preserved from its writing; use the current outcome table and numbered registry for the final disposition.

# Sanity check: why B.4.33 does not exclude the untwisted Engel filling

**Date:** 15 November 2026. **Status:** exact comparison of the already constructed Part A family with the proposed B.4.33 obstruction. Requested by root during the final referee. No new independent theorem number is assigned.

## The linear local system does not distinguish the two cases

Over the ordinary base, the untwisted Engel two-tori have the same period matrix `Pi_c` and linear peripheral matrices `A1,A2` as the actual sphere family. The quotient `V=Lambda_Q/Q delta` consequently has the same zero common fixed space. In particular it would be wrong to explain Part A's proper filling by inventing a nonzero invariant vector in V.

The identities `Pi_(c-12)=Pi_c(I-12E)` and `[A_j,E]=0` hold for both families. The finite-order matrices are the Jacobian monodromy, which is unaffected by an affine logarithmic transform. They do not by themselves determine the family of affine torsors or its parameter-closing maps.

## The untwisted finite collars permit identity closing

For the untwisted family the finite logarithmic-transform vectors are zero. The period-family finite actions are linear, and the root-to-ordinary affine collars have `sigma_(j,c)=0` in these group presentations. More generally any base line-bundle frame factors are independent of c and hence of the degeneration logarithm. Therefore the identity Lie-coordinate map under `c -> c-12` is compatible with the finite root charts as well as the ordinary torus quotient. The actual Part A family is defined directly with the single-valued multiplier `u`, so this is also clear from its construction.

After `u=r^12`, the r-central twelve-cycle labels have no finite-place translation shifts. Their local transport around the two finite places and the cusp is `(0,0,0)`, unlike the sphere family's `(4,-3,-1)`. The ordinary scalar closing can be chosen as `K_untwisted=1`.

There can still be base-dependent changes of line-bundle frame: `O_S(P-O)` need not be globally rigidified along the zero section. Such changes are holomorphic units in the base and independent of r. They have zero radial-loop class and do not create a gamma contribution. Thus this comparison does not assume that the compact global C*-bundle itself has a globally chosen nonvanishing vector over its zero section.

## Why the same closing fails for the sphere twists

The sphere collars are instead

`sigma_(j,c) = w_j Pi_c v_j`, with `gamma(v_1)=1` and `gamma(v_2)=-1`.

If one attempted the identity ordinary closing under `c -> c-12`, its induced root-chart translation would be

`sigma_(j,c)-sigma_(j,c-12) = 12 epsilon_j w_j delta`.

In the coordinate `W=exp(-2 pi i zeta_2)`, this is multiplication by `s_j^(-12 epsilon_j)`: a pole at one root centre and a zero at the other. It is not an invertible extension of the finite-place family. The actual coherent sphere closing supplies the scalar `K(t)=t^4/(t-1)^3`; its root representatives `K s1^-12` and `K s2^12` are units. Their divisor orders on the marked base are `(4,-3,-1)`. The cusp order -1 is exactly the retained affine logarithmic-transform imbalance `p=-1` in the prescribed conventions.

This distinction is not removable by reusing the sphere closing in Part A: that closing, without the sphere collar corrections, has zeros or poles at the untwisted finite places and is not an automorphism of the untwisted completed family.

## The untwisted cusp has a common extending zero

The untwisted two-parameter cusp is the standard model built in A.1--A.2 with period multipliers `(q c1,c2)` and `(c3,u q c4)`. The ordinary toric zero `(Z,W)=(1,1)` extends simultaneously on both axes. In the unsubdivided node chart

`xy=u z v`, `q=zv`,

its section is explicitly `x=1`, `z=1`, `v=q`, `y=u q`. This lies away from the singular locus, so it lifts unchanged through the chosen smooth subdivisions and through normalized coordinate-root base changes. Any harmless period-unit or line-frame adjustment gives the corresponding unit section.

Choose this same origin on the two axial restrictions. Then `sigma_untwisted=o_untwisted` and the axial clutching section is `F_untwisted=0`. Its radial gamma label is zero. More generally an r-independent change of the two local frames gives an r-independent clutching section, again with zero radial gamma label.

For the sphere family the actual coherent parameter descent instead gives the sigma of B.4.9 and the corrected rotating origin of B.4.18. Their difference is the nonzero F of B.4.24, with radial period jump gamma. This exact F is part of the sphere affine data; it is not a formula for the untwisted family.

## Result of the stress test

The global-meridian and loop-invariance mechanisms are compatible with Part A: its extending local sections have normalized loop defect zero. The last B.4.33 contradiction does not arise because the untwisted clutching gamma class is zero, so the corresponding equality is `0=0`, not `0=Dn`.

The actual separation is therefore:

| Datum | Untwisted Engel family | Actual sphere family |
|---|---|---|
| Ordinary linear matrices | A1, A2 | A1, A2 |
| Common fixed space in V | zero | zero |
| Finite affine twist vectors | zero, zero | epsilon, minus epsilon-prime |
| Compatible radial scalar closing | 1 | K=t^4/(t-1)^3 |
| Cusp scalar order | 0 | -1 |
| Axial clutching radial gamma label | 0 | 1; n after r=R^n |
| Consequence | Proper toroidal fillings in A are consistent | Positive trace degree retains the B.4.33 obstruction |

The exact period, collar, divisor-order, and common-zero identities are checked in `code/ab_push_untwisted_sanity.py`, with saved output and JSON. This note uses the explicitly constructed Part A family and the fully audited C.3.5/B.4.19 formulas; it does not infer an affine filling merely from common linear monodromy.

# Root referee of A.2.5 and A.4.9

Date: 15 November 2026. Both proposed statements were read in full and accepted within their explicitly marked finite toroidal category.

For A.2.5, the new projective-three-space component supplies an actual counterexample to the proposed universal blow-up shape: a degree-one holomorphic map to R would inject its rank11 second cohomology into rank1. The replacement algorithm uses a finite marked strict cone complex and ordinary smooth stars and inverse stars. Its termination is supplied by weak factorization of each proper subdivision map. It does not invoke strong factorization. The chart substitutions determine multiplicities and exceptional projective bundles, and the cotangent-complex formulation correctly handles nonreduced central equations.

For A.4.9, the exceptional Gysin maps and powers of the tautological class give an integral derived sheaf map whose stalk basis is1,−ξ,…,−ξ^(c−1). Thus the splitting follows from a stalkwise quasi-isomorphism, not from equality of ranks. Proper base change restricts it to the actual inverse-image central support, including horizontal centres. The alternating local-branch resolution is integral; its orientation local system records branch permutations without division by a factorial. The explicit coordinate-projective-space restrictions explain the same unit cancellations in the normalization resolution. This is a direct central-space computation and is independent of first computing cohomology of the ambient neighbourhood.

The resulting finite algorithm supplies the requested second computation for arbitrary input fans. The proof does not claim an applicable polarized limiting mixed Hodge structure: the separately stated non-Kähler limitation of Clemens–Schmid remains necessary.

# Independent root check of the E.2.7 working draft

The draft was read on 15 November 2026 before checkpoint05. It is not yet input into the draft19 PDF or included in its112-statement registry. The following review accepts its conditional conclusion; integration is planned after the checkpoint freeze.

The extra hypothesis is essential: the intrinsic maps of the punctured known family extend to a relative holomorphic map whose central restriction is nonconstant. E.2.6 supplies this for positive canonical slope. The result does not assert that every smooth specialization has that property.

On a proper submersion to the disc, the rank-deficient locus of the relative fibration is analytic. Its proper image meets the central target curve in finitely many critical values, so it has no vertical target-curve component. The three labelled punctured critical branches extend as degree-one finite curves after normalization. Collisions cause poles in the cross-ratio gauge but do not cause an essential singularity. A fixed central regular target value therefore defines an actual proper smooth surface family over the full disc. Its noncentral fibres are the ordinary tori, so its H1 monodromy is the identity.

The corresponding marked base coordinate is meromorphic. Its small loop is null-homotopic or conjugate to a power of one peripheral generator. The classifying parameter is well defined by the fixed-data shift group2kl Z; translations in the family comparisons act trivially on torus H1. A zero of order d would contribute S_{-h}, h=2kld>0. The two finite peripheral matrices commute with this shear and have orders3 and4, so a finite peripheral power cannot cancel it. At the cusp, N(u)=w and N(gamma)=-delta. The identity I+nN-hE first forces n=0 on u and then h=0 on gamma. Conjugation does not affect this argument because the monodromy group preserves E. Thus a zero parameter is impossible.

Boundedness supplies a holomorphic extension of the normalized period, and the maximum-modulus principle excludes a value on the outer circle. This conclusion does not require a separatedness theorem. An interior period limit still does not identify the central complex manifold with the existing model at that period.

The exact matrix program was independently rerun and passed; its output is code/root_e_push_E_zero_end_rerun.out. The analytic and geometric steps above are not inferred from that program. The general deformation-chain question remains Open.

# Purity at an isolated base point with a good torus Jacobian

15 November 2026, seventh working hour. Root proof; E independently supplied a simplification of the isotropy step. Full candidate source is E_torus_purity.tex, reserved statement E.2.11. Integration awaits full-source referee.

The precise hypotheses are a proper flat map from a smooth (n+2)-fold to a bidisc, connected n-dimensional fibres, a smooth proper holomorphic torus group G over the entire bidisc, and an actual G-torsor structure outside the origin. No section of that torsor is needed. The conclusion is that the map is smooth and the torsor action extends across the origin.

The argument extends the invariant vertical vector fields coefficientwise by Hartogs across the codimension-two fibre. Compact fibres give completeness; period identities extend by holomorphic uniqueness. A fixed real torus acts by holomorphic automorphisms because each real linear combination of the holomorphic period vectors is a holomorphic Lie section. The identity component of any stabilizer is a compact complex subgroup of the central torus. Its representation on the scheme-fibre Zariski tangent is holomorphic, hence trivial; it is trivial on the quotient mapping to the base tangent. Compact averaging splits the representation and then averages holomorphic coordinates to show that the group would act trivially near the point. This contradicts freeness on the punctured base.

The independent vertical fields give local orbit/transverse coordinates by their commuting flows and the inverse function theorem. The transverse map of surfaces has isolated fibres by equidimensionality, hence is finite. Its Jacobian, if zero, gives a curve of critical values. This excludes a sole critical value at the bidisc origin. The finite-stabilizer argument is repeated after smoothness to establish a torsor, not merely a submersion.

The smooth extension of G is essential. It is being verified from the actual source period coordinates in E.2.12, not inferred merely from trivial monodromy. B.4.15 does not contradict the lemma because that model is not jointly flat. No general discriminant-purity claim is made for arbitrary proper flat maps, which can have discriminant of higher codimension.

The primary-literature searches are recorded in root_E_torus_purity_search1 and root_E_torus_purity_search2 retrieval metadata. Queries included complex tori with pure discriminant, torus fibrations and codimension two, holomorphic torus isolated critical values, and Hartogs extension of torus actions. No exact theorem matching these analytic hypotheses was found. No priority is claimed. The proof supplies its compact-group linearization by averaging and its transverse branch argument by an explicit Jacobian divisor, rather than importing a groupoid slice theorem or algebraic purity theorem.

# Tenth-hour main-filling audit

This is the current mathematical audit, begun after checkpoint09. The checkpoint was saved at 21:39 UTC with the clean 220-page draft33 and 150 statements. Its full manifest and archive integrity were checked. The later results described below were not included in that immutable checkpoint.

## Fixed-partial-family noncompletion, B.4.30

Root read the complete working proof and final TeX. The decisive analytic input is the ordinary log-de Rham comparison of Illusie, Kato and Nakayama (2005), Theorem6.3, printed p.34, with Theorem6.2's hypotheses on p.32. Root independently opened and read both exact statements. The2007erratum concerns algebraic Theorem7.1, which is not used. The degree-one exact sequence makes the space of closed logarithmic forms saturated in the canonical extension, since the reduced connected central fibre makes R1 f_*O free of parameter torsion. It therefore equals the explicitly known extended rank-two form subbundle. Local angular periods lie in the primitive invariant lattice at a maximal degeneration. The integer-weight power-series argument gives convergent logarithmic characters. A finite chart chain compares two sections that both extend to one cap; it does not compare either individually with an arbitrary externally chosen origin.

Root requested two precision corrections before integration: equality after localization must follow from coherent quotients supported on the parameter divisor, and the multisection-splitting root and multiplicity-clearing root must be combined before the final toric subdivision. A product of their bounds suffices. Both corrections were made and the final proof passed independent D review.

The attempted dependence on the full analytic flattening theorem was replaced by an explicit weaker construction. Root opened Parusiński's original MPI/90-58 manuscript, Corollary1.2 and its full coefficient-ideal proof, manuscript pp.3–5. An excess-one local finite projection has hypersurface image F(y,z)=0. Blowing up the ideal of the z-series coefficients, then dividing by its local generator, makes every fibre equation nonzero. The image equation lies in the extended coefficient ideal by the separated z-adic completion, so this is convergent analytic division. A finite product of coefficient ideals handles the compact origin fibre simultaneously. The ideals are units off the origin, since the given punctured map is already equidimensional. A further smooth surface base modification is dominated by point blowups. Normalized finite slices are Cohen–Macaulay surfaces and hence finite flat over that smooth base; no flatness of the entire transformed source is needed.

Root also opened Enokizono and Hashizume's original arXiv2404.05124v3, Lemma2.14 and its proof, pp.6–7. That toroidal subdivision statement is used within its analytic scope. Their projective main semistable-reduction theorem is not applied to an arbitrary non-Kähler cap. In the transverse surface case the height-one slices have dimension at most two; ordered star insertions of all lattice points produce compatible empty, hence unimodular, triangles.

The multiplied trace and Hartogs construction, including the constant period lattice Y ambiguity, then yields the torsor forbidden by B.4.27. B.4.30 concerns the actual fixed partial model B.4.19. It was not silently enlarged to arbitrary fillings.

## Global meridians, B.4.31

Root proposed the finite-orbit mechanism and then read AB's full written proof. D independently passed it. Over the complement of finitely many marked base values, the compact normalized ordered double-branch spaces are proper smooth curve families with finitely many connected-component labels. Their normal annular circles have well-defined H1 classes up to finite sheet choices and sign. Moving a base point along a connected open double curve changes a based representative only by conjugation; it cannot add a tangential H1 class. Transport along finitely many generators of the base fundamental group can be performed at one sufficiently small nonzero parameter. It realizes the actual Gauss–Manin transport and permutes the finite set of circle classes.

Parameter monodromy is unipotent I-m δ⊗γ, so a local angular class with finite parameter-monodromy orbit is in its kernel. On the quotient of that kernel by Zδ, the two peripheral matrices contain unipotents with distinct fixed lines. No nonzero vector has finite orbit. Thus every local angular image is an integral multiple of the primitive δ. Pairwise angular circles span the higher-crossing angular homology rationally, and primitivity restores the integral conclusion. Multiplicities created by graph resolution are retained and handled directly.

The initial smooth semistable hypothesis was then removed with an explicit reduction. The intrinsic marked-map proof B.4.4 only requires a smooth dominating proper fourfold: r is a nonzero divisor, so its direct-image sheaf is torsion-free. An arbitrary proper analytic filling has a unique reduced main component containing the prescribed punctured family. Normalize and resolve it, unchanged over the puncture, then apply the marked pencil argument and resolve its graph and central support. No global semistable-reduction theorem in relative dimension three is needed.

## Global loop defect, B.4.32

Root read the full working proof and final TeX, after AB and E also checked the central mechanism. For a Jacobian section, the parameter-loop connecting class lies in Λ/(T_r-I)Λ. Passing to V=Λ_Q/Qδ removes lift ambiguity. Restriction under r=R^e multiplies this class by e in V, so division by the root degree gives the correct invariant for local extending sections. Additivity gives the corresponding degree normalization for a finite trace.

The reference origins are those of the actual partial family B.4.19. Its ordinary overlaps have the scalar n v δ term. The exact finite-root connector becomes w_j Π_part v_j after the c=-12v cancellation, so it has no parameter-loop defect. The punctured cusp connector is G(s)+6vδ and has only a δ defect. The Lie-frame transformations are parameter independent and fix the δ column. Root checked their first-row period covariance directly; the exact full symbolic checks are retained by the agents. Thus these are consistent reference loop classes without a claim that the reference origins glue as points.

B.4.31 makes the local residue vector of every unknown cap equal to (0,integer). The saturation and chart-chain argument then compares any two extending cap sections by O* times M_r^gp. Their difference has zero class in V, even when the sections meet different central components. The normalized defect therefore patches over the global ordinary marked base. The fixed lines of the two actual quotient matrices are Q(1,2,-4) and Q(1,3,-3), with zero intersection. The global defect is zero. This does not bound a common essential translation; only its period defect is used in the final candidate argument.

## Current literature check

On15November2026 root repeated searches for complex-six-sphere degeneration, filling and semistable-limit results, including recent work following Engel. Root reopened the original arXiv submission records for Engel2609.38442 and Viaclovsky2610.39102; each displayed only its first version. Root reopened Engel's original PDF and read Remark8.1, Conjecture8.2 and the subsequent six-sphere limit guess, printed p.22, as well as the adjacent Seifert-quotient discussion. The searches did not locate a published arbitrary-filling nonexistence theorem. This is a search record, not a claim of priority or exhaustive literature coverage.

The unrelated Hopf-compactification caution was checked in Abasheva and Déev's original arXiv2303.10764, Sections2.1–2.4. Their non-torsion degree-zero elliptic line spaces can have different analytic compactifications. That result supplies no prescribed two-parameter smoothing or affine comparison for this task. It was useful in preventing an unsupported residual-elliptic-quotient argument. Full retrieved texts and external scans are excluded from the deliverables; source metadata is in evidence/root_tenth_hour_sources.json.

## Arbitrary-filling nonexistence, B.4.33: full source PASS

Root read the complete written proof and the final uniformity and cusp-origin clarifications. D and AB independently read the full source and passed it; E authored and checked the construction. B.4.33 constructs an associated A-torsor from multiplied cap traces over the whole point-modified local cusp bidisc. Generic exceptional and q-axis divisors use the full-rank comparison; the unknown strict r-axis uses B.4.31. Hartogs fills the remaining isolated points. H1(S,A)=0 then supplies an ordinary origin for a positive contracted power of the actual torsor. On the strict r-axis its γ loop component is zero by B.4.32; along the actual q-axis its γ loop component is zero by the injective ghost-valuation argument of B.4.24. The exact difference F has γ component n after r=R^n. Thus the two zeros would give 0=Dn for positive integers D,n, which is impossible.

The all-S torsor construction is essential. It does not identify an unknown r-axis cap with the reference fan. The compact set is K=β⁻¹(0), including the no-blowup case K={0}. Finitely many finite multisections on a neighbourhood of K cover the inverse image of a smaller bidisc by properness. The roots are local transverse roots of one common positive degree; a global root along the boundary curve is not assumed. Finitely many divisor multiplicities and bounded multisection ramification degrees make one norm multiplier sufficient. Other branch-curve intersections and proper-lift indeterminacies are isolated, and the stated Hartogs argument treats them. Along the actual cusp the cap still maps properly to the prescribed fan, so the σ comparison follows directly from its unit characters and finite trace norms without asserting an unqualified global lift through every modification.

Root specifically stress-tested the result against the proper untwisted Engel filling of Part A. AB and D independently confirmed that the linear matrices and their zero common invariant space in V are the same in both problems. The distinction is the actual affine closing: the untwisted family closes c→c−12 by the identity and has local cusp comparison F=0, whereas the six-sphere twists force the scalar K=t^4/(t−1)^3. Its cusp order is −1 and the resulting exact comparison has nonzero γ-loop component. Reusing the twisted F for the untwisted family would be an error; the proof does not do so. The explicit independent calculation and note are retained.

After this complete written-source review, the status changes at 22:08:45 UTC resolved B.4.1, B.5.1 and C.5.1 by refuting their proper analytic filling premise. The central topology, least exponent and analytic Chern data are undefined, not zero. The two constructed smooth-monodromy computations and smooth spin-c coboundary remain valid separate results. The exact editorial changes are recorded in code/final_main_status_changes.json. Earlier provisional statements remain in the immutable checkpoints and retained historical versions.
